# Homeplot — Development Plan (Main Reference Doc)

**This is THE main development plan.** Every future Claudia dispatch and every future planning conversation should start here. It supersedes the earlier one-off `Homeplot_Development_Plan.pdf` (generated once, sent to the teacher, never stored in the repo — the exact problem this file exists to fix: a doc Claudia is told to "reference" has to actually live somewhere Claudia can read it on every dispatch, not a PDF that only ever reached the teacher's inbox).

**Built for:** K-8 students with high support needs (autism, ADHD, dyslexia, communication disorders).
**Owner/teacher:** Kayden, Vermont Literacy Network.
**Stack:** React + React Three Fiber (3D world), Zustand + Supabase (Postgres, Storage, realtime sync), Vercel (hosting, auto-deploy on push to `main`).

**How to use this doc with Claudia:** Part A is what already exists (build on it or revise it explicitly, don't re-propose it). Part B is the open backlog, organized by feature front, each pointing at its own doc where one exists — the best place to start a gameplay-design conversation. Part C is the teacher's own raw notes on the pets system and platformer, preserved close to verbatim. Part D is the standing design/safety/accessibility rules every new feature gets checked against.

## ⚠️ ACTION NEEDED FROM THE TEACHER — a database migration hasn't been run

**2026-10-01, production outage, hotfixed in code but not fully resolved:** saving any activity/question set started failing ("Could not find the 'student_description' column of 'activity_library' in the schema cache") because `supabase/schema.sql` already has `student_title`/`student_description` columns defined for the `activity_library` table (`alter table activity_library add column if not exists ...`), but that migration was never actually run against the live Supabase database — this sandbox has no Supabase credentials/CLI, so a schema.sql edit is local-only and never auto-applies to production. The code was hotfixed to stop sending those two fields so saving works again right now, but that means **a student's own title/description typed into an activity is silently not being saved** until this is fixed for real.

**What she needs to do, in the Supabase SQL Editor (already open in her browser per the screenshot that caught this):**
```sql
alter table activity_library add column if not exists student_title text;
alter table activity_library add column if not exists student_description text;
```
Once that's run, tell the next session (or this one) so the two fields can be added back to the save payload in `src/lib/sync.ts`'s `activityToRow`. This is the same class of bug flagged earlier this session as a standing lesson — a schema.sql migration being written doesn't mean it ran — and it's worth a one-time check of the rest of schema.sql's `alter table` statements against the live database for any other silently-unapplied columns, rather than waiting for each one to surface as its own outage.

**Also pending (2026-10-01 and 2026-10-04), same SQL Editor, both safe to run any time; until then the app works but these don't save to the database:**
- `chess_games` (Slime Chess personal leaderboard): the full `create table if not exists chess_games ...` block plus its policies (given in chat 2026-10-01; also in `supabase/schema.sql`).
- `style_looks` (Style: saved looks; the teacher's look also saves on her device meanwhile):
```sql
create table if not exists style_looks (
  owner_id text primary key,
  look jsonb not null default '{}',
  updated_at timestamptz not null default now()
);
alter table style_looks enable row level security;
drop policy if exists style_looks_select on style_looks;
create policy style_looks_select on style_looks for select to anon, authenticated using (true);
drop policy if exists style_looks_insert on style_looks;
create policy style_looks_insert on style_looks for insert to anon, authenticated with check (true);
drop policy if exists style_looks_update on style_looks;
create policy style_looks_update on style_looks for update to anon, authenticated using (true) with check (true);
drop policy if exists style_looks_delete on style_looks;
create policy style_looks_delete on style_looks for delete to authenticated using (true);
```

## PART A — Shipped Features (the platform as it exists today)

Organized into four groups per direct teacher instruction: **Game & Play**, **Educational/Academic**, **ABA/SEL/Behavioral**, and **Platform/Other**. Every individual feature gets its own entry: what it is, exactly how a student or teacher interacts with it, and why it exists. A feature that touches more than one group (pets, for example, are a play feature whose training system is also a real ABA design) is written up in full once, under whichever group is its primary purpose, and cross-referenced by name from the other group rather than repeated.

### GROUP 1: GAME & PLAY FEATURES

#### A1. The 3D World: Town Square

The open-air hub every student lands in after login (not a 2D task list first, by direct teacher instruction after reviewing an earlier version). A student walks their avatar around using a repositionable on-screen D-pad, keyboard arrows, or click/tap-to-walk with a hover-preview marker showing exactly where they'll land. The camera follows behind and above the avatar; a student can drag to look around, use explicit look buttons, and tilt the camera vertically, with movement speed adjustable per student (0.5x to 2x) in Settings. Every wandering character (Player, Neighbors, ambient Townspeople) is scaled to read as a real person (Kenney Mini Character models brought up to human height after an early version had everyone looking like ants on the lawn).

Every building in the square (Bank, Store, Post Office, Welcome Center, Computer Desk, Home, Pet Shelter, Island Dock, Cinema, Arcade, or a teacher-custom role/external link) can be walked up to and opened with a one-tap Confirm card; a "Closed/Coming Soon" role exists so an unfinished building reads as intentional, not broken. Collision is real: buildings block movement using their actual rotated footprint (not a generic circle), so a student can never clip through a wall. A Map view shows an overhead grid with double-click-to-teleport or double-click-to-open-a-building.

Navigation/HUD: a single top-right radial pie menu holds Tasks, What now?, Help/Break, Settings/Map/My Stuff (under "More"), My Home, and Companion, all in one place per direct teacher instruction (a deliberate, teacher-approved override of the general "Help must never be gated behind a menu" rule elsewhere in the app, see Part D). Clicking the player's own avatar opens the same pie ("master menu" behavior). A "What's New" changelog book (see A39) auto-opens the first time something new ships that affects a student.

A computer desk placed in the square is the task entry point (a retro browser-styled frame opens the student's daily rotation), so "go do my schoolwork" is a real in-world action, not a separate app section. The world-object catalog enforces a hard zero-weapons rule automatically. A Wizard ThunderSword prop is the visual/interaction surface for the Tier 3 regulation lock described in A28; Help/calm-down always stays reachable through it even while it's otherwise non-dismissable.

#### A2. Home Room (Private Room)

Each student's own private room, picked from a catalog of real-scaled house exteriors visible on their shared "home" building in Town Square. Opens in a read-only View mode by default with a real Build Mode toggle for decorating. Walls are drawn Sims-4-style (click, drag, release) into multi-room floor plans; doors and windows snap to walls. Students paint their own walls and floors, and place furniture/yard decor from the same catalog Build Mode uses (see A4), scoped privately to their own room only. The Pet Care panel (see A5) lives here: feed/pet/play/rename/set-companion/sell, with feelings-word tags and a save-confirmation flash so an action always visibly registers.

#### A3. Creative Island

A Minecraft-style free-build space with the full teacher-parity object catalog, reachable by boat from Town Square's dock or directly from Home Room. Locked by default; a teacher unlocks it per student (`islandBuildUnlocked`, see A40's Student Manager). Same real collision, View mode, and custom-role support as the rest of the world. Exists specifically so building/arranging (constructionist play, the thing Minecraft Education does well) has a home that isn't scoped to the confines of one student's own room.

#### A4. Build Mode / World Editor

The placement/decorating tool underlying Town Square, Home Room, and Creative Island. Catalog: 1,000+ real 3D models across dozens of packs, each with a generated thumbnail and a real-world size class (1.0 unit = player height; see `SIZE_REFERENCE.md`). A student or teacher places an object with a ghost preview, moves it with a crosshair/D-pad, rotates it in 90-degree steps, and resizes it either with size presets (Mini through Giant) or a free-typed exact number, with a live per-object unit readout. Undo/redo is available; the teacher's shared-world changes go through a Draft to Publish/Discard cycle (so students never see a half-finished edit); a student's own room/island changes are always live. A paint brush and bucket recolor ground, sky, and asset tint, with a half-tile grid for precision. Every asset can carry a custom role (see A1), and a teacher can retint, resize, move, or delete even the game's own original fixed layout (buildings, market stalls, road tiles), not just objects placed after the tool existed.

- **Stacking objects: overlap + height keys — SHIPPED 2026-10-01, direct teacher request: "in build mode, allow me to overlap build mode assets. if i press the 9 key, the object should decrease in height and if i press 0 it should raise in height so i can put objects on tables and such."** Root cause of "can't overlap": while a catalog item was armed for placing, every existing object's invisible tap box still caught the tap (and stopped the ghost from following the pointer), so nothing could ever be dropped onto a table; placed objects are now pass-through while placing (`WorldObjectRenderer`'s new `inert` prop) and catch taps normally again once placing ends. Height: **0 raises, 9 lowers** (0.1 units per press, Shift for 0.02 fine steps, never below the ground, max 20), acting on the armed ghost while placing (resets to the ground for each newly picked item) or on the selected object / multi-selection otherwise. Keyboard only, by direct teacher follow-up ("i dont want a raise/lower button thing. i just want to use keyboard controls"): the on-screen Raise/Lower buttons that briefly shipped were removed; the placing hint bar just reminds "0 raises, 9 lowers". Every move, duplicate, group rotate/resize/move, and drag now keeps an object's height instead of snapping it back to y=0, the footprint outline and toolbar float with a lifted object, and the height saves to the existing `world_objects.position` json (no migration needed). Town Square, Home Room and Island already render the saved height. Not added: the separate Island and Home Room editors still place on the ground only.
- **Selection rule + chess role fix — SHIPPED 2026-10-01, direct teacher instructions: "i only want more than one object selected if i click and hold shift while clicking the objects" and "i also cant put a chess game role assigned to the chess board."** A plain click now always selects exactly one object, even one that belongs to a group (it used to pull in every group member, which also swapped in the group toolbar that has no Name & role menu, the likely reason the Chess role couldn't be set on a grouped chess set). More than one object is selected only by Shift-clicking (Shift-click on a grouped object still adds its whole group). The ➕ Multi-select touch toggle was removed per the same instruction. Also, an object's invisible tap box no longer pokes up past the top of the model, so a table can't steal the tap from a chess set (or anything else) sitting on it. The Chess Set role itself (Name & role menu, "Chess Set (Slime Chess game)") was already present; no database change was needed.
- **Height keys follow-up + chess sets are always chess — SHIPPED 2026-10-01, teacher reports "its not raising/lowering my object" (in Town Square Build Mode) and "make sure the chess game role is able to add to the chess board too".** Reproduced in a real browser: the keys did work, but each 0.1 step was too small to see from the default camera angle (looking down from high up, a lift mostly reads as the object sliding slightly back). Steps are now 0.25 per press (Shift: 0.05), and a lifted object (or placing ghost) shows a green post down to the ground, a ground ring, and a "↕ 1.00" height tag so every press is visible. The camera-controls help panel now lists 9 / 0. Chess: any Chess Set model now gets the Slime Chess role automatically when placed, and an already-placed Chess Set with no role is treated as Slime Chess in Town Square and on Creative Island (`withDefaultRoles` in townLayout.ts); a teacher can still pick a different role for it on purpose.
- **Toolbar always in view + real overlap by dragging — SHIPPED 2026-10-01, teacher reports: "this tool bar is not working and it also needs to always be visable to the user, not in a corner, not off to the side. alwyas fully in view. also if im zoomed in the bar should be a reasonable size ... when im zoomed out it needs to also be bigger", "the toolbar buttons arnt clicking to work", and "i still cant put multiple assets in the same spot though".** Root causes: the selected-object / group / wall toolbar floated inside the 3D scene above the object (drei `Html` with distanceFactor), so it shrank when zoomed out, could slide off-screen with the object, and every tap on it also reached the 3D scene underneath (which could deselect or start other actions before the button registered). It is now a plain screen toolbar docked top-center of the Build Mode view (`ToolbarDock` in WorldEditor.tsx), rendered outside the 3D canvas: same size at every zoom level, always fully visible, popovers open straight down under it, and taps only hit the button. The old corner delete badge is gone (Delete lives on the toolbar). Overlap: objects were only pass-through while placing from the catalog; dragging a selected object onto another one was still blocked by the other object's tap box. Objects are now also pass-through mid-drag, and the release of a drag no longer counts as a "tap on empty ground" (which used to deselect the object right as you let go, so its toolbar vanished). Verified in a real browser: dragging one table onto another overlaps them, the toolbar stays up after the drag, Rotate right works from the dock, and a Chess Set placed with 0 pressed three times lands on top of the table at height 0.75 with the chess role.
- **Turn the Build Mode view from the laptop trackpad — SHIPPED 2026-10-01, direct teacher request: "i want to be able to rotate my fingers on the touchpad of my laptop to turn the view of what im seeing when im in build mode."** New `TrackpadRotate` in WorldEditor.tsx orbits the camera around the spot it's looking at (the same orbit right-drag does): a two-finger **twist** on a Mac trackpad in Safari (Safari's gesture events; Safari's pinch also zooms the scene now instead of the page), a two-finger **sideways swipe** on any trackpad in any browser (up/down still zooms), and a two-finger twist on a touch screen / iPad. The scene turns the same way the fingers move. Limitation, stated plainly: Chrome, Edge and Firefox never tell a web page about a trackpad twist (only Safari does), so in those browsers the sideways swipe is the gesture. The camera-controls help panel lists it. Not verified on a physical trackpad from this environment; if the turn direction feels backwards, flip the sign in `orbit(e.deltaX * 0.006)`.

#### A5. Pets System

A full adoptable-animal system: dogs, cats, small critters, farm animals, birds, aquatic animals, and wild animals (38 adoptable species across those categories, plus 6 held-back novelty characters; as of 2026-10-06 the whole system is paused and v2 is cats and dogs only, see Part B), each modeled at a real player-relative scale (a Great Dane reads noticeably bigger than a Chihuahua, a fish stays genuinely tiny). Every species is open to every student (no per-student scarcity) unless a future teacher tool locks one down.

**Pet Shelter** (Town Square building): every catalog pet is shown and pettable ("give a pat," a small animation) whether or not a student can afford it. A brand-new student has a one-time free-pet coupon: pick any pet, no cost, ever again. After that, pets are purchased directly with Class Cash, or pulled from the **Mystery Adoption Box** ($200, one open per real calendar day) — see A27 for its variable-ratio design. Up to 4 pets can be owned at once (`PET_OWNERSHIP_CAP`); the Shelter also offers a reward-free **Donate** option (see A35).

**Pet Journal**: a permanent, Pokédex-style log of every species a student has ever adopted, even ones later sold, filterable by category. An undiscovered species shows as a "???" silhouette. This is what makes "collect them all" mean something even though only 4 pets can be live-owned at once.

**Care loop** (Home Room): Food, Social, and Health bars (0-100), each paired with a plain-language feelings word at low and high values (e.g. Food low = "Hungry," high = "Full") rather than just a number. Stats decay softly, only while the student is actively in Town Square (never while they're away), floored at 20 so a pet is never neglected to zero, and pets never die. A student feeds, pets, and plays with their pet from a care card; a pet can be renamed anytime and sold back for 40% of its price (with a confirm-tap safety) if a student's 4-pet home is full.

**Companion/follow mechanic**: a pet unlocks "walk beside you" in Town Square only after 5 logged task completions (`PET_FOLLOW_TRAINING_THRESHOLD`), tracked by the same counter used for the ABA shaping ladder (see A26). Training comes exclusively from real learning, never from clicking care buttons repeatedly: every finished assignment is +1, and (since the 2026-10-06 pets review) every 10 right answers anywhere (native games, Quiz mode, quizzes, the gas pump) is +1 too. Only one pet can be the active companion at a time. Pets live in every Home Room room and the yard, react to care taps (hop/wiggle plus a feelings line), and can be tapped to open a training session (see Part B, Pets review).

**Growth stages**: a pet renders visibly smaller as a Baby (0-4 training), Juvenile (5-9), and full-size Adult (10+), using the same training counter, so "raising" a pet from puppy to adult is a real, free visual payoff of ordinary schoolwork, exactly per the teacher's original spec ("pets should start out as babies... and grow to be full adults with attention, love, and native games").

**Color customization**: a 12-swatch paint-brush picker on the pet's Home Room care card (the same palette Build Mode's own paint tool uses) recolors that pet's own model wherever it renders, in Home Room and following as a Town Square companion. Free, uncapped, reversible any time, purely cosmetic autonomy.

#### A6. Transportation System (Cars, Boats)

Full design in `TRANSPORTATION.md` and `BOATS_DESIGN.md`. Any placed car-model object is driveable: click to mount, real steering/gas/brake pedal physics (acceleration, braking, coasting friction, a real turning radius), click again to dismount with a confirm step. A dismounted car parks exactly where it was left. Boats use the same mount/dismount confirm-card pattern ("Board the boat?"/"Get off the boat?") but reuse the ordinary D-pad/WASD as throttle-and-turn rather than a dedicated pedal control surface (the design doc's explicit "no new control surface" rule), and stay within painted water via a gentle bump-and-slide boundary rather than a hard wall. Boats now have real wake VFX and synthesized engine/splash/contact-thud SFX (Phase 2b, see the dated entry below), plus a shared per-student vehicle-sound toggle and reuse of the existing Reduce Motion toggle for VFX (Phase 2d). Trains (Phase 3) are also now shipped: any placed locomotive is driveable strictly on-rail along teacher/student-placed track (straight and curve pieces, real connector-based Build Mode placement validation), Go/Stop/Reverse controls, same confirm-card mount/exit pattern, same chase camera, its own synthesized chug sound. **Planes and the Drone (Phase 4) are also now shipped**, see the dated entry below — this completes every vehicle type in `TRANSPORTATION.md`'s recommended build order. Not yet shipped: boats and cars aren't placeable on Creative Island yet.

- **2026-09-25, Planes and the Drone (Transportation Phase 4, the last vehicle type in the design's build order), teacher instruction "boats and other transportation."** Real, already-uploaded models confirmed against the asset manifest before starting: `public/world/models/vehicles/airplane-toy.glb` and `public/world/models/vehicles/drone.glb`. Per direct teacher instruction on record in `TRANSPORTATION.md` ("same mechanics/camera/controls as the plane"), the Drone is not a separate implementation — it shares 100% of the Plane's physics, camera, and controls (`isPlaneModel`/`isDroneModel` in `townLayout.ts`, both feeding the same `vehicleKind: 'plane'`), differing only in mount-card copy ("Fly the drone?") and its own model. Deliberately excluded from `isDroneModel`: the unrelated `scifi/Camera_Drone.glb` decorative prop, a different themed item under a different category, not verified as what "a drone" meant here.
  - **Flight**: four phases — grounded (parked, Takeoff available), ascending/descending (scripted, all input ignored, exactly per the design doc's "no precision skill required"), flying (turn + altitude live, constant cruise speed, no accel/decel, "closer to Pilotwings' easy mode than a flight sim"). No new control surface: the same D-pad every other vehicle already reuses now doubles as turn (left/right) + altitude (up/down) while flying, and the pedal-button slot holds a single context-sensitive Takeoff/Land button (shown only when pressing it is actually meaningful — nothing during the transitions). While airborne, building/obstacle collision is skipped entirely (the plane is flying over the town, not through it) — only the world's own outer edge still applies, as the same soft clamp every vehicle boundary already uses, never a hard wall or a crash, matching the design's explicit "the plane cannot crash" rule.
  - **JUDGMENT CALL, flagged plainly: the altitude band (floor 8 / ceiling 14 units) is a reasonable estimate, not a measurement.** The design doc's own spec ties the floor to "the tallest building's rooftop + a buffer," but this app's data model has no per-building height field at all (buildings are placed as scaled 3D models, not tracked by a numeric height) — there was nothing to compute that floor from. Picked comfortably above where this app's tallest building-category models are likely to render, per `SIZE_REFERENCE.md`'s own scale conventions; worth a human eyeballing it live and adjusting `PLANE_MIN_ALTITUDE` in `TownSquare.tsx` if a plane ever visibly clips a tall building.
  - **Judgment call — exit only available once grounded.** The design doc's own "Landing... the plane cannot crash" framing implies Land is the intended-only way down; exiting mid-flight has nowhere for the student to stand, so clicking the plane while airborne is a clean no-op instead of opening a confirm card that can't really process an exit yet. The exit confirm-card only opens once the plane/drone is actually back on the ground (same reused confirm-card component every vehicle already uses).
  - **Camera**: the existing third-person chase cam, reused for the grounded/ascending/descending phases unchanged; while flying, the SAME formula gets two numbers overridden — camera height rises with the plane's own live altitude, and the look-at point drops to ground level instead of head height. That single change is what produces the "steep angled bird's-eye chase cam, roughly 60-70 degrees off horizontal — NOT a true 90-degree top-down" the design doc calls for, with no separate camera mode/branch needed, and it covers the Drone identically for free.
  - **Sound**: `src/lib/vehicleAudio.ts`'s `VehicleSoundController` gained a `plane` kind (continuous engine drone + a light wind-noise layer, per the design doc), shared by both. Same "synthesized, not recorded, needs a human ear to confirm it's calm not grating" flag as Boats 2b/Trains above applies here too.
  - **No fuel/refuel-quiz mechanic** — same standing reasoning as Boats/Trains above: `TRANSPORTATION.md` §1's "no broken/out of fuel state" was never overridden for any vehicle but Cars.
  - **No camera-motion reduction applied to planes specifically** — unlike Boats' wake trail, this build never implements any procedural sway/bob/banking for the plane camera in the first place (design doc: "no roll/pitch input at all"), so there's nothing for the existing Reduce Motion toggle to suppress here; noted so this doesn't read as a missed toggle wiring later.
  - **Not built this pass** (matching the design doc's own "no separate design section... unless a future instruction asks for something Drone-specific" — no such instruction exists): a tighter altitude band or hover-in-place control unique to the Drone. Both vehicles fly identically, exactly as instructed.

#### A7. Cinema

A pure watch-for-fun video room, reached by walking up to the Cinema building. A teacher stocks it (YouTube links or direct Supabase uploads, see A40's Game/Cinema Videos manager); the student browses a Netflix-style horizontally scrolling "Now Showing" poster shelf, with search, tag-chip filtering, an estimated runtime shown before pressing play, and a heart to favorite a video (favorites sort first, everything else keeps the teacher's own add order). Unlimited replay, no completion tracking, no mastery attached; a video a student should be graded on watching belongs on a real Video task (A17) instead.

**Tag reuse — SHIPPED, direct teacher instruction: "when a tag/category is added for a video, those tags should save and be able to be added in a multiselect dropdown menu (reusing for new videos)."** `CinemaVideosManager.tsx`'s add-video and edit-video forms now both use a shared `TagsEditor` component (`src/components/TagsEditor.tsx`, extracted from what was previously a private helper inside `ActivityLibrary.tsx`, now reused by both screens): type-and-add a new tag, or tap any tag already used on another video to reuse it instantly. A plain HTML multi-select dropdown was deliberately not used — per the standing iPad-first rule, it's a poor touch target (no ctrl/cmd-click equivalent, tiny options) — so this reuses the app's existing tap-to-toggle chip pattern instead. Students already see these tags as filterable chips on the Cinema shelf (A7's tag-chip filtering, unchanged).

#### A8. Arcade (Scratch Games)

A Cinema-styled browse-and-play screen for MIT Scratch (scratch.mit.edu) projects, built on direct teacher request ("my students are obsessed with Scratch"). A teacher pastes a public Scratch project URL; the Arcade shows its real Scratch-hosted thumbnail and plays it inline via Scratch's own official embed, with the same shelf/search/tag/favorite pattern as Cinema. Pure play-for-fun, unlimited replay, no tracking.

#### A9. Music & Radio

A shared, teacher-authored music library (title, YouTube link, tags). A student starts music by clicking the Concert Hall building, a placeable Boom Box prop, or a radio button that only appears while driving a car. Playback is always audio-only (a visually hidden YouTube player, never a video surface). A Spotify-styled Now Playing bar gives real play/pause, previous/next (cycling the whole library), a draggable seek bar, a volume slider, and the same tag-chip filtering the picker uses. Music automatically stops when a student exits the car it was started from.

#### A10. NPCs, Dialogue, Mailbox & Passport

Two tiers of non-player characters: 4 named **Neighbors** (Scout, Penny, Pip, Wren) tied to a launch "Meet the Neighbors" quest, and ambient **Townspeople** who wander the square with no quest attached. Every NPC has a real multi-exchange branching conversation, several complete variants per character (not one fixed script), and a distinct assigned text-to-speech voice. Talking to an NPC shows the exchange as a phone-style chat log (NPC left, student right), with the student picking short response options rather than just tapping Continue. Repeat visits deliberately surface a different variant so conversations stay fresh (a direct novelty-decay countermeasure); once every joke a character knows has been heard, the system falls back to a rotating pool including a plain, joke-free option too, since "not all communication needs to be jokes" was a direct teacher instruction.

Certain punchlines are tagged as jokes: the first time a student reaches one, it's permanently banked into their **Joke Book**, a real page-turning book (CSS 3D page flip, not a flat list) viewable from the backpack inventory (A11), alongside a **Friends** list of every NPC ever talked to.

**Mailbox** (Post Office building): shows the themed item each Neighbor "sends" the first time a student meets them, so the Post Office isn't a walkable-but-inert building. **Passport** (Welcome Center building): a Town-Hall-style summary of the student's own identity, streak, Neighbors met, jokes collected, and earned badges, an Animal-Crossing-style equivalent to Scout's "shows you around" role.

#### A11. Avatars & Emotes

A student has two separate appearances. Their **portrait** is a 2D picture from the Kenney Blocky set (18 characters in `avatarCatalog.ts`, one free starter, the rest $20 to $25 in Class Cash, kept in `ownedAvatarIds`) shown at login, on cards, and on the Passport. Their **in-world character** is a 3D model in Town Square: the default `player.glb` (a Kenney Mini Character with a 7-bone skeleton: root, torso, head, two arms, two legs, and 32 built-in animation clips, of which only idle and walk are played today), or the **Cake Character** skin, a static mascot mesh attached to the torso bone, unlocked by answering 100 Bakery Match questions correctly (`unlockedCharacterIds` and `equippedCharacterId`) and shown in Town Square only. Both are planned to be replaced by **Style** (Part B, [`STYLE.md`](./STYLE.md)). Students also pick from a catalog of emote images that pop up over their head (Happy, Love It, Great Job, LOL, Sad, Frustrated, Idea, and more). Six of the emotes are free starters, including all four that name a genuinely hard feeling (Sad, Frustrated, Heartbroken, Grr): a deliberate design choice so a student having a hard day doesn't have to pay for the word for it while every positive feeling is free. A backpack **Inventory Hotbar** (opened from the pie menu's "My Stuff") shows everything a student owns without the full shop attached: characters, emotes, the Joke Book, Friends list, and pets.

#### A12. Marketplace

The unified cosmetic/power-up shop, reached by walking up to the Store. Tabs: Characters (avatars), Emotes, Writing (fonts and text/highlight colors for Notes), Whiteboard (marker colors), Voices (read-aloud voice skins), Prizes (teacher-defined real-world or in-game rewards, redeemed by showing the screen to the teacher), Power-Ups (Skip Passes, which let a student cross one non-required task off their list without doing it), Pets (a redirect to the Pet Shelter, A5), My Stuff, and Receipts (a full purchase history). Every purchase goes into a real shopping cart first, with a running total and balance check, before a final Confirm Purchase; a receipt screen afterward shows what was bought and the new balance. A student who can't afford something sees exactly how much more they need, never just a disabled button. See A31 for the optional "Count It Out" checkout mode and A32 for the cart's needs-vs-wants reflection prompt.

#### A13. Daily Spin Wheel

A real spinning prize wheel (rendered with a physics-accurate spin library, not a fake animation), available once per real calendar day. Every spin is a guaranteed win across 10 segments: a Skip Pass, a cashback percentage (3% or 5% of balance), one dedicated pet slot, four distinct cash amounts, and three random marketplace items, all deterministically the same for every student on a given date (so it's fair, not exploitable) and freshly rerolled the next day. If a student lands on something they already own, or their pet home is already full, it silently falls back to a small cash consolation rather than a true dead spin. Finishing the whole day's assignment (both subjects) earns a second **Bonus Spin**, now defaulted on for every teacher who hasn't explicitly turned it off.

- **Moved to Town Square arrival, 2026-10-04 (direct teacher instruction: "ensure the daily spin wheel launches the first time the student logs on each day, not when they get to teh computer. it should open in the pop up as soon as they land in the town square."):** the automatic open now lives in `TownSquare.tsx` (it used to be on the Computer, `StudentHome.tsx`). It opens as a pop-up when the student lands in Town Square on a day they haven't spun yet (or while a bonus spin is waiting), after the arrival card and the What's New book so pop-ups never stack, and never before a brand-new student's first-login walkthrough. The Computer's Spin widget still opens it on demand.

- **Teacher "Reset for testing" tool, 2026-10-04 (direct teacher request: "reset azalea streak and daily progress/wheel so i can test", then "put it in student overview for teacher side"):** each student's panel on the teacher Students page now has a 🧪 Reset for testing section (with a confirm) that sets the streak to 0, clears today's Math and Literacy progress, and resets today's Daily Spin and any bonus spin (`resetStudentForTesting` in `store.ts`). Money and past history are untouched.

#### A14. Quiz Theme Picker

A small "change my quiz's look" button inside any quiz activity, letting a student pick a visual skin (Standard, Pixel, Adventure, Fantasy) for the quiz screen's chrome. The choice is saved to the student, not just the session, on the explicit design principle that predictability matters more than novelty for this population: a themed quiz should look the same every time a student opens it, not re-skin itself randomly.

#### A15. The Platformer Game

A native, from-scratch side-scroller (Blooket-style: gameplay wraps a real question set, see A24 for the retrieval-practice rationale and the Native Game Standard). A student picks a character skin, then runs, jumps, and dodges spike hazards and gaps across 4 increasingly difficult levels (tighter ground, more spikes, wider gaps, and a faster player as levels rise) while collecting coins. Three starting hearts; falling in a hole costs a heart. If all hearts are lost, recovery is a fixed gauntlet: 5 correct question answers in a row (not partial credit, and independent of how many hearts were lost), any miss resets the streak to zero. A question interrupt fires automatically at least once a minute of active play, even with zero mistakes, so retrieval practice keeps happening regardless of how well the platforming itself is going. In-game coins convert to real Piggy Bank money the moment the activity finishes. Touch controls use pointer capture so a held button can't accidentally drop mid-press, a real accessibility fix for imprecise touch input.

---

### GROUP 2: EDUCATIONAL/ACADEMIC FEATURES

#### A16. The Task Engine & Daily Rotation

The core unit of academic work is a **Task**, assembled by the teacher into a student's daily **Rotation**, one list per subject (Math, Literacy) per day. A task can be marked required (can never be skipped with a Skip Pass), daily (repeats every day, starred in the library), or a **Final Check** (completing it marks the whole subject done for the day and triggers the streak/Playground unlock, instead of requiring every other task to also be checked off). Tasks display as **numbered stepping-stones**: any task with an explicit order number must be completed in ascending sequence; once every numbered task is done, every remaining (unordered) task unlocks as a free-choice **Choice Board**, pick anything in any order. A Final Check is automatically forced to the very end of the sequence regardless of how the teacher ordered everything else. Each task can carry a reference image/link, a teacher-authored or auto-generated visual step-by-step guide ("What do I do?"), and its own reward (money by default, or a specific free marketplace item, a one-off custom prize, or a bonus spin). A task must actually be opened before its checkbox becomes tappable, so nothing can be checked off sight-unseen; checking one off always asks "are you sure?" first, and unchecking asks the same in reverse.

#### A17. Task Types

Every content shape a teacher can assign, each its own screen:
- **Quiz**: one question at a time, multiple choice (with optional shuffled questions/answers and teacher-set images with real alt text), matching, or fill-in-the-blank with typo tolerance. Answering freezes on the current question with a clear right/wrong result before advancing; a wrong answer gets requeued to come back around rather than just marked and dropped. A question missed 3 times in a row is permanently retired for that attempt and flagged to the teacher's Review Inbox (a "Quiz Struggle") rather than silently disappearing.
- **External Link**: opens in an in-app browser frame; since most real external sites (Amplify, Polypad, YouTube, etc.) block being shown inside an iframe, the default behavior is an honest "here's where you're headed" card with an explicit tap to actually leave the app, rather than a broken embedded frame. A teacher can flag specific embed-friendly URLs (like Scratch's own embed links) to play truly inline instead.
- **Off-screen/Paper**: a plain-language instruction card for work done away from the screen, with an optional required photo upload of the finished work before it can be checked off.
- **Video**: a themed movie-theater-framed YouTube player; "I watched it!" only becomes tappable once the player itself reports the video played through to the end, not just on a manual tap.
- **Reading Passage + Questions**: a passage (with an optional image) read in place, then flows straight into an attached Quiz.
- **Flashcard Drill**: front/back cards a student flips through and self-checks.
- **Word Chain**: a word-ladder style chain of hint-and-answer steps.
- **Sentence Editing**: a student retypes/corrects a broken sentence to match a target, with an optional hint.
- **Article Reader**: a real web article, cleaned of ads/nav/site chrome, with student-adjustable font size/line height, tappable text highlighting in distinct colors per open tab, and per-highlight notes.
- **Sentence Builder**: a colored-slot graphic organizer (colourful-semantics style) where each blank keeps a consistent color for its sentence role across every organizer a student ever sees, so color becomes a reliable structural cue rather than decoration; connector words the teacher fixes (like "because," "and") sit between blanks automatically.
- **Pick One**: 2-4 video/link options a student freely picks between, never required to do more than one.
- **Platformer Game + Quiz**: see A15/A24.

#### A18. Question Sets, Content Library & CSV Import

A searchable, filterable library of reusable Question Sets (quiz or drill kind), each with a cover image and tags, browsable as a card grid. A full editor lets a teacher build a set by hand, or upload a CSV in a fixed template format that both inserts straight into the activity being edited and saves as a new named set in the library in one step, so nothing has to be entered twice.

#### A19. Common Core Standards Picker

A curated reference set of official Common Core State Standards (CCSS) for Math and English Language Arts/Literacy, grades K-8, sourced and trimmed from the official standard text (not paraphrased), used when a teacher tags a task or a Focus (A20) with a specific standard code. A standard code a teacher puts at the front of a Focus's own detail text is automatically stripped before that text is ever shown to a student, since the code itself is teacher reference, not student-facing content.

#### A20. Focus Sets / Curriculum Spotlight System

Two related systems, both teacher-authored:

**Literacy Focus Sets**: a per-student weekly window of phonics patterns, morphemes, and spelling practice words, shown as a quick reference while the student works on Literacy.

**Focuses**: a class-wide curriculum spotlight across four independent lanes (Math, Literacy, Social-Emotional, Personal Finance), one active focus per lane at a time. A Focus is deliberately never shown to a student as "your weak spot" or "you need to work on this"; it surfaces as a quiet "This week in our classroom" banner on relevant screens (Piggy Bank, Marketplace, subject dashboards), and its word list occasionally (about 1 in 3 conversations, never every single time) drops naturally into NPC small talk instead of an explicit on-screen callout. This framing is a direct, explicit design guardrail against surveillance/deficit framing for a population where an on-screen "target" can land very differently than intended.

#### A21. Activity Library, Plan Templates & Weekly Schedule

A reusable Activity Library (create an activity once, drag it into any student's plan, which copies it fresh rather than linking it live, so editing the library original later never breaks an already-assigned copy). Plan Templates save a whole named daily plan for reuse. A Weekly Schedule maps a template to a specific subject and weekday per student, auto-loading fresh every matching day; an Assignment can instead span a date range, loading once and carrying the student's progress forward across the whole window. A daily plan itself moves through a Draft, Upcoming, Active, Deleted lifecycle in the teacher's Assignments view.

#### A22. Score History & Mastery Tracking

Every full pass through a quiz (every question answered correctly at least once) logs a record: which task, when, how long it took, and how many were answered correctly on the very first try. A teacher reviews this per student in Score History. Questions a student is genuinely struggling with (3 misses in a row) surface privately in the teacher's Review Inbox rather than just disappearing from the quiz, so a real difficulty never goes unseen just because the retry cap protected the student from getting stuck on it in the moment.

#### A23. Literacy Manipulatives / Grammar Sandbox

**Rebuilt end-to-end (2026-09-22), superseding every mode-switching description below** (Part B's Literacy Workspace items 1-11 are the build history that led here, kept as a record, not the current shape). Direct teacher redesign after seeing the shipped app live (a real screenshot): "the literacy manipulatives is just not up to par... the design of the literacy manipulatives is too scripted, too prompted. it should really just be a drag and drop... an interactive whiteboard to explore all the tools... entirely unscripted and not aligned with a curriculum... this is open play, dont build the activity component until all design, features, navigation, and tools in literacy manipulatives is developed completely." Concrete asks, all applied: word lists should not be tiles; Montessori grammar symbols should never sit on a word tile, only exist as their own plain shapes; the top bar moves to the left side; remove "Give me a start" and "Read board" entirely; Mark and Draw merge into one tool that also reaches the student's owned/purchased marker AND highlight colors; no scripted target-word/fixed-formula activities in the sandbox.

**Current architecture:** one shared canvas (`GrammarSandbox.tsx`), no mode switching. The left sidebar (moved off the old top toolbar) is the only navigation: a persistent action row (🖍️ Draw toggle with Pen/Highlight sub-modes and a real color palette, ↩️ Undo, 🗑️ Clear) above collapsible material categories. Every material drags from the sidebar onto the canvas and can be freely repositioned; the one surviving "smart" behavior is the noun+verb number-agreement snap (a structural grammar rule applied to any two words, not a curriculum target — the sandbox's original, never-criticized first feature). Categories:
- **🔺 Grammar Symbols** (renamed from "Grammar Shapes," direct instruction) — the real 9 Montessori shape+color symbols as their own blank, textless draggable objects, satisfying "just the shapes should be available." The standalone "Sentence Grammar" category (naming/action word tiles) was removed entirely per direct instruction ("remove sentence grammar for now entirely") — its content (`SANDBOX_NOUNS`/`SANDBOX_VERBS`, the `grammar` `PlacedKind`, the noun+verb snap) stays in the code, just not reachable from the sidebar right now.
- **📜 Symbol Sentences** — new category (direct teacher instruction, uploaded `Grammar_Cards.pdf`, a real Montessori grammar-box deck: full sentences shown purely as a left-to-right row of colored symbols, no words). `symbolSentences.ts` holds the deck's own 11 "pages" of 3 sentences each (33 total), transcribed from the deck's own answer-key page (unambiguous, teacher-authored text) with each word hand-tagged to a `MontessoriWordClass` (ordinary English grammar, not a specialized scheme). Each tray card previews its sentence as a small row of the real symbol images inside a bordered pill (visually echoing the uploaded deck's own card look); dragging one onto the board drops the whole row of plain `shape` items at once via `startDragNewSymbolSentence` (same spawn mechanism as any other tray material, just placing several side by side in one gesture) — once down, every symbol is an ordinary placed shape, freely separable and rearrangeable exactly like a symbol dragged in individually, per "these can be dragged and dropped same as individual symbols."
- **🧩 Morphemes** — root/prefix/suffix jigsaw pieces (real reference image, `PuzzlePiece`). Direct follow-up instruction restored real-word-only linking: dropping an affix near a root only snaps together (and only then) when `MORPHEME_COMBOS` confirms a real resulting word; a definition card appears next to the connected pieces composing the real word from the affix's own plain-language meaning plus the root's existing Word Origins etymology fact. An invalid attempt simply doesn't connect, no shake, no definition — consistent with the sandbox's standing non-punitive rule.
- **🔡 Alphabet** — the full A-Z tile set, both cases reachable: double-tapping a tray letter flips its case (what gets dragged out next), and double-tapping an already-placed letter flips that instance's case independently, per direct instruction.
- **🔠 Graphemes** — a real, comprehensive English grapheme tile set (every spelling `soundWallData.ts`'s Sound Wall content already tracks, deduped), direct instruction: "a set of all graphemes taught in the UFLI scope." **Honest limitation, logged rather than silent:** this environment's network policy still blocks UFLI's own published materials (same standing block noted throughout this doc), so this is an ordinary, real grapheme set, not verified against UFLI's specific scope-and-sequence.
- **🟦 Sound Boxes** (renamed from "Sound Frames," direct instruction) — blank 2/3/4/5-box frames a student drags letters onto freely; no target word, no correctness check (replaces the old word-target-driven Sound Boxes mode from the previous mode-switching build, a different, scripted thing despite the same name).
- **⌨️ Text Box** — direct teacher instruction: "add type text input fields so kids can type. SST option. text fields can be double tapped to edit text, moveable, resize text options." Drag "Add a text box" onto the canvas; the placed box shows the typed text (or a "Double-tap to type" placeholder) and moves like any other material via the normal drag handlers, but double-tapping opens an edit card with a real `<textarea>` (auto-focused), A-/A+ buttons to grow or shrink the text (12-48px), a 🎤 speech-to-text button, and Done/🗑️. **Speech-to-text is back for this one field only**, a deliberate, explicit exception to the app-wide STT removal from the day before (2026-09-22, "remove STT" — that instruction was specifically about ArticleReader's highlight-reading feature, not a blanket ban, and this is a new, separate, explicit request): a small local `useTextBoxVoiceToText` hook reimplements the same browser-`SpeechRecognition` pattern the since-deleted shared `lib/useVoiceToText.ts` used, scoped to just this file/feature rather than reintroducing the shared utility app-wide.
- **📐 Sentence Formulas** — direct follow-up instruction brought this back, reusing `sentenceFormulas.ts`'s real 22-formula content, but as a sidebar-launched canvas widget rather than a full mode: category tabs plus a formula list; picking one drops a fixed-position frame card onto the canvas (not draggable, so its internal clickable blanks never fight canvas-drag) showing colored rectangle blanks in sentence order. Each blank is both a plain text input (type your own word) and a "▾" dropdown of the real word bank (subject-appropriate verbs/tense-and-plural-aware, via the same WHO-driven agreement logic built earlier this session) — satisfying "drag and drop or type" without the real cross-widget-drag risk that literal drag-and-drop would add. Once every blank is filled, a voice-picker row appears (the student's own unlocked Marketplace voice skins, via `speak()`'s existing `voiceSkinId` override) to hear the finished sentence in any owned voice. **Deliberately still not draggable** even after the later "everything ... can be dragged and dropped" instruction: a formula card is a composite multi-field widget rendered through its own layout path, not a single tray tile, so converting its placement to real drag risked breaking the "internal clicks never fight canvas-drag" property it was built for. Flagged here rather than silently skipped.
- **📚 Word Lists** — still a genuinely plain reference list (no button/pill styling at all), per "the word lists should not be in tiles," but each row is now also draggable onto the canvas (as a plain text tile, reusing the `letter` `PlacedKind`) per direct instruction that everything in the sidebar should be drag-and-droppable; a separate small 🔈 button next to each word keeps the original tap-to-hear behavior without starting a drag.

**Explicitly removed from the sandbox** (content modules kept in the repo untouched, for the future teacher-authored Activity mode, not deleted): Mad Libs, the old word-target Sound Boxes and Blending Board, the fixed-bucket Word Sorts, Morpheme Web's separate radial tap-to-attach mode (redundant with the jigsaw Morphemes category under the new one-canvas model), and the Montessori decision-tree Sentence Builder (its `GRAMMAR_STATES` engine stays in `montessoriGrammar.ts`, unused by this screen now). All "🎲 Randomize/Surprise me/Fill randomly" buttons and the "Read board" button are gone with the modes that had them. `Student.showSentenceBuildingHints` (the calm-status-hint toggle) is now unused by this screen (the old Sentence Formulas/Sentence Builder modes it applied to no longer exist here) — left in the data model rather than removed, since it costs nothing to keep and StudentManager.tsx's toggle for it is otherwise harmless if a future Activity mode reuses the concept.

**Top toolbar + save file + Undo/Redo — SHIPPED, direct teacher instruction after seeing the shipped screen: "the buttons show in this image should create a top tool bar that can be collapsed to not show. in addition to the buttons shown, i also want a save file... in addition to undo/redo."** Help/Exit/materials-sidebar-toggle and Draw/Undo/Redo/Clear/Save/My Boards moved off the left sidebar into their own horizontal bar across the top of the screen, with a "▲ Hide bar / ▼ Show toolbar" collapse toggle (independent of the materials-sidebar's own ⟨⟨/⟩⟩ toggle, which now lives in this same bar). **Undo/Redo:** `redoRef` mirrors `historyRef`'s existing stack-based approach — a fresh action (`pushHistory`) clears the redo stack, `undo` pushes the replaced state onto it, `redo` pops it back, same `HISTORY_LIMIT`-bounded pattern already proven for Undo. **Save file, a genuinely new persisted feature** (everything else on this canvas was explicitly ephemeral/un-saved by design, per the sandbox's own standing "just open exploration" rule — a save log is additive, not a change to that rule): new `SavedWhiteboard` type (`types.ts`) and `saved_whiteboards` Supabase table, same named-independent-saves shape `Note`/the Notes word processor already uses (`saveWhiteboard`/`deleteWhiteboard` store actions, full sync/realtime wiring). "💾 Save" prompts for a name (defaults to "Board N" if left blank) and stores `JSON.stringify(placed)` plus a `canvas.toDataURL()` snapshot of the draw/highlight layer; "📂 My Boards" opens a real log of every board this student has saved, newest first, each with a Load and a 🗑️ delete button. Loading restores both the tile layout and redraws the saved artwork onto the draw canvas, and resets Undo/Redo history (a loaded board is a fresh starting point, not something to undo back out of).

**Real Montessori grammar-symbol images + morpheme puzzle-piece restyle — SHIPPED (2026-09-23), direct teacher uploads.** Grammar Symbols now renders the teacher's own designed reference PNGs (`public/literacy/grammar-symbols/`) instead of hand-drawn SVG shapes — `montessoriGrammar.ts`'s `MONTESSORI_WORD_CLASS_INFO` gained an `imageUrl` field, and `ItemVisual`'s shape branch renders `<img src={info.imageUrl} />`. The teacher uploaded two rounds: a first zip of flat solid-color shapes plus separate decorated "-icon" variants (icon drawn inside the shape, not wired into the UI), then a second, final zip ("Untitled design 1") of a cleaner Canva-made set with the icon already baked into each shape (a tapping hand for noun, running figure for verb, magnet for article, swap-arrows for pronoun, eyes for adjective/adverb, chain-link for conjunction, keyhole+exclamation for interjection, open-box+arrows for preposition) — that second set is what's now live, and the separate `-icon` files/field were deleted as redundant once the primary images carried the icon themselves. Colors were re-measured pixel-exact from the final PNGs (Canva's default "Bold" palette): noun #000000, article #5CE1E6, adjective #004AAD, verb #FD4636, preposition #7ED957, adverb #FFBD59, pronoun #8C52FF, conjunction #FF66C4, interjection #DCB96B. Separately, the morpheme `PuzzlePiece` SVG (root/prefix/suffix jigsaw tiles) was restyled to match a teacher-supplied jigsaw-piece reference mockup (labeled "MOCKUP ONLY" by the teacher, so treated as a shape reference rather than a literal asset to ship, since the pieces need to render arbitrary per-morpheme text and color): added a decorative top knob bump to the existing rounded-rectangle body, on top of the pre-existing left/right tab-and-notch connectors that still carry the real root/prefix/suffix linking meaning.

**Sidebar fixes — SHIPPED (2026-09-23), direct teacher report with screenshot.** Collapsed sidebar categories below an open one were rendering as unreadable slivers of color with no visible title. Root cause: `.lm-category` sets `overflow: hidden` (for its rounded-corner clipping) but is also a flex child in `.lm-sidebar`'s column layout with no explicit `flex-shrink`; per the flexbox spec, a flex item with non-visible overflow gets its automatic minimum size forced to 0, so instead of the sidebar's own `overflow-y: auto` kicking in and scrolling, the browser was free to squash every category down toward zero height to make everything "fit." Fixed with one line, `flex-shrink: 0` on `.lm-category` — categories now hold their natural height (title always visible, open or collapsed) and the sidebar scrolls normally once content overflows.

**Critical drag-from-sidebar fix — SHIPPED (2026-09-23), direct urgent teacher report ("i am unable to drag anything from the left hand bar. fix now") after the grammar-symbol images shipped.** Two compounding bugs, both real regressions from the same-day image work:
1. The new `<img>` tags (Grammar Symbols tiles, Symbol Sentences preview icons) are natively draggable in every browser by default; a pointer-drag on one triggered the browser's own built-in "drag this image" gesture instead of (or interfering with) the sandbox's custom pointer-based drag system. Fixed with `draggable={false}` and `pointerEvents: 'none'` on both `<img>` tags, so the pointer/click always targets the wrapping `Draggable` div, never the image itself.
2. Separately, and likely the larger issue: spawning a new tile from the tray (`startDragNewItem`) relied on `setPointerCapture` targeting the tray element the pointerdown started on, but that element has no `onPointerMove`/`onPointerUp` handlers (only the already-placed canvas copy does, once rendered) — so a captured drag could silently stop tracking the cursor the instant it began, with the new tile landing whatever fixed (often off-canvas, since `canvasRelative()` measures from the canvas element and the tray sits to its left, i.e. a negative x that `.lm-canvas`'s `overflow: hidden` then clips to invisible) position it spawned at. Fixed with a new `bindGlobalDragTracking` helper: `startDragNewItem` and `startDragNewSymbolSentence` now attach `window`-level `pointermove`/`pointerup` listeners for the duration of the gesture, so the new tile (or, for a Symbol Sentence, the whole spawned row, each item keeping its relative offset) reliably follows the cursor regardless of which DOM element nominally has pointer capture, and lands wherever the pointer is actually released. Re-dragging an already-placed tile (`startDragPlaced`) was left on its existing React-prop-based handlers, since press/move/release all happen on the same visible element there and wasn't the reported failure.

**Zoom, per-item delete, Sound Boxes rename/z-index, Text quick-add — SHIPPED (2026-09-23), direct teacher instructions.** Four small requests batched into one pass:
- **Zoom** ("add zoom feature for white board"): new `zoom` state (50%-200%, 25% steps), toolbar `➖ / 100% / ➕` controls next to Undo/Redo. `.lm-canvas` and the draw `<canvas>` both get `transform: scale(zoom)` from the top-left corner, and `canvasRelative()` now divides pointer coordinates by `zoom` so placed items keep real, unscaled x/y regardless of the current zoom level (drag placement and the draw layer both stay correctly aligned at any zoom, since `getBoundingClientRect()` already reflects the CSS transform).
- **Per-item delete** ("add delete feature for all things (symbols, etc.)"): every generic placed item (shapes, letters, graphemes, morphemes, grammar tiles, sound-box frames) now gets a small always-visible ✕ badge at its top-right corner, calling the same `removePlacedItem` Sentence Formula frames and Text Boxes already used for their own ✕/🗑️. Always-visible rather than hover-revealed, since hover doesn't exist on touch and a predictable, always-reachable control fits this population better.
- **Sound Boxes rename + z-index fix** ("change sound frames to 'Sound Boxes', ensure that letter and grapheme tiles face in front of the boxes, not behind" — direct teacher report with screenshot showing a grapheme tile tucked under a box outline): sidebar label renamed (internal `frame` `PlacedKind`/state keys unchanged, cosmetic only); the generic placed-item loop now wraps each item in a positioned div with an explicit `zIndex` (`1` for `frame`, `2` for everything else) instead of relying on DOM/drag-order stacking, so a sound-box frame can never visually cover a letter or grapheme tile regardless of which was placed first.
- **Text quick-add** ("put 'Text' (text box) by draw on top tool bar"): a `⌨️ Text` button now sits next to `🖍️ Draw` in the top toolbar, calling a new `addTextBoxAtDefault` that drops a blank text box near the top-left of the board (stacking downward for each additional one added this way) — a plain click has no drag context to place from, so unlike the sidebar's draggable "Add a text box" tile this one always lands at a fixed default spot; the sidebar tile stays for anyone who wants to choose where it drops.

**Graphemes subcategories, drag-to-delete, puzzle-piece fixes, morpheme TTS, Sentence Formula grammar check — SHIPPED (2026-09-23), direct teacher instructions, 8 items batched into one pass:**
1. **Graphemes reorganized** ("grapheme tiles should be organized into sub categories and remove single graphemes/letters"): `GRAPHEMES` (one flat alphabetized list) replaced with `GRAPHEME_GROUPS`, built from the Sound Wall's own existing `CONSONANT_CATEGORY_LABELS`/`VOWEL_CATEGORY_LABELS` (Stops, Fricatives, Long vowels, Diphthongs...) — reusing the Sound Wall's real taxonomy rather than inventing a new one. Single-letter spellings are dropped (`g.length <= 1`), since they duplicate the Alphabet tiles; the sidebar now shows one `SubcategoryRow` per sound category, same pattern as Morphemes/Word Lists.
2. **Drag-to-delete via the sidebar** ("allow dragging objects (like frames, boxes) back into the left tool bar and that act should delete it from view"): `onDragEnd` now checks the release position against the canvas's own left screen edge (the sidebar sits directly left of it) — releasing past that edge deletes the item instead of running the usual snap checks.
3. **Puzzle pieces: left/right notches only** ("make puzzle pieces only have left and right notches (not top or bottom)"): reverted the earlier top-knob restyle (2026-09-23, Phase 6) — `PuzzlePiece` is back to a plain rounded rectangle with just the tab/notch circles on its left and right edges.
4. **Puzzle pieces click together** ("puzzle pieces should visually click together when they make a real word"): `runMorphemeSnapCheck`'s old 4px gap between snapped root+affix pieces is now an 8px overlap, so the tab bump visually nests into the neighboring notch instead of just sitting adjacent to it.
5. **TTS for morpheme definitions** ("add TTS for definitons of combined words using mrophemes"): each definition card (only shown once a combo is a real word) now has a 🔈 button that reads the word, definition, and etymology aloud via the existing `speak()`.
6. **Sentence Formula grammar check** ("a check notification... check for tenses, accuracy, plurals, etc. and give a notice if it is a correct sentence or if it needs improvement. tell exactly what needs to improve. always TTS option"): once every blank in a formula is filled, a new card (same visual pattern as the morpheme definition cards) checks each typed/picked word against the same tense-and-plural-aware word bank (`wordBankFor`/`actionWordsFor`) that already drives the dropdown's own choices — not a new or fabricated grammar judgment, the identical data already used to decide what counts as "correct." Calm SEL framing throughout ("A few things to check," never "wrong"), and the 🔈 to hear the notice is always present regardless of outcome.
7. **"My Voice" renamed** ("remove 'my voice' from tts, default and the actual voices (like robot) should be shown"): the free `voice-default` marketplace catalog entry's display name changed from "My Voice" to "Default" (its `id` and all the sentinel logic keyed on that id, e.g. `ToolsPanel.tsx`'s equip/unequip handling, are untouched — display-only rename). Separately, `GrammarSandbox.tsx`'s Sentence Formula voice-picker row (which already has its own hardcoded "Default" button) now excludes `voice-default` from the `ownedVoices` list it maps over, so a real voice like 🤖 Robot no longer sits behind a confusing duplicate "Default"/"My Voice" pair.
8. **Description-word labels split by word class** ("description words in drop down needs to be evaluated, sometimes the required description is an adverb, not an adjective... use context clues and ensure the word lists are accurate"): audited every formula using `description-adj`/`description-adv`/`complement` against `SENTENCE_FORMULAS` — each already correctly applies adjective-only or adverb-only slots to the right grammatical position (predicate adjective after is/are/feels/seems/looks; adverb modifying the action verb), and `ADJECTIVES`/`ADVERBS` in `montessoriGrammar.ts` were re-checked word-by-word, no mislabeled entries found. The real gap was presentation: both slot types shared the identical "DESCRIPTION" label, so a student had no clue why one blank's dropdown offered adjectives and another's offered adverbs. `SLOT_LABELS` now splits them: `description-adj`/`complement` → "DESCRIBE," `description-adv` → "HOW" (matching the "adverbs answer how/when/where" convention this population is already taught).

**Paragraph building, capitalization check, alphabetized/searchable dropdowns, WHERE prepositions — SHIPPED (2026-09-23), direct teacher instructions:**
1. **Completed sentence shown in a text field** ("when a correct sentence is made using formulas, it should appear in a text field (completed sentence with punctuation, capitalization)"): once every blank is filled, a read-only `<input>` now shows the assembled sentence with its first letter capitalized (`displaySentenceText`), right above the existing 🔈 voice-picker row.
2. **Capitalization check** ("sentence formulas must check and prompt capitalization"): three honest, well-established rules (not a general grammar judgment) merged into the same check card as the word-bank check — the sentence's own first fillable word must start with a capital letter; "I" is always capitalized wherever it appears; and a WHO filled with a name that matches `PROPER_NOUNS` case-insensitively but wasn't capitalized to match gets a specific "should be written '[Name]' with a capital letter" notice.
3. **Alphabetized, scrollable, searchable dropdowns** ("drop down menus need to be alphabetized and scroll feature allows more to be shown. typing the start of a word also starts searching for the word. helping students with poor spelling"): the inline picker popup was pulled out into a new `FormulaBlankField` component — the word list is now alphabetized, capped at `maxHeight: 180` with `overflowY: auto`, and has its own search box above it that filters by prefix as the student types (so typing just the first letter or two finds the word, a real spelling accommodation).
4. **STT for Sentence Formula blanks** ("SST is option for text inputs"): `FormulaBlankField` gives every blank its own 🎤 button using the same `useTextBoxVoiceToText` hook the Text Box tool already uses.
5. **Paragraph building** ("allow sentence formulas to be used to build paragraphs. a sentence formula can be used, inputted words, and then paste into the text field. if another sentence formula is created, the paste... adding to the same text field currently active"): a new "📋 Add to paragraph" button on every completed formula appends its capitalized sentence to whichever Text Box the student most recently created or double-tapped into edit mode (tracked in new `activeTextBoxId` state, set from both text-box creation paths and `TextBoxWidget`'s new `onActivate` callback) — building a real multi-sentence paragraph across several formulas into one running Text Box, creating a fresh one automatically if none exists yet or the active one was deleted.
6. **WHERE needs a preposition** ("where did it happen sentences needs prepositions between action and where" — direct report that "Ms. Kayden plays home." isn't a real sentence): `WHERE_WORDS` now bakes each location's own natural preposition into the word itself (`home` → `at home`, `the kitchen` → `in the kitchen`, `the pond` → `at the pond`...) rather than adding one fixed preposition to the formula, since the correct preposition genuinely varies by location and a single fixed one would double up on entries that already carry their own (`under the tree`). Fixes both "Where Is It?" and "Where Did It Happen?" formulas, which shared the same underlying bank and bug.

**Sentence Formula tense settings + duplicate, 2x Grammar Symbols, symbol hover tooltip — SHIPPED (2026-09-23), direct teacher instructions:**
1. **Tense settings + duplicate button** ("to the left of the x, i want a settings button for sentence formulas. the setting will change... the tenses (past, present, future... aligning all words in the dropdowns to be in the same tense and adjusting any words currently within the frame to be the selected tense.) besite the settings button should be a douplicate button. students should also be able to duplicate formulas on their whiteboards, saving the words within them"): each Sentence Formula card header now has three same-size 30px buttons left of the existing ✕ (Settings ⚙️, Duplicate ⧉, then ✕, all smaller than the earlier default `.btn-sm` sizing). ⚙️ opens a small Past/Present/Future popup; picking one sets the new `tense` field on that placed formula (default `present`, matching all prior behavior) and calls `setFormulaTense`, which re-conjugates every already-filled ACTION blank via `retenseActionWord` (a reversible base-form lookup, `sentenceFormulas.ts`) so existing words in the frame update to match, per the direct instruction. Past tense uses hand-authored real forms for the fixed 10-verb `SANDBOX_VERBS` pool (regular -ed and irregular forms alike, e.g. run→ran, play→played); future tense is the ordinary "will + base" construction and needed no new word data. Both the ACTION dropdown bank and the grammar check now read from `actionWordsForTense(..., tense)` instead of the old present-only `actionWordsFor`, so the dropdown, the check, and the completed sentence all agree on the selected tense. ⧉ clones the formula (fresh instanceId, offset position, same filled words and tense) directly onto the whiteboard, per "duplicate formulas on their whiteboards, saving the words within them."
2. **Grammar Symbols doubled** ("the current grammar symbols need to be 2x the size on default"): the tray/canvas symbol tile grew from 48px/40px image to 96px/80px everywhere `ItemVisual`'s shape branch renders (tray and placed canvas items share the one component), and Symbol Sentences' spawn-row math (`startDragNewSymbolSentence`'s `tileW`) updated to match so a dragged sentence row still lines up edge-to-edge at the new size.
3. **Hover/focus tooltip on Grammar Symbols** ("i want the name of the symbol... a small message should show 'Noun' and a simple definiton", referencing her own "Parts of Speech" reference sheet): `montessoriGrammar.ts`'s `WordClassInfo` gained a `name` field (the proper part-of-speech name — Noun, Verb, Preposition...) alongside the existing `label`, which was rewritten from a short kid-phrase into a real plain-language definition matching her reference sheet's wording (e.g. noun: "Person, place, thing, or animal"). Every Grammar Symbol tile is wrapped in a small pure-CSS tooltip (`.lm-symbol-tip`, shows on `:hover`/`:focus-within` so keyboard/switch access works too) that pops the name (bold) and definition above the symbol.

**Selection-based delete, compact tray symbols, unit Symbol Sentences, moveable/joinable Sentence Formulas, Sound Box snapping, new grapheme scope — SHIPPED (2026-09-24), 9 direct teacher instructions batched into one pass:**
1. **Delete only shows on selection, not always** ("ensure that after i drag and drop, the x is not shown on the assets on the whiteboard. only show x upon moving or selected. click to view x on touchscreen"): the always-visible per-item ✕ badge is gone; a new `selectedId` state shows it only for whichever single item was most recently picked up or tapped, and stays visible through a drag and after, until something else is selected or empty canvas is tapped (clears it).
2. **Smaller Grammar Symbols in the sidebar** ("shrink the size of the grammar symbols when they are in the drop down menu, right now they take up too much space"): `ItemVisual`'s shape branch takes a new `compact` prop, used only in the sidebar tray listing (48px vs. the full 96px a dragged/placed symbol still gets) — one shared component, two sizes by context.
3. **Symbol Sentence "pages" relabeled + hover preview** ("labeled similarly to how sentence formulas are labeled... when you highlight over a symbol sentence in the menu, it should pop up with the example reference sentences"): each of the 11 groups got an icon + short descriptive name (Descriptive 🎨, Adverbial ⚡, Joining Words 🔗, Where It Happened 🧭, Complex Sentences 🧩, Interjections ❗...) instead of "Page N," matching the Sentence Formula category style. Hovering/focusing a tray card now reveals its real sentence text via the same `.lm-symbol-tip` tooltip the Grammar Symbols use.
4. **Symbol Sentences now drop as one unit** ("when you drag and drop symbol sentences, they should exist as a unit, with one option for x to close, not individual based on word"): a new `symbolSentence` `PlacedKind` replaces the old spawn-N-separate-shapes approach — one placed item holding a `symbolSentenceId` reference, selects/drags/deletes as a whole.
5. **Symbol Sentence settings: type each word** ("when you drag and drop symbol sentences and select the unit, the x should appear to delete but a settings icon should also appear. within the settings... an option for text input fields similar to the current sentence formulas"): a selected Symbol Sentence shows ⚙️ next to ✕; the settings popup has one small text input per symbol (placeholder = that symbol's real word), typed words shown under their symbol on the board — a bridge toward full Sentence Formulas as the curriculum progresses, per her own framing.
6. **Sentence Formulas are now moveable** ("sentence formulas can be moved around the whiteboard"): the card's title text is a drag handle (`startDragPlaced`/`onDragMove`/`onDragEnd`, the same generic drag path every other material uses); the blanks and buttons below are untouched and still never fight canvas-drag.
7. **Sentence Formulas can be joined into a paragraph** ("sentence formulas can be joined together, a connection animation, to stack them into a paragraph form"): dropping one card with its top near another's bottom edge snaps it directly beneath (using the target's real measured DOM height) and records the link in a new `joinedToId` field; both cards briefly pulse (`.lm-formula-glow`, a new CSS keyframe). Dragging an upstream card carries every card stacked below it along via `getChainDescendantIds`; picking up a joined card detaches it from whatever was above it (its own descendants still follow).
8. **Letters and graphemes snap into Sound Boxes** ("ensure graphemes and alphabet snap into the sound box frames"): dropping a letter/grapheme tile near a placed Sound Box frame now snaps it to whichever cell it's closest to (`runSoundBoxSnap`), matching real Elkonin-box behavior.
9. **New grapheme category scope** ("sort graphemes in these categories: Consonant Structures, VCe, Ending patterns, r-controlled vowels, Long Vowels and Diphthongs, advanced and alternative"): `GRAPHEME_GROUPS` replaced the earlier Sound-Wall-derived categories with her own hand-specified list verbatim (six categories, ~60 spellings). A few spellings (ar, ea, ew, ue, ou, ey...) legitimately appear in more than one category since the same letters can spell more than one sound — kept exactly as she listed them rather than deduplicated across categories.

**Settings + text field on single Grammar Symbols — SHIPPED (2026-09-24), direct follow-up instructions: "have the same setting button appear for single grammar symbols, however when settings appear, if you click to add a text field, it should appear and stay, same as adding a text box" / "same text box feature should appear with single grammar symbols".** A selected single Grammar Symbol (`kind: 'shape'`) now shows the same ⚙️/✕ button pair Symbol Sentences got; ⚙️ opens a small popup with one "⌨️ Add a text field" button. Rather than inventing a new lightweight input, this literally spawns a real, independent Text Box item positioned just below the symbol via a new `addTextFieldNearShape` helper — the exact same persistent, double-tap-to-edit, resizable, speech-to-text-capable widget the sidebar's own Text Box tool already is, so "add a text field" and "same text box feature" are satisfied with zero new component code, just a spawn point next to the symbol instead of the default top-left corner.

**Backspace/Delete key deletes the selected object — SHIPPED (2026-09-24), direct teacher instruction: "if an object is selected and backspace or delete is clicked, delete the object."** A `window` `keydown` listener checks `selectedId` (the same single-selection state the ✕ badge already uses) and removes that item on Backspace or Delete — but only when focus isn't inside an `<input>`/`<textarea>`/contentEditable element, so Backspace still just erases a character while typing in a Text Box, a Sentence Formula blank, or a settings text field.

**Grammar Symbol artwork fully replaced — SHIPPED (2026-09-25), direct teacher upload ("Grammar_Symbols.zip"), "update the grammar symbols (in all places including the sentences)".** A full swap of the teacher's own designed symbol set, replacing every prior PNG in `public/literacy/grammar-symbols/` (same 9 filenames, so `MONTESSORI_WORD_CLASS_INFO.imageUrl` paths didn't need to change) and re-measuring every color pixel-exact from the new artwork: noun red triangle #D2222E (was black), article pink small triangle #F7B3CF (was cyan), adjective light blue triangle #4BB8E6 (was dark blue), verb green circle #42AC74 (was coral-red), preposition brown crescent #723C19 (was green), adverb dark blue small circle #1968AB (was orange), pronoun yellow inverted triangle #FAF003 (was purple triangle), conjunction purple double-arrow+chain #9877B9 (was pink rectangle), interjection orange teardrop #F87220 (was gold keyhole). Because `MONTESSORI_WORD_CLASS_INFO` is the single source of truth every rendering site reads from (`ItemVisual`'s shape branch, the sidebar tray, Symbol Sentences' preview icons and placed-on-board rendering, the Sentence Formula blank color-coding via `SLOT_MONTESSORI_CLASS`, the hover tooltip), "in all places including the sentences" was satisfied by touching exactly one data file plus the 9 replaced PNGs — no component code changed. `PuzzleShape`'s type (dead code, nothing renders from it — confirmed by search) was also updated to name the new shapes accurately (`triangle-inverted`, `double-arrow`, `droplet` replacing the old `rectangle`/`cone`) for anyone who revives it later.

#### A23-ROADMAP. Literacy Manipulative Platform — Build Spec gap analysis + phased plan (2026-09-24)
