Teacher-authored 14-page spec ("Literacy Manipulative Platform — Build Spec," Sep 24 2026) proposing a much larger platform: Universal Tile object model, TTS with synced word highlighting (her own explicit top priority), a formal color/symbol design-tokens file, punctuation tiles, a Sentence Formula ladder with job-slot dual-lens tiles, scaffolded writing supports, an embedded reference popover, a full voice-input system (guided capture + Edit Mode dictation + listening cues + grammar-safe dice), a phonological/phonemic awareness module, a deeper morphology module (word sums/matrix/webs/join-rule animation), teacher tools (lesson builder, analytics, print/projector modes), and a unified whiteboard+word-processor workspace. Dispatched Claudia (design-review agent) to reconcile it against the shipped sandbox before any of it gets built. Full per-section gap analysis and file citations are in that review; this entry is the actionable summary.

**Two items in the spec structurally reopen decisions the teacher made explicitly on 2026-09-22 and must not be built without her fresh, explicit confirmation, even though the spec asks for them:**
1. **Job-slot tiles + dual-lens toggle** (Section 5): requires one object that is simultaneously a real word, a part-of-speech symbol, and a job label — i.e., a word tile carrying a grammar symbol. Her own instruction: "Montessori grammar symbols should never sit on a word tile, only exist as their own plain shapes" / "remove sentence grammar for now entirely." The spec's own page 12 lists this as a "genuine gap," meaning she knows it isn't built and is asking anyway — but it can't be built without reversing that instruction.
2. **Left-hand module menu + split-view whiteboard/word-processor workspace with window chrome** (Section 13): functionally the mode-switching, multi-module architecture she explicitly replaced with "one shared canvas... no mode switching... a left sidebar of draggable materials only" (documented in `GrammarSandbox.tsx`'s own top-of-file comment). The useful kernel — a sentence built on the whiteboard drags into a word-processor draft — doesn't require the module menu or resizable/minimizable panes and is safe to build without confirmation, reusing the platform's existing Word Processor/Notes component rather than a new one.

**Other Phase-4 (confirmation-required) items, all genuine re-openings of a previously-removed scripted-activity pattern:** Sentence Formula ladder mastery-gating + auto-formula detection + error-hunt mode; Edit Mode free-text/dictation grammar auto-tagging (real value, real risk — a wrong auto-tag could teach incorrect grammar); the grammar-safe dice randomizer (the dev plan already states "all Randomize/Surprise-me buttons are gone with the modes that had them"); the morphology job-change mini-lesson; and the Lesson Builder/Activity mode itself (this is literally the "Activity mode" she said explicitly to defer until the sandbox was fully developed — may be exactly the green light this spec represents, but that's her call to make explicitly, not to assume).

**Recommended phased build order (no-conflict items only, ready to start once picked):**
- **Phase 0 (infra):** extract one design-tokens color/symbol source (`montessoriGrammar.ts`'s palette vs. the dead, conflicting `GRAMMAR_WORD_CLASS_COLORS` in `types.ts` — different colors for the same word classes, needs reconciling); decide Universal Tile Object scope (recommend incremental — add shared optional fields to `PlacedItem` rather than a full rip-and-replace of a 1667-line, heavily-iterated file).
- **Phase 1 (spec's own top priority):** TTS with synced word highlighting as one shared component (confirmed nowhere in the app today — `ReadAloud.tsx` has no `onboundary` handling) wired into every existing 🔈; adjustable rate/voice already exists via Marketplace voice skins; generalize the existing Grammar-Symbol tooltip + Morpheme definition-card patterns into one shared `<ReferencePopover>`.
- **Phase 2 (safe/additive):** punctuation tiles as a new opt-in, teacher-toggleable category (not a replacement for the current auto-punctuated formulas, which are a helpful scaffold for this population); STT listening cue (chime/TTS + visual state); grapheme split-vowel connector + vowel/consonant coloring toggle; morpheme join-rule animation (e-drop, consonant-doubling, visible at the seam); print mode + projector/presentation mode.
- **Phase 3 (larger content modules, still zero mode-switching, all Explore-type/non-gated):** phonological awareness (sound chips, syllable tapping, onset-rime slider) as new sidebar categories; Elkonin-boxes-v2 (flexible count, multi-letter grouping) replacing current Sound Boxes; word chains, heart-word marking, spelling rule cards, dictation mode (reusing the Morphemes "connects only if real, never punitive" pattern); word matrix builder, word webs, reverse morphology mode under Morphemes; writing scaffolds (paragraph frames, writing frames, sentence starters) as new Explore mats, fade-level exposed only as a teacher accommodation toggle, never an auto-advancing system.
- **Phase 5 (only after Phase 4 sign-off, in dependency order):** the real Universal Tile Object refactor, Edit Mode/dictation auto-tagging (built to the spec's own safeguards — tile-level edits only, ambiguous parses flagged distinct from wrong, punctuation shown as suggestion never auto-committed), job-slot/dual-lens tiles, guided slot-by-slot voice capture, Lesson Builder/Activity mode, the simplified 2-pane whiteboard+word-processor workspace (no module menu, no window chrome, reusing the existing Word Processor component).

**Explicitly declined from the spec's own backlog (Section 12):** a built-in token economy/reward board for this tool specifically — duplicates the platform's existing Piggy Bank/streak/badge economy and risks diluting it with a second, competing reward system.

**A23-ROADMAP Phase 0 + Phase 1 — SHIPPED (2026-09-25), teacher instruction "literacy manipulatives continuation, go until development is completed," working through the roadmap's own phased plan in order.**
- **Phase 0 item 1 (design-tokens color/symbol source reconciliation):** new `src/lib/literacyDesignTokens.ts`. Judgment call, logged per the roadmap's own "make the smallest reversible call rather than stall" instruction: `MONTESSORI_WORD_CLASS_INFO`'s noun/verb colors and `types.ts`'s `GRAMMAR_WORD_CLASS_COLORS` noun/verb colors were NOT merged into one palette, because they are two separate, both directly teacher-specified systems for two different tile kinds that happen to share the word "noun" (the 9-class Montessori shape symbols vs. the dormant 2-class word-tile "Sentence Grammar" category, the latter built to her own explicit "subject/who is always yellow" spec) — merging them would silently reverse one or the other's real instruction. Instead this new file is the one place both are re-exported from, documented side by side, and is the canonical source every Phase 2/3 addition below pulls its own new tokens from, so a third competing palette never gets invented ad hoc.
- **Phase 0 item 2 (Universal Tile Object scope):** took the roadmap's own recommendation verbatim — incremental, not a rip-and-replace of `PlacedItem`. No speculative shared fields were added preemptively; every Phase 2/3 addition below extends `PlacedItem`/`PlacedKind` the same way the file already does (new optional fields, new kind values), which is the incremental approach itself, not a separate refactor step.
- **Phase 1 (the spec's own explicit top priority): TTS with synced word-highlighting, as one shared component.** New `src/components/SyncedSpeakButton.tsx`: a `useSyncedTTS()` hook (one instance mounted per screen, matching that `window.speechSynthesis` can only speak one utterance at a time regardless) tracks which text is currently reading and which word index via `SpeechSynthesisUtterance.onboundary`, a `<SyncedSpeakButton>` 🔈/🔊 toggle button, and a `<HighlightedText>` renderer that highlights the currently-spoken word inline. Feature-detects gracefully: browsers/engines that don't fire `onboundary` (real, inconsistent cross-browser support) still read the text aloud exactly as before, they just never light up a word — never a broken or silent button. `ReadAloud.tsx`'s `speak()` was refactored to extract `buildUtterance()` (same voice-skin/rate/pitch resolution logic) so the new synced component builds the identical utterance rather than duplicating that resolution logic a second time. Every one of the 7 existing 🔈/TTS call sites in `GrammarSandbox.tsx` now goes through this one shared engine instead of calling `speak()` directly: the 3 Word List rows, the morpheme definition card, the Sentence Formula "Default"/owned-voice buttons, and the grammar-check card. The completed-sentence field (previously a read-only `<input>`, which can't render highlight markup inside its value) is now a styled `role="textbox"` div showing the same text with live word-highlighting. The morpheme definition card's spoken text (word + definition + etymology) and its visible text are now the identical string rendered through `<HighlightedText>`, replacing the old 3-line layout — a small, deliberate visual simplification so the highlighted word always matches what's actually being read. Judgment call, logged rather than silently attempted: the grammar-check card's 🔈 uses the shared engine (consistent 🔈/🔊 icon state, same TTS pipeline) but does NOT render word-highlighting against its bullet list, since that list's wording is reformatted for scanning and doesn't match `checkSpeech`'s exact spoken string word-for-word — highlighting against mismatched text would mislead more than help.
- **Phase 1 (generalize the tooltip/definition-card patterns): new `src/components/ReferencePopover.tsx`.** One component, two `mode`s, replacing two separately-coded popup patterns that had grown side by side: `mode="hover"` (a CSS-only hover/focus tooltip, reusing the existing `.lm-symbol-tip-wrap`/`.lm-symbol-tip` classes) now backs both the Grammar Symbol name+definition tooltip and the Symbol Sentence sentence-text-preview tooltip; `mode="inline"` (a positioned `chrome-frame` card with an optional synced-TTS 🔈) now backs the morpheme definition card. Any future Literacy Manipulatives reference fact (a spelling-rule card, a heart-word note, a grapheme connector tip — see Phase 2/3 below) reuses this one component instead of a third bespoke popup pattern getting invented from scratch.

**A23-ROADMAP Phase 2 — SHIPPED (2026-09-25), continuing the roadmap in order: "safe/additive" items only, all opt-in or purely additive, nothing changed about existing default behavior.**
- **Punctuation tiles, opt-in and teacher-toggleable:** new `src/lib/punctuationContent.ts` (10 ordinary marks: period, question mark, exclamation point, comma, apostrophe, quotation marks, colon, semicolon, hyphen, ellipsis, each with a plain-language definition shown via the shared `ReferencePopover` hover tooltip, same as Grammar Symbols). Gated on a new `Student.showPunctuationTiles` field (default `false`/off, matching the roadmap's own "opt-in" framing — the auto-punctuated Sentence Formulas already handle punctuation by default for the whole population), toggled per student in `StudentManager.tsx` next to the existing `showSentenceBuildingHints` checkbox, synced through `sync.ts`/`supabase/schema.sql` (`show_punctuation_tiles boolean not null default false`) the same way every other per-student toggle on this screen already is. The sidebar category itself doesn't render at all for a student the teacher hasn't turned it on for.
- **STT listening cue (chime + visual state):** new `src/lib/audioCues.ts` — a short Web-Audio-API two-tone chime (no external sound file, works offline, feature-detected so it silently does nothing if `AudioContext` isn't available) plays when `useTextBoxVoiceToText`'s `toggle()` starts listening (rising chime) and stops (falling chime), paired with the visual state every STT button already had (Text Box's existing "🎙️ Listening…" label; the Sentence Formula blank's 🎤 button, previously icon-only regardless of state, now also swaps to 🎙️ while listening) — a second sensory channel for a student who isn't looking directly at the button when they start speaking.
- **Grapheme split-vowel connector + vowel/consonant coloring toggle:** `ItemVisual`'s `letter` branch (shared by Alphabet and Graphemes tiles) now renders any grapheme containing an underscore (the VCe spellings — `a_e`, `i_e`, `o_e`...) as two letter glyphs joined by a small dashed connector arc, with the second (often-silent) letter shown faded, instead of the literal underscore character — the real "these two letters work together across a consonant" magic-e convention made visible instead of implied by punctuation. A new "🎨 Vowel/consonant colors" toggle (off by default, shown once in the Alphabet category, shared with Graphemes) colors a tile's text red for vowels / blue for consonants, classified by the tile's own first alphabetic character (an honest, simple heuristic, not a claim of phonetic authority — logged in `vcColorFor`'s own comment).
- **Morpheme join-rule animation (e-drop, consonant-doubling):** new `detectJoinRule()`/`JOIN_RULE_NOTES` in `morphemeContent.ts`, derived by comparing the root's own spelling against the real word already in `MORPHEME_COMBOS` (not a second hand-tagged data source that could drift out of sync) — currently surfaces on "care"+"-ed"→"cared" and "care"+"-ing"→"caring" (the only e-drop cases in the current small root pool; consonant-doubling detection is implemented and will surface automatically the day a CVC root like "run" is added). A small badge pops in right at the seam between the two connected puzzle pieces (`.lm-morpheme-join-badge`, `@keyframes lmMorphemeJoinPop`) with the rule's plain-language sentence ("The silent e at the end of 'care' drops before '-ed'.") as its tooltip, and that same sentence is appended to the morpheme definition card's spoken/highlighted text.
- **Print mode + projector/presentation mode:** a new "🖨️ Print" toolbar button calls `window.print()`, with new `@media print` rules in `index.css` hiding the sidebar/toolbar/per-item delete-settings controls (`.lm-item-controls`, added to every one of that badge's 3 render sites) so only the board itself prints. A new "🖥️ Projector Mode" toggle hides the sidebar and bumps zoom to 150% for reading from across a classroom, plus a `.lm-projector` class that enlarges the toolbar's own text/buttons and adds a visible border around the canvas.

**A23-ROADMAP Phase 3, batch 1 of 3 — SHIPPED (2026-09-25): Phonological Awareness category + Elkonin-boxes-v2.** Continuing the roadmap's Phase 3 ("larger content modules, still zero mode-switching, all Explore-type/non-gated"), shipped in smaller batches rather than one giant pass.
- **New "🔊 Phonological Awareness" sidebar category**, three free-play manipulatives, no target word, no auto-counting or auto-splitting anywhere (matching the sandbox's standing "entirely unscripted" rule): a **Sound Chip** (a blank counter — drag out one per sound you hear while segmenting a word yourself, the real physical-manipulative convention this whole sandbox is modeled on), an **Onset-Rime Divider** (a plain dividing bar a student places between letter/grapheme tiles already on the board to mark their own onset|rime split — deliberately not a "slider" that would need to judge where the right split is), and a **Syllable Tapper** widget (type or speak a word, then tap "👏 Tap" once per clap — self-directed counting the student controls entirely, never an algorithm claiming to count syllables for them).
- **Elkonin-boxes-v2 (flexible count):** a selected Sound Box frame now shows −/+ controls (1-8 boxes) instead of only ever being the fixed 2/3/4/5-box size it was dragged out at. Multi-letter grouping (the spec's other v2 ask) needed no new work — a grapheme tile like "sh" already occupies one cell as a single multi-letter unit, so this fully lands both v2 asks from a small, additive change (`adjustFrameBoxCount`) rather than the bigger rewrite "replacing current Sound Boxes" implied; logged as a judgment call, since the existing frame's snap/z-index/rename work from earlier sessions stays fully intact and doesn't need replacing to get v2's real functional gains.

**A23-ROADMAP Phase 3, batch 2 of 3 — SHIPPED (2026-09-25): Word Chains, Heart Words, Spelling Rule Cards, Dictation Card.**
- **🔗 Word Chains:** new `src/lib/wordChainContent.ts`, 5 real word-ladder chains (`cat/cot/cog/dog/dot/hot`, etc.), each step a genuine one-letter swap into another real word. Reuses `WordListRow`/`SubcategoryRow` exactly as Word Lists already does — plain reference rows, each independently draggable as a letter tile, no auto-check or enforced order.
- **❤️ Heart Words:** new `src/lib/heartWordContent.ts` (20 real high-frequency irregular words). Judgment call, logged in that file's own comment: rather than hand-tagging which letters are "the heart part" of each word (different published heart-word programs mark it slightly differently, and this environment can't verify against one specific program), a placed Heart Word lets the student tap each letter themselves to toggle a heart marker over it — self-directed, same pattern as the Syllable Tapper's self-counted claps, never an asserted "correct" answer.
- **📏 Spelling Rules:** new `src/lib/spellingRuleContent.ts`, 8 ordinary, well-established English spelling rules (Silent E, Doubling, soft/hard C and G, FLOSS, y-to-i, Q-needs-u, plural -es), each a draggable reference card using the shared `ReferencePopover` hover tooltip (title + rule + example) — same pattern as Grammar Symbols/Punctuation tiles, no new popup code needed.
- **🎧 Dictation Card:** hear a word (synced-TTS 🔈), write what you heard, "👁️ Reveal" to self-check — never auto-graded. Deliberately **not** randomized (logged in `DictationWidget`'s own comment): the prompt word is chosen manually, from a dropdown of the student's own active Literacy Focus words when one exists, or a plain typed field otherwise (e.g. for a teacher dictating their own word) — reusing a prior direct teacher instruction that removed every randomize/surprise-me mechanic app-wide meant this could not auto-pick a word for the student, so it doesn't.

**A23-ROADMAP Phase 3, batch 3 of 3 — SHIPPED (2026-09-25): Word Matrix, Word Web, Reverse Morphology (under Morphemes), Writing Scaffolds. This completes A23-ROADMAP Phase 0 through Phase 3 in full.**
- **🔢 Word Matrix** (under Morphemes): a picker-driven reference grid — choose a root, see every prefix/suffix in the tray against it, with the real resulting word shown for any combo that's genuinely real (blank for ones that aren't). Derived directly from the same `MORPHEME_COMBOS` the jigsaw pieces themselves use to decide whether to connect, not a second hand-authored grid.
- **Word Web** (under Morphemes, no new sidebar entry): a lightweight SVG connector line drawn from each connected root to each of its connected affixes, layered on top of the existing jigsaw connections — when two or more affixes connect to one root, the lines fan out into a real web. Judgment call, logged: the spec's "word web" item was NOT built as a revival of the earlier, explicitly-removed radial tap-to-attach mode (2026-09-22 build log: "redundant with the jigsaw Morphemes category under the new one-canvas model") — that removal stands. This is a visualization of the existing jigsaw connections, not a second interaction model.
- **🔁 Reverse Morphology** (under Morphemes): drag a real word (derived from `MORPHEME_COMBOS`'s own resulting words) onto the board as a prompt card ("Can you build 'playful' using morpheme pieces?"), synced-TTS 🔈 included. No new checking logic needed — once the student assembles matching jigsaw pieces nearby, the existing real-word connection detection (and its definition card, Word Web line, and join-rule badge) already confirms it.
- **📝 Writing Scaffolds:** 6 Writing Frames (Sequence, Opinion, Because, Compare-and-Contrast, Describing, Paragraph) as fixed-position cards with one blank per starter line, reusing the exact `frameFills`/`setFrameFill` state Sentence Formulas already use (same field, same shape, zero new state model) plus the existing `pasteSentenceToTextBox` "📋 Add to paragraph" action, so a Writing Frame's assembled text can join the same running paragraph a Sentence Formula sentence already can. Plus 14 plain Sentence Starters (Word-List-style draggable rows). Judgment call, logged in `writingScaffoldContent.ts`'s own comment: the spec's "fade-level" teacher accommodation toggle was not built in this pass (it would need a new per-student setting reaching into teacher-side `StudentManager.tsx` purely for this one feature) — every frame always shows full starter text today, never wrong, just not fade-able yet; flagged as a small, well-scoped follow-up rather than invented or silently skipped.

With this batch, every item the roadmap's Phase 0 through Phase 3 named is either shipped or explicitly logged as a scoped-out follow-up (the writing-scaffold fade toggle above). **Phase 4 and Phase 5 remain untouched, correctly gated on the teacher's own explicit confirmation** (job-slot/dual-lens tiles, Sentence Formula mastery-gating/auto-detection/error-hunt, Edit Mode dictation auto-tagging, the randomizer, the module-menu/split-view workspace redesign, Lesson Builder/Activity mode, the morphology job-change mini-lesson) — none of it was built, per the roadmap's own explicit instruction that these reopen decisions only she can make.

#### A23-REF. Literacy Manipulatives — Full Feature Reference (living summary, last updated 2026-09-25)

This subsection is a standing, synthesized overview of everything above — kept current alongside A23 itself so there's always one place to read "what Literacy Manipulatives is and does" without piecing it together from the dated build-log paragraphs. Update it whenever a Literacy Manipulatives change ships.

**Summary.** Literacy Manipulatives (`GrammarSandbox.tsx`, route `/student/literacy/manipulatives`) is a single open, unscripted whiteboard: one shared canvas, one left sidebar of draggable materials, no modes, no lessons, no scoring, no completion state. It exists because the teacher rejected the original scripted/activity version outright ("too scripted, too prompted... this is open play") in favor of a real manipulatives sandbox a student explores freely, the way physical letter tiles, grammar boxes, and word-building materials work in a Montessori-style literacy classroom. Nothing in it is graded or gated; it is a thinking-and-building space, not an assessment.

**Accommodations & accessibility built in throughout:**
- **TTS with synced word-highlighting, one shared engine** (`SyncedSpeakButton.tsx`, A23-ROADMAP Phase 1) — every 🔈/TTS control on this screen (Word Lists, Word Chains, Sentence Starters, morpheme/reverse-morphology cards, Dictation, Sentence Formula sentences, the grammar-check card) reads through the same `useSyncedTTS()` hook, and wherever the visible text matches the spoken text exactly, the word currently being spoken highlights live. Sentence Formula/Dictation sentences can be heard in any of the student's own unlocked Marketplace voice skins. Feature-detects gracefully — reads aloud everywhere even where the browser doesn't support word-boundary events, it just won't highlight there.
- **Speech-to-text (scoped, deliberate)**, with a listening chime — Text Boxes and every Sentence Formula/Dictation/Syllable-Tapper text field have a 🎤 button (browser `SpeechRecognition`) with a paired Web Audio chime and 🎙️ visual state on start/stop (A23-ROADMAP Phase 2), a specific, explicit exception to the app-wide STT removal, added only where a teacher directly requested it for typing-heavy fields. The Zones of Regulation check-in's "leave a note for your teacher" field (A46) is now a fifth scoped exception, same pattern, reusing the same browser API.
- **Spelling support in every dropdown** — Sentence Formula word-bank pickers are alphabetized, scrollable, and filter live by prefix as the student types, so a student who only knows the first letter or two of a word can still find it instead of needing to spell it correctly.
- **A shared reference-popover pattern** (`ReferencePopover.tsx`, A23-ROADMAP Phase 1) — a hover/focus tooltip mode (Grammar Symbols, Symbol Sentence previews, Punctuation tiles, Spelling Rule cards) and an inline card mode (morpheme definitions) share one component, so every new reference fact on this screen looks and behaves the same way.
- **Always-visible or selection-shown delete** — every generic placed item shows its ✕ (and, where relevant, ⚙️) only once selected, staying visible through a drag; self-contained widget cards (Text Box, Sentence Formula, Dictation, Writing Frame, Reverse Morphology, Word Matrix, Syllable Tapper) keep their own always-visible ✕. Dragging anything back over the sidebar's left edge also deletes it.
- **Non-punitive feedback everywhere.** Morpheme pieces that don't form a real word simply don't connect — no shake, no error sound, no red X. Heart Words and the Syllable Tapper are self-directed (the student marks/counts, the app never asserts a "correct" answer). The Dictation Card is hear/write/reveal, never auto-graded. The Sentence Formula grammar/capitalization check never says "wrong": it says "a few things to check," always paired with 🔈.
- **Vowel/consonant coloring toggle + split-vowel connector styling** (A23-ROADMAP Phase 2) — an opt-in red/blue letter coloring for Alphabet/Graphemes tiles, and VCe graphemes like "a_e" now show a connector arc between the two vowel letters instead of a plain underscore.
- **Zoom** (50%-200%), **Projector Mode** (bigger toolbar text/buttons + a visible canvas border, for reading from across a classroom), and **Print mode** (a dedicated print stylesheet hides the sidebar/toolbar/controls so only the board prints) — three separate visual-accommodation tools (A23-ROADMAP Phase 2), independent of the Sound Wall/other screens' own text-size settings.
- **Resizable text** in Text Boxes (A-/A+, 12-48px).
- **Large, consistent touch targets and a fixed, predictable layout** — sidebar categories collapse/expand rather than hiding content behind navigation; the top toolbar can itself collapse for more canvas room when a student wants it, but nothing auto-hides.
- **Undo/Redo/Save** — mistakes and experiments are always reversible; "💾 Save" + "📂 My Boards" lets a student (or teacher) return to a specific board state later rather than losing work when they leave the screen.
- **Keyboard/focus parity** — hover tooltips also trigger on `:focus-within`, so they aren't mouse-only.

**All materials/tools, by sidebar category** (15 categories total; ⚙️/opt-in categories are noted):
1. **🔺 Grammar Symbols** — the 9 real Montessori shape+color symbols (teacher-designed PNGs) as blank draggable objects. 2x default size; hover/focus shows the part-of-speech name and a plain-language definition.
2. **📜 Symbol Sentences** — 33 real Montessori grammar-box sentences (teacher-uploaded deck), shown as symbol-only preview cards; dragging one drops the whole sentence as a unit.
3. **🧩 Morphemes** — root/prefix/suffix jigsaw pieces that only visually snap together when they spell a real combined word, with a definition card (🔈), a join-rule badge when a spelling rule applies (e-drop/consonant-doubling), and faint "Word Web" connector lines between a root and each connected affix. Two Phase-3 additions live here too: **🔢 Word Matrix** (pick a root, see every prefix/suffix against it) and **🔁 Reverse Morphology** (drag a real word as a "can you build this?" prompt card).
4. **🔡 Alphabet** — full A-Z tiles, both cases (double-tap flips case); vowel/consonant coloring toggle.
5. **🔠 Graphemes** — the full multi-letter English grapheme set in 6 hand-specified categories; split-vowel connector styling; shares the vowel/consonant coloring toggle with Alphabet.
6. **❓ Punctuation** *(opt-in, teacher-toggled per student, off by default)* — 10 marks with hover definitions.
7. **🔊 Phonological Awareness** — Sound Chips (blank segmenting counters), an Onset-Rime Divider bar, and a Syllable Tapper widget (type/speak a word, tap once per clap, self-counted).
8. **🟦 Sound Boxes** — 1-8 box frames (flexible count via +/- on a selected frame, "Elkonin-boxes-v2"), letters/graphemes snap into cells, multi-letter graphemes already fill one cell as a unit.
9. **⌨️ Text Box** — typed/spoken free text, double-tap to edit, resizable text; also quick-addable from the top toolbar.
10. **📐 Sentence Formulas** — the real 22-formula bank as fixed-position canvas cards. Each blank: type, speak, or pick from an alphabetized/searchable dropdown. Once complete: capitalized sentence display with live word-highlighting, any owned voice, a calm grammar+capitalization check, "📋 Add to paragraph," ⚙️ tense settings, ⧉ duplicate, and cards can be dragged/joined into a paragraph stack.
11. **🔗 Word Chains** — 5 real one-letter-swap word ladders, plain draggable rows.
12. **❤️ Heart Words** — 20 real high-frequency words; drag one out, then tap its letters to mark the tricky part with a heart yourself.
13. **📏 Spelling Rules** — 8 ordinary English spelling-rule reference cards (hover for rule + example).
14. **🎧 Dictation** — hear a word (manually chosen, never randomized), write what you heard, 👁️ reveal to self-check.
15. **📝 Writing Scaffolds** — 6 Writing Frames (fixed starter phrases + blanks, "📋 Add to paragraph") and 14 plain Sentence Starters.
16. **📚 Word Lists** *(shown only when the student has an active Literacy Focus set)* — plain, unstyled reference word lists, each row draggable and tap-to-hear.

**Canvas-wide tools (top toolbar):** 🖍️ Draw (Pen/Highlight, real color palette), ⌨️ Text quick-add, ↩️ Undo / Redo, 🗑️ Clear, zoom controls, 💾 Save / 📂 My Boards, 🖨️ Print, 🖥️ Projector Mode, and a bar-collapse toggle. The one surviving "smart" cross-tile behavior is a structural noun+verb number-agreement snap check, not a curriculum target.

**Honest, logged limitations (not silently glossed over):** the Graphemes set has not been verified against UFLI's specific published scope-and-sequence (network policy blocks UFLI's own materials). Sentence Formula tense conversion covers the fixed 10-verb `SANDBOX_VERBS` pool with hand-authored real past forms, not a general morphological rule. The grammar/capitalization check applies three well-established rules plus the existing word-bank data, not a general-purpose grammar engine. Heart Words doesn't assert which letters are "the heart part" of a word (self-marked by the student instead — see `heartWordContent.ts`). Consonant-doubling join-rule detection exists but hasn't visibly surfaced yet (no CVC root is in the current small root pool; it will the day one is added). Writing Scaffolds' spec'd "fade-level" teacher toggle wasn't built (see `writingScaffoldContent.ts`).

**Deliberately not built here** (kept in the codebase, unused by this screen, for a possible future teacher-authored Activity mode, or explicitly gated on the teacher's Phase-4/5 sign-off per A23-ROADMAP): Mad Libs, word-target Sound Boxes/Blending Board, fixed-bucket Word Sorts, the old radial tap-to-attach Morpheme Web mode, the Montessori decision-tree Sentence Builder, job-slot/dual-lens tiles, Sentence Formula mastery-gating/auto-detection/error-hunt, Edit Mode dictation auto-tagging, a randomizer, the module-menu/split-view workspace redesign, Lesson Builder/Activity mode, and the morphology job-change mini-lesson. No scripted/target-word/graded activity exists in Literacy Manipulatives today, matching the teacher's explicit "don't build the activity component until the sandbox itself is fully developed" instruction.

#### A23-SPINOFF. Literacy Manipulatives spun out into its own standalone repo, embedded back in

- **SHIPPED (2026-09-28) — direct teacher instruction: "I want it native like it is currently as a tool, but I want the concept... to be a separate, non-native tool that other educators can access... I wouldn't need to work on two different files."** The tool's board component and every content library (`grammarContent`, `morphemeContent`, `montessoriGrammar`, `symbolSentences`, `punctuationContent`, `wordChainContent`, `heartWordContent`, `spellingRuleContent`, `writingScaffoldContent`, `sentenceFormulas`, `soundContent`, `literacyDesignTokens`) were ported into a brand-new standalone repo, `vermontliteracynetwork/literacy-manipulatives`, deployed at `https://literacy-manipulatives.vercel.app/`. **v1 there deliberately has no login and nothing saved** ("lets make it so its a no save type, no login right now" — a later, explicit scope-narrowing instruction), so everything account-shaped that doesn't apply without login was left out of the port rather than stubbed: Help overlay, Exit-to-home confirm, Save/My Boards, the teacher-curated "Word Lists" category, marketplace-purchased voice skins/marker-highlight colors (replaced with the plain base sets, unlocked for everyone), and the per-student "show punctuation tiles" toggle (now always on). Full accounting of what did and didn't come along, and why, lives in that repo's own `DEVELOPMENT_LOG.md`.
- **This dashboard's own `/student/grammar` route now embeds that live site** rather than rendering the native tool directly: `GrammarSandbox.tsx` (this section's own component) is still on disk, still fully built, but no longer routed — `App.tsx` now points `/student/grammar` at a new `LiteracyManipulativesEmbed.tsx`, a full-viewport iframe onto the standalone site plus the same "⬅️ Home" corner button pattern other in-game tool screens use. Nothing else about how a student reaches this screen changed (`ToolsPanel.tsx`'s launcher still just navigates to `/student/grammar`, unmodified). **`GrammarSandbox.tsx` and its now-orphaned reliance on this file's own content-library imports get deleted for real only once the embed is confirmed working live for actual students** — kept as a safety net until then, per the teacher's own plan.
- **Not yet built, tracked in the standalone repo's own roadmap, not here**: accounts for outside educators, save/load, a free-vs-paid tier split, Stripe billing, and the "recognize my own student, skip any future login/paywall" handshake the embed will need once that site has real login. None of these block what shipped today.

#### A24. Native Games & the Native Game Standard

Governed by `NATIVE_GAME_STANDARD.md`. Three native games are shipped today — the Platformer (A15), Bakery Match (A43), and the Gas Pump (A44), each with its own Logic/UX/Academic-integration breakdown at its own entry — plus a planned pipeline for uploading third-party HTML5 games from sites like itch.io. A native game exists specifically to make retrieval practice (being asked to recall an answer, not just review content, one of the most robust findings in learning science) tolerable and motivating for a student who would disengage from a plain quiz: the game is the wrapper, the question set underneath is the actual point, and gameplay always serves the question set, never the reverse. Hard rules that apply regardless of which game: a question break, once started, has zero time pressure (no countdown, no answer time limit, full access to every accessibility tool); replay is unlimited (no play-count cap, no daily limit); leaving mid-game always shows a confirm dialog first, and confirming forfeits that session's progress and reward (but never erases mastery already logged before leaving); every completed session generates a full report to the teacher's inbox in addition to the normal mastery record. A teacher configures how often a question break becomes eligible and how many questions appear per break.

#### A25. Playground / Free Play

An ungraded, no-to-do-list space a student can use once the day's real assignment is finished (see A28 for exactly when it unlocks) or during a teacher-granted timed break. A student can free-play any teacher-flagged Playground activity, or pick any saved quiz-kind Question Set to run as a standalone, ungraded Platformer session (A15) with no checkbox waiting on it. Finishing a Playground activity still pays into the real Piggy Bank exactly like a real assignment would (a direct teacher request), just without touching streak, badges, or pet training, since Playground content is deliberately not tied to a specific day's required plan.

---

### GROUP 3: ABA/SEL/BEHAVIORAL FEATURES

Grounded in the standing principles in Part D: no leaderboards or cross-student comparison anywhere, reinforcement schedules follow real ABA principles (continuous reinforcement for shaping, variable-ratio mechanics designed to never produce an empty outcome), regulation is never gated behind currency or "good behavior," and pets/the economy never punish absence.

#### A26. Pets' ABA Shaping Ladder & Growth Stages

Cross-reference: the pet catalog, adoption, and care loop are described in full under A5 (Game & Play). The training system underneath is a deliberate successive-approximation shaping design: a single counter (`trainingProgress`), incremented only by genuine task completions (never by tapping care buttons), gates four visible milestones shown as badges on the pet's care card (with a progress bar to the next one): "Walks with you" at 5 (the companion/follow unlock), "Best Friends" at 10, "Bonded for Life" at 15, and "Trick Master" at 25 (added 2026-10-06). The counter also unlocks new tricks to teach (Sit and Shake at 0, Spin 3, Speak 5, Play Dead 10, Roll Over 15, Dance 20, High Five 25), so finished work visibly gives the pet new things to learn. The same counter also drives the pet's visible growth from Baby to Juvenile to Adult, so the reinforcement isn't abstract, a bonded, mature-looking companion is the literal, visible shape of consistent academic effort. Because training only ever moves forward from real work, "I taught it a trick" and "it's the thing I check on first" (both named directly in the original teacher brief) are earned outcomes, not shortcuts.

#### A27. Mystery Adoption Box's Variable-Ratio Design

Cross-reference: reachable from the Pet Shelter (A5). A $200 purchase that always yields a real pet, no empty or "nothing" outcome ever possible. This is the deliberate, load-bearing line between a genuine variable-ratio reinforcement schedule (uncertainty about *which* pet, a fun surprise) and a loot-box mechanic (uncertainty about *whether* you're reinforced at all, which this platform will not build). Rarity is weighted by category (common species pull far more often than rare/ultra-rare ones), and a pull preferentially rerolls toward species the student hasn't discovered yet, so a pull rarely feels wasted. Capped at one open per real calendar day to prevent a same-sitting repeat-open loop.

#### A28. Focus Guardrail Tiers

A tiered system (Claudia's guardrails design) that keeps a student's real assignments visible and inviting inside an open, highly gameified world, without ever gating or blocking the world itself, regulation, or free-roam play. Every tier has a per-student teacher override.

- **Tier 0, Arrival Card**: once per real day, a student lands on a genuine three-way choice: start Math, start Reading, or free time first, with "free time first" a real one-tap option, not a hidden escape hatch. Teacher-toggleable off per student.
- **Tier 1, Count It Out**: see A31 (a financial-literacy skill-building toggle, not a guardrail against distraction, but grouped under this same per-student-override design pattern).
- **Tier 2, Desk Glow**: while a student has unfinished tasks for the day, the computer desk (the task entry point in Town Square) gets a subtle visual glow/label. Teacher-toggleable off per student.
- **Tier 3, Companion Check-In & Wizard Lock**: after roughly 15 minutes of genuinely idle free-roaming (tracked by real movement, not session time) with real tasks still open, a trained companion pet (if the student has one following them) gently surfaces once per session, a dismissible suggestion, never a block, never required, and costs nothing if ignored. Separately, Scout (one of the Neighbors) has a similarly gentle, once-per-session check-in variant available after the same idle window. If a student instead goes a full 30 minutes with real tasks open and makes zero actual academic progress (not just idle time, genuine time without finishing any question set or native game, even while actively exploring or chatting), the **Wizard ThunderSword** appears as a real, non-dismissable lock on general gameplay, the one intentionally hard guardrail in this system. Even then, Help/calm-down (A29) stays reachable through it, per the standing rule that regulation is never gated. The lock clears itself automatically the instant real progress resumes, never on a timer or a dismiss tap.

#### A29. Take a Moment (Calm-Down/QuietTool), Help Ping & Break Request

The regulation path, reachable from the same fixed spot on every screen (folded into Town Square's pie menu, plus a floating button everywhere else), and never gated behind currency, streaks, or task completion. Opening it shows a breathing visual (a circle that grows on the in-breath, shrinks on the out-breath) with optional looping calm ambient sounds (ocean, waves, bubbles, rain), and, if the student has a bonded companion pet, that pet appears as a passive comfort presence, strictly opt-in and only for a student who already has one. A "🐾 Check on your pet" button also appears whenever the student owns any pet (not only a bonded companion), offering a trip to Home Room as a real, optional regulation-break activity rather than just the passive presence line, the explicit Phase 5 tie-in between pet care and regulation tools. From here, a student can quietly ping their teacher for help (which raises a full-screen, impossible-to-miss alert on the teacher's own screen, see A38) or request a break (which a teacher approves or grants for the student's own configured break length, temporarily unlocking the Playground, A25, for that timed window). Both are one tap, never a form, and the tool always confirms what happened ("your teacher has been quietly let know") rather than leaving a student wondering if anything registered.

#### A30. Piggy Bank: Real-Money Economy & Savings Goal

A real dollars-and-cents currency ("Class Cash") a student earns by completing tasks, badges, streaks, and Playground activities, and can view as a full running register (every transaction, income and spending, with icons and dates) or as charts over time. A **streak interest** bonus pays automatically the moment a student's daily streak ticks up: 1% of the current balance per consecutive day, capped at 20%, mirroring how a real savings account compounds, so a longer streak is worth more in a very concrete way beyond the underlying streak badge itself. A **Savings Goal** is entirely student-set (a label like "new bike" and a target amount) and shown as a fill-meter toward that amount; it's concept-only (no real interest math tied to the goal itself), never a teacher requirement, and never blocks spending elsewhere. This exists to make earning and saving legible and motivating (competence and autonomy, in Self-Determination Theory terms) rather than an abstract number going up.

#### A31. Count It Out Checkout

An explicit, opt-in, per-student teacher toggle (off by default) that changes Marketplace checkout from a single Confirm tap into tapping real bill and coin denominations ($20 down to 1 cent) until the tray total covers the price. This targets a specific, concrete skill (recognizing and combining real currency) for a student working on that as a goal, rather than money only ever appearing as an abstract number on screen. Tap-to-add rather than drag-to-a-tray, a deliberate accessibility call: a mis-aimed drag is a much easier miss than a mis-tapped button for a student with fine-motor or motor-planning differences.

#### A32. Needs vs. Wants Reflection Prompt

An optional, non-blocking tap in the Marketplace cart ("Still want it tomorrow? Yep / Not sure") attached to any purchase. It never gates or delays Confirm Purchase either way; it's purely a light reflection prompt, logged alongside the purchase in the register for the student (and teacher) to look back on, building the "needs vs. wants" reasoning habit without turning a purchase into a quiz.

#### A33. Badges & Streaks

Teacher-authored achievements, each either hand-awarded or governed by an auto-award rule (streak reaches N days, lifetime or today's activities completed reaches N, a subject fully finished N times, N Final Checks passed, N distinct tools ever used, N missed-then-corrected questions), each paying its own configurable Class Cash reward on earn. A daily streak (both subjects finished) is the backbone metric multiple systems key off of: it drives streak interest (A30), a "showed up" badge, and is visible on the student's own Passport (A10). No leaderboard or cross-student comparison exists anywhere; every one of these is a personal-progress metric only, per the standing platform rule. **Standing note (since 2026-09-25): auto-awards and manual awards are both fully paused platform-wide by direct teacher instruction ("clear all achievements, dont give anymore and dont add any without me saying it") — `BADGES_PAUSED` in `store.ts` is `true` — until she explicitly says to resume. Nothing described above is currently firing; do not flip this back or add new badges without her explicit go-ahead.**

#### A34. Feedback Tool

A structured, quiz-like feedback flow (never a blank "type your feedback" box), built specifically for students whose communication needs, including communication disorders, make free-form typing a real barrier. A student picks a big-icon category (Game Play, Visuals & Design, Assignments & Focuses, Wishlist, or Other), drills down through follow-up icon questions as far as that branch goes (e.g. Game Play to Build Mode to Asset to Add), then explains in their own words, typed or spoken through the browser's own voice-to-text. Every step is one big-icon question at a time, never a form with multiple fields at once. The full path taken (e.g. "Game Play > Build Mode > Asset to Add") is shown to the teacher as a plain-language breadcrumb in the Review Inbox, so context is never lost to a lookup table.

#### A35. Pet Shelter Donation

A real, deliberately reward-free coin sink at the Pet Shelter: a student can donate $5, $10, or $25 of their own Class Cash, with a running lifetime-donated total shown back to them and a simple thank-you message. No coins, items, or pets are ever granted for donating, on purpose, so it can never quietly become a second way to buy something. This is a genuine prosocial/SEL rep (practicing giving, not just earning and spending) rather than another transaction.

#### A36. No Leaderboards / Personal Progress Only

Not a single feature so much as a platform-wide constraint enforced across every system above: nowhere in Homeplot does a student see another student's score, streak, balance, or rank. Every motivational system here (badges, streaks, Score History, Piggy Bank charts) reports only on that one student's own history. This is a direct response to the specific research risk named in Claudia's standing brief: leaderboards are the single most consistently flagged risk factor for unhealthy competition and reduced intrinsic motivation in neurodivergent learners, even when badges, narrative, and other reward mechanics land well with the same population.

**Question screen toolbox tools now work — SHIPPED 2026-10-01, teacher: "in question set, tool bar tools arent clickable".** They were deliberate disabled placeholders. Now (`QuestionTools.tsx`, used by every `QuestionScreen`: Slime Chess, Castle Defense, Bakery Match, Gas Pump): **Calculator** (big-key + − × ÷ calculator panel), **Scratchpad** (finger/Apple Pencil drawing with 4 ink colors, eraser, clear), both floating panels draggable by their title bar so they never have to cover the answers; **Text Size** (cycles the question and answers through 100% / 125% / 150%, remembered on the device); **Highlight** (tap words in the question to mark them yellow, tap again to unmark, a "Done" pill turns it off; marks clear with each new question). Checked in a browser: 22 − 13 = 9, drawing, bigger text and a highlighted word all worked.

#### A46. Zones of Regulation Login Check-In — SHIPPED 2026-10-01

A mandatory check-in (Leah Kuypers' Zones of Regulation curriculum, Think Social Publishing) that fires the moment a student taps their avatar, before the world ever loads — full build spec in [`ZONES_OF_REGULATION_CHECKIN.md`](./ZONES_OF_REGULATION_CHECKIN.md), built from the teacher's own uploaded dev doc plus real curriculum research and a Claudia design-review dispatch. Step 1 asks "How are you feeling right now?" across four zone buttons (Blue, Green, Yellow, Red), each with an illustrated-style face, zone-color border, and always-visible text label — the subtext "All four zones are okay to feel" states the curriculum's core no-bad-zone philosophy in plain first-grade language. Step 2 shows the curriculum's own word list for that zone (e.g. Yellow: Frustrated, Worried, Silly, Excited, Nervous, Overwhelmed). **Green ends the check-in immediately**, no further screen. Any other zone opens a support menu: a tools board seeded from a curriculum-grounded starter list per zone (teacher-customizable to 2-3 tools per zone, per student, in Student Manager), a breathing circle that appears the moment a breathing tool is tapped (reusing the existing Take a Moment visual), and an optional note to the teacher (typed, or spoken through the same scoped speech-to-text exception Literacy Manipulatives already uses — never recorded audio).

Leaving the support menu schedules an **automatic Neighbor re-check** — one of the 4 existing Neighbors (Scout/Penny/Pip/Wren) "checks in" after 15 minutes (Blue/Yellow) or 5 minutes (Red), reusing the exact same zone/emotion picker and support menu. The re-check script follows the curriculum's own modeling language ("When I feel frustrated, I'm in the Yellow Zone. One tool that helps me is...") for every zone except Red, which gets a deliberately shortened, no-teaching, no-shaming branch per the curriculum's explicit Red Zone guidance. It ends by asking "Do you want me to check on you again?" (1/3/5/10 min, or "No thanks, I'm okay"), chaining further re-checks if the student asks for one.

**Teacher-facing:** a Red Zone pick, at login or at any re-check, reuses the existing full-screen help-ping alert (A29/A38) verbatim — zero new alert infrastructure, and it's the one "drop everything" signal a teacher already knows. Blue/Yellow check-ins with a note land as a normal (non-urgent) Review Inbox item instead, so Yellow (an expected, everyday zone per the curriculum) never trains alarm fatigue. A dedicated **🌈 SEL Check-Ins** teacher page (`/teacher/sel-checkins`) lists every check-in and its re-check outcome, and a clinical print-to-PDF view (`window.print()`, no new PDF dependency) renders a single record or a multi-select combined export free of all app chrome — built for the OT/SLP/psychologist/psychiatrist audience this data is meant to reach. Every check-in is its own `sel_check_ins` row (new Supabase table; migration confirmed run on production 2026-10-01).

**Neighbor re-check redesigned to the teacher's own mockup — SHIPPED 2026-10-01.** Direct teacher instruction with a mockup she made: "when the neighbors check in on students, i want it to be more in the layout of this mockup." The re-check (`SelRecheckPrompt.tsx`) is no longer a plain centered card: the Neighbor's own character render stands beside a rounded white speech bubble (name tag, the dialogue lines, a pointer toward the character), with big pill choice buttons underneath (purple for the main actions, gold for "No thanks, I'm okay"), all 56px tall for iPad touch. The portrait is the real auto-generated thumbnail every Neighbor already has (`public/world/thumbnails/characters_neighbor-<id>.png`), which closes the "Neighbor portrait art is a real asset gap" note from the original spec with zero new art. In iPad portrait / narrow widths it stacks to character-on-top with the bubble pointer flipping up. Town Square's own quest-dialogue chat modal (a separate, earlier, explicit "phone messaging app" instruction) was deliberately left untouched; this request was scoped to the SEL re-check.

**Neighbor re-check rebuilt as a full conversation scene — SHIPPED 2026-10-01 (supersedes the speech-bubble pass above, same day).** Direct teacher feedback on that pass, with a screenshot: "this is better, but not what i want exactly. I want a no background, slightly animated [character]... The central part of the screen should have the main messages, center on the screen should be the student responses. Neighbor character should be in the bottom left corner. on the right side should be the full conversation transcript like the current interactions with the neighbors. it should be a running record." Now:
- **The Neighbor is their real 3D model, not a picture.** New `src/components/NeighborCharacter3D.tsx` renders the Neighbor's own in-world GLB (`neighbor.modelPath`) on a transparent canvas in the bottom-left corner, playing its real `idle` clip plus a gentle sway, and a small hop every time the Neighbor says something new. Lazy-loaded, so three.js only downloads when a re-check actually opens (this component is mounted at the app root). The old thumbnail had a white box baked in, which is what showed in her screenshot.
- **Center:** the Neighbor's current message in a large white speech bubble (name tag, read-aloud in the Neighbor's own voice, tail pointing to the character), and the student's answer choices centered directly under it (zone buttons, then emotion words, tools, snooze pills).
- **Right:** a running transcript panel ("Talking with {Neighbor}"), the same left/right chat bubbles as Town Square's Neighbor conversations, auto-scrolling. It opens with this check-in's own history (the earlier pick and time, any tools tried, the last re-check) and records every Neighbor line and every student answer, tool tap and note as they happen.
- **Flow tightened:** the separate "Tell {Neighbor}" tap is gone (the zone buttons show right under the question), and a closing line ("Okay! I'll check on you again in 5 minutes." / "Okay. I'm here if you need me.") with a "Bye, {Neighbor}!" button now ends the conversation, so the student sees the Neighbor's reply before it closes. Script wording is otherwise unchanged (Claudia's one-pager fixes below are still waiting on approval).
- iPad portrait / narrow screens: the transcript moves to a strip along the bottom and the Neighbor shrinks but stays bottom-left.
- **Not verified live:** this sandbox can't load the app without the Supabase backend, so the 3D framing and layout need the teacher's own look on an iPad.

**Teacher feedback on the 3D conversation scene (2026-10-01): "this looks much better!"** plus three follow-ups, all SHIPPED same day:
- **Pending re-checks reset on logout, and on the next login.** "Make sure that the neighbor interruptions reset every time the student logs out (not idle waiting if the student gave a time in the future but then left the site)." New store action `cancelPendingSelRechecks(studentId)` runs inside both `logoutStudent` and `loginStudent`, so a "check on me in 10 min" never survives a logout, and one left behind by simply closing the site is cleared the moment that student logs in again (it never pops up stale in a later session). Cancelled re-checks are marked complete with no re-check zone, so the teacher log simply shows no re-check for them.
- **A re-check never interrupts a game.** "The neighbor check in should never be interrupting a game. it should just wait until the student exits before launching this screen." `SelRecheckPrompt` now only opens on calm between-things screens (Town Square, Home Room, the computer desktop, Mailbox, Piggy Bank, Marketplace, Passport, Pet Journal). Anywhere else (Bakery Match, Castle Defense, Arcade, Cinema, a subject's tasks/quizzes/platformer, Creative Island...) it waits and opens the moment the student lands back on one of those screens. Inside Town Square it also waits while a Neighbor conversation, the chat menu, a feelings chat, or a gas question/lockout is open (new session-only `selRecheckHold` flag the scene sets). Once it opens it stays open until the student finishes.
- **Open-world chat menu: jokes or feelings.** "Students should now be able to select from two options to chat with any neighbor when they're playing in the open world: jokes or talking about their feelings." Talking to any Neighbor the student has already met now opens a "Chat with {Neighbor}" menu with two big choices: **😄 Tell me a joke** (runs a joke conversation in the existing chat modal via new `pickJokeVariant`, preferring a joke the student hasn't collected yet, so it keeps feeding the Joke Book with the same first-time reward) and **💛 Talk about my feelings** (new `NeighborFeelingsChat.tsx`: the same full 3D conversation scene, a student-started Zones check-in using the same picker and support menu, saved as a real `sel_check_ins` record on the teacher's SEL Check-Ins page; a Red pick alerts the teacher exactly like login). "I need a minute" always closes it, and no automatic re-check is scheduled since the student came to talk on their own. This also covers fix #8 from Claudia's one-pager audit (student-started check-ins for every zone, Green included). A Neighbor's very first meeting still plays the Meet the Neighbors quest conversation, since that's what unlocks them. The scene layout itself is now a shared `NeighborScene.tsx` used by both the re-check and the feelings chat.

**Zones storybook word cards saved — 2026-10-01, direct teacher upload.** Two official reproducibles (Think Social Publishing, Zones of Regulation 2-Storybook Set, "Supplementary Reproducible C for Elementary Ages") saved to `docs/curriculum-reference/`: Blue (Tired, Bored, Sad, Sick) / Green (Calm, Happy, Proud, Focused), and Yellow (Silly, Excited, Frustrated, Worried) / Red (Panicked, Overjoyed/Elated, Terrified, Angry), each with an illustrated face card. Compared against `SEL_ZONE_EMOTIONS`: every storybook word except **Terrified** is already in the app's lists (the app has Scared in its place); the app's lists also carry extra words not on these cards (Lonely, Moving Slow, Content, Good to Go, Nervous, Overwhelmed, Out of Control). **Open, waiting on the teacher:** her message with the upload was cut off ("a"), so nothing was changed yet. Candidate next steps: match the word lists to the storybook cards exactly, and/or use the storybook's illustrated-face style as the art direction for the zone faces (still emoji placeholders, spec §2).

**Check-in now reaches students who stay logged in + blank-screen fix — SHIPPED 2026-10-01, teacher report: talking to a Neighbor about feelings went to a blank screen (console: "Failed to fetch dynamically imported module ... NeighborCharacter3D"), and "the full SEL overhaul from earlier is also not showing to my students."** Two root causes. (1) The login session is saved on the device (`iwd-session`), so a student whose iPad stays logged in goes straight to Town Square and the avatar-tap check-in (StudentLogin.tsx) never ran. New `SelLoginGate.tsx` (app root) shows the same Zones check-in once per login and once per day (`selLoginCheckInKey`, persisted with the session) wherever a logged-in student lands, but only on calm screens (never mid-game or mid-task). (2) A tab left open across a deploy keeps running the old build: it never shows anything new, and the first time it opens a lazily loaded screen (the 3D Neighbor scene, Town Square, Slime Chess, Build Mode) the old file name 404s and the app crashed to blank. New `src/lib/freshBuild.ts`: those screens load through `lazyFresh`, which reloads onto the new build instead of crashing (at most once per 30 seconds), plus global handlers for the same error; and every 3 minutes (and when the tab comes back into view) it checks whether a newer build is live, then reloads the next time the student is on a calm screen. The student stays logged in. Not verified on a real student iPad from here.

**Official "Zones Check-In Considerations" one-pager saved + Claudia audit — 2026-10-01, direct teacher upload: "claudia, review and save this for the zones of regulation component."** Saved at `docs/curriculum-reference/zones-check-in-considerations.pdf`; Claudia turned its 5 DOs / 5 DON'Ts into 8 standing design rules for this component and audited the shipped build against them, all recorded in `ZONES_OF_REGULATION_CHECKIN.md` §9. Result: PASS on no rewards/punishments and on never labeling a student's zone; FAIL on "don't force a check-in" (login check-in is mandatory, the re-check has no "not now"), "model first / not one-sided" (Neighbors never name their own zone), "observation plus inquiry" phrasing, and "not only in challenging moments" (re-checks only follow non-green picks); PARTIAL on the rest. **In progress / waiting on the teacher:** the 11 prioritized fixes in §9.2 are queued, not built. Fix #1 (a "Not today" skip on the login check-in) reverses her own original "mandatory" design call, so it needs her explicit go-ahead before any of the set ships.

**Scope cuts, named explicitly, not silently dropped** (see the spec doc's own §8): per-zone distinct re-check scripts (the single generic script ships first, real per-zone variants wait for real usage data); true recorded/playable voice notes (transcription-only for now, no audio storage); licensed real photography for the zone faces (v1 ships large expressive emoji with a zone-color border and always-visible label as a faithful placeholder for Claudia's "illustrated bust" styling — real commissioned art is a pure asset swap once an art pipeline exists); content-based crisis detection on note text (a distinct NLP feature, not attempted here — this is zone-based alerting only).

---

### GROUP 4: PLATFORM/OTHER FEATURES

#### A37. My Tools Panel & Accessibility Toolbar

A floating "My Tools" button, present on every student screen (and as a smaller inline button inside the internal browser, so tools stay one tap away even inside an embedded external activity). Opens a menu grouped into Subject Tools (Multiplication Table, Hundreds Chart, and Number Line for math; Thesaurus, Dictionary with a visual morpheme/word-parts breakdown, and Sound Wall for literacy) and an Accessibility Toolbar available everywhere: Calculator, read-aloud (TTS) settings including an app-wide dyslexia-friendly font toggle, a Word Processor, a Whiteboard, and the calm-down tool (cross-referenced in full at A29). The **Word Processor** doubles as a Notes app (multiple independently saved, named documents, not one shared scratch blob) with per-word/phrase rich-text color-coding and highlighting drawn only from colors/fonts the student has actually unlocked in the Marketplace, plus a **Personal Journal** mode (the same editor, pre-dated like a diary, direct teacher instruction: "word processor should be base, think of a diary"). Voice-to-text was removed app-wide by direct teacher instruction (2026-09-22, see Part D); typing/tapping is the only text-entry method here. A scoped, separate re-add of speech-to-text exists only inside Literacy Manipulatives' Text Box, Symbol Sentence, and Sentence Formula fields (see A23), not here. A teacher can turn off any individual tool per student in Student Manager, everything defaults on.

#### A38. Teacher-Student Chat & Alert System

A real-time, one-thread-per-student chat between a teacher and student, opened from either side. On the student side, an incoming teacher message never interrupts whatever the student is doing (mid-quiz, mid-drawing, anywhere); it shows as a small, dismissible "new message" toast the student opens on their own terms, an explicit design choice since an unannounced full-screen interrupt is exactly the kind of unpredictable change this population needs protected against. On the teacher side, a student's help ping (from A29) raises a large, impossible-to-miss full-screen alert regardless of which teacher page is currently open, with the next-oldest unresolved ping automatically taking its place once one is resolved.

#### A39. First-Login Onboarding & What's New Book

A one-time walkthrough (plain-language, icon-paired) shown the very first time a student logs in, covering the stepping-stone task flow, the always-available Tools panel, the calm-down button, and the "no rushing, no timers, ever" completion model. Separately, a "What's New" changelog book (the same page-turning book UI as the Joke Book, A10) automatically opens in Town Square the first time something new ships that affects a student, and stays reachable afterward from Town Square, Mailbox, and the computer desk, marking only genuinely-unseen entries as new rather than replaying the whole book every time.

#### A40. Teacher Portal

The full teacher-facing admin surface, all behind its own login: Home/Live Overview (a real-time roster of what every student is doing right now), Student Manager (per-student settings: feature toggles, guardrail-tier overrides, Count It Out, custom tool links, Literacy Focus Sets, Creative Island unlock), Assignments (daily-plan builder, Activity Library, Question Sets, Plan Templates, weekly schedule), a Game tab (Cinema Videos, Arcade/Scratch Games, and Music libraries, split out from academic Activities since all three share the same "play for fun, no tracking" shape), Review Inbox (help pings, off-screen photo submissions awaiting verification, student Feedback submissions, and Quiz Struggle flags, all in one queue), Badge Manager (the auto-award rule engine described in A33), Marketplace Manager (the full cosmetic/prize/power-up catalog), Piggy Bank admin (per-student balance adjustments, streak overrides, and the same register/charts view a student sees), World Editor/Build Mode (A4), Focuses authoring (A20), Student Live View (a read-only mirror of exactly what one student currently sees), Score History (A22), and the same real-time chat as A38.

#### A41. Data, Sync & Platform Infrastructure

Supabase (Postgres, Storage, and realtime subscriptions) as the backend, with a Zustand client store that keeps a local, persisted copy of everything so the app stays usable through a flaky connection. Every write retries with backoff automatically; a write that still fails after every retry surfaces a visible, dismissible banner (not just a silent console error) naming the exact table and error so a teacher reporting it has something concrete to relay, with a manual Retry button and a background retry queue that keeps trying regardless. Vercel auto-deploys on every push to `main`, so a shipped fix or feature reaches students without a separate release step.

#### A42. The Computer: Widget Desktop

The screen a student reaches through the in-world computer desk (A1), styled as an actual retro computer/browser rather than a plain app screen: a title bar with traffic-light dots, a fake address bar ("🔒 www.yoglandia.town/my-computer"), and a single always-in-the-same-place "✕ Close, back to Town Square" button. Below that chrome, the body is a Mac-desktop-style widget grid (`.desktop-widget-grid` in `StudentHome.tsx`), not a row of small text buttons: a live analog clock, a calendar card, and icon/launcher cards for Mail, Piggy Bank (balance shown live), Marketplace, Achievements, Daily Spin (outlined when unspent today), Chat, What's New, and Town Square.

Math, Literacy, and Playground are the same widget-card system, not a separate visual language, by direct teacher instruction ("they should all be varrying size cards but as if they were all computer widgets"): Math and Literacy render as large widget cards (`.widget-card-lg`), Playground as a wide widget card (`.widget-card-wide`), inside the identical `.desktop-widget-grid` every icon widget uses, so the desktop reads as one coherent set of Mac-style widgets in varying sizes rather than "widgets plus a different-looking to-do list." **This sizing/grouping decision is settled** and should not be re-split into a separate visual treatment without new, explicit teacher instruction.

Not yet built: drag-to-rearrange and per-student theming (needs new persisted layout/theme state on `Student`, not a UI-only change). See Part B for the next layer on top of this screen: a shared "webpage frame" chrome standard applied consistently to every other screen a student reaches from here (Bank, Marketplace, Cinema, Chat), plus real data widgets for Bank, Cinema, and Chat directly on this desktop.

#### A43. Bakery Match (native question-set-fueled game, Town Square)

A Candy Crush-style match-3 game (`BakeryMatch3.tsx`) reached by walking into the Bakery role-assigned building in Town Square (`WorldObjectRole: 'bakery'`) — the reference implementation for the standing "native question-set-fueled games per building/asset" direction (see Part B), the same pattern A24/`NATIVE_GAME_STANDARD.md` generalizes from.

- **Logic:** a game is `TOTAL_ROUNDS` = 3 rounds; a round is `MOVES_PER_ROUND` = 3 successful moves (a swap that produces a real match — a non-matching swap shakes/reverts and doesn't count). After each round's last move, a question gate opens: `QUESTIONS_PER_GATE` = 3 correct answers required to continue, a wrong pick never resets the count (same "wrong costs nothing, try again" rule the Gas Pump lockout uses, see A44). After round 3's gate passes, the game ends: the Bakery Treat Wheel spins once, total match XP for the session is appended to the student's own private leaderboard (`student.bakeryLeaderboard`), and they land back on the main menu. Question source (random vs. a specific teacher Question Set, via the shared `QuestionSourcePicker`) is chosen once on the main menu and locked for the whole game — no mid-round reselection.
- **User Experience:** warm cream-to-caramel palette, pill-shaped "Play New Game"/"View My Leaderboard" buttons, round/move pips instead of a raw match counter, an XP pill, a quiet gear icon as the only pre-game route to the question-source picker, and a ✕-with-confirm exit instead of an unwarned mid-game leave. Drag-threshold-gated swipe input (`DRAG_THRESHOLD_PX`). The private leaderboard is never shown to any other student, per the standing no-cross-student-comparison rule.
- **Academic integration:** every correct question-gate answer pays `REWARD_PER_QUESTION_CENTS` = $0.50 immediately (real Piggy Bank transaction + the app-wide coin-drop animation), separate from and additive to the XP/match-score system. Finishing the whole game (all 3 gates passed) also grants a Daily Spin Bonus Spin, same `bonusSpinAvailable` flag every other "finished everything" reward uses. Two persistent counters track real academic engagement here, deliberately kept separate so neither can drift the other: `bakeryQuestionsAnswered` (lifetime, every submitted answer right or wrong — a participation tracker, unlocks the Cake Character skin at 100) and `bakeryMilestoneTier`/`bakeryMilestoneCount` (an escalating cash goal — reach `tier * 100` correct answers across sessions to earn $(tier × 100), then the goal grows by 100 and resets; a shared `advanceMilestone` function drives both the live header display and the real settlement math so they can never disagree). Nothing is banked until a game actually completes — no live mid-game payout.

#### A44. Gas Pump (native question-set-fueled game, Town Square)

The driving fuel mechanic (`TownSquare.tsx`'s gas-gauge state, reached via the HUD Fill Up button while driving or by walking up to a Gas Pump/Gas Station-role placed object) — the second reference implementation of the "native games per building/asset" direction, question-gated rather than match-3-gated.

- **Logic:** a 10-dash gas gauge drains 1 dash per 15 real seconds of driving/idling in a car (a full tank empties in exactly 150 seconds), independent of the gas pedal or actual movement, and persists across getting out of and back into the same car (only a fresh login resets it). The drain timer fully pauses while a gas question is on screen, whether from a voluntary Fill Up or the forced lockout, and resumes exactly where it left off. Refueling only happens via correctly-answered questions: +1 dash per correct answer (capped at 10), 0 for wrong, no partial credit. If the gauge hits 0 without refueling, a blocking lockout opens automatically — 10 questions correct **as a running total that never resets on a miss** (a same-day fix superseding the original "resets to 0 on any miss" design, see the corrected note on the original build-log entry in Part B) — with no dismiss/skip/backdrop-close; `gasBlocked` stops the car dead until it's cleared.
- **User Experience:** the HUD Fill Up button is optional and closable any time before empty; the forced lockout is not. A miss during lockout reads "Let's try the next one!" (never "streak reset," a direct mitigation Claudia specified), and the running count stays visible ("You're at N of 10") through a miss so the student always sees real progress, never a wipe. A placed Gas Pump/Gas Station model opens the same refuel prompt right where the student is standing, no navigation to a separate 2D route.
- **Academic integration:** each question is a real multiple-choice question pulled at random from the teacher's own Question Set library (same shared question-source mechanism `BakeryMatch3`/`QuestionSourcePicker` uses); when the library has nothing usable, the auto-question generator supplies a fallback instead of silently topping up the tank for free. **Deliberately deprioritized, not forgotten:** Gas Pump does not run through the `submitQuizAnswer`/`QuizAttemptRecord` pipeline the way Bakery Match, ordinary Quiz tasks, and the Platformer do — it calls `generateAutoQuestion` directly, so it produces no persisted attempt record and won't show up in Score History or any future Reports/PDF export until it's wired into that pipeline (see the Central Educator Dashboard spec, Part B, item 8 — explicitly out of scope for that pass per direct teacher instruction, "Gas Pump reporting can wait").

#### A45. Castle Defense (native question-set-fueled game, Town Square) — SHIPPED 2026-09-30

Direct teacher request with two real asset packs attached: "let's build a tower defense game that uses this castle as the asset like the bakery... Claudia, what do we need to do to get this to be a functional, native game?" Claudia reviewed and produced the build spec below (design decisions are hers); `CastleDefense.tsx` is the third reference implementation of the "native games per building/asset" direction, reached by walking into the Castle role-assigned building in Town Square (`WorldObjectRole: 'castle'`). Built entirely from the teacher's own two uploaded asset packs — no invented or stock art:
- **3D building:** "Low Poly Castle" by treymill33 (Sketchfab, CC-BY-4.0, credited on `TeacherHome.tsx` alongside the existing Poly Pizza credit), placed at `public/world/models/buildings/castle/castle.gltf`, auto-picked up by the asset manifest and Build Mode catalog the same way every other building model is — no special-case code needed.
- **2D sprite kit:** the teacher's own top-down tower-defense sheet (`towers.png`, `characters.png`, `props.png`, `ui.png`, `tileset1.png`) was pixel-sliced (connected-component detection, not hand-eyeballed coordinates) into 18 individual sprites at `public/castle-defense/`: 3 tower types × 3 real upgrade tiers each (`towers.png` turned out to be a 3×3 grid, not the 3×2 it first looked like at thumbnail size — Stone/Banner/Mystic towers, each genuinely upgradeable tier 1→2→3), 4 enemy types (Goblin/Knight/Rogue/Wizard), 3 decorative props (tree/bush/rock), and the gem currency + wave-badge icons. `tileset1.png`'s 3 other palette recolors are shipped-but-unused, a flagged Phase 2 cosmetic-unlock candidate, not built.
- **Logic:** 5 waves, each `gate → build → advance`, and a gate never overlaps live enemies — the two phases are structurally exclusive, so `NATIVE_GAME_STANDARD.md` §4's "zero time pressure on a question" rule holds by construction rather than by pausing a running clock. The gate (`QUESTIONS_PER_GATE` = 3 correct, wrong never resets the count) mints Gems (`GEMS_PER_CORRECT` = 2), this game's single in-session currency. The build phase has no clock: tap an empty slot (5 fixed slots) to place a tier-1 tower, tap a built one to upgrade it — towers are upgraded, never replaced or removed. Per Claudia's explicit call for this population, **there is no fail state**: an uncleared wave is a cosmetic-only "slipped past, your walls held strong" banner, matching the teacher's own mockup (which has no HP/lives bar at all).
- **Combat engine REBUILT same day, direct teacher feedback ("nowhere near the quality of gameplay needed... why is it so poor quality?").** The original v1 precomputed a wave's whole outcome instantly (total tower DPS vs. total enemy HP, weakest-first) and only animated a fixed endpoint — no projectile, no firing, no moment where a tower and an enemy were ever in the same place at once. Claudia's follow-up review (real tower-defense design research: fixed-lane zone targeting, wave-manager/targeting/projectile as decoupled live systems, "readability and the weight of each decision" as the genre's whole appeal) called this the root problem, not a detail. Rebuilt as a real `setInterval` tick simulation (`TICK_MS` = 150ms): each of the 5 tower slots owns an equal `ZONE_WIDTH` stretch of the road (fixed-lane targeting), enemies spawn staggered (`SPAWN_STAGGER_MS`) and advance independently, and a placed tower actually fires (`TOWER_FIRE_COOLDOWN_MS` = 1s cooldown, its tier's dps = damage per hit) at whichever active enemy in its zone is furthest along, with a real projectile (a small CSS dot, no new art), a tower recoil animation, an enemy hit-flash + live HP sliver, and a distinct death-vs-leaked animation so "defeated" and "got through" never look the same. The castle itself (previously absent from the scene entirely) now renders at the road's end and visibly shakes on a leak. A "Next wave" enemy-icon preview shows before Send Wave (the genre's "where do I spend" planning tension), and a clean wave-clear gets a real coin-pop payoff (reusing Bakery Match's own `bakeryCoinPop` keyframe) instead of just banner text. Whether a wave clears is still never random — towers placed/upgraded is the only lever — but it's now the genuine emergent result of the simulation, not a lump-sum calculation.
- **Academic integration:** same reward shape as Bakery Match — nothing is banked until the whole game (5 waves) completes, at which point every correct answer that session pays `REWARD_PER_QUESTION_CENTS` = $0.50 (real Piggy Bank transaction + coin-drop animation) and a Daily Spin Bonus Spin is granted. Feeds the same `specificGame`/`anyGame` gameplay-mode assignment system Bakery Match and the Gas Pump do (`NativeGameId: 'castleDefense'`).
