- **Escalating cash-milestone goal — SHIPPED same day, direct teacher follow-up instruction: "add the same logic as bakerymatch in terms of answering X number of questions awards you."** Exact same mechanism as Bakery Match's own goal (`bakeryMilestoneTier`/`bakeryMilestoneCount`): `castleDefenseQuestionsAnswered` is a lifetime participation tracker (every correct gate answer, shown on the main menu), and `castleDefenseMilestoneTier`/`castleDefenseMilestoneCount` is an escalating goal — reach `tier * 100` correct answers (counted from 0 each time) to earn $(tier × 100), then the goal grows by 100 and resets — via the same `advanceMilestone` function shape, settled once at game completion alongside the per-question reward above, never live mid-game. A tappable 🎯 goal pill on the game topbar (matching Bakery's own) shows live progress and opens an explainer modal. **This one required a real schema change**, unlike the rest of this feature: `castle_defense_questions_answered`/`castle_defense_milestone_tier`/`castle_defense_milestone_count` were added to `supabase/schema.sql` (idempotent `alter table ... add column if not exists`, same pattern as every other column addition in that file) and wired fully into `types.ts`/`sync.ts`/`store.ts`. **This migration needs to actually run against the live Supabase database before these three fields will sync across devices or survive a page refresh** — until then the values still work correctly within a single browser session (the local Zustand state updates immediately) but a sync write to the missing columns will fail and the fields will reset to their defaults on the next hydration.
- **Two direct teacher UI fixes, same pass:** the 🪙 coin emoji in the money-earned pill and the post-game earnings celebration was rendering as a plain dark circle on the teacher's system (a known cross-platform emoji-font gap for that specific glyph) — swapped to 💰, a much older/more universally-supported emoji, in both places. And a new opt-in `lockOnWrongAnswer` prop was added to the shared `QuestionScreen` component (`src/components/QuestionScreen.tsx`): when true (Castle Defense only — Bakery Match and the Gas Pump keep their existing retry-the-same-question behavior unchanged, a prior explicit teacher instruction, since this defaults to false), a wrong pick locks every choice button immediately instead of letting the student keep clicking the remaining ones, and replaces the small "try a different question" link with a prominent primary "Next Question ▶" button — reusing the existing `onSkip` callback (already wired everywhere) so there's no dead end even with the stricter lockout.
- **Explicitly not built this pass, flagged not forgotten:** no persistent cross-device leaderboard (unlike Bakery Match's `bakeryLeaderboard`) — only the milestone/lifetime-tracker fields above were added, not a full per-game history list, since that wasn't what was asked for this round. A private per-game leaderboard is still a reasonable later addition. Also not built: real pathfinding (a single fixed waypoint path, towers never block or reroute it — an explicit, flagged simplification, not an oversight), the 3 unused tileset palettes, and sound effects for the new projectile/hit/recoil events (the existing `playSfx` bakery-sound-file pattern is reused for pop/hit/clear cues where a matching file already exists; dedicated castle-defense SFX would need new audio assets, not something the teacher's two asset packs included).
- **Verified:** `npx tsc -b` and `npm run build` (which regenerates the asset manifest and master asset chart, both now correctly list the Castle model) pass clean.
- **Gameplay rebalance + map redesign, 2026-10-01, direct teacher instruction ("needs a lot of improvement") answered by a fresh Claudia code review, plus a follow-up visual reference request.** Claudia's review found the combat sim animated correctly but the strategic layer under it was thin and one branch of it actively miscalibrated:
  - **Tower types REBALANCED + given distinct mechanics.** All three used to differ only in a cost/dps curve, and the real math made Pink (Mystic) strictly worse gem-for-gem than Stone or Wood at every tier — not a real choice, a trap. Now each does something the others don't (`tick()`'s firing loop): Stone is the single-target baseline; Wood cleaves a second enemy in its own zone for `WOOD_CLEAVE_MULTIPLIER` of its dps (real value against a cluster, not against a lone target); Pink hits for less but slows its target's travel speed for `PINK_SLOW_TICKS` ticks via a slow-debt-against-elapsed-time accumulator (stays correct even if a tab is throttled/backgrounded, same reasoning the original wall-clock-based progress calc already relied on) — crowd control, not raw damage. Costs/dps retuned alongside so no type is dominated. The "Build a Tower" picker now shows each type's one-line ability under its name, and projectiles render in a distinct color per type (CSS only).
  - **Wave 3's composition retuned** — total enemy HP per wave was 9/16/17/19/24 (wave 2→3 barely escalated, +1 HP, right when towers should start feeling tested); now 9/16/21/25/29, a real curve every wave.
  - **A "Perfect Wave" (0 leaks) now gives a small gem bonus and a gold-tinted badge** (CSS filter on the existing `ui-badge.png`, no new art) — the first reward this game gives for skillful play specifically, not just for any clear. A wave with leaks used to play the same sound cue Bakery Match uses for a CORRECT answer (`playSfx('match')`), a mixed signal; now plays the neutral `'pop'` cue, with `'combo'` reserved for a true Perfect Wave.
  - **The battlefield itself was rebuilt from a two-row layout (a flex row of tower slots above a straight brown strip) into one scene with a winding path**, direct teacher reference (screenshots of real pixel-art TD maps): `PATH_POINTS`, 6 waypoints forming exactly 5 segments — one per existing fixed-lane targeting zone, so `tick()`'s targeting math didn't need to change at all, only how `progress` maps to an on-screen point (`pointAlongPath`). Towers now sit beside the midpoint of the path segment they defend (offset perpendicular to it, matching where the reference screenshots place towers relative to the path) instead of floating in a row above it; the 3 existing prop sprites (tree/bush/rock) are scattered 8 times across the scene as real terrain instead of 3 small icons in a strip; the castle renders at the path's final waypoint. **Explicitly did NOT add a photo-realistic grass/dirt texture**, even though real tileable grass and dirt textures already exist in this repo (`public/world/textures/handpainted/`, `public/world/textures/wests/`, used for the 3D world) — checked them first, but they're photo-based, and a photo-real ground under this crisp flat-color pixel-art sprite kit would clash, the same reasoning Town Square's own grass-texture code comment already documents for its low-poly characters. The path and grass variation are CSS-only (an SVG stroke in the theme's existing brown/tan, layered flat-color gradients for the grass), no new image assets, consistent with the standing "no art this session can't actually generate" discipline.
  - A `https://www.instructables.com/Guide-to-Blooket-Tower-Defense/` reference link the teacher sent for the same request could not be fetched (blocked by this environment's network egress proxy) — not a blocker, the screenshot references and Claudia's own code-level review were enough to act on.
  - **New shared asset library, same pass, direct teacher upload: "use these as assets for the castle defense and other pixel style mini games that may come in the future."** Four packs, extracted and curated into `public/pixel-ui/` (not folded into the castle-defense-specific `public/castle-defense/` folder, since this is explicitly meant for reuse beyond this one game): `explosions/` (a full Craftpix explosion/fire/smoke/lightning library, 126 numbered-frame PNGs across 11 effect types, license at craftpix.net/file-licenses/), `pixelkiln-oakheart/` (39 UI sprites — buttons, panels, bars, dropdowns, tabs, badges — from the free 1x sample of Pixelkiln UI Pack Vol. 1's Oakheart/fantasy theme; usable commercially, credit appreciated but not required per its own license), `pixel-ui-free/` (bars, 20 icons, and the "wood" 9-slice theme from heyheythere's Pixel UI Free; **CC-BY-4.0, attribution required** — credited on `TeacherHome.tsx` with the exact line its license specifies), and `cyberpunk-etahoshi/` (48 sci-fi UI sprites from the teacher's own purchased "Pixel Fantasy Cyberpunk RPG UI Pack" by etahoshi — no license file was bundled with the download; used here as the teacher's own licensed asset for her own app, not redistributed, same trust level as her other uploaded packs this session). Only the 1x/non-duplicate files were kept (Pixelkiln ships 1x/2x/Godot-project copies of the same 39 sprites; 1x is what a flat 2D screen like this needs).
  - **Wired in now, concretely:** a new shared `src/components/ExplosionBurst.tsx` (self-terminating, so a parent that keeps an enemy's dead-status element mounted for the rest of the game — which Castle Defense already does — doesn't restart the burst on every tick re-render) plays the `explosions/Explosion/` 10-frame sequence on every enemy kill, layered over the existing CSS death-shrink animation rather than replacing it. Built as a standalone component specifically so the next pixel-style native game can reuse it with zero new code.
  - **Deliberately NOT done this pass, named not dropped:** reskinning Castle Defense's own HUD (topbar pills, modal cards, buttons) in the Pixelkiln/Pixel-UI-Free/Cyberpunk chrome is a real, separate visual project — picking which theme fits which future game, re-matching this game's warm green palette against a new UI skin's own colors, and redoing every modal/button in `index.css`'s `.bakery-*`/`.castle-*` rules is more than a bolt-on to an already-large pass. The assets are in place and ready; the reskin itself is open backlog.
  - **Verified:** `npx tsc --noEmit` and `npm run build` both pass clean.
- **Full-screen Kingdom Rush / Bloons style map — SHIPPED 2026-10-01, direct teacher feedback with a screenshot of the live game beside Kingdom Rush, Bloons TD and Kingdom Defense screenshots: "its getting better, but look at image 1... make the game look more like the other attached images."** The previous pass still read as a small boxed card (a striped-green box inside a pale card, a thin sharp-cornered path, a separate HUD card above it, an emoji castle, towers on white tiles that overlapped each other and the road). Rebuilt as:
  - **The map is the screen.** The battlefield now fills the viewport (16:10, sized to the screen height, iPad landscape and portrait both), inside a dark wood frame on a deep forest background. A wooden control bar under the map holds the hint, the next-wave enemy preview (or "Attackers left" during a wave) and a big green Send Wave button, so no control ever covers the road.
  - **New geometry module, `src/lib/castleMap.ts`.** One 160x100 unit space matching the map's aspect ratio. The road is a smooth Catmull-Rom S-curve through 18 control points, sampled and measured by real distance, so attackers walk at an even pace through bends, enter from off the left edge, and end at the castle gate. Zone targeting in `tick()` is unchanged (5 equal stretches by distance); each build plot is placed automatically beside the middle of its stretch, on whichever side has more open grass, so plots never land on the road or on each other.
  - **New static art, `src/components/CastleMapArt.tsx`** (memoized so the 150ms tick never re-renders it): a layered sand road (grass rim, dirt edge, sand, center highlight, pebble dots), a lily pond, a dense seeded forest ring of the kit's own tree sprite around every edge, scattered bushes/rocks/trees inside, and a drawn castle (stone walls, red and blue roofs, flags, wooden gate) replacing the old 🏰 emoji. The meadow is CSS only: bright green with soft patches and tiled white/yellow/pink flower dots and grass tufts at offset sizes so the repeat never lines up.
  - **Kingdom Rush style build plots and towers:** round dirt pads (empty ones ringed with stones and a bobbing + sign), glowing gold when the student can afford to build or upgrade there, an upgrade arrow badge on towers that can level up, 1-3 gold tier stars under each tower, and towers that visibly grow with each tier. Plots and attackers are depth-sorted so nearer things overlap farther ones.
  - **Attackers:** sized to the map, feet on the road, a soft shadow, a walk bob, flipped to face the direction they're walking, and a red/green health bar over the head. Shots now fly from the top of the firing tower to the target (they used to drop from above the target).
  - **Floating HUD in dark wood plates** with pixel icons from the Pixel UI Free pack (gem, star, coin, red X exit): gems + wave badges top-left, goal / money / exit top-right. The wave result is now a parchment card (Pixelkiln ornate panel as a 9-slice border) that pops up in the middle of the map.
  - **One pacing change:** `TRAVEL_MS` 5500 to 7000 so attackers cross the much larger board at a readable speed. Side effect: each tower's stretch now holds an attacker ~1.4s instead of ~1.1s, so towers land a few more hits and waves are a little easier. No fail state exists, so this only makes Perfect Waves somewhat more common; worth watching with real students.
  - **Verified:** a one-off static render of the new map (landscape 1180x820 and portrait 820x1180) was checked once before shipping, and `npx tsc -b` + `npm run build` pass clean. The live wave (towers firing, explosions, result card) still needs the teacher's own look on an iPad.
  - **Teacher feedback (2026-10-01): "THE REDESIGN OF THE CASTLE DEFENSE IS SO MUCH BETTER THAN THE ORIGINAL RENDERINGS."** The full-screen map direction is confirmed; keep building on it rather than revisiting the layout.
  - **Two follow-up fixes, same day, direct teacher feedback with screenshots:** (1) "ensure layers are correct. make sure pop up menus always stay in front": the depth-sorted build plots (z-index up to ~110) were painting over the Build a Tower picker (z-index 50) because the battlefield wasn't its own stacking context. `.castle-battlefield` now has `isolation: isolate`, so everything inside the map stays inside it and every pop-up always sits in front. Trees are also now kept clear of a plot by their whole canopy, not just their base, so a tree can no longer grow up over a build plot. (2) "remove additional fields that dont have anything when the wave is being sent": empty build plots are hidden during a wave; only plots with a tower show while attackers walk, and the empty ones come back for the next build phase.

  - **In progress, blocked on a re-upload (2026-10-01):** the "actually difficult" rework the teacher asked for (castle hearts with a retry-the-wave do-over, escalating waves with Rogue/Knight/Wizard traits and an Iron Captain boss, a simulation-tuned difficulty curve, the Archer Tower, Spike/Barrel/Barricade road traps and a Lightning strike, using her two new Craftpix packs) was fully built and tested, but the cloud workspace was reset before it was committed, and her two uploaded zips (archer towers, magic & traps) were wiped with it. The design and tuned numbers are kept in the session notes; it gets rebuilt as soon as she re-uploads those two zips.

#### A47. Slime Chess (native game, Town Square chess set) — SHIPPED 2026-10-01

Direct teacher request with five uploads (a Quaternius round table, "Chess Set" by Jarlan Perez, the Jelly Chess art pack, a Bubble Buttons pack, and a free UI sample): "add this table as a general furniture asset. the chess set should have a role and become a chess game native game using the jelly chess assets! the general theme will be slime, bubbles, bright vibrant colors, fun pop noises when players are moved. ensure full game logic for real chess games. as this game is for neurodivergent kids, potential move pathways, hints/clues, recommendations, and explicit text bubble explanations if it doesn't work (like if they attempt a move that won't work) should be visible. use the other assets within the game."
- **World:** the table is a Build Mode furniture item (`interior/table-round-small-b.glb`, saved under a new name because a different `table-round-small.glb` already existed) and the chess set is `interior/chess-set.glb`, sized as a small table-top object (`SIZE_CLASS_OVERRIDE['Chess Set']`). New `WorldObjectRole` **'chess'** (`types.ts`, `ROLE_VIEWS.chess = '/student/chess'`, in both the Town Square and Island role pickers): a teacher places the chess set, gives it the Chess role, and walking up to it opens the game. The Chess Set model is CC-BY, credited on `TeacherHome.tsx`.
- **Panel cleanup + gear pie menu — SHIPPED 2026-10-01, teacher feedback: "the chess view is such a great first attempt!!" with four edits:** (1) "collapse all the buttons ... (except for town square) into one button with a settings icon ... clickable and open to pie like menu with all the existing buttons": Hint, Undo, Danger, Sound and New game now live in a round pie menu (`SettingsPie` in SlimeChess.tsx: one pastel slice per button, an X in the middle, tap outside / Escape to close, kept fully on screen at iPad sizes, closes on rotate/resize). Hint/Undo/New game close the menu; the Danger and Sound toggles keep it open so the change is visible. (2) "move the new settings icon to the far right hand corner ... inside the current white bubble for 'Your turn'. the town square button should be the same size, immediately to the left": done, both 64px tap targets. (3) "put only the image of captured pieces under ... 'Your Turn'": the separate "captured" panel is gone; the turn bubble shows just the pictures of the pieces that player has taken (always the student's own haul against the computer, the current player's in two-player mode). (4) "remove this log entirely": the move list is removed.
- **Question before every turn — SHIPPED 2026-10-01, direct teacher request: "lets add the question set aspect. one question should be asked to the student before each turn".** Before each of the student's turns (every turn for both players in two-player mode), the shared `QuestionScreen` asks one multiple-choice question; the board unlocks once it's answered correctly. Sourcing matches Castle Defense / Bakery Match: an active assignment targeting **Slime Chess** (new `NativeGameId` `'chess'`, now pickable in the daily plan builder's native-game list) or any-game assignment comes first and its answers count toward that assignment; otherwise random from the teacher's quiz sets, or one set the student picks on the chess menu ("Questions before each turn"), with the auto-generated question as fallback. A wrong answer offers "Next Question" (same no-penalty model); Undo never re-asks; the question's X (Leave Anyway) leaves the game. Not built yet / open for the teacher: no cash or milestone reward per chess question (Castle Defense pays 50 cents per question) and no lifetime chess-question counter (would need a new student column); say the word if chess answers should earn money too.
- **Less motion, locked-out opponent pieces, three indicators, a thinking computer — SHIPPED 2026-10-01, teacher edits: "1) add a setting in the chess game for reduced motion. 2) in single player mode, dont let the player click the opposite pieces. in multiplayer (same device), dont let the opposite player click their pieces if it is not their turn. 3) ... reduce indications to can move, can capture, risky 4) in single player, add a 5-10 second moment of hesitation before the computer makes their move".** (1) "Less motion" toggle in the gear pie menu (replacing the old Danger toggle): stops every wobble/bounce/pulse/splat and the floating bubbles, pieces jump instead of slide (`.sc-calm` in index.css). Starts on automatically if the student's existing Reduce Motion setting (`worldReduceMotion`) is on or the device asks for reduced motion. Not saved between visits yet. (2) Tapping a piece that isn't yours (the computer's, or the other player's when it's not their turn) now does nothing; it can still be captured once one of your own pieces is picked, and a picked piece tapped onto a square it can't reach still gets the plain-language "why not" bubble. (3) Removed the "their reach" preview and the "could be captured" danger glow entirely (feature and legend), leaving can move / can capture / risky. (4) The computer now waits a random 5 to 10 seconds before moving (`COMPUTER_THINK_MIN_MS`/`MAX_MS`), with "Computer is thinking..." showing the whole time.
- **Computer thinks 6 seconds — SHIPPED 2026-10-01, teacher: "reduce the computer waiting time as they're taking their turn to six seconds."** `COMPUTER_THINK_MS` 10000 → 6000 (replaces the earlier 10-second wait). Unchanged: the 3-second look at the board after the computer moves, before the next question.
- **Personal chess leaderboard + XP — SHIPPED 2026-10-01, teacher: "for chess, lets make a personal leaderboard with points xp assigned to captured points like traditional chess" (worldchess.com/chess-terms/chess-piece-point-values).** XP per game = the traditional value of every computer piece the student captures (Pawn 1, Knight 3, Bishop 3, Rook 5, Queen 9; `PIECE_VALUE` in chessCoach.ts). Live "⭐ N XP" pill in the turn bubble; the game-over card says how much XP the game earned and calls out a new personal best. **My Chess Leaderboard** on the chess start screen: lifetime total XP, games and wins, and the student's own top 5 games by XP (captured-piece pictures, level, result, date), with the point values printed under it. Personal only, no other students shown. Every game against the computer is saved to a new `chess_games` Supabase table (one row per game, re-saved with the same id if an Undo reopens a finished game); leaving or starting a new game mid-way saves it as "Not finished" with the XP so far. Two-player games aren't saved (shared device, two players). **Waiting on the teacher: run the `chess_games` SQL** (in `supabase/schema.sql`, also given in chat). Until then games still show for the rest of that visit, the rest of the app loads normally (the table is not on the must-load list), but saves fail and the "trouble saving" alert will appear when a chess game ends. Open: no XP for winning itself, and XP doesn't turn into Marketplace money; say if either is wanted.
- **Accurate placement warnings for every piece — SHIPPED 2026-10-01, teacher: "ensure placement warnings are accurate for all pieces in all possible moves, including pawns", then "logic for capturable pieces needs to be refined ... its not showing the correct bubble every time the piece is presented a spot it could be captured".** `riskyTargets` (chessCoach.ts) now makes each candidate move and lists the other side's real legal replies: a square is risky whenever any reply captures the piece there, even a fair trade. Because only legal replies count, it is right for pinned attackers (no false warning), a King that can't capture onto a guarded square, moves that give check (replies limited to answering it), **en passant** (a Pawn moving 2 squares beside an enemy Pawn is flagged, which the old `isAttacked` check missed), and promotion (judged as the new Queen, one bubble instead of 4). The yellow "!" now also shows on capture squares (it used to be hidden there, so a risky capture only got a faint orange ring). Illegal-move explanations: an en passant capture that would expose the King now gets the real pin explanation naming the attacker (chess.js hides the en passant square in that case, so it previously said "nothing to capture"), and a too-late en passant attempt explains that the chance has passed. Checked against 10 positions with a script.
- **Question timing + whose turn — SHIPPED 2026-10-01, teacher: "in a single player game, after the student has answered the question, the system needs to wait 10 seconds before the computer player takes their turn"; "questions should only appear at the top/start of a player's turn and never on a computer's turn. in multiplayer rounds, make sure you indicate whos turn it is to answer the question on the question itself and in a message that loads before the question screen"; "the computer player's move must be shown ... give a 3 second wait between the computer players move and the question"; "in multiplayer games, there should be the same 3 second wait between the move the student makes, and the launch screen".** The computer now always waits 10 seconds after the student's move (which always comes after that turn's question). Questions only ever open at the start of a player's own turn: against the computer, 3 seconds after its move with the board fully visible (the board is locked until the question is answered); with two players, 3 seconds after each move, then an "It's Blueberry's turn!" card (team King, about 2 seconds), then the question, which carries a "Blueberry's question" pill (new optional `whoLabel`/`whoIcon` props on `QuestionScreen`). Interpretation note: the teacher wrote "It's Blueberry Knight's turn"; the card uses the team name (Strawberry/Blueberry, or the chosen flavor pair) since a turn belongs to the whole team, not one piece. Easy to change.
- **Leave-game confirmation — SHIPPED 2026-10-01, teacher: "while in chess game, (and all games) give confirmation menu if attempting to leave game".** Slime Chess: the Town Square button and New game (from the gear menu) now ask "Leave this game?" / "Start a new game?" once anyone has moved and the game isn't over. Arcade (Scratch games): both "Game Shelf" and the browser-bar Back button ask first while a game is open. Already had one: Bakery Match, Castle Defense, the Platformer, and every QuestionScreen's X. Not covered: the device's own browser back gesture (the app's router can't block it without a larger router migration).
- **Real chess:** full rules through `chess.js` (castling, en passant, promotion with a 4-piece picker, check, checkmate, stalemate, threefold repetition, 50-move rule, insufficient material), each ending explained in plain language. Play the computer (Easy slips about a third of the time so beginners can win, Medium looks 2 moves ahead, Hard looks 3 ahead within about 1.2 seconds so it stays iPad-friendly) or a friend on the same iPad. Unlimited Undo (takes back the computer's reply too).
- **Help that's always visible (the core of the request), in `src/lib/chessCoach.ts`:**
  - **Move pathways:** tap a piece and every legal square gets a bouncing green bubble, captures get a pink ring, and squares where the piece could be captured for free get a yellow bubble with "!". Tap an opponent piece to see its reach in red bubbles ("watch those squares").
  - **Danger glow:** any of your pieces that could be captured and isn't protected pulses red (toggle "Danger: on/off"); a King in check pulses with a red badge.
  - **Hints / recommendations:** the Hint button draws a yellow arrow and explains why in kid words (for example "Try moving your Knight from b1 to c3. It gets a sleepy piece off the back row and into the game", or "It captures their Bishop (worth 3 points), and nothing can capture it back"). Unlimited.
  - **"Why not?" speech bubble:** every move that doesn't work gets a specific explanation, never a silent no: the piece's movement rule ("A Bishop slides diagonally..."), what's in the way ("Your own Pawn on e2 is in the way. Only Knights can jump!"), pawn rules (no backward, 2 squares only on the first move, diagonal only to capture), your own piece on the square, check ("Your King is in check! This move doesn't protect it..."), pins ("This Bishop is protecting your King... This is called a pin."), and each castling rule. Picking a piece also explains how it moves and what the bubbles mean. Every bubble has read-aloud, and the move list is written in plain language ("Blueberry Knight jumped to f6, capturing a Pawn").
- **Look and sound:** slime/bubble theme (vivid purple-pink-mint background with rising bubbles), Jelly Chess pieces and tiles in 3 flavor themes (Berry Blast: Strawberry vs Blueberry, Citrus Splash: Lime vs Lemon, Grape Fizz: Grape vs Icy), pieces that jiggle, hop when picked, squash-and-stretch when they land, and splat when captured. Bubble Buttons icons for Hint/Undo/Danger/Sound/New game/Home, Jelly pills for the menu, the free UI pack's spiky badges (check / game over) and soft pills (Play again / Change settings). Sounds are synthesized with Web Audio (`src/lib/slimeSounds.ts`): a bloop per move pitched by piece, a splat for captures, a boing for check, a soft low "nope" for a move that doesn't work, and a bubble fanfare to win. Sound toggle on screen.
- **iPad:** board sized to the screen's short side; landscape puts the coach panel beside the board, portrait stacks it below; all controls 44px+ (most 56-88px); reduced-motion respected.
- **Verified:** typecheck and build pass; the coach's explanations, hints and AI speed were tested headlessly against real positions; the menu and a few moves vs the computer were checked once in a browser (landscape and portrait).
- **Open, not built (flagged for the teacher):** no question gate or Piggy Bank reward is attached yet (chess is played for its own sake; tying a reward to finishing a game is a quick follow-up if she wants it), and no saved game history/leaderboard.

## PART B — Open Backlog, by Feature Front

### Tappy Plane (one-tap flying game). SAVED FOR A FUTURE NATIVE GAME (direct teacher instruction 2026-10-04)

- Kayden's words, verbatim: "claudia, save this game. we will add this as a native game in the future"
- **Asset pack (saved so it can't be lost):** `public/games/tappy-plane/`, "Tappy Plane" by Kenney Vleugels (kenney.nl), **CC0** (`license.txt` in the folder; no credit required, a courtesy "Kenney" credit is welcome). Contents: 4 plane colors (blue, green, red, yellow) with 3 propeller frames each (`PNG/Planes/`), a sky background, 5 ground styles (grass, dirt, ice, rock, snow) each with matching up and down rock spikes, bronze/silver/gold stars, puff clouds for the engine trail, UI art (Get Ready, Game Over, tap hints, bronze/silver/gold medals, buttons, panel), letter and number sprites, two spritesheets (`Spritesheet/sheet.xml`, `planes.xml`) and the Kenvector Future font. The vector source files were left out (not needed for the web).
- **Game shape (the Flappy Bird style the pack is made for):** tap anywhere to flap the plane up, gravity pulls it down, fly through the gaps between rock spikes, collect stars. One big tap target, so it suits iPads well.
- **Claudia's intake notes (to settle with Kayden before building, per `NATIVE_GAME_STANDARD.md`):**
  - Fits the standing native-games pattern: needs a Town Square home (for example an airfield or hangar building with its own role), question sets fuel it, real rewards on completion.
  - Question breaks between flights (or every N gaps, teacher-set), with zero time pressure, all accessibility tools, and leave-game confirmation, like every native game.
  - This genre fails fast (one touch of a rock ends the run), which can be harsh for students with high support needs. Proposed defaults: a gentle mode where bumping a rock makes a puff and the plane bounces back with nothing lost, wider gaps and slower scrolling to start, and a friendlier end card than the pack's "Game Over" art (for example "Nice flight!"). The engagement override (animations, sounds, music welcome) applies.
  - Stars and medals map naturally to rewards (stars collected in a run; bronze/silver/gold medals for distance), all personal-only, no leaderboards (A36).
  - Ground styles (grass, ice, snow, rock, dirt) can rotate per level or be picked by the student.
- **Status:** saved only. No code yet. Build when Kayden says to.

- **Flow (as specified):** pick student profile → (first login of the day only) a full-screen **Daily Streak** card before Town Square: the day count (big flame/calendar number, the days of the week marked like the Duolingo reference), "To keep your streak, complete 20 questions today", button **"I got this!"** → a pop-up **grid of all native games** (Bakery Match, Slime Chess, Castle Defense) plus a **Quiz mode** tile (just the questions, no game) → the student can close it with X → Town Square.
- **Streak meter (pill):** a pill-shaped tracker at the top of the screen with a 🔥 fire icon on the left and a 0 to 20 fill meter. Follows the student on every screen; draggable anywhere; double-tap the fire to collapse it to just the fire icon, double-tap again to expand. Every **correct** answer anywhere counts (native games, quizzes, the gas pump, any question screen). Reaching 20 saves the streak for the day.
- **Rewards (tap-to-open treasure chests, opened one at a time, Brawl Stars style):** day 1: one chest, $5. Day 2: two chests, $1 to $5 each, total at most $10. Days 3 to 9: one chest per streak day, $1 to $5 each, total at most $10. Days 10 to 19: $1 to $10 each, total at most $20. Days 20 to 29: $1 to $15 each, total at most $30. "Continue this logic": every further 10 days adds $10 to the daily cap and $5 to the per-chest max.
- **Streak Freezes:** $20 in the Marketplace; usable **proactively** (protect a day ahead of time, like a weekend or a known absence) or **retroactively** ("oh no" fix for a missed day). Every student starts with **one free freeze**. The **Daily Spin** gets a Streak Freeze wedge with a 5% chance.
- **Claudia's intake notes (to settle with Kayden before building):**
  - **This supersedes the earlier "login streak" redefinition** in the Phase 2 backlog above (item 5 and the "streak is being redefined" note): the streak is now kept by **20 correct questions a day**, not just logging in, which also resolves Claudia's earlier tradeoff note (streak money from logging in alone). The day count still shows each day they logged in. Needs updating when built: streak interest (A30), the Passport streak (A10), the paused streak badges (A33, still paused), and the old Final Check streak trigger.
  - **Weekends:** the old plan made Saturday/Sunday automatically safe; this spec uses freezes for weekends instead. Confirm weekends are no longer automatic.
  - **Chest count grows forever** (one chest per streak day means 93 chests on day 93, like her Brawl Stars example). Propose a cap on chests per day (for example 5 or 10, with the money cap unchanged) so opening stays fun and quick. Also confirm the band edges (day 10 and day 20 appear in two ranges; proposed: 10 to 19, 20 to 29, ...).
  - **"Correct answers only" for the 20:** consistent with "wrong never costs anything" (misses don't subtract), but a student who struggles with accuracy needs more attempts to save the streak. Option to discuss: count attempts after a miss-then-correct, or let the teacher lower the 20 per student.
  - **Game grid:** her list is Bakery Match, Chess, Castle Defense; confirm whether the Platformer, Gas Pump and future games (Space Bowling, Tappy Plane) join the grid automatically.
  - **The meter on every screen:** must never cover question text, answer buttons, the toolbox, or the Help/break button, and must be big enough to drag with a finger on iPad. Double-tap to collapse needs a visible hint the first time.
  - **Pop-up order on login:** Streak card → game grid → (Town Square) arrival card, What's New, Daily Spin, Bawk's Seamstress task. Never stacked, never mid-question.
  - Engagement override applies: chest-opening animation and sounds are welcome. No leaderboards or comparing streaks between students (A36).
- **Added 2026-10-04 (her words: "streak view should also be viewable in main pie menu"):** a 🔥 Streak wedge in the Town Square pie menu opens the streak view any time.
- **Shipped (Claudia made the open calls from her earlier choices; change any of these):**
  - **Engine** (`src/lib/streak.ts`, the `streak:<studentId>` row in `style_looks`, no new SQL): 20 right answers in a day saves the day and adds 1 to the streak. Every right answer anywhere counts: every game's question screen (Bakery Match, Slime Chess, Castle Defense, Space Bowling, the Gas Pump, Quiz Mode) and assigned quizzes, the Platformer and passages. Wrong answers never subtract.
  - **Daily streak card** (first Town Square visit of the day, before anything else): big flame with the day count, the last 7 days (🔥 saved, 🧊 frozen), "To keep your streak, answer 20 questions right today", read aloud, **I got this! 💪**. That opens the game grid (every native game plus **Quiz Mode**), which they can close with ✕. Town Square's own pop-ups (arrival card, What's New, Daily Spin, Bawk) wait until the card is done, so nothing stacks.

Drag it anywhere (remembered on the device). Double tap the 🔥 to shrink it to just the fire, and double tap again to open it. A hint says so the first time. Tapping the bar opens the streak view.
  - **Streak view** (tap the pill, or the new 🔥 **My Streak** wedge in Town Square's menu): count, best ever, the week, today's progress, freezes, a buy button, and freeze-a-day-ahead buttons for the next 3 days. If the streak just broke, there's a "get it back" button.
  - **Treasure chests** when a day is saved: tap them one at a time; each shakes, pops open and shows its dollars, which go straight into the bank (falling coins, register row "🔥 Streak chest (day N)").
    - Amounts: day 1 is one $5 chest. Day 2 is two chests, $1 to $5 each, at most $10. Days 3 to 9: $1 to $5 each, at most $10. Days 10 to 19: $1 to $10 each, at most $20. Days 20 to 29: $1 to $15 each, at most $30. Every further 10 days adds $5 per chest and $10 per day.
    - **Claudia's call:** at most 7 chests a day (one per day of the week), with the money cap unchanged, so opening stays quick.

    - **Claudia's call:** a missed day uses a freeze automatically (the card then says "A Streak Freeze saved your streak while you were away!").
    - Students can also freeze an upcoming day ahead of time.
    - With no freeze, the streak breaks. Buying one that same day and tapping "Use a freeze to get it back" restores it.
  - **Weekends:** not automatically safe (her spec uses freezes for that).
  - **Quiz Mode** (`/student/quiz-mode`): just the questions, no game, one after another. It's on the Game Dashboard, the streak grid and pick-your-game assignments. It pays $1 per right answer like every native game and counts toward an open "pick any game" assignment.
- **Claudia's code review of the day's student features (same day), all 10 findings fixed:**
  1. Quiz screen crash risk: a hook added for "Say it" sat after an early return in `QuizTask.tsx`. Moved up.
  2. The streak card's "Pick a game!" grid never showed: marking the card seen unmounted it first. It's now marked seen only when the grid closes or a game is picked.
  3. Quiz Mode re-showed the same question after a right answer and never paid: its question screen was keyed on the streak count, which changed mid-answer. Keyed on the question now.
  4. The fire pill could sit on top of quiz answers: it hides on quiz screens, which show their own progress.
  5. A saved pill spot could end up off screen after turning the iPad: it's pulled back on screen on load, resize and rotation.
  6. Pinch zoom fired from a thumb on the D-pad plus a finger looking around: it only counts two fingers on the world itself now.
  7. Double tap: the window is wider (0.7 seconds) for students who tap slowly, a look-drag no longer counts as the first tap, and the first tap shows "👆 Tap Scout again to talk".
  8. "Say it" had no way forward if the microphone was off or heard nothing: it now says why and offers **Skip this one**, announces what it heard, and matches "5" to "five" and ignores punctuation.
  9. Bawk's reminder could pop up while driving or over the streak chests: it now waits for both.
  10. Streak pill buttons now work with a keyboard, VoiceOver and Switch Control, with clearer labels. Stronger text contrast on chests and the pill count. The ✕ buttons say "Close". The Buy a freeze button says how much more money is needed.

The default Daily Streak goal is now 10 right answers a day (`ECONOMY_DEFAULTS.streakGoal` in `src/lib/economy.ts`). The card, the meter (0/10), the streak view, Quiz Mode and the "set today to one short" test button all follow it. If she ever saved a different number in Economy Settings, that saved number still wins; the Game tab's Economy Settings card shows the current value.
- **Teacher test tools (same day):** the Student overview's "Reset for testing" card now shows each student's Daily Streak (days, best, today's right answers, freezes).
  - The reset also clears the Daily Streak (back to 0, 1 free freeze, the streak card shows again).
  - New buttons: **Set today to 19 right answers** (one more saves the day, to test the chests) and **Give 1 Streak Freeze**.
- **Still to settle with her:**
  - Streak interest (A30) and the Passport streak (A10) still use the older "both subjects done" streak. They should probably switch to this one.
  - A per-student goal lower than 20 for students who need it.
  - The streak badges stay paused (A33)., ground paint, size rule, orbit camera. SHIPPED 2026-10-04 (direct teacher instructions)

- Her words: "remove fog from all views, especially build mode top view. 2) sky textues should auto change based on the uploaded skies every 5 minutes the studnet is playing. this action should just change casually in the background"; "dont make the view foggy or translusent at all when i zoom out"; "extend the rendering view so the sky is just a solid dome of the texture, continuous repeating pattern merging at seams. allow me to free paint texture on the ground including dirt and other textures"; "in live mode, allow me to do a 360+ turn with my right click. i want to be able to see my character full on and have an ability to turn another turn completely around the character"; "make sure the new characters are truly to scale with the assets. currently the expectation is that the scale in build mode equates to 1.00x scale. i want to change that to be that everything is adjusted to equal 5.00x the player/charcters hight" (asked which assets: she answered **every asset**).
- **Fog removed** from Town Square, Build Mode and Creative Island.
- **Sky dome rebuilt** (`SkyDome.tsx`, 11th pass): it travels with the camera and sizes itself inside the far plane, so zooming out never clips it into shards; the texture is projected on three axes and mirrored as it repeats, so it is one continuous pattern with no seams and no pinched pole.
- **Skies rotate** in Town Square: every 5 minutes of play the sky moves to the next uploaded sky (starting from her pick) and gently fades in over the old one.
- **Ground painting**: the existing Ground brush (Paint panel → Ground, drag on the ground) now lays texture by world position, so overlapping dabs of the same texture merge into one continuous stroke, and each dab's edge fades softly into the grass (`GroundPatchMesh.tsx`, shared by Build Mode and Town Square). Dirt, sand, snow, stone, path, cobblestone, wood, water and the rest are all paintable.
- **Right-click orbit in Live Mode** (Town Square, mouse/trackpad): right-click drag spins the camera all the way around your character, 360 degrees and beyond, to see them face on; left drag keeps the gentle look-around; walking re-centers behind you as before.
- **5x size rule** (Build Mode top bar → 📏 5x size): one tap (with a confirm) resizes every non-flat Town Square asset so 1.00x = five times a student's character height; new assets start at 1.00x; the scale slider still multiplies on top; roads, rugs and other flat ground pieces keep their own size; Home Room and the Island are not affected. Every object's previous size is saved, so tapping the button again restores them all. Stored in the `world-size` row of `style_looks` (no new SQL). **Not turned on automatically**: it changes every placed object for students at once, so it waits for her tap.

- **Where:** `/student/space-bowling` (`src/routes/student/SpaceBowling.tsx`, 3D lane in `src/games/spaceBowling/Scene.tsx`, rules in `logic.ts`, sound in `audio.ts`). A placed space billboard model (any model with "space" and "billboard" in its file name) gets the new `space-bowling` role automatically; the role is also in the Build Mode role list. It is a native game for assignments (`spaceBowling` in `NATIVE_GAME_LABELS`).

Launch screen: Space Alien costume preview with a progress meter toward 500 right answers (crossing 500 unlocks `costume:space-alien`), player setup, sliders for rounds (default 10, min 5, max 20) and questions between turns (default 3, min 1, max 10), question source picker. Sound settings (⚙️): music and sound effects each Off/Low/Medium/High (remembered on the device). Music: her three tracks (Space Sprinkles, Through Space, Blast Off) in rotation.

Credits are on the Teacher home page (Poly by Google models CC-BY, alien cat pins by gloomrockcreates CC-BY-4.0, bowling strike sounds by juskiddink via qubodup CC-BY-SA 3.0, voice clips by Aimee Smith CC-BY 4.0, Kenney CC0). Not used yet (kept for later passes): the Bowling GUI pack, the venue and bowling lane models, the bowling pin .blend, the Kenney Sports pack.

### Space Bowling fixes + Neighbors as game opponents. SHIPPED 2026-10-04 (direct teacher instruction, with a screenshot of the lane)

- Her words: "edits to make: 1) make sure bowling pins are facing forward toward the player 2) the ball should drop or disappear at the end of the turn so it is out of sight 3) in all native games, any time it is a computer player, it should instead be a random neighbor. chats in the future (after playing and completeing a game together, cna be part of the chat dialogue tree "Remember that time we played bowling and I won?" 4) major edit needed, ensure bowling pins fall independently from one another, not in one unit 5) ufo powerup doesnt actually remove the pins but needs to5) pins must have a falling animation"
- **Root cause of the clumped pins and the UFO bug:** three.js renames the model's "Alien cats" node to "Alien_cats" when it loads, so the old lookup by name silently fell back to the whole model. All ten cats were loaded as ONE pin (the head pin). Knocking the head pin tipped the whole set as a block, and the UFO, picking two random pins, almost never picked that one, so nothing disappeared. The ten cats are now found by shape (the node with ten children), so every pin is its own object (`src/games/spaceBowling/Scene.tsx`).
- **Pins face the player:** every cat turns the same way, face toward the camera (checked in a render from four sides).
- **Pins fall on their own, with a real falling animation:** each knocked pin is its own little physics body. It gets its own push and spin from where it was hit, then tumbles under gravity, bounces and slides on the deck, and can slide off the back into space. Pins the ball hits go first; pins knocked by other pins follow a moment later, like a chain reaction. Standing pins next to a falling one wobble. Fallen pins shrink away about 2.6 seconds later. Checked in headless renders frame by frame.
- **UFO:** it now really beams up two pins (they float up into the saucer and are gone; checked in a render).
- **Ball out of sight:** past the pins the planet drops off the end of the lane into space and shrinks away; the Strike Shuttle zooms up and away.
- **Neighbors instead of the computer, in every native game with a computer player (Space Bowling and Slime Chess):** a random Neighbor or Townsperson (their Style name) plays instead. Space Bowling's "Me vs Computer" is now "Me vs a Neighbor"; chess's "The Computer" is now "A Neighbor"; every "the computer is thinking/aiming/wins" line uses the Neighbor's name. Shared code: `src/lib/gameRivals.ts`.
- **Game memories in chat:** a finished game against a Neighbor is remembered (the `games:<studentId>` row in `style_looks`, last 30, no new SQL). The next time the student talks to that Neighbor in Town Square, the Neighbor brings it up first, once per game: "Remember that time we played Space Bowling and I won? I am still doing my happy dance!", "Remember when we played Slime Chess and you beat me? I have been practicing ever since!", or "Remember our Space Bowling game? It was a tie! We need a rematch." The student picks a reply (e.g. "Rematch soon!" or "You got lucky!") and the normal conversation continues.
- **New background.** Her words: "use this image as the background for the space bowling game" (her pink and purple galaxy picture). Saved as `public/games/space-bowling/skybox/galaxy-pink.jpg` and used as the only background, both behind the 3D lane (cropped to fill the screen in either iPad orientation, never stretched) and behind the start screen. This replaces the rotating Kenney space skyboxes, which stay in the folder unused.
- **Meteor shower lanes numbered 1 to 3.** Her words: "after a metor shower, the buttons should change to 1-" then "1-3". The three wide lanes now show 1, 2 and 3 (same colors as before) instead of arrows, the hint says "Tap 1, 2 or 3 to roll", and keys 1 to 3 already worked.
- **Power-ups pop up in the middle.** Her words: "power ups should pop up in the middle. of your screen after questions are answered for your to choose from rather than stayon on the left". The side column of power-up buttons is gone. Once a student's questions are done (and after any meteor shower lands), a centered card asks "Use a power-up?" with a big button for each power-up they have (icon, name, count, what it does; read aloud available) and "Not now, just roll". After they choose, it stays closed for the rest of that turn.
- **More Space Bowling rules (her words, in order):**
  - "asteroid belt powerups should not be available at every turn, but rather should appear in about 30% of turns per game, evenly distributed between players": planned once per game. Each player gets the asteroid on the same number of turns (30% of the rounds, rounded, at least 1), at random rounds.
  - "only one powerup should be allowed per player turn": the student's power-up popup closes for the rest of the turn once they choose, and the Neighbor uses at most one per turn (Strike Shuttle first, then UFO, then Meteor Shower).
  - "after a metor shower has been adminsitered, chances of hitting pins are less": on a meteor-shower turn the ball wobbles off its line more, hits a narrower band, knocks fewer neighbors over, and edge lanes gutter more often (`knockPins(..., shaken)` in `logic.ts`).
  - "strike shuttle power up should go full speed (not turning)pointing at the first bowling pin and should have a cloud of fire and dust out the back of it, when it collides, all pins should fall": the shuttle no longer spins. It flies nose first (checked in a render) at almost twice the ball's speed, with a trail of fire puffs that cool to grey dust behind it, and the whole rack goes down the moment it hits the head pin.
- **Neighbor on screen (SHIPPED 2026-10-04, Claudia's two-hour stretch, from the next-ideas list):**
  - In a game against a Neighbor, their Style character stands in the bottom-left corner of the lane with their name and a speech bubble, mouth moving as they talk: "My turn! Watch this!", "STRIKE! Woo hoo!", "Ooh, so close! You got this!", "Your turn! Pick a lane!"
  - The final score card shows them too: "You beat me! Rematch soon?" / "I won this time! Good game, friend!"
  - **Slime Chess too:** the Neighbor's portrait sits above the coach bubble with their name and a line that changes with the game: "Your move!", "Hmm, let me think...", "Here I go!", "Checkmate! Good game, friend!", "You got me! Great game!".
- **Next ideas:** a "Rematch" button in the chat that opens the game with that same Neighbor.

### Grammar Gus: Remix tools, Label It! and Symbol Match. SHIPPED 2026-10-07 (plan 17.6 and 18.7)

- Built under her standing instruction to keep developing the full Grammar Gus plan in the background.
- **🎛 Remix** button next to the Hopper opens a tray of remix tools (plan 17.6). Every tool goes through the grammar engine, and if a tool cannot keep the sentence working it does nothing and Gus says why:
  - **🎲 Roll this part:** a random new word for the selected spot.
  - **🤪 Silly Swap:** swaps naming, action and how words for sillier ones. The silly score never goes down, and the sentence keeps 3 stars.
  - **➕ Longer / ➖ Shorter:** adds a describing word, a how word or a where phrase, or takes one off. It never removes WHO or the action.
  - **🔁 He / She / It:** swaps the WHO for the matching pronoun (cat to it, girl to she, crowd to they) and back.
  - **⏰ Time Zap:** cycles Yesterday, Now and Tomorrow and Gus reads the new sentence.
  - **🏷 Label It.**
  - The tray is hidden in blueprint mode, where the blueprint decides the shape.
- **🏷 Label It!** (plan 18.7, from her worksheet): opens from the Remix tray, or after any 3-star video.
  - The sentence's words sit in a row, and bracket plates (WHO, WHAT THEY DID, WHAT IT HAPPENED TO, HOW THEY DID IT, WHERE, SHOUT) sit in a tray in the housing colors.
  - Tap a plate, then a word it covers. A right tap locks the colored bracket under the words with a click and a gear.
  - A wrong tap wobbles, and Gus gives the plate's hint ("WHO is the one doing it. Which pieces tell WHO?"). After two tries Gus slides it in. There is no score loss.
- **🔣 Symbol Match** (same dialog, second tab): the symbols over the words are hidden. Tap one of her symbols, then its word. Same rules: a gear for each right tap, a hint for a wrong one, and Gus helps after two tries.
- **Did it differently:** Label It and Symbol Match use tap a plate, then tap a word, instead of dragging. It is easier on an iPad and for students who find dragging hard. Dragging can be added later.
- Tests: 4 new tests (60 mixed remix steps always leave a valid sentence, Shorter keeps WHO and the verb, pronoun choices, plates match the housings). Full suite: 233 passing.
- **Not built yet from plan 17.6:** "Swap two words" exists already as drag-to-swap on the machine. "Mix a new shape" waits for the Workshop rail.
- **Still in progress next (same background build):** Garage skins and colorways (plan 8.7 and 19), then word packs (plan 17.5). Golden Gear Contest still waits on her call on the $5 policy.

### Grammar Gus: Gus's Orders (goal mode). SHIPPED 2026-10-07 (plan 3.8)

- Built under her standing instruction to keep developing the full Grammar Gus plan in the background.
- **🎯 Orders** on the machine's top bar. Gus posts an order as a picture card in the housing colors: WHO (emoji + describing words), DID, HAPPENED TO, HOW, WHERE and TIME. Example: "WHO 🐢 dazzling turtle, DID jump, WHERE underneath 🐰 rabbit, TIME Now".
- **Any sentence that makes the same scene fills the order.** Scenes are compared, not words:
  - "a" or "the" both count.
  - "over" or "above" both count (same path on screen).
  - "cook" or "bake" both count (same animation).
  - An adverb with the same look counts too.
- **▶ Watch** plays the ordered scene in the cinema (the "ghost video" from the plan).
- **💡 Hint** names one thing that is still different, in kid words, without failing anything. Examples: "The dog is not big yet." "Where? I ordered it over the van." "Check the time crank. I ordered Yesterday."
- **No timer, no limit, no failing** (plan 3.8):
  - A 3-star sentence that misses the order still plays and still earns its normal gears, with one hint.
  - Filling the order earns 11 gears and a sticker (🏅 ⚙️ 🎩 🧪 and more), kept on the student's Gus row. The last six stickers and the total show on the order strip.
  - "🎯 Next order" appears after a filled order.
- Orders come from the same engine as the Hopper. They are always 3-star sentences the machine can build, in 10 simple shapes, and respect "Gentle verbs only".
- Orders and blueprints take turns: starting one closes the other.
- **iPad:** the machine title hides below 900px wide, so the header buttons (Orders, Blueprints, Journal, Calm, sound) always fit in portrait.
- Tests: 3 new order tests (40 random orders are all 3-star and buildable, same-scene synonyms match, hints name the difference). Full suite: 229 passing.
- **Not built yet from plan 3.8:** teacher-written orders (a small editor), and a sticker book page to browse stickers.
- **Next after this:** remix tools (done, see the Remix entry above), Garage skins and colorways (plan 19), Label It and Symbol Match (plan 18.7), word packs. Golden Gear Contest still waits on her call on the $5 policy.

### Grammar Gus: the Blueprint Library (paragraph frameworks, Knock-Knock jokes). SHIPPED 2026-10-07 (plan section 11)

- Built under her standing instruction to keep developing the full Grammar Gus plan in the background.
- **📜 Blueprints** button on the machine's top bar opens the **Blueprint Library**. Each blueprint is a Mad-Lib recipe for a whole short text, shown with her chart symbols. Six blueprints ship:
  - **Knock-Knock Joke** (the plan's flagship): Knock knock. Who's there? [setup word]. [word] who? [punchline]. The student picks the setup word (any noun on the list, or a shout like Wow), then builds the punchline in one of three shapes. The setup word is already placed in the punchline machine, so the joke "answers itself" ("Zebra who? The tiny zebra drank loudly!").
  - **Silly Story** ("Once upon a time," / a shout problem / "In the end," + pronoun).
  - **Silly News Report** ("Breaking news!" ... "That is all for today!").
  - **Day in the Life** ("In the morning," / "At lunch," / "At night,").
  - **Riddle** (two clues with "I" bolted on, "What am I?", then the answer word).
  - **Rescue Story** (shout and problem / the helper / a happy ending).
- **How it plays:**
  - The machine sets itself up for each line: the right housings open, and bolted-on words (like "I" in the riddle) show a 🔒 and cannot be moved.
  - A blueprint strip on the machine shows the line's symbols and a "✓ matches" badge. START will not run a sentence that does not match the blueprint; Gus names the symbols it wants.
  - The film strip turns into the blueprint: fixed lines are gray and locked, the line being built is outlined in gold, and sealed lines can be tapped to replay.
  - Each build line is an ordinary sentence. It runs through the usual checklist and star review, and only a 3-star sentence can be sealed. The Hopper reads the blueprint and fills a line in its shape, and in stories it likes to bring back a character who is already on stage.
- **Videos:**
  - The knock-knock joke plays as one 10-second doorway video: a kid knocks, speech bubbles say "Knock knock!" / "Who's there?" / "Zebra." / "Zebra who?", the zebra pops up at the door, then the punchline plays.
  - The other blueprints play line by line with gold title cards for the fixed lines and lead-ins ("Once upon a time,").
  - The riddle answer appears on stage with a speech bubble ("I am an owl.").
- **Blueprint stars** (plan 11.7):
  - 3 stars when every line is done and the link rule is met: the joke's punchline uses the setup word; stories bring a character back and stay in one time (or "On purpose").
  - 2 stars with one tip when the link rule is missing. Gears: 8 for 3 stars, 3 for 2.
  - Saved blueprints go in the Journal with their name and replay with ▶.
- Autosave includes the blueprint in progress.
- **Engineering:** the machine model moved to `engine/machine.ts` so the screen and the blueprint engine read the machine the same way. New files: `data/frameworks.ts` and `engine/framework.ts`. 11 new tests cover:
  - Every blueprint can be completed by the Hopper, all lines 3 stars, and its video plays.
  - The joke video stays at 10.0 seconds or less across 40 random setup words and punchlines.
  - Shape checks, bolted-on words, framework stars, lead-in lower-casing and "a" or "an" in the riddle answer.
  - Full suite: 222 passing.
- **Did it differently from the plan:** lead-ins ("Once upon a time,") are framework text in front of the scored sentence, so the sentence itself is still checked as a stand-alone sentence. At Guided and Challenge the student still capitalizes the first word of their own sentence; the paragraph text lower-cases it after the lead-in.
- **Not built yet (later blueprint work):** "Why Did the...?" (needs the question grammar), Silly Recipe and Show and Tell (need two verbs joined by "and"), Letter to a Friend (needs her name list), the Pun Pack cards, the teacher blueprint editor (11.10) and a voice read-through.
- **Next after this:** teacher reports (done, see the report entry above), Gus's Orders, then remix tools, Garage skins, Label It, Symbol Match and word packs. Golden Gear Contest still waits on her call on the $5 policy.

### Grammar Gus: pixel content pass (every word list noun drawn, every verb animated, adverb effects, mixed-case pixel letters). SHIPPED 2026-10-07 (plan section 6, milestone 7)

- Built under her standing instruction to keep developing the full Grammar Gus plan in the background.
- **New pixel rigs** (`render/rigs.ts`). Every noun on her word list now has its own drawing instead of a placeholder blob (a test checks all of them, 4 animation frames each):
  - **Birds:** bird, bat (membrane wings, red eye), chicken (comb and wattle), goose (long neck), owl (big eyes, ear tufts). Wings flap.
  - **Critters:** rat and mice (pink tail and ears), clam (opens and shuts), snail (spiral shell, eye stalks), frog, fish, dolphin, bug (ladybug), turtle.
  - **Snake:** a wavy body that slithers, with a flicking tongue.
  - **Vehicles:** car, van, bus, bike, wheel, plane, and the tank, drawn like the word list's 🛢️ picture (a rolling water tank, not a weapon). Wheel spokes turn.
  - **Things:** ball (rolls), kite, apple, book, crayon, dime, rose, shoe, straw, swing (sways), balloon, popsicle and rock.
  - **Rain:** a cloud with falling drops. **Scenery** (door, window, table, floor, kitchen) can also be the WHO of a sentence.
  - Any WHERE ground ("over the car") now shows the real picture instead of a labeled crate. The kitchen has a stove.
- **Every verb plays its own animation** (`director/clips.ts`, `render/stage.ts`). Before, only run, walk, jump, chase and attack moved and everything else wiggled with the word floating above:
  - **Travel:** fly (an arc up and back down), swim (a water band and bobbing), slide (glides with no steps).
  - **Body moves:** fall (a hop, a plop and dizzy stars), climb (up the ground object, or up a ladder that appears), spin, melt (sinks into a puddle in its own color).
  - **Hide:** goes behind the scenery, or behind a bush that pops up with eyes peeking out.
  - **Actions:** kick (a thing flies off; a person or animal only gets a gentle bump), hug (hearts), chop (a little axe and wood chips), mix (bowl and spoon), eat (food disappears bite by bite; an animal hops away surprised instead of being eaten), drink (a cup and straw).
  - **More actions:** clean (bubbles and sparkles), break (shakes, then cracks; people and animals only shake), miss (the target hops out of the way, a puff and "?"), sing (floating music notes), talk (a speech bubble with dots).
  - Characters walk over to whoever or whatever they act on first, then act. Things without legs sprout two little legs to walk or run.
- **Adverb effects** (plan 6.4) now draw:
  - loudly: sound waves. quietly: "shh". softly, gently and lightly: a drifting feather.
  - tenderly: hearts. warmly: a glow ring. messily: paint splats. wildly: a zigzag bolt and a bouncy path.
  - innocently: a halo. proudly and zealously: stars.
- **Mixed-case pixel letters:** the cinema's pixel font now has lower case (g, j, p, q and y drop below the line). Tense tags read "Yesterday", "Tomorrow" and "Now", and shouts read the way they were typed ("Wow!").
- **Calm mode** still skips the particles and keeps only the props that explain the action (talk bubble, notes, bowl, cup).
- Tests: a new content test file (every noun drawn, every verb clip real for animals and people, legless things sprout legs, lower-case font). The director snapshots were updated on purpose: 15 example patterns used the old wiggle stand-in and now play their real clip. Full suite: 211 passing.
- **Not verified on a real iPad yet:** how the new animations look at full speed.
- **Next after this:** Knock-Knock and Silly Story frameworks (done, see the Blueprint Library entry above), then teacher reports, Golden Gear Contest (needs her call on the $5 policy), Gus's Orders, remix tools, Garage skins, Label It, Symbol Match and word packs.

### Grammar Gus: paragraph machines (stories of up to 4 sentences). SHIPPED 2026-10-07 (plan sections 7, 3.14, 3.18.9 and 5.5)

- Built under her standing instruction: "claudia, keep developingin the background until the ful dev plan for this is completed. when it is, give me a list of what to test".
- **Seal it** (`engine/story.ts`, film strip on the machine): after a sentence plays, the student taps "🔏 Seal it". The sentence drops into a film strip frame and the machine resets to a fresh build, with a new workbench color for each sentence. Up to 4 sentences (the plan's MVP; 8 later).
- **The cast carries over:** "a white cat" in sentence one and "the white cat" in sentence two are the same cat on screen. He, she, it and they point to the most recent matching character. Cast chips under the film strip show who is in the story.
- **▶ Play All:** one movie, curtains open once, a quick 0.3 second pixel dissolve between sentences (a plain cut in calm mode), curtains close once.
- **Story stars** (the Story Clipboard): 1 star for a story, 2 when every sentence stays in the same time (or the student taps "🗓 On purpose" to say the time change was on purpose), 3 when a character comes back in another sentence. Up to 3 bonus gears for: meeting someone with "a" then calling them "the", starting sentences with different words, and every he/she/it/they pointing to someone already in the story. Gus gives one tip for the next step.
- **↩︎ Unseal** takes the last sentence back onto the machine. **📓 Save** puts the whole story in the Journal; Journal entries now have a ▶ replay button that rebuilds and replays the movie.
- **Autosave:** the machine and the story in progress are saved on the iPad (per student) and come back after a reload or an accidental close.

### Grammar Gus: the Inspector's Clipboard, Guided and Challenge levels, teacher settings. SHIPPED 2026-10-07 (plan milestone 6 and sections 3.6, 3.9 to 3.15)

- Built under her standing instruction to keep developing the full Grammar Gus plan.
- **Inspector's Clipboard** (`engine/checklist.ts`, plus the 📋 button and drawer on the machine):
  - The plan's checklist items, in kid wording (7 words or fewer), grouped as The big pieces, Match, Describe, Join and Finish.

### Grammar Gus: iPad-first layout, drag-and-drop factory pieces, gizmos, Gus comes alive. SHIPPED 2026-10-07 (direct teacher instructions)

- Her words, verbatim:
  - "we need to prioritze optomization for ipad size"
  - "the machine pieces need to be drag and drop individually. claudia, add a gamifcation feel to the design. a simple snap to click, drag to rearrage, different color pieces, pieces that are tectured and shaped like insdustrial factory pieces. slighly different designs for each. button and other gizmos to enhance gamifcation feel"
  - "add human/player/character animations to gus to give me a little live feel, slighly moving"
  - "claudia, keep developingin the background until the ful dev plan for this is completed. when it is, give me a list of what to test" (standing instruction for the whole Grammar Gus plan; see the progress list below).
- **iPad layout:**
  - The game is one iPad screen in both orientations, with no page scrolling. Checked at 1180x740, 1024x690 (landscape with Safari bars), 820x1100 and 768x950: page scroll height equals the window every time.
  - Landscape: the machine sits left with the cinema right, and the Parts Bin runs full width along the bottom.
  - Portrait: a short cinema strip (video beside its caption and buttons), then the machine, then the Parts Bin, which fills the rest.
  - Only the Parts Bin scrolls. Gus's chat box is docked into the layout and never covers the game.
  - Optional housings (SHOUT, WHAT IT HAPPENED TO, HOW, WHERE) wait as "+" chips until needed. The second describing word appears once the first is filled.
  - Targets are 48px and up; word tiles are 60px. No text selection or long-press callouts, no hover-only anything, and safe-area insets are respected.
- **Factory pieces** (`ui/PartSvg.tsx`): every word is a Cartoon Industrial part shaped from its grammar symbol, in the symbol's color, with a light top, a darker bottom band, rivets, scuffs and a highlight.
  - The nine parts: Noun Boiler with a porthole; Pop Valve or Spotlight Lamp for a / the; Dab Sprayer with a pump, plus a smile dial for feeling words and a paint tank in the word's own color; Swap Valve with a Y pipe; Verb Engine with a flywheel and piston; Pressure Gauge with a needle; copper Arch Pipe; Union Coupling clamp; Steam Whistle.
  - Each word gets one of three slightly different looks.
  - Empty sockets show the dashed outline of the shape that fits.
  - When the machine runs, flywheels spin, needles swing, pistons pump and whistles steam.
- **Drag and drop** (pointer events, so it works with a finger, a pencil or a mouse):
  - Drag a part from the Parts Bin onto the machine. Matching sockets glow green and the part snaps in with a squash and a spark.
  - The wrong shape bounces off, and Gus explains the shapes do not fit.
  - Drag a part between same-shape sockets to rearrange or swap. Drag one off the machine to recycle it.
  - Drop a part on a "+" chip to add that housing.
  - Drag the HOW THEY DID IT housing by its handle to the front of the sentence for an opening HOW word ("Softly, the girl sings."); the engine adds the comma.
  - Tap-a-socket then tap-a-part still works for students who find dragging hard.
- **Gizmos:**
  - WHO and DID lamps (the plan's two lamps, lit from the real grammar check).
  - A pressure gauge that climbs as the required parts fill.
  - A READY lamp when the sentence will run (steady, never flashing).
  - A horn toy, plus the START lever, time crank and Surprise Hopper.
- **Gus comes alive:** his model has no skeleton, so the life is whole-body acting:
  - Breathing, a slow weight shift, and a glance off to the side every few seconds.
  - Chatty nods and a hop whenever he says something new.
  - Calm mode keeps only the breathing.
- **Still not verified on a real iPad.**
- **Progress on the full plan:**
  - Done: milestones 1 to 4 plus these interaction passes.
  - Next, in order:
    - The Inspector's Clipboard checklist and the Guided and Challenge levels.
    - Paragraph machines with the film strip and Play All.
    - The pixel content pass (more rigs and clips).
    - Teacher settings and reports.
    - Knock-Knock and Silly Story frameworks.
    - The contest, Orders, remix and Garage.

### Grammar Gus's Silly Sentence Contraption (first native tool/game). FIRST PLAYABLE SHIPPED 2026-10-07 (direct teacher instruction)

- **Her words, verbatim, in order:**
  - "claudia, we are going to build our first native tool/game. this will be styled like a cartoon/industrial/factory/bubble machine game and will function like a sandbox, drag and drop, silly machine builder game."
  - She uploaded the full build plan: `PLAN.md` (v11, 3,100 lines) and `grammar-gus-plan.zip`, with her Sentence Chart screenshots, the 33-pattern list, her word list and her nine recreated grammar symbols.
  - Gus himself: "this should be grammar gus" (`hamster_beaver_scientist.glb`).
  - "grammar gus should have a pop up and conversation box like Bawk does while the students are in the grammar machine view"
  - "grammar gus, in personality should be silly but a little pretentous in terms of ensuring that everything is grammatically correct. Gus can be a little silly and sarcastic in a joking manner when the machine doesnt run on attemp because it wouldnt be grammaticalyl correct"
  - "students can access the grammar gus machine game by clicking a card icon in their computer and y clicking this asset with the assigned grammar machine role" (`factory_cartoon_incinerator.glb`).
  - Visual references: a physics puzzle game ("Put physics to work!") and the Contraption Maker cover; then "use these as reference" for the Contraption Maker electricity parts sheet; then a 3D industrial kit (silver pipes with red valve wheels, a tank with a gauge, an orange cabinet, a control panel, yellow grates), a flat-vector machine parts sheet (blue riveted steel, copper pipes with flanges, gauges, red-knob levers) and a neon slot-machine UI.
  - "get to a spot where i can test as a student"
- **Where the plan lives:**
  - `docs/grammar-gus/PLAN.md` (the whole plan) and `docs/grammar-gus/plan/` (split by section).
  - `docs/grammar-gus/BUILD_NOTES.md` (the plan's own Claude notes) and `docs/grammar-gus/reference/` (her chart screenshots, pattern and word list PDFs, Contraption Maker reference).
  - Her symbols are served from `public/games/grammar-gus/symbols/`.
  - The plan is the source of truth. Rule changes need her approval (plan 25.8).
- **Shipped (plan milestones 1 to 4, plus a first student screen so she can test):**
  - **Data** (`src/games/grammarGus/data/`):
    - Her 33 patterns.
    - Her word list, plus the extra words her own 33 examples need (fish, dolphin, swam, proudly, safely, at, Phew...) so every example is buildable.
    - The Color pack, and the start of the Action Pack (attack, chase, hug).
    - Adjective ranks for describing-word order, the opposites lists for the rubric, and Gus's lines.
  - **Grammar engine** (`engine/`, pure TypeScript, no UI):
    - A small automaton of the plan 17.4 grammar gives `legalNext` (the Shape Palette), `completeness` (the two lamps) and the role of every word.
    - It also finds exactly what is missing (the cheapest set of parts that would finish the sentence), which drives the steam-leak hints.
    - `validateSentence` covers every row of the plan 3.5 table and the Help levels of 3.6 (Full, Guided, Challenge).
    - `compose` handles verb forms, a/an by sound, "the" with mice/children/rain, describing-word order, capitals, I, the commas of patterns 13 to 21 and 19, and the shout "!".
    - Also: SemanticFrame, cast resolution (a / the / pronouns, "the cat" picks the other cat), Gus's star rubric (3.18, all fixtures), the silly score, and the seeded Surprise Hopper generator with locks (only 3-star sentences).
  - **Director** (`director/`): sentence to a JSON scene script.
    - Enter, act and settle beats; adverb speed and effects; preposition paths; fallbacks so the screen is never blank.
    - The 10-second budget function; only 3-star sentences get a script.
  - **Pixel Cinema** (`render/`):
    - A 160 x 90, 32-color, 12 fps renderer written in plain TypeScript (identical frames on every device, testable headless).
    - Red pixel curtains with a gold valance open first and close last; a star stamp at the end.
    - Procedural quadruped rig (cat, dog, cow, pig, horse, deer, zebra, tiger, rabbit features) and biped rig (hair, spy hat, doctor coat).
    - Run, walk, jump, chase and pounce, with squash and stretch. Every other verb plays a wiggle with the word floating ("eat!") until its clip is made, and other rigs draw as a named blob (plan 6.8).
    - Color and size adjectives, stripes and spots. Speed lines, dust, effort lines, a snail trail.
    - Yesterday: sepia, scanlines, a YESTERDAY tag and a calendar flip. Now: a NOW dot. Tomorrow: ghost dither that turns solid as they act.
    - Calm mode: three-step curtains, no particles.
  - **Tests** (`src/games/grammarGus/__tests__/`, run with `npm test`; vitest added). 197 tests, all passing:
    - All 33 examples valid at Full and done-by-hand at Challenge.
    - Agreement, tense, a/an, comma and capital tables; every steam-leak case; each Help level blocks exactly its cases.
    - 6,000 seeded generated sentences per column count with constraint checks, plus random locks.
    - 10,000 Hopper sentences all 3 stars; a 10,000-sequence Workshop fuzz test; every rubric fixture.
    - Director scripts for 33 patterns x 3 tenses, all 10.0 s or less (snapshots stored), 2,000 longest random sentences in budget.
    - Cast fixtures (white cat / black cat), determinism (same frames), and a coverage matrix (every noun x verb, verb x adverb, verb x preposition, noun as ground) that renders without errors.
  - **Student screen** (`/student/grammar-gus`, `ui/GrammarGusMachine.tsx`):
    - Housings in her colors with labeled plates: SHOUT, WHO (a or the, two describing words, naming word, or "Use he, she, they..."), WHAT THEY DID, WHAT IT HAPPENED TO (appears when the verb needs it), HOW THEY DID IT, WHERE. Copper pipes link them, and a stamp sits at the end.
    - A riveted START lever with a red knob, and a time crank (Yesterday / Now / Tomorrow).
    - Tap a socket, then tap a word tile in the Parts Bin (64px tiles, emoji on nouns, read aloud through Gus).
    - START runs the machine only on a grammatical sentence:
      - **Leak:** steam, the problem socket pulses, the cinema stays closed with a "?", and Gus makes a joke then gives a plain fix.
      - **Run:** rumble, parts fire in order, chimney puffs, then the pixel video (3 stars) or Gus's star review (1 or 2 stars, with Fix it).
    - Surprise Hopper, New machine, Replay, Slower, Calm, Mute.
    - Cheese gears (6 / 3 / 1), and a Journal of saved 3-star sentences (`gus:<studentId>` row, no new SQL).
    - Works for a teacher with no student logged in (nothing saved).
  - **Gus's pop-up** (`ui/GusGuide.tsx`): Bawk's layout, with her Gus model (shrunk to 2.3 MB at `public/games/grammar-gus/grammar-gus.glb`) on the left, hopping on every new line, and a chat box across the bottom with read-aloud in a new "Prim & Proper" voice.
  - **Gus's personality** (`data/gusLines.ts`): silly and a little pretentious, with playful sarcasm when the machine will not run ("Splendid. Everyone is standing around doing absolutely nothing.").
    - Every joke is followed by a plain, literal instruction ("Add a WHAT THEY DID word."), so a student who reads sarcasm literally still knows what to do.
    - The joke is always about the sentence or Gus, never about the student. Review cards are 12 words or fewer and start positive.
  - **Access:**
    - A 🧪 Grammar Gus card on the student computer.
    - A new **Grammar Machine** world-object role (Build Mode and Creative Island role lists), which opens `/student/grammar-gus`.
    - Her factory asset is in Build Mode at `public/world/models/buildings/grammar-machine-factory.glb`: place it and give it the Grammar Machine role.
