**What's still separate infrastructure, not silently skipped, not done in this pass:** the Central Educator Dashboard spec's two largest items — merging Scores/Reports in (needs a new `assignmentId` field threaded through `QuizAttemptRecord`/`submitQuizAnswer`, a new persisted per-question log, and a new client-side PDF dependency) and the CCSS AI question-generation pipeline (needs a brand-new Supabase Edge Function and an Anthropic API key provisioned as a server-side secret) — are real, separate infrastructure builds Claudia's own spec already sized as "the largest, most architecturally novel item," not screen-reshuffling. Both need a live Supabase migration and, for the AI pipeline, a real secret this environment has no way to provision or verify blind. Marked here, not built silently around.

**Four fixes/changes shipped 2026-09-30, same pass:**
- **Question-retry dead end, fixed everywhere.** Direct teacher bug report: "when a question is answered incorrect and the try again message appears, the student cannot move to the next question." Investigation (static review of `QuizTask.tsx`, `PlatformerTask.tsx`, `submitQuizAnswer` in `store.ts`, plus attempted live reproduction — infeasible in this sandbox, no Supabase backend configured) found no crash, but a real design inconsistency: `QuestionScreen.tsx` (shared by Gas Pump and Bakery Match) had no way to move past a question without answering it correctly, unlike `QuizTask`/`PlatformerTask`, which both let a student move to a different question after one wrong attempt (the store already requeues the missed one). Fixed by adding an optional `onSkip` prop to `QuestionScreen.tsx` ("🔀 Try a different question instead", shown only after a wrong answer) and wiring it in both call sites: `BakeryMatch3.tsx` and the Gas Pump screen in `TownSquare.tsx`. Flagging honestly: since a live crash could not be reproduced in this environment, this is the best fix for the described symptom, not a confirmed root-cause match — worth the teacher confirming it resolves what she saw.
- **Radio/Boom Box silent for some students, fixed.** Teacher report: worked for one student, not for another (Geoff), who had to manually hit pause then play before it would start. `GlobalMusicPlayer.tsx`'s YouTube iframe relied solely on the `autoplay=1` URL param; WebKit/iOS (iPad is this app's priority device) silently drops that param once the async IFrame-API load (script fetch, then the `onReady` callback) falls outside a tight user-gesture window — exactly reproduced by "needs a manual play tap first," since that tap is a real synchronous gesture. Fixed by calling `e.target.playVideo()` explicitly in `onReady`, the same call the manual Play button already made.
- **Academics: Question Sets/Focuses/Activities panels moved to the bottom.** Direct teacher instruction from a screenshot of the live page. `AssignmentsIndex.tsx` now renders the assignment grid/filters first and the three collapsible panels below them, unchanged otherwise.
- **Cover images removed from activities — first the UI, then, on a same-day follow-up ("remove activity cover images entirely"), the field itself.** Direct teacher instruction: "they are big, unnecessary, and not useful." First pass removed the `referenceImageUrl` authoring field from `ActivityLibrary.tsx`'s TaskEditor (including the auto-fill-from-YouTube-thumbnail behavior that fed it) and every display site: the student's big in-task image (`SubjectDashboard.tsx`), the Activity Library card thumbnail and Playground chip (`ActivityLibrary.tsx`, now icon-only), the Playground picker thumbnail (`PlaygroundView.tsx`), the to-do list thumbnail (`TaskChecklist.tsx`), and the Lesson Plan Builder's small 🖼️ badge — with the type field itself deliberately left on `Task` for old-data compat. The teacher's follow-up asked for the field gone entirely, so it's now removed from `Task` in `types.ts` and from every remaining reference: `ActivityLibrary.tsx`'s `blankTask()`/`activityToTaskSnapshot`, `store.ts`'s activity-to-task conversion, and both directions of `sync.ts`'s `activity_library` row mapping. The Supabase `reference_image_url` column itself is left in place (an app-level `DROP COLUMN` is a real, irreversible schema change this session won't make unprompted) — it's just never read or written by the app anymore, same "harmless orphaned column" shape as other retired fields. (The per-video-option thumbnail inside the "choose a video" `linkChoice` editor is a different, small functional thumbnail — left as is, not what this complaint was about.)
- Verified: `npx tsc --noEmit` and `npm run build` both pass clean.

**Three fixes shipped 2026-10-01, direct teacher bug reports with screenshots:**
- **Map view rendered as a skewed diagonal sliver against black, clicks landing nowhere near what they looked like.** Root cause: `Player`'s `useFrame` pointed the map camera almost straight down (`camera.position` at `(0, mapHeight, 0.01)`, `camera.lookAt(0, 0, 0)`) while leaving Three.js's default `camera.up` of `(0, 1, 0)` untouched — forward and up end up nearly anti-parallel, which makes `lookAt`'s internal cross-product (the one it uses to derive the camera's "right" axis) collapse toward a zero vector; normalizing that near-zero vector is numerically unstable, so the camera's actual on-screen rotation came out skewed rather than a clean top-down square. Since ground-click navigation raycasts through this same camera, it was faithfully following the skew too — one root cause explained both halves of the report. Fixed: `camera.up.set(0, 0, -1)` before the top-down `lookAt` (matching `CoordinateGrid`'s own documented "up on screen is north/-Z" convention), and `camera.up.set(0, 1, 0)` restored every frame the normal third-person camera is active so toggling the map off never leaves the walking camera skewed afterward.
- **The What's New book's visual, direct report: "horrible."** Root cause: `BookPanel.tsx`'s page art (`book-page.png`, a near-square 416×448 image with an ornate dog-eared-corner border) was being force-stretched via `backgroundSize: '100% 100%'` into a wide 420×228 landscape box — visibly squashing and warping the border art. Fixed: the book's own dimensions now match the page art's real aspect ratio (no stretch, no crop, no letterboxing), `backgroundSize` switched to `contain`, and the flat solid-color cover got a gradient + a real bound-spine highlight strip (CSS only, no new image assets) so it reads as an actual book rather than a plain rectangle.
- **"If you click a wrong answer, you can still click every other choice, including the correct one — it should lock in and move to the next question."** This was `QuestionScreen.tsx`'s original, direct-teacher-instructed "retry the same question until correct" design (the same component's own header comment documents that prior instruction) — today's report supersedes it: `lockOnWrongAnswer` is no longer an opt-in prop Castle Defense alone requested; locking every choice the instant any answer is picked (right or wrong) and requiring the already-built "Next Question ▶" button to proceed is now the one standing behavior for every caller (Bakery Match, Gas Pump, Castle Defense) — all three already supplied the required `onSkip` prop from the dead-end fix two days earlier, so no caller needed new wiring.
- Verified: `npx tsc --noEmit` and `npm run build` both pass clean.

**Question Sets — cover images removed entirely, type-icon badge added, description promoted, shipped 2026-09-30.** Direct teacher follow-up on a screenshot of the live Question Sets list. `QuestionSet.coverImageUrl` is removed from `types.ts` (not just hidden) and every reference: `QuestionSetsManager.tsx`'s cards, `LessonPlanBuilder.tsx`'s `SetCard` (its "🖼️ Cover" edit button is gone too), `QuestionSetDetail.tsx`'s editor (the "Tags & cover image" section is now just "Tags"), `store.ts`'s `duplicateQuestionSet`, and both directions of `sync.ts`'s `question_sets` mapping. In its place: a small type-icon badge (🧠 quiz / 🗂️ drill) in the card's top-left corner, mirroring the star/focus button's exact size and position on the top-right — so the corner real estate that used to hold a decorative cover now identifies the set's kind at a glance instead. The description field (added earlier the same day for the Focus merge) now renders directly under the title using the exact `set-card-meta` class the question-count line already used, so both read at the identical size, per direct instruction. **Real gap closed in the same pass:** `description`/`isFocus`/`focusedAt` were added to the `QuestionSet` type and the teacher-facing UI earlier today but never actually reached `sync.ts`'s real `pushQuestionSet`/`rowToQuestionSet` functions — starring a set or writing a description would have silently lived only in local browser state and been lost on next load. Fixed: both fields now round-trip through Supabase (`question_sets.description`/`is_focus`/`focused_at`, new columns in `schema.sql`), same as everything else on the set. `question_sets.cover_image_url` itself is left in the table, just unused. Verified: `npx tsc --noEmit` and `npm run build` both pass clean.

**Activity Library — per-type icons + type filter, shipped 2026-09-30, direct teacher ask:** "each type of activity should have their own icon... so its easy to identify. i should also be able to filter based on type of activity." A new `TASK_TYPE_ICONS` map (`types.ts`) gives each of the 12 `TaskType`s a consistent glyph (quiz/drill reuse the same 🧠/🗂️ QuestionSetsManager already uses for those two question-set kinds, so the same type reads the same icon everywhere). Every Activity Library card now shows its type icon as a small badge in the thumbnail corner, regardless of whether the card has a cover image or the teacher's own decorative icon underneath. A new type-filter chip row sits above the existing tag filter (only shown when a subject has more than one type in use), OR-matching (an activity's type is in the selected set) since a card only ever has one type, unlike tags. Verified: `tsc --noEmit` and `npm run build` both pass clean.

**Reference redesign — Create Activity + Activity Library, done today, logic → design → function, no open questions left for the teacher to answer:**
- **Design system foundation (new, reused by every row above going forward):** `TeacherNav.tsx` now adds a `teacher-mode` class to `document.body` on mount (every teacher screen renders `TeacherNav`, so this is automatic, no per-screen wiring needed) and removes it on unmount, so it never touches the student game. `index.css`'s `body.teacher-mode` block redefines the accent to a calmer indigo (`--purple: #4f46e5`), swaps the "Baloo 2" playful display font for the already-loaded 'Lexend' on headings/buttons/zone headers, thins borders and drop-shadows from the comic-book 4px/hard-shadow style down to a subtle 1px/soft-shadow card style, and replaces every rainbow zone-header gradient with a flat, bordered header. This is a real, durable foundation, not a one-screen patch — the next row redesigned reuses these tokens rather than inventing its own.
- **Information hierarchy fix (Logic + Design):** per "Question Sets should be recognized as the backing/academic basis of the game," `TaskEditor` in `ActivityLibrary.tsx` was reordered so the type-specific academic content (the `QuizEditor` question list, the passage/article/drill/word-chain editors) renders immediately after the Icon/Title/Type basics — it used to sit after Reward and Cover Image, which buried the actual instructional content under cosmetic fields.
- **Progressive disclosure (Function):** per "too many options are shown at once," Cover image, the optional reference link, the daily/Final-Check toggles, and the custom step-guide editor now live behind one native `<details className="task-editor-more">` ("⚙️ More options"), closed by default and auto-opened only when editing an activity that already has one of those fields set (so existing data is never hidden from a teacher who's already using it). Reward stays visible (every activity needs one) but moved below the academic content instead of crowding the top row.
- **Verified:** `npx tsc --noEmit` and `npm run build` both pass clean on this change.

**Reward moved from activity-creation time to assignment time; Question Set authoring simplified; Playground pool removed entirely — SHIPPED 2026-09-30, three direct teacher instructions in the same pass.**
- **Reward now decided per-assignment, not per-activity.** Direct instruction: "rewards should never be assigned during the creation of activities, but rather only when an assignment is being created." The whole Reward section (money/marketplace item/special item/bonus spin, with its amount/item/icon fields) is removed from `TaskEditor` in `ActivityLibrary.tsx` — the Create Activity form and the inline Activity Library edit panel no longer offer a way to set it. It moves to `NewDailyPlanBuilder.tsx` as a new `RewardPicker` component, shown under every task once it's actually on a plan, right alongside `GameplayModePicker` — same "decided at assignment time" shape that feature already established, same `onChange(patch: Partial<Task>)` pattern. `Task.reward`/`Task.rewardCents` are unchanged as fields and `completeTask` still reads them exactly as before; only the authoring surface moved. This was already flagged as queued, real architecture in the Create Activity form overhaul entry above (item 3) — turns out no new data model was actually needed, since a plan's `Task` list was already the per-assignment snapshot that entry anticipated needing.
- **"Quiz" renamed "Question Set" everywhere, and its authoring form trimmed to match — direct instruction: "instead of quiz, the text needs to be question set (NOTE: QUESTION SETS ARE NOT JUST QUIZZES)."** `TASK_TYPE_LABELS.quiz` (`types.ts`) changed from "Quiz (practice or checkpoint)" to "Question Set (practice or checkpoint)" — a single source of truth, so the Create Activity Type dropdown, the Activity Library's type filter chips, and every card's type-badge tooltip all picked up the rename together rather than needing three separate edits. Screenshot-driven trim to the authoring form itself: when a Question Set activity's type is selected (the default), `QuizEditor` now renders in a new `simplified` mode that shows only "📤 Upload a CSV" and "📄 Get CSV template" — the "Insert from a saved set" dropdown and the manual "Multiple choice / Add question" row are hidden, per "if i click question set, only the upload and copy template buttons should appear... i dont need the insert from saved set nor do i need the multiple choice button/dropdown." A Native Game activity's in-game quiz questions keep the full toolkit (manual authoring is the normal path there) — `simplified` is only passed for `task.type === 'quiz'`. `SetLibraryControls.tsx` gained the underlying `minimal` prop that does the actual hiding, shared by both call sites so `QuestionSetDetail.tsx`'s own real Question Set editor is untouched.
- **The Playground pool concept is removed entirely, direct instruction: "remove the concept of a playground pool entirely. all aspects related to playground should be removed," clarified as "the playground concept will be replaced by the native games and question set logic"** (the `anyGame` completion mode shipped earlier today — free-play across every native game while still keeping an assignment's question count moving, which is exactly what the Playground pool's "flag some activities as free-play, unlock after the day's work" model was standing in for). Removed, not hidden: `PlaygroundView.tsx` (the student route), `ActivityLibraryItem.inPlayground` (the per-activity flag and its teacher-facing toggle/badge everywhere it showed), `PlaygroundPool` (the teacher-side manager panel), `playgroundAccess.ts`/`getPlaygroundAccess` (the unlock-timer logic), `PLAYGROUND_REWARD_CENTS`, `completePlaygroundActivity`, the "🎉 The Playground is unlocked!" widget on the student Computer desktop, and the "🎪 Go to the Playground!" branch of `SubjectCompleteScreen`. The already-dead `playground_threshold` per-student column (its authoring UI had been removed in an earlier pass, per the dev plan's own backlog notes) is dropped from `schema.sql` too, along with the CSS it all used (`.zone-playground`, `.widget-playground`, `.playground-strip`/`.playground-chip`, `.badge-playground`).
  - **Asked the teacher directly rather than guessing: Gallery (curated images) and Silly Personality Quizzes were only ever reachable through the Playground hub** (their own Back buttons pointed at `/student/playground/view`), but are separate teacher-authored content systems, not the activity pool itself — genuinely ambiguous whether "all aspects related to playground" meant them too. **Teacher's answer: remove them as well.** `Gallery.tsx`/`SillyQuizzes.tsx` (student routes), `GalleryManager.tsx`/`SillyQuizManager.tsx` (teacher authoring, pulled out of the Game tab in `GameManager.tsx`), the `GalleryItem`/`SillyQuiz`/`QuizOutcome`/`QuizOption`/`SillyQuizQuestion` types, every related store action (`add/update/deleteGalleryItem`, `add/update/deleteSillyQuiz`) and `sync.ts` plumbing (push/delete/row-mapping functions, realtime subscription wiring, the `gallery_items`/`silly_quizzes` table definitions in `schema.sql`) are all deleted. Deliberately NOT dropped from the live database (no `drop table` emitted) — `schema.sql` isn't auto-applied to production anyway (see the urgent hotfix entry above), so this is purely a "the app no longer reads or writes these" cleanup; any already-synced Gallery/Silly Quiz content in Supabase is simply orphaned, not destroyed.

### Native question-set-fueled games per building/asset — standing design direction

**Direct teacher instruction (2026-09-29): "Just like how the bakery building opens with a bakery game, we are going to continue adding native games (fueled by question sets) for other assets and buildings. Claudia, keep that logic in mind."** Not a single feature to build now — a standing pattern for every future building/placeable-asset role: same shape as Bakery Match (A/role-gated building → a native mini-game → question-set-gated challenge screens between rounds → real rewards on completion), reused rather than reinvented per building. Relayed to Claudia to carry forward into every future native-game concept she reviews or proposes, not just this one instance. Bakery Match itself (see Part A) is the template to generalize from: its `QuestionSourcePicker` (dice-random vs. a specific question set) was already built reusable "for any future question-set-gated game," and its round/challenge-screen/reward structure is the reference shape, not a one-off.

**Candidate native games list — direct teacher input (2026-09-30), noted only, no development yet.** Each follows the same standing pattern above (role-gated building/asset → native mini-game → question-set-gated challenge → real reward). Castle Defense shipped 2026-09-30 — see A45 in Part A. Further candidates, none scoped or started:
1. **Farm Grow** — farm plots; answers plant and tend crops through growth stages; harvest for currency.
2. **Fishing Dock** — pier or boat asset; answers power a timing-based cast; fish go in a collection book.
3. **Mine Cart Run** — mine entrance; a Mario-style auto-runner where answers choose the track switch.
4. **Dungeon Rooms** — castle cellar or ruins; each door is a puzzle locked by a question set, with item-gated progress.
5. **Post Office Sort** — post office; sort letters and parcels into the right bins by answering.
6. **Pet Show Ring** — pet-care building; answers power obstacle-course runs for your trained pet, using its unlocked tricks. Not competitive, so no rankings.
7. **Lighthouse Signal** — lighthouse; answers light the beam to guide boats safely in.
8. **Library Quest** — library; answers reveal hidden books on shelves, completing a shelf fills the Finds Book.
9. **Construction Site** — building-site asset; answers supply blocks for the town's next building, tied to the Builder Plot idea.
10. **Gem Mine** (Curio Shop style) — cave or shop; answers dig up gems that fill collection pages.
11. **Rescue Shelter** — animal shelter; a stray-pet intro game where questions gently build trust before adoption.
12. **Kitchen Cook-Off** — restaurant; answers pick ingredients in order to cook recipes (sequencing practice).
13. **Train Station Dispatch** — train station; answers route trains to the right platforms.

**Student-favorite game genres — direct teacher input (2026-09-30), specifically flagged as what her students already respond to, noted only, no development yet.** Same standing pattern (role-gated building/asset → native mini-game → question-set-gated challenge → real reward):
1. **Endless Runner** — train station or mine; answers pick lanes or jumps.
2. **Tower Defense** — the castle (this is Castle Defense, shipped 2026-09-30, A45 in Part A — restated here as a confirmed student favorite).
3. **Idle/Clicker Tycoon** — the farm or bakery shop; answers boost a running business.
4. **Time-Management** — restaurant; answers serve customers in order.
5. **Memory Match** — library or curio shop; questions pair with answers.
6. **Word Search** — library or post office; answers reveal hidden words.
7. **Whack-a-Mole** — garden or farm; answers pop out of the ground.
8. **Crossy Road Hopper** — town roads; correct answers hop you across.
9. **Plinko Drop** — a carnival or arcade building; answers choose where the ball drops.
10. **Bubble Shooter** — a fishing pond or candy shop; aim at the bubble holding the right answer.

### Central Educator (Academics) Dashboard — spec list, re-derived from the teacher's own words (2026-09-29)

Nothing built yet. This replaces an earlier discursive design-review entry on the same feature (it mixed settled decisions, open forks, and Claudia's own reasoning into one narrative block instead of separating "what to build" from "why," which is why it wasn't translating into actionable work). This version is a checklist a developer can act on line by line. Do not re-open items marked "settled" without new, explicit teacher instruction. If more gets added to this feature later, keep it in this same checklist shape rather than reverting to prose.

Two rounds of direct teacher brief feed this: (1) 7 Academics questions + 3 CCSS questions, answered in detail with an uploaded reference-UI PDF (Prodigy-style dashboard as the layout direction, Legends of Learning as a liked content style), plus a direct architecture pick (a real AI question-generation pipeline over expanding the existing fixed generator); (2) a reinforcement instruction — "one global dashboard for all academic functions... card-based view that I can edit, change, fix, adjust, and create everything including assignments (assigned activities per day, and also a weekly viewer to plan in advance) focuses, etc." — followed by two corrections: "Gas Pump reporting can wait, focus on the teacher UI redesign platform-wide," and "the mockup is not what i want, assignments, activities, and scores should become one dashboard view."

**1. Single dashboard shell/navigation**
- [x] **DONE 2026-09-29:** Assignments and Activities (Question Sets, Focuses, Create Activity, Activity Library, Playground Pool) are one nav entry, one screen (`AssignmentsIndex.tsx`, nav label "Academics") — direct teacher instruction, final directive. `/teacher/activities` and `/teacher/playground` redirect there; `PlaygroundManager.tsx` is deleted. Scores stays separate — that merge is still gated on the `assignmentId` data-model work in item 3, not a nav change.
- [ ] MUST use the Prodigy-style page chrome she referenced: left icon sidebar, top class-selector bar, dashboard-home summary panels including a class-wide ranked score table (legitimate as a teacher-facing report, not a student leaderboard). **Still open** — the shipped merge reused the existing `TeacherNav` top bar and collapsible chrome-frame panels (consistent with the app's existing pattern), not a new left-sidebar shell. Building the Prodigy-style sidebar/dashboard-home chrome on top of this is a larger, separate visual project — **READY TO BUILD** whenever it's prioritized, not blocked by anything above.
- [ ] Global subject filter (All/Math/Literacy), one filter for the whole dashboard, not per-section. Settled. **READY TO BUILD**, not yet done — today's merge kept each panel's own existing subject handling (Question Sets has its own filter chips, Focuses shows all lanes, Activities has its own picker) rather than adding one dashboard-wide filter on top.

**2. Card-based content unit**
- [ ] MUST render all primary content (assignments, activities, scores) as cards, not table rows, as the default content unit. **READY TO BUILD** — new shared card component, reused across items 3-5.

**3. Merged Assignments + Activities + Scores view (day mode)** — direct correction, verbatim: "assignments, activities, and scores should become one dashboard view." A single card must show, per student per day: which Assignment it's from, what activity (Task) it is, and its score/status, together, not split across panels.
- [ ] **Data gap, verified:** `Assignment` (`types.ts` ~682-692) has no link to a specific `Task` or score record. `QuizAttemptRecord` (`types.ts` ~769-780) has `taskId`/`taskTitle` but no `assignmentId` — matching a score to an assignment today only works by matching `taskId` against a `Task.id` inside the `PlanTemplate` the `Assignment.templateId` points to, which breaks for a task reused across multiple assignments. **NEEDS DATA MODEL WORK:** add `assignmentId?: string` to `QuizAttemptRecord`, populated where `submitQuizAnswer` builds the record (`store.ts` ~2497-2526).
- [ ] Cards for a Task with no attempt yet MUST show "not started"/"in progress" status, not omit the score. **READY TO BUILD** once the field above exists.
- [ ] Merged card MUST carry the per-card "Download Report" PDF action (see item 8). **NEEDS DATA MODEL WORK** (item 8's fields), not independently blocked otherwise.

**4. Week planning view**
- [ ] MUST be a distinct view from the day view, for advance planning. Columns MUST be Mon-Fri, 5 columns: `DayOfWeek` (`types.ts` line 646) is `'mon'|'tue'|'wed'|'thu'|'fri'`, nothing in this codebase uses a 7-day week. **READY TO BUILD** — `WeeklyScheduleEntry` (`types.ts` ~669-675) already models the per-student/subject/day binding this needs.
- [ ] MUST let the teacher create/edit an assignment for a future day directly from the week grid, not just view it. **NEEDS TEACHER DECISION** — same fork as item 6.

**5. Focuses**
- [x] **DONE 2026-09-29:** Focus authoring already lived in `AssignmentsIndex.tsx` (`FocusesPanel`) and now sits on the single merged Academics screen alongside Question Sets and Activities, same collapsible-panel shape. Not converted to the card-grid visual language yet (see item 2/7) — the content is unified, the visual system for it is a separate, still-open item.
- [x] **DONE 2026-09-29:** SEL and Personal Finance dropped from Focus-authoring only. `AssignmentsIndex.tsx`'s `FOCUS_SUBJECTS` trimmed to `['math', 'literacy']`; `Marketplace.tsx`'s finance-only `FocusBanner` call removed; `PiggyBank.tsx`'s trimmed to `['math']`.
- [x] `FocusSubject` type union left unchanged (`'math'|'literacy'|'sel'|'finance'`), as planned, so old synced `sel`/`finance` Focus records stay readable.

**6. Inline/in-place editing**
- [ ] Every card MUST support real in-place edit ("edit, change, fix, adjust") without navigating to a separate page. **Real gap:** today's actual editors (`NewDailyPlanBuilder.tsx`, `LessonPlanBuilder.tsx`) are full dedicated pages; there is no existing "expand-card-and-edit-inline" pattern anywhere in the teacher portal.
- [ ] **NEEDS TEACHER DECISION, the single largest open fork in this entire spec — ask this first, in isolation:** does "edit everything in place" mean full inline building of a day's task list from nothing (add/remove/reorder tasks, pick a Neighbor, set reward, all inside the card), or in-place editing for quick fields (reward, due date, standard, delivering Neighbor) with an "expand to full editor" escape hatch for building from scratch? Her words read as the former; the former is a materially bigger build than the latter. This decision changes whether the dashboard is a medium or large build, so answer it before the rest is estimated.
- [ ] Regardless of the answer above, "+ Add" MUST exist directly in the card grid in both day and week view, not buried in a separate builder entry point. **READY TO BUILD** as the minimum viable version either way.

**7. Visual redesign (Prodigy-style, platform-wide reach)**
- [ ] This screen's visual language MUST move off "too kid like" toward the Prodigy reference and Legends of Learning's content style. **READY TO BUILD** for this screen.
- [ ] Confirmed the shift is meant platform-wide in intent (Students, Inbox, Build Mode, Badge Manager, Marketplace Manager, Piggy Bank admin, eventually all of it). **NEEDS TEACHER DECISION** on sequencing: build this screen first as the reference implementation and roll the visual system out after, or treat it as one platform-wide pass now. Claudia's recommendation: the former (ship one real example before generalizing), but it's the teacher's call since she explicitly said platform-wide.
- [ ] Weekly chart MUST show "tasks completed," not "% right on first try" (explicitly cut). Derivable today directly from the existing `quizAttempts` array (filter by `completedAt` in the current week), no new field needed. **READY TO BUILD.**

**8. Reports/PDF**
- [ ] MUST offer a per-access PDF report, downloadable per student per activity.
- [ ] **Data gap, verified:** `QuestionAttemptLog` (`types.ts` ~753-757) has no per-question duration field; the full per-question log (`QuizRuntimeState.log`) is live runtime state, overwritten on the next attempt, never persisted next to the summary `QuizAttemptRecord`. **NEEDS DATA MODEL WORK:** add `msTaken?: number` to `QuestionAttemptLog` (captured from an explicit "question shown at" timestamp, not an after-the-fact diff, so a Help/calm-down break mid-quiz doesn't count as time-on-task); add `questionLog: QuestionAttemptLog[]` to `QuizAttemptRecord`, persisted where `submitQuizAnswer` already builds the summary record (`store.ts` ~2497-2526).
- [ ] No PDF library exists in `package.json` today. **NEEDS DATA MODEL WORK** (new dependency): add one client-side PDF library (`jspdf` or `@react-pdf/renderer`).
- [ ] Bakery Match already reports through `submitQuizAnswer`, covered automatically once the two fields above ship. Gas Pump calls `generateAutoQuestion` directly in `TownSquare.tsx`, bypasses this pipeline entirely, produces no persisted attempt record. Per direct teacher instruction, Gas Pump's integration is **OUT OF SCOPE for this pass** — explicitly deprioritized, not forgotten.

**9. CCSS AI question generation**
- [ ] MUST generate real AI-authored questions from a picked CCSS standard, chosen explicitly over expanding the existing fixed/random `generateAutoQuestion`.
- [ ] **Data gap, verified:** the existing CCSS picker (`AssignmentsIndex.tsx` lines 422-448, `MATH_STANDARDS`/`LITERACY_STANDARDS` from `commonCoreStandards.ts`) only writes the standard's text into the free-text `detail` field on a `Focus` — it does not store a structured code. `Focus` (`types.ts` ~740-751) has no `standardCode` field today. **NEEDS DATA MODEL WORK:** add `standardCode?: string` to `Focus` so a generation request has a real key to work from, not parsed-back-out text.
- [ ] **NEEDS DATA MODEL WORK, larger:** zero LLM-calling infrastructure exists in this codebase today (no `supabase/functions/` directory). Contract: new Edge Function `supabase/functions/generate-question-set/index.ts`, input `{standardCode, standardText, teacherRules, exampleQuestion?, questionCount?}`, output `{questions: MCQuestion[], modelUsed, generatedAt}` (reuses the existing `MCQuestion` shape, no new question type). API key lives only as an Edge Function secret, never shipped to the client.
- [ ] Every generated question MUST land as an editable draft in the existing Question Set editor before it can ever be assigned to a student. Hard requirement, settled, no ambiguity.
- [ ] Standard-swap mid-week MUST prompt the teacher at the moment of the swap ("keep the current question set" / "clear it and start fresh"), a per-swap prompt not a standing toggle. Settled. **READY TO BUILD** once `standardCode` exists.

**10. SEL/finance lane retirement** — duplicate of item 5's second bullet, restated for completeness since she named it separately: SEL and Personal Finance retire from live Focus authoring/rendering only. **DONE 2026-09-29**, same change as item 5.

### Weekly Planning + NPC Quest-Discovery Redesign — DESIGN COMPLETE, ready to build, nothing built yet

**Build order:**

**Direct teacher instruction (2026-09-29): prioritize the gameplay elements added today.** Sequenced across every piece recorded in this entry, reasoned from real dependencies (what has to exist before the next thing makes sense), cost (what's cheap to ship as a quick win), and value (what she's most clearly asked for). Five phases; nothing in a later phase should start before its phase-listed dependencies are real and working.

#### Phase 1 — Cheap, independent wins (no dependency on anything else in this entry)

1. **✅ Done (last two strays fixed 2026-10-04 in Town Square's subject label and Bawk's reminder): Reading → Literacy copy audit.** Every user-facing "Reading" label (starting with the Tier 0 Arrival Card) becomes "Literacy." Zero risk, zero dependencies, do this first and get it off the list.
2. **✅ SHIPPED 2026-10-04 (Claudia's two-hour stretch): Voice-to-text graded check (the new `SpeakQuestion` quiz type).**
   - Teachers add a **"Say it (answer out loud)"** question in any quiz editor: the word to say, plus optional extra accepted answers.
   - Students tap a big 🎤, say the answer, and it's graded with the forgiving `isCloseEnoughAnswer` matcher (any spoken word that's a near-match counts).
   - If marked wrong, they see "I heard: ..." with **Try again**, **Next question** or **That's what I said!**. That last one counts as right and is logged with `overrideConfirmed` on the attempt.
   - Works in Quiz/Practice tasks and the Platformer. A device without speech recognition falls back to typing.
   - The speech hook is now shared (`src/lib/useVoiceToText.ts`, Grammar Sandbox uses it too).
   - **Not yet:** routing repeated overrides into the Review Inbox (no teacher screen shows per-question logs yet). Native games stay multiple choice only.** Needs no new comparison logic (the existing typo-tolerant `isCloseEnoughAnswer` matcher already does the grading) and no new capture mechanism in principle (the existing browser-`SpeechRecognition` wrapper pattern already used three places in this codebase just needs a fourth, arguably-time-to-share instance). A real, standalone content-type win, fully independent of the Neighbor/quest system.
3. **Chart/graph Task Type.** New Task Type, reuses the student's own already-synced Transaction data for the Piggy Bank-tied version. Directly answers her own "a concept we're starting but haven't really done yet" priority. No dependency on anything else here.
4. **🟡 SHIPPED IN PART 2026-10-04 (Claudia's two-hour stretch): Economy Settings screen.** Teacher Game tab, top card, `src/lib/economy.ts` (the `economy` row in `style_looks`, no new SQL), every value pre-filled with today's behavior and a "Back to defaults" button.
   - **Editable now:** game pay per right answer ($1), Daily Streak goal (20), Streak Freeze price ($20), day 1 chest ($5), most chests per day (7), the Daily Spin's Streak Freeze chance (5%), its cash amounts ($1, $2, $2.50, $3, $5, $10) and its cashback percents (3%, 5%).
   - **Not yet:** the wheel's item wedges, the Quest 1 grand prize, and the streak interest rate (still held out until the streak definition question is settled with her).
   - Original plan: **Economy Settings screen — everything except the streak interest rate.** Expose the wheel's cash-amount pool, cashback tiers, segment composition, and the Quest1 grand prize as real teacher-editable settings, pre-filled at today's exact defaults. Hold the streak-interest-rate field out of this pass specifically — see Phase 2, item 5 — so this screen isn't shipped exposing a number that's about to be redefined out from under it.

**Why first:** none of these four touch the Neighbor system, the to-do list, or any new interaction model. They're real, visible progress the teacher can see immediately while the bigger structural work happens underneath.

#### Phase 2 — Foundational data model (nothing in Phase 3 can work without this)

5. **Streak redefinition:** login-based streak, weekend-safe Saturday/Sunday, the Streak Freeze Marketplace item. Independent of the quest-discovery UI, but should land before Phase 1 item 4's streak-interest exposure is finalized, so that setting reflects the real, current metric.
6. **`MathFocusSet` data model**, bolted onto the *existing* Student Manager/Assignments authoring screens first — it does not need to wait for the full one-screen weekly planner (Phase 5) to exist.
7. **The per-assignment "which Neighbor delivers this" field**, added to the existing Assignments builder as a small, additive dropdown, same reasoning as item 6: ship the field now, consolidate the surrounding UI later. **This is the single true prerequisite the rest of the Neighbor-gameplay layer depends on — nothing in Phase 3 can exist before the system knows which Neighbor holds which activity.**
8. **The "Today's Activities" generation layer** (the presentation layer pulling a variable daily mix from both subjects' existing rotations, capped at 3-5, plus the real persisted per-student/per-day discovered/undiscovered state). Depends directly on item 7.

**Why second:** items 7 and 8 are the actual foundation everything student-facing sits on. Building the pie menu or the to-do list before these exist would mean building UI with nothing real to show.

#### Phase 3 — The core student-facing discovery loop

9. **The restructured to-do list** (locked "See Pip" rows vs. real, discovered rows). Depends on item 8.
10. **The confirmation menu as the actual save point** (go now / view to-do list — either choice flips an activity to discovered). Depends on item 9.
11. **The Town Crier's Pass power-up + the teacher "show full to-do list" override toggle.** Depends only on 9-10 (there has to be a real gate before a bypass means anything) — does **not** need to wait for the topic pie menu (item 12). Cheap to add right after 9-10 ship, and high-value since it's the actual safety valve satisfying the platform's "never a hard block" rule.
12. **The topic pie menu + mandatory Greeting gate**, including the real content-authoring lift of splitting each participating Neighbor's dialogue into its four buckets (Greeting / Small Talk / Assignment / Completion-report). Depends on 7 + 8 + 9 + 10.

**The key sequencing call here, worth stating explicitly:** items 9-11 already produce a genuinely complete, shippable "discover your assignment, go do it" loop using today's existing instant-conversation-on-approach behavior. Item 12 (the topic menu / Greeting gate) is the single most labor-intensive piece in this entire design — a real dialogue-content rewrite, not just new UI — so it should land as a visible upgrade on top of an already-working loop, not as the thing the entire feature is blocked behind. Don't gate all of Phase 3's real value on the hardest single item in it.

#### Phase 4 — Completion loop and the harder edge cases

13. **The return-to-Neighbor completion conversation + the `QuestGift` catalog**, MVP scope only (placeable Home Room items + cash-equivalent gifts — wearable/clothing gifts stay out of scope until a real slot-based equip system exists, which this platform doesn't have today). Depends on item 12 (needs the Completion-report topic wedge as its entry point) and on 9-11 (an activity has to be genuinely completable first).
14. **The Catch-Up Lock** — rollover capture (hooking into the existing daily-refresh logic before it overwrites yesterday's unfinished tasks) plus the hard-lock overlay, with Help/calm-down required to stay reachable through it exactly as it already does through the Wizard Lock. The capture half is cheap and could in principle move earlier purely as a safety net so no data is lost during rollout, but the full user-facing behavior (auto-unlocked rows on the to-do list, the lock screen itself) needs items 9-12 in place to mean anything, so it's sequenced here.
15. **The no-activity-day fallback** (4 native-game activities, 2 literacy + 2 math, Focus-aware content). Depends on item 6 (`MathFocusSet`, for Focus-awareness) and item 8 (`DailyActivityEntry`'s fallback slot). **Per the new native-games-per-building standing direction: this should be built treating "native game" as the pluggable category it now actually is (the Platformer and Bakery Match both qualify today, more may exist by the time this ships), not hardcoded to the Platformer specifically.**
16. **The literacy spelling-pattern stopgap** (teacher-authored sets only, no generator fallback for spelling patterns specifically) ships alongside item 15 as its honest scope boundary, not as a separate later item.

#### Phase 5 — Lower-urgency polish, no blocking relationship to anything above, any time

17. **The Neighbors management screen** in the Game tab (personality/quirk fields, the "view all neighbors" ask). Genuinely lower-stakes now that "role" is confirmed flavor-only, not a load-bearing subject binding.
18. **The CAS-style asset-viewer/turntable enhancement** to Build Mode's existing catalog picker. A real QoL win for curating item 13's placeable `QuestGift` assets, but not a blocker for it — item 13's MVP already works against the existing, un-enhanced picker.
19. **The full one-screen weekly planner UI consolidation** (pulling `MathFocusSet`, `LiteracyFocusSet`, the Common Core picker, and this week's activities onto one real screen). Items 6 and 7's underlying *data* ship early in Phase 2, bolted onto existing screens on purpose — this item is the larger navigation/UX project that makes the whole thing feel like one coherent planning surface instead of scattered tabs. Genuinely high value (it's the direct fix for her "assignments and focuses are shown in too many different spaces" complaint), but nothing else in this entire build order mechanically depends on it, so it can run as its own parallel-track project rather than gating any student-facing gameplay.

**A natural next candidate for the native-games-per-building direction**, named as requested, not a scoped proposal: **a native Bank game under Penny** (Penny already exists as a Neighbor with a real building — "I run the Bank right over there" — and an existing finance Focus lane). It would directly reuse the chart/graph Task Type's work on a student's real Piggy Bank/Transaction data (Phase 1, item 3) and the finance-lane content the Focus system already supports, giving a question-set-fueled, Bakery-Match-style challenge-screen loop built around real money-counting and finance skills against the student's own actual economy data. A weaker, less-developed secondary candidate: a Post Office/mail-sorting game under Wren, themed around literacy/vocabulary sorting. Either would need its own full design pass before being build-ready, the same way Bakery Match itself was.

**Started 2026-09-29, direct teacher brief, expanded over several follow-up messages the same day.** Not a small feature — a redesign spanning teacher-side weekly planning, a new student-facing gameplay loop, a rewards/economy visibility layer, a 3D asset viewer, and a Neighbors management screen. Claudia is doing a design/research pass (no code touched) before anything is scoped or built; this entry is a durable record of where that stands. **Do not build anything under this heading until it's marked DECIDED below.**

**The ask, in short:** (1) One central weekly-planning view for a teacher: a Common Core/curricular standards picker (math + literacy), the existing Word Origins/phonics/morpheme per-student focus system, and a capped list of "3-5 activities" per day, replacing the currently-scattered multi-screen authoring flow. (2) A student-facing "mystery"/quest-discovery loop: Neighbors act as quest-givers, showing an exclamation mark when holding an undiscovered activity; walking up starts a real back-and-forth ABA-style "what do you have for me today" conversation (informational only — no item changes hands here); a Go-now/View-to-do-list confirmation menu is where the activity actually gets marked "discovered," persisted everywhere the to-do list shows; the to-do list (moved from the main computer screen into the pie menu) shows locked "See Pip"-style rows until discovered; finishing everything triggers the existing Bonus Spin. A further mechanic: returning to the Neighbor who gave an activity *after* finishing it, for a completion conversation, earns a bonus gift — a specific curated asset (food/clothing/accessory), not a random pull. (3) Assignment/focus visibility is scattered across too many teacher screens and needs real consolidation. (4) Game "logic" (reward amounts, prize-wheel contents, etc.) is currently opaque/decided ad hoc rather than a visible, teacher-configurable setting with sensible defaults. (5) A Sims-4-Create-A-Sim-style 3D asset viewer/turntable (excluding textures/UI-kit assets, resized via the existing unit-size-class system) — also where a teacher would curate which assets are eligible as Neighbor-gift items and their attach metadata. (6) A Neighbors management screen in the teacher Game tab (view every NPC, assign roles, default personality, a customizable "spice"). Teacher-side UI is explicitly meant to stay plain/functional, not themed like the student-facing game.

**Claudia's design review — consolidated findings (two passes, 2026-09-29):**

- **Headline finding: much more of this already exists than the brief assumed, which changes the real scope a lot.** Multi-exchange, branching NPC dialogue already exists (`worldQuest1.ts`'s `ConversationStep`/`ConversationOption`) — the "boss hands out an assignment" conversation is new **content** (dialogue variants), not a new engine. The `!` exclamation-mark indicator already exists for Pip/Wren today, tied to real unfinished per-subject work. The Bonus Spin already fires on "both subjects complete" (A13) — its trigger just needs updating to "today's generated activity list complete." `LiteracyFocusSet` (per-student, date-windowed word list) is already exactly the literacy "word list" ask — **there is no math equivalent today**, a real named gap; Claudia's proposed default shape if one gets built: a strategy/skill focus, a fact-family/operation focus, and practice problems/vocabulary terms, mirroring `LiteracyFocusSet`'s three-part structure — offered as a starting point for the teacher to react to, not a confirmed decision. A real, sourced Common Core standards picker already exists and is wired into the class-wide Focus editor (A19, `commonCoreStandards.ts`) — not independently re-verifiable against the official standards from this sandbox, flagged for the teacher to spot-check her own grade bands' coverage.
- **Economy/reward "logic," traced value by value.** Already real, data-driven, teacher-editable today: per-task reward (`Task.rewardCents`, $1 fallback), per-badge reward ($2 fallback), Playground default ($0.25 fallback). **Hardcoded with zero teacher visibility, matching the complaint exactly:** the Daily/Bonus Spin Wheel's entire composition (`dailySpin.ts` — fixed cash amounts, fixed 3%/5% cashback tiers, fixed wedge-count structure), the streak interest rate/cap (`store.ts`, now being redefined anyway — see below), and the Quest1 grand prize amount. Proposed fix: a new **Economy Settings** surface (alongside Piggy Bank admin, A40), pre-filled at today's already-tuned defaults, extending the "default + per-item override" pattern the task/badge system already uses to the wheel and interest rate. Open: which of these the teacher actually wants surfaced (wheel amounts/odds only, or the wedge structure itself, e.g. turning off the pet wedge).
- **The CAS-style asset viewer.** Build Mode (A4) already has 1,000+ models, generated thumbnails, and a real unit-based size-class system (`docs/SIZE_REFERENCE.md`, 1.0 unit = player height) — what's genuinely new is a live rotatable turntable preview in place of a flat thumbnail, scoped to real placeable meshes only. Two build options Claudia flagged, her recommendation is (a): enhance Build Mode's existing catalog picker with an orbit-preview stage (smaller, reuses the existing pipeline), vs. (b) a standalone browsing screen closer to the "central spotlight" framing but genuinely new screen-level work. Teacher's call, not yet made.
- **The Neighbors management screen.** A partial version already exists (World Editor's Roster tab: all 4 Neighbors + Townspeople, a cosmetic title override, a 9-option voice-tone preset) — that's the entire extent of "personality" that exists today, just buried in a builder tool instead of the Game tab. "Give them roles": the hardcoded `NEIGHBOR_FOCUS_LANE` constant (Pip=math, Wren=literacy, Scout=SEL, Penny=finance) is the real gap here, and the teacher's own follow-up (below) confirms this needs to become genuinely reassignable, not just made visible. "Default personalities": nothing exists, a real new field. "Spice": the teacher's own term, no existing analog — needs her definition before it can be designed; closest reusable mechanism is the multi-variant dialogue system already used for jokes.
- **Two real conflicts against standing platform rules, surfaced rather than quietly resolved.** (a) A28's Focus Guardrail Tiers (Desk Glow, Companion Check-In, Wizard Lock) should layer alongside the new quest system, not be replaced by it — these key off idle time/zero-progress, never the streak, so they're unaffected by anything below. (b) The platform's standing "never a hard block, always a way back" rule — **resolved** by the teacher's own answer on subject-icon browsing (below): the computer desktop's existing, always-available Math/Literacy views (A42) stay untouched and ungated by NPC discovery, so a student who can't or won't go find a Neighbor on a hard day can always just open Math or Literacy directly, exactly as today. The quest loop is additive motivation, never the only door in. One real question this still leaves open: does that computer view show *every* activity assigned for that subject today regardless of discovery state, or only ones already discovered via a Neighbor (full transparency vs. preserving the quest's mystery) — genuinely open, not decided.
- **Teacher-side diagnosis, inspected against the real code.** Answering "what is this student doing this week" today requires checking **four separate screens**: the Assignments list (7 filter tabs), its class-wide-only Focuses panel, the per-student Weekly Schedule/Activity Library tabs, and Student Manager (where `LiteracyFocusSet` is actually authored, with no link back to the other three) — plus Badge Manager and World Editor's Roster tab for the newer asks. The daily-vs-weekly (`repeat` vs `span`) distinction itself is legitimate and worth keeping; the real problem is no single screen rolls up focus + word list + activities + rewards per student. Proposed fix: one new per-student weekly planner screen that generates the same underlying records these existing surfaces already produce, rather than replacing any of them.

**Decided/confirmed by the teacher (2026-09-29), reconciled against the platform by Claudia:**
- **Day shape**: activities are a variable day-to-day mix drawn from both subjects (some days math-heavy, some literacy-heavy), not a fixed split — confirms Claudia's recommended design: keep the existing per-subject Math/Literacy rotations exactly as they are underneath, and generate each day's capped "3-5 activities" list by pulling a variable count from each. Streaks/guardrails/reporting stay untouched underneath.
- **Subject renamed**: "Reading" becomes "Literacy" everywhere student-facing (covers both reading and writing) — a copy/labeling audit (Tier 0 Arrival Card and anywhere else "Reading" appears as a subject label), not a data-model change; the underlying `Subject` type is already `'math' | 'literacy'`.
- **Subject-organized browsing vs. subject-agnostic quest-giving**: the computer desktop keeps separate Math/Literacy icons (A42, unchanged) as a subject-filtered browsing view; Neighbor conversations are NOT subject-restricted — any Neighbor can hand out either subject's activity. This is what resolves the no-hard-block question above.
- **Per-subject completion gets a small, deliberately modest celebration** (confetti + a bit of music) when that subject's activities are done for the day — explicitly not a big blown-out event, reuses the existing `subjectComplete` flag already computed live elsewhere in the app. The Bonus Spin stays the day's one real headline moment, now triggered by the full generated activity list being complete rather than "both subjects."
- **Neighbor-to-subject binding is per-assignment, not fixed.** Direct correction to the earlier fixed-lane assumption: "Pip doesn't always have to be math" — when the teacher creates an assignment in the new weekly planner, she picks which Neighbor hands it out. Neighbors are otherwise general/undefined until the separate Neighbors-management-screen work gives them a personality. Most day-to-day assignments will still be math and literacy in practice, just not lane-locked to one Neighbor. Still open: is the existing `NEIGHBOR_FOCUS_LANE` system retired entirely in favor of this, or does it stay for whatever else it currently drives while this is a separate, additive per-assignment picker — Claudia's read is the latter, not yet confirmed by the teacher.
- **Neighbor conversations are informational-only.** No item ever changes hands during the "what do you have for me today" conversation itself — it's purely verbal. The confirmation-menu choice afterward (go now / view to-do list) is the actual save point that flips the activity to "discovered," and that state needs to persist and show consistently everywhere the to-do list appears (real per-student, per-day, per-activity state, not client-only).
- **New: return-to-Neighbor completion bonus**, offered as something for Claudia to expand on rather than a locked spec. After finishing an activity, going back to the Neighbor who gave it for a completion conversation earns a bonus gift — a specific, curated asset (food, clothing, or an accessory) tied to that quest, explicitly not a random pull (distinct from the existing Mystery Adoption Box's variable-ratio design, A27). Ties directly into the asset-viewer ask: that tool is also where a teacher would curate which assets are eligible as Neighbor gifts and their attach metadata (wearable slot, etc.).
- **The streak is being redefined — a real change, not a misunderstanding, traced system-by-system.** Old, currently-documented definition (A33): a daily streak requiring both subjects finished. New definition, teacher's own words: the streak is just **logging into the system**, with Saturday and Sunday automatically streak-safe (no login required), plus a new $20 Marketplace item, a **Streak Freeze**, usable either proactively (protects an at-risk streak) or retroactively (restores one already lost). Traced downstream: streak interest (A30) and the "showed up" badge (A33) now key off this Login Streak, not subject completion — Claudia flagged one honest tradeoff worth keeping on record rather than glossing over: this means a student can now earn streak interest from logging in alone, with zero academic work that day, a real departure from this platform's otherwise-consistent "reward reflects real engagement" principle. Not overridden, just named, since the teacher gets to make this call and has. A28's Desk Glow/Companion Check-In/Wizard Lock tiers are unaffected (they key off idle time/zero-progress, never the streak). The Bonus Spin stays keyed to finishing the day's real activity list, not login — showing up and finishing the work are now two clearly separate, non-conflicting signals. **Two genuinely new pieces of logic this requires, not just a definition change**: (1) weekend-aware streak-break checking (the gap-check needs to skip Sat/Sun, not just check "logged in yesterday"); (2) a new Marketplace item type that behaves unlike anything else in the catalog — needs to either auto-consume itself to protect an about-to-break streak, or be spent after the fact to restore one, which requires remembering the pre-break streak count for some window. **Open**: how long that restore window stays open (same day only, or indefinite until used) — not decided.

**Resolved by the teacher's direct answers and Claudia's final self-resolution pass (2026-09-29), replacing the prior open-questions list:**
1. **Neighbor count/distribution per day — resolved: no fixed cap.** Since delivery is a per-assignment teacher choice (below), however many distinct Neighbors the teacher happens to pick on a given day is however many are "active" that day — 1 to all 4 (Pip, Wren, Scout, Penny).
2. **Every activity requires an NPC conversation to unlock — resolved: yes, by design** ("the answer will be yes, because that'll be part of the gameplay") — see the bypass mechanisms and rollover exception below.
3. **What's felt "confusing" about today's teacher UI, her own lived experience — still open**, not inferable from anything said; doesn't block anything, since the one-screen weekly planner is the fix regardless of which specific friction she meant.
4. **Computer's Math/Literacy view vs. discovery state — resolved: full transparency, unfiltered, exactly as it works today.** Reasoning: her design instinct throughout consistently favors an honest, always-available parallel path alongside the gamified layer (Town Crier's Pass, teacher override toggle, the computer split itself) — never gating the honest path behind the fun one — and this is also required by the standing "never a hard block" rule below.
5. **Streak Freeze restore window — resolved with a default: no expiration**, usable anytime until spent, restores to the pre-break streak count. Assumed default, easy to tighten later if she wants a window.
6. **`NEIGHBOR_FOCUS_LANE` — resolved: retired outright, not kept in parallel.** Keeping both would make the `!` indicator mean two different, sometimes-contradictory things depending on which system a day's content came from — breaks the "the mark always means exactly one thing" discipline this platform already protects. Once an assignment carries its own delivering-Neighbor field, the `!` logic reads from that field; `NEIGHBOR_FOCUS_LANE` becomes dead code to delete.
7. **Asset viewer build shape — resolved: enhance Build Mode's existing catalog picker with an orbit-preview stage** (the smaller option), confirmed as the default plan since nothing since has contradicted it.
8. **Neighbor "spice" — resolved with a minimal buildable default, not fully specified:** a single free-text "Quirk" field per Neighbor (teacher-typed, e.g. "always mentions the weather") that the dialogue-variant system can optionally reference — refinable later, doesn't block building the Neighbors screen now.
9. **Missed/unfinished-day carry-forward — corrected, not "no carry-forward" as originally guessed.** Superseded by the teacher's explicit rollover rule: incomplete activities do carry forward and become a hard priority lock (see Rollover/Catch-Up Lock below).
10. **Return-to-Neighbor completion-gift mechanic shape — resolved, Claudia's own design call as invited:** a new, small `QuestGift` catalog, resolved deterministically per Neighbor/quest (not the Mystery Adoption Box's variable-ratio design, A27 — architecturally separate, closer in spirit to A33's badge system). The completion conversation reuses the existing dialogue engine and the platform's existing specific-effort-praise pattern. Gift assignment is optional with a fallback: teacher can pick a specific gift when authoring the assignment, unset falls back to a modest default — same default+override pattern used for task/badge rewards. Claim window: no expiration, claimable anytime once the activity is done.
11. **Does "show full to-do list" require a teacher-unassigned/system-auto-pick option for the Neighbor field — resolved: yes.** Matches her repeated "I don't always want to choose everything, but I do need the ability to" pattern — support leaving the delivering-Neighbor field unset, system auto-picks (e.g. round-robin across least-recently-used active Neighbors) as the default.
12. **Does a slot-based wearable/equip system already exist, for clothing/accessory gifts — resolved, verified by reading `types.ts`: no.** Every existing `equipped*` field swaps a whole avatar/character model or a tool's cosmetic skin; there is no hat-slot, held-item-slot, or body-attachment concept anywhere today. **"Clothing/accessories" as literal Neighbor gifts is a genuinely new equip system to build, not a metadata-tagging job** — real new scope, not previously counted.

**Q4 is now settled: every activity requires a Neighbor conversation to unlock, by design** — the teacher's own words: "the answer will be yes, because that'll be part of the gameplay." Two distinct bypass mechanisms on top, not the same lever twice:
1. **A purchasable Marketplace power-up (~$10) that reveals the full to-do list and skips the Neighbor-finding gameplay.** Claudia checked the existing precedent (the Skip Pass, `Student.skipTokens`, a consumable count decremented one at a time) and proposed the new power-up follow the same shape — a consumable token, not a permanent unlock — reasoning that a renewable per-day choice preserves the student's own ongoing option to enjoy the quest loop on days they want it, rather than a one-time purchase permanently removing it. **Proposed name: "📯 Town Crier's Pass"** (in-world-logical reason the whole to-do list becomes visible at once, matches the "[Something] Pass" naming the Skip Pass already establishes) — alternates offered: "🗺️ Full Map Pass," "🔭 Scout's Shortcut." Teacher's call on the final name.
2. **A separate, teacher-controlled per-student "show full to-do list" setting**, a standing `StudentManager.tsx`-style toggle (same pattern as `showPunctuationTiles` and similar existing per-student accommodations) — a teacher's standing accommodation call for a student who shouldn't have to do the wayfinding at all, independent of and coexisting with the purchasable power-up.

**The no-activity-day fallback, corrected count: 4 total native-game activities** (2 literacy Neighbors each pulling their own literacy question set into a native game, 2 math Neighbors doing the same for math). Delivery mechanism reuses what already exists rather than building new: "native game" concretely meant just the Platformer (A15) at the time this was written, and A25 (Playground/Free Play) already has the exact "run a question set as a standalone Platformer session" mechanism — the fallback activities use the same mechanism, just delivered through a Neighbor conversation instead of Playground, and they count toward the real day's completion since they stand in for real assigned work. **Superseded by the standing "native question-set-fueled games per building/asset" direction above:** "native game" is now the general category (Platformer, Bakery Match, and whatever's added next under that direction), not Platformer specifically — this fallback, and a Neighbor's normal assignment handoff (§15 above), should draw from whichever native game fits the content once more than one exists, not default to the Platformer by name.

**A real, honest gap this surfaces, not glossed over:** `generateAutoQuestion` (`autoQuestions.ts`, the platform's existing auto-question fallback, also used by the Gas Pump) currently picks one of its 5 generator types **completely at random**, with no awareness of any Focus or standard — it needs to become Focus-aware (weighted toward that week's active Math Focus/morpheme list) to actually satisfy "reference the focuses and standards for that week." Separately, and more seriously: today's literacy auto-generator only covers morpheme-definition questions — **there is no phonics/spelling-pattern generator**, blocked for the same standing reason as the UFLI content elsewhere in this doc (no way to verify a real published scope-and-sequence from this environment). This directly matters for the one student whose actual Literacy Focus is a spelling pattern (named in the teacher's very first message on this whole topic) — the fallback has nothing to draw from for that student today. Claudia's recommendation, not yet confirmed: prefer an existing, teacher-tagged real Question Set from the library first, and only fall back to generated content when nothing's tagged — safer instructionally, reuses A18 rather than leaning entirely on generation — but flagged as her call given the real accuracy tradeoff, not decided unilaterally.

**Resolved, continuing the same list:**
13. **The fallback day's 4 Neighbor identities — resolved:** given exactly 4 Neighbors exist today, and "one activity per Neighbor per day" cleanly extends the same "one activity per conversation" discipline, the fallback day exhaustively uses all 4 named Neighbors, one activity each. Scales cleanly if a 5th Neighbor is ever added (any 2 literacy-flavored, any 2 math-flavored).
14. **Fallback content source — resolved, leaning toward the safer option:** prefer a real, teacher-tagged Question Set matching that week's Focus first; fall back to `generateAutoQuestion` only when nothing's tagged. Real instructional-accuracy risk on one side, simple content-reuse safety on the other — same reasoning already used to keep full CCSS-generation parked.
15. **Literacy-generator gap (morphemes only, no spelling-pattern generator) — still genuinely open**, dependent on how much risk she's willing to accept for that specific student profile before shipping; not inferable.
16. **Final name for the to-do-list-reveal power-up — resolved: "📯 Town Crier's Pass"** (a $10 consumable Marketplace power-up, same shape as the existing Skip Pass/`skipTokens` — a plain per-use count, not a permanent unlock, so the discovery gameplay stays a renewable choice rather than being permanently foreclosed). Alternates on record if she wants a different name later: "🗺️ Full Map Pass," "🔭 Scout's Shortcut."

**Neighbor conversations become a Sims-4-style topic pie menu, gated behind a mandatory Greeting — direct teacher instruction, real change to the interaction shape, not just content.** Clicking/approaching a Neighbor no longer drops straight into a linear scripted conversation (confirmed as today's actual behavior, `handleTalk` in `TownSquare.tsx`, calls `beginConversation` immediately with zero selection step — the exact "jump straight in" pattern she's ruling out). Instead: a pie menu opens; before that Neighbor has been greeted this visit it shows exactly one wedge, "👋 Greeting"; picking it runs a short, genuinely new, dedicated ABA-style opening exchange ("Hi Pip!" → "Hey there!"), then the same menu re-opens with whichever topic wedges actually apply that day (an Assignment topic if this Neighbor holds an undiscovered activity; a Completion-report topic once an activity's been finished, tied to the return-for-a-gift mechanic above; ordinary Small Talk, the existing joke content repackaged as one topic among several instead of the only thing that happens; Goodbye). Confirmed this **sits on top of the existing branching dialogue engine, not a replacement** — the existing `ConversationStep`/`ConversationOption` machinery is sound and reused as-is; what's new is only the outer topic-selection layer. Also confirmed: her "give the student a few different real ways to ask" request needs zero new engine capability — a `ConversationStep`'s existing `options` field (2-3 phrasings converging into the same next step) already does exactly this; it's authoring, not engineering.

**What this actually costs, beyond the pie-menu UI itself:** every existing Neighbor dialogue variant today opens with its greeting baked directly into the first line (e.g. Scout's is literally "Hi! I'm Scout. Welcome to Yoglandia!"), so this requires real content restructuring, not just new content bolted on — a Neighbor's material has to split into four real buckets (Greeting, Small Talk, Assignment, Completion) where today there's one linear script, per participating Neighbor.

**Scoped to the 4 quest-giver Neighbors for now, not the separate ambient Townspeople small-talk system** (Claudia's recommendation, her own call, not yet confirmed by the teacher) — flagged as a real, visible inconsistency worth naming rather than silently deciding: once this ships, clicking a Neighbor gets the new gated topic menu while clicking an ambient Townsperson (which, by this platform's existing design, includes former Neighbors once already met once) still drops straight into the old pattern. Recommendation: build for Neighbors first since it has real functional purpose there (gating assignment delivery), treat extending it to Townspeople as a deliberate later decision.

**One system-initiated exception recommended, flagged as Claudia's own call:** Scout's existing idle check-in (A28 Tier 3, `SCOUT_CHECKIN_VARIANT`) should stay a direct bypass of the new gate entirely — it's a deliberately low-friction, system-initiated regulation nudge, and forcing a Greeting tap in front of it would work against its own purpose.

**Fits the platform's own existing UI-economy rule** — the pie menu's own established "5-6 wedges is the ceiling" budget — without inventing new interaction chrome; the candidate wedge set stays at or under 4 simultaneous wedges even in the busiest case, and should reuse the exact same radial wedge-rendering pattern already built for the self-menu/companion-picker.

17. **"Established" (greeted) persists per day, or resets on every fresh approach — resolved: resets on every fresh approach**, re-greeting required each time. Her framing is explicit and repeated that this is real social-skill practice, not flavor ("give the student real contextual opportunities to explore conversation") — repetition is the actual mechanism real-world social-script practice relies on. Named tradeoff, accepted: more taps for a student who checks in with the same Neighbor multiple times a day.
18. **Should the pie-menu pattern extend to ambient Townspeople — resolved: no, not in this build.** Neighbors only; Townspeople keep their existing direct-conversation pattern, a named, accepted inconsistency (same character type, different interaction rules) to revisit later as its own deliberate decision, not blocking this build.

**Math question-set content — the analog to literacy's word lists, direct teacher list.** Fact families/number pairs ("which numbers make 10"), fill-in-the-blank addition/subtraction, multiplication/division fact families, finding area, finding perimeter — all content-authoring work inside the existing Quiz/`MCQuestion` shape, zero new engineering; the first three already have a direct working precedent in `generateAutoQuestion`'s existing fact-family generators. **Charts and graphs is the one real new item**, explicitly named by the teacher as "a concept we're starting but haven't really done yet," wants heavy visual emphasis, specifically **tied into the banking/finance side (Piggy Bank)**. This is a genuinely new Task Type (A17's existing list — Quiz, External Link, Off-screen/Paper, Video, Reading Passage+Questions, Flashcard, Word Chain, Sentence Editing, Article Reader, Sentence Builder, Pick One, Platformer+Quiz — has nothing that displays a chart and asks a question against it), plus real integration work to pull a student's own live Piggy Bank transaction history (A30) into an authentic graphing exercise ("how much did you earn this week," "which day did you spend the most") rather than a synthetic chart with made-up numbers — strong, well-grounded idea, worth building as real new scope, not folding into "just more question content." The CCSS-generates-its-own-question-set example she gave alongside this ("adding and subtracting three-digit numbers" as a Focus that generates the set directly) reads as her describing the target end-state for the already-parked auto-generation pipeline, not new instruction to build it now — nothing has retracted her earlier "that can be later down the road."

**Literacy word-list/word-work question types, direct teacher list.** (1) A voice-to-text graded check — student sees the word, speaks it aloud, the transcription is checked against that one expected answer, not free dictation; (2) define the word, multiple choice — ordinary `MCQuestion`, already fully supported; (3) define word parts/morphemes — already has a working precedent in `generateAutoQuestion`'s `generateMorphemeDefinitionQuestion`; (4) which question type applies depends entirely on how the teacher builds that specific set — confirms `LiteracyFocusSet`'s existing shape (`phonicsPatterns`/`morphemes`/`practiceWords`) is the right structure as-is, no special-casing needed. **Item 1's real new piece, precisely stated:** this platform already has live speech-to-text (the Web Speech API, wrapped in `useTextBoxVoiceToText`, already used in Literacy Manipulatives' Text Box/Sentence Formula blanks and in Notes/Feedback, per A23/A37) — what's genuinely new isn't the microphone, it's grading spoken input against one specific expected answer instead of accepting whatever gets transcribed as free-text content. **Accuracy risk flagged, Claudia's own addition:** speech-to-text has real accuracy variance for children's voices and articulation differences relevant to this population; an exact-transcript-match check risks marking a student wrong who said the word correctly but got mis-transcribed — a worse failure mode here than a normal typo. Recommend a lenient/fuzzy match (same spirit as the existing typo-tolerant fill-in-the-blank pattern in A17's Quiz type) plus a "that's not what I heard, tap here" override, matching this platform's existing non-punitive conventions (e.g. Grammar Sandbox's "invalid attempt just doesn't connect, no X, no shake" rule).

**Rollover/incomplete-activity lock rule, direct teacher instruction — a new, separate lock state, not a 4th Focus Guardrail Tier.** Her rule precisely: any activity not finished by end of day carries forward to the next day; carried-forward activities become priority and are **auto-unlocked on the to-do list, bypassing the Neighbor-conversation gate entirely** (shown fully visible, not a locked "See Pip" row); while a student has *any* rolled-over incomplete activity, **no other gameplay is available at all** — screen locks to only the to-do list and finishing those specific activities, across every task type. **Reasoning for a new state rather than "Tier 4":** A28's tiers all respond to a student's own current, live, in-session behavior (idle time, lack of real-time progress) and are explicitly designed to clear the instant behavior changes; this rule is structural and date-based instead — a student can be locked the instant they log in on a new day, before doing anything idle, purely because yesterday wasn't finished, a trigger shape none of A28's tiers share. Naming it: the **Catch-Up Lock** — activates automatically at the start of any day with a carried-forward incomplete activity, independent of A28's tiers while active (moot, since the student can't reach general gameplay to be idle in). **One requirement imposed on top of her literal words, flagged as Claudia's own addition, not something she stated for this specific rule:** Help/calm-down (A29) stays reachable through the Catch-Up Lock exactly as it does through the Wizard Lock, per the standing Part D rule that regulation is never gated behind currency, streaks, or task completion — her "no other gameplay is available at all" isn't read as intending to override the one rule this whole redesign has repeatedly protected; everything else about the lock is exactly as strict as she described. **Self-resolved implementation details, per the standing meta-instruction, stated with reasoning:** the current day's own fresh activities simply pause until the backlog clears, then that day proceeds normally, not doubled up with the just-cleared backlog; carried-forward items stay auto-unlocked for as long as they remain incomplete, even across a second or third rollover (re-gating an already-overdue item would work against prioritizing catch-up); the lock is global, not per-subject (her words read as total, not subject-scoped); Bonus Spin/streak/confetti need no special-case interference logic since they already can't fire until the day's own activities are reachable, which can't happen until the backlog clears.

**Economy Settings screen (A8) — resolved: expose everything, not a curated subset.** All Daily/Bonus Spin Wheel composition (cash amounts, cashback tiers, wedge on/off toggles, odds), streak interest rate/cap, and the Quest1 grand prize amount, pre-filled at today's exact tuned defaults, alongside the existing per-task/per-badge reward editors. Her own repeated framing ("I don't always want to choose everything, but I do need the ability to") is best satisfied by showing everything and letting her ignore what she doesn't want to touch, since "give the ability" only holds if it's actually there when she wants it.

**Implementation shapes for every genuinely-new data-model piece, real field names/types/Supabase tables, cited against exact existing files/lines — not just the concept level.**

- **Load-bearing existing behavior confirmed before designing any of this:** `applyTemplateToStudent` (`store.ts` ~3149-3173), run daily by `applyTodaysScheduleIfNeeded` (~3201-3248), **completely overwrites** each subject's rotation and resets progress every day — an unfinished task is silently discarded today, there is no rollover mechanism anywhere yet. This overwrite is the exact point the Catch-Up Lock's capture logic has to hook into, before it runs.
- **`MathFocusSet`** — mirrors `LiteracyFocusSet` (`types.ts` ~688-696) field-for-field: `strategySkills`/`factFamilies`/`practiceProblems` (mirroring `phonicsPatterns`/`morphemes`/`practiceWords`) plus an optional `ccssCode`. New `mathFocusSets` store array, same publish/delete action pair as the literacy version, authored on the new weekly planner screen (not `StudentManager.tsx`). New `math_focus_sets` Supabase table, column-for-column matching `literacy_focus_sets`' real schema (`supabase/schema.sql` ~378-386).
- **`DailyActivityEntry`** (Today's Activities + persisted discovery state) — `sourceKind: 'rotation' | 'rollover' | 'fallback-generated'`, `taskId`, `deliveringNeighborId` (null = system auto-picks at discovery time), `discovered`/`discoveredAt` (the real save point, §14a), `questGiftId`/`giftClaimed(At)`.
