New `generateTodaysActivities(studentId)` store action, called from inside `applyTodaysScheduleIfNeeded`: if rollover items exist, generate only those (pre-discovered, gate bypassed) and skip fresh same-day entries entirely; else pull today's real tasks from both rotations, capped at 5 — **self-resolved edge case:** a heavier-than-5 day never hides or drops a task, only the first 5 (stepping-stone order) get NPC-discovery treatment, the rest stay fully reachable through the untouched computer Math/Literacy view, mirroring the existing "never pad a light day" rule in reverse; else the 4-Neighbor fallback (§16b). New `daily_activities` Supabase table.
- **Catch-Up Lock** — `RolledOverActivity` (full `Task` snapshot copy, not a live reference, same convention as the Activity Library's copy-not-link pattern) in a new `rolledOverActivities` store map. **Capture** hooks into `applyTodaysScheduleIfNeeded`, right before a subject's rotation overwrite: any task not in that subject's `completedTaskIds` gets snapshotted first. **Clear** is one small addition inside the existing `completeTask` action (`store.ts` ~2184). **Lock state itself isn't stored, it's derived live** exactly like `showWizardLock` already is (`TownSquare.tsx` ~3138): `hasCatchUpLock = rolledOverActivities[studentId]?.length > 0`. Reuses the Wizard Lock's existing overlay/z-index pattern but as a fully separate, independently-triggered state (date-based, not idle-time-based) — **required addition, Claudia's own per Part D:** `HelpOverlay`'s existing `aboveLock={showWizardLock}` prop (~3761) must also read `hasCatchUpLock`, so calm-down stays reachable through this lock exactly as it does through the Wizard Lock. New `rolled_over_activities` Supabase table.
- **`QuestGift` catalog** — builds directly on an existing near-miss rather than inventing new: `worldQuest1.ts`'s `Quest1Neighbor.itemLabel`/`itemRewardCents` (~84-85) already handles "a Neighbor hands out a specific named item," paid as a cash equivalent today specifically because no real catalog item exists to attach it to yet — same graceful fallback pattern reused here. `QuestGiftKind: 'placeable' | 'wearable' | 'cashEquivalent'`. **Recommended real-shippable-now scope: launch with `placeable` (real Build Mode/Home Room decor) and `cashEquivalent` only** — `wearable` stays in the type for later but is gated out of the teacher-authoring UI, since (confirmed by directly reading `types.ts`, not guessed at) this platform has no slot-based wearable/equip system today, every `equipped*` field swaps a whole avatar or a tool's cosmetic skin, none attach an item to a body slot; building that is real, separate, bigger scope that shouldn't block the gift mechanic shipping now. New optional `questGiftId` field on `Task`, same default+override convention as every other reward field. New `quest_gifts` Supabase table.
- **Chart/graph Task Type** — new `'chartGraph'` `TaskType`, new `ChartGraphContent` (`chartKind: 'bar'|'line'|'pie'`, `dataSource: 'piggyBankWeekly'|'piggyBankByCategory'|'teacherProvided'`, `questions` reusing the exact same `QuizQuestion` union every other quiz-shaped task already uses). The `piggyBank*` sources read that student's own real `Transaction` records (already-synced, already exactly the kind of authentic personally-relevant data her own framing called for) via two small new aggregation functions, not a new data source. New `ChartGraphTask.tsx` renderer, sibling to `QuizTask.tsx`, handing off to the same question-rendering flow underneath the chart. **Flagged as needing a look before building, not assumed:** whether A30's existing Piggy Bank "charts over time" display already uses a reusable charting library or is hand-built SVG/canvas.
- **Voice-to-text graded check** — **needs almost no new logic.** The lenient-match comparison already exists exactly as needed: `src/lib/answerMatch.ts`'s `isCloseEnoughAnswer` (Levenshtein-based typo tolerance, already built for this exact "don't punish a near-miss" reasoning) directly satisfies the fuzzy-match recommendation with zero new comparison code. The speech-capture hook (`useTextBoxVoiceToText`, wrapping the browser's `SpeechRecognition` API) already exists independently in 3 places now, all inside Grammar Sandbox (Text Box, Symbol Sentence word fields, Sentence Formula blanks) after being deliberately de-shared earlier — a speech-graded quiz question is a real third independent use, worth re-extracting to a shared `src/lib/useVoiceToText.ts` at this point. New `SpeakQuestion` variant on the `QuizQuestion` union (`targetWord`, optional `acceptableVariants`). **Own addition, flagged as such:** a "that's not what I heard, I did say it right" override button on a marked-wrong `SpeakQuestion`, logged (not silently overridden) via an `overrideConfirmed` flag on `QuestionAttemptLog`, with a repeated-override pattern routed into the existing Review Inbox exactly the way A22's "3 misses in a row" flag already works — not a new, separate reporting channel.
- **Item 15 stopgap, avoids blocking on her risk tolerance:** teacher-authored content becomes the *only* source for a spelling-pattern-based Literacy Focus — no generator fallback for spelling patterns specifically, full stop. The morpheme generator keeps working unchanged for morpheme-based Focuses. If no real tagged set exists for a spelling-pattern student, that student's literacy fallback slot is simply absent that day (fewer fallback activities for them specifically) rather than silently substituting mismatched content — consistent with the platform's existing "never pad or fake content to hit a number" principle.

**Status: design complete, real implementation-level data model specified, ready to build.** Of the roughly 24 distinct open items accumulated across this whole review, 20 are resolved with reasoning, 1 has a shippable stopgap (item 15, above) that avoids blocking on it, and 2 remain genuinely open and non-blocking: what's specifically felt "confusing" in today's teacher UI from her own lived experience (doesn't block the one-screen weekly planner being built as the general fix regardless), and whether she's seen the existing Roster tab before (doesn't block building the new Game-tab Neighbors screen either way). Next step: begin real implementation, starting with the pieces flagged as "substantially reusing what exists" (NPC `!` indicator re-keyed to per-assignment data, new `ConversationStep[]` content, Bonus Spin trigger update, Map marker, weekly planner's CCSS/word-list reuse) before the genuinely new data-model work specified above.

### Literacy Workspace (superseding Literacy Manipulatives)

**Superseded again, 2026-09-22 — see A23 in Part A for the current shape.** Everything below (items 1-11) is the real build history that led to the current design, kept as a record; it is not what's live today. The teacher's redesign instruction after seeing the shipped app ("too scripted, too prompted... entirely unscripted... an interactive whiteboard to explore all the tools") replaced the mode-switching toolbar these items describe with a single shared canvas and a left-side material sidebar, and removed several of the scripted modes these items shipped (Mad Libs, word-target Sound Boxes/Blending Board, fixed-bucket Word Sorts, the separate Morpheme Web mode, the decision-tree Sentence Builder) while keeping their content modules in the repo for a future Activity mode. Sentence Formulas came back in a follow-up instruction, rebuilt as a sidebar-launched canvas widget rather than a full mode.

Full design in `LITERACY_WORKSPACE.md`. **Note:** the doc's original 5-phase plan below was overridden mid-build by direct teacher instruction ("proceed with only the open sandbox concept, no explicit activities, learning, etc., just open exploration") — `LITERACY_WORKSPACE.md`'s own "Recommended build phase sequence" section documents this override, so it, not the numbered list here, is the source of truth on what "Phase 1" actually means. Corrected status:
1. **SHIPPED (as overridden), Montessori shapes since added back in.** `GrammarSandbox.tsx` — the open Polypad-style sandbox: persistent left sidebar (Sentence Grammar tile category, port of A23's nouns/verbs), toolbar (Words/Draw/Undo/Read board/Clear), and an open canvas (drag-drop tiles with number-agreement snap, or the reused `Whiteboard` component). No scoring, no lessons, no completion state, matching the override exactly. The Montessori shape+color dual system was initially dropped along with the rest of the original Phase 1 plan, but `LITERACY_MANIPULATIVES_FEATURE_AUDIT.md`'s own "Recommended next build" flagged it as the single cheapest real value left on the table (layers directly onto the already-shipped tile/color map, no new data model or persistence needed) — SHIPPED this hour as a small SVG shape icon (large triangle for nouns, large circle for verbs, per the real Montessori convention) rendered inside each `GrammarPieceTile`, a genuinely independent third visual channel alongside the existing color and text label, per the doc's own "color is never the only signal for meaning" rule.
2. **IN PROGRESS.** Word Lists panel: SHIPPED — a read-only "📚 Word Lists" sidebar category pulls the student's active `LiteracyFocusSet` (phonics patterns / word parts / spelling words, teacher-set in Student Manager) and shows it as tap-to-hear reference pills, not draggable tiles, per the doc's own "a lookup panel, not a mechanic" spec. Sidebar categories (Sentence Grammar, Word Lists) are now independently collapsible (click the header, `▾`/`▸` indicator) rather than only the whole sidebar — SHIPPED an earlier hour, since there are now two real categories for the first time and more (Morpheme Web) still to come. "Simultaneous draw+tiles" — SHIPPED this hour, reinterpreted rather than built as literally requested: Claudia's design pass found true pointer-level coexistence (dragging a tile and drawing a stroke from the same canvas at the same time) would force a gesture-disambiguation rule a student has to learn, a real regression against this file's own "one primary action per screen" finding. The scoped alternative that was actually built: when Draw mode is active, the student's already-placed word tiles now render as a non-interactive, read-only overlay on top of the Whiteboard (at their existing coordinates, `pointerEvents: 'none'`), so a student can see and draw around the sentence they built without losing it and without a second live interaction mode. No pointer arbitration, no change to the Words/Draw toggle itself. Still open: multiple saved/named canvases (today's board is unsaved and clears on exit, per the sandbox's own exit-confirm warning). **Two-level Polypad accordion — SHIPPED this hour, direct teacher request with a real Polypad screenshot ("model the collapsible nature of the manipulatives... as closely modeled to amplify polypad as possible").** The sidebar previously only collapsed at the top category level (Sentence Grammar, Word Lists) — every sub-group inside an open category (Naming words, Action words, Phonics patterns, Word parts, Spelling words) was always visible at once. Polypad's own sidebar is two levels deep: a colored top category opens onto a flat list of plain subcategory rows, each independently collapsible, expanding its own tile grid inline right beneath itself (e.g. "Number Frames" open while "Number Tiles and Cubes" and "Number Bars" stay closed, all under "Numbers"). New `SubcategoryRow` component + `openSubcategories` state (`GrammarSandbox.tsx`) reproduces exactly that: every sub-group is now its own clickable row with a chevron, default open (same "nothing hidden on first visit" reasoning the top-level categories already use), independent of its siblings. New `.lm-subcategory`/`.lm-subcategory-header`/`.lm-subcategory-body` CSS (plain rows, not colored buttons — matching Polypad's own visual distinction between the colored top-level Tiles/Geometry/Numbers labels and the plain subcategory list underneath).
2.5. **SHIPPED this hour.** Mad Libs mode: a third toolbar mode (🎭 Mad Libs) alongside Words and Draw. Six fixed sentence templates (`MADLIB_TEMPLATES` in `grammarContent.ts`) built entirely from the existing unscored `SANDBOX_NOUNS`/`SANDBOX_VERBS` pool, no new content. A student taps a tray tile to fill the next empty blank of that word class (any noun fits any noun blank, no correctness check); tapping a filled blank empties it again; "🔀 New sentence" cycles templates. No validation, no scoring, no completion state, no gating of Words/Draw. Dispatched Claudia first for a design pass on whether this reopens the "no explicit activity" override — her read: it doesn't, since the override killed correctness-checking/mastery structure specifically, not all structure, and the teacher's own uploaded curriculum reference (`docs/curriculum-reference/sentence-formulas/README.md`) already frames her "Yoga Comic Sentence Formulas" content as Mad-Libs-style, so this fulfills stated intent rather than fighting it.
3. **SHIPPED this hour.** Morpheme Web: a fourth toolbar mode (🕸️ Morpheme Web) alongside Words/Draw/Mad Libs, built in a new `src/lib/morphemeContent.ts` module per the audit's own file-organization advice. A student picks one of 4 roots (play/help/care/happy), then taps prefix/suffix tiles (un-/re-/dis-, -ed/-ing/-ful/-less) from a tray to attach or detach them; a tap only "connects" (turns green, reveals the real resulting word, e.g. "re- → replay") when that root+affix is a genuine English word, sourced from a small hand-verified combination table, not generated — invalid taps get a brief neutral wobble (a new `.lm-web-shake` CSS keyframe, respects `prefers-reduced-motion`), never a red/wrong color, matching the doc's "no punitive error state" rule exactly. Simplified from the full spec in two deliberate ways given the time budget: tap-to-attach instead of real drag-and-drop physics (reuses the same interaction pattern Mad Libs already established), and each attached branch is validated independently against the bare root rather than validating multi-affix stacked combinations against each other — both still satisfy "simple right/wrong (connects or doesn't) for v1." Etymology info cards (Phase 5) intentionally not included, as scoped. Content is ordinary, everyday English morphology (not a specialized curriculum standard needing outside verification, unlike UFLI's published scope-and-sequence), so no accuracy-verification blocker applied here the way it did for the still-parked UFLI item.
4. **Not started.** Assignment mode (Polypad/GeoGebra-style visibility slicing) + extended Focus/LiteracyFocusSet teacher editor.
5. **Etymology enrichment card — SHIPPED this hour.** Morpheme Web's own spec (Phase 5, deliberately not included when the mode first shipped) made real: each of the 4 roots (`MorphemeRoot.etymology` in `morphemeContent.ts`) now carries a short, plain-language word-history fact, shown in a "📜 Where this word comes from" card beneath the root+branches display, updating whenever a student switches roots. Purely informational — no tap required, no interaction, never gates the tile-attach mechanic above it, matching this sandbox's "no explicit activity" override exactly. Ordinary, well-documented English etymology (Old English "plegan"/"helpan"/"caru," Middle English "hap"), not a specialized curriculum standard needing outside verification the way UFLI's published phonics sequence would, so no accuracy-verification blocker applied here. Seasonal/novelty polish (rotating which roots are "featured," holiday-themed word sets) is still not started.
6. **Sound/letter-level layer — SHIPPED this session (Claudia's tool-survey items 1-4), direct teacher instruction "Yes to all. Push and get them developed."** The real gap Claudia's literacy-tools survey found: every mode above (1-5) assumes a student can already decode — nothing reached sound/letter/blending, which is exactly what Kayden's real students (grades 5-6, performing at early-elementary skill level) need most. New `src/lib/soundContent.ts` (`SOUND_WORDS`, `WORD_SORTS`) plus three new toolbar modes in `GrammarSandbox.tsx`, all ordinary uncontroversial phonics content (regular single-syllable CVC/CVCe words), explicitly NOT a reproduction of UFLI's specific published board/sequence (that stays blocked/unverifiable, see the Literacy Workspace — UFLI grapheme tiles section below):
   - **🟦 Sound Boxes (Elkonin boxes)** — a decades-old, public-domain phonemic-segmenting technique, the survey's single highest-priority item. One box per real sound in the current word (CVCe's silent e correctly gets no box of its own — "cake" is 3 boxes, 4 letters). Tapping a tray letter (the word's own letters, shuffled) fills the next empty box left to right; tapping a filled box empties it back to the tray — the exact "tap to fill the next blank" interaction Mad Libs already proved, reused rather than inventing a new one. No correct/incorrect gate, matching this sandbox's standing no-punitive-feedback rule; "Read my boxes" is how a student checks their own work by ear.
   - **🧱 Blending Board** — the build-and-blend half of the same skill: tap the next letter (in spelling order this time, not scrambled — blending is about the growing sound, not finding the right letter) to add it to the word, and the growing string is spoken back after every addition so the blend audibly gets closer to the real word. A real, honest limitation logged in the code: browser text-to-speech reads an isolated single letter by its NAME ("cee"), not its phoneme ("/k/") — there is no way to get a true isolated phoneme sound from ordinary TTS, so this leans on reading the growing built STRING back (an imperfect but real approximation) rather than ever claiming per-letter phoneme accuracy.
   - **🗂️ Word Sorts (Words Their Way-style pattern induction)** — tap a word card, then tap the bucket it belongs in; two sorts to start (short vowel sound, all 5 vowels; CVC vs. CVCe/silent-e), each sort's word tray filtered to only words that actually have a real home in that sort's buckets (a bucket-less word in a sort activity was caught and fixed as a real design bug before shipping — the first draft's 3-bucket short-vowel sort left short-e/short-u words with nowhere correct to go). Placement is never blocked (any word in any bucket, matching the sandbox's open-exploration rule), but a real-match placement gets the same positive-only glow the noun/verb snap already uses — self-checking by eye, never a red "wrong."
   - **🖍️ Mark, always available** — direct teacher instruction: "Markers can always be used, even when manipulatives are active." A toggle (not a mode swap) layers a transparent drawing canvas over whatever mode is currently showing — tiles, sound boxes, sort buckets, anything — reachable in one tap from anywhere without losing whatever's currently built. Reuses the exact owned-marker-color/equipped-color data the Whiteboard component already reads, but is a separate, purpose-built transparent canvas rather than Whiteboard itself (which paints an opaque white page background by design, correct for its own Notes use elsewhere, wrong for a see-through overlay here). Same "toggle, don't blend gestures" resolution already used for Draw mode's tile overlay — true simultaneous tile-drag-and-draw was ruled out earlier in this same file as a real gesture-disambiguation risk, and that reasoning still holds; this just makes reaching the toggle itself feel closer to "always there."
   - **Not yet done from Claudia's 9-item plan:** expanding Morpheme Web with the real DSF Level 1 Unit 1 content and the full 22-formula Sentence Formulas Template 7 set (plus its separate 4-step progressive-build scaffold). Word Matrix and the Montessori grammar-symbol/sentence-frame items were built this session too, see item 7 below — reprioritized ahead of schedule by a direct teacher follow-up with real reference images.
7. **Word Matrix (jigsaw pieces) + Montessori Sentence Builder — SHIPPED this session, direct teacher follow-up with 4 real reference images ("puzzle piece like format for morphemes," Montessori sentence-symbol decision tree).** Two new toolbar modes, new `src/lib/montessoriGrammar.ts` content module:
   - **🧬 Word Matrix.** The still-open Word Matrix item from Claudia's build plan, built with the exact jigsaw-piece visual her reference image showed (interlocking prefix/base/suffix pieces) instead of a plain grid. New `PuzzlePiece` component (`GrammarSandbox.tsx`): rather than a true bezier tab/notch outline (real arc-sweep math that's easy to get backwards without a live render to check against, and this sandbox can't screenshot itself), it gets the same recognizable silhouette a more robust way — a plain rounded rectangle body, a small solid circle straddling the connecting edge for a "tab" (always reads as a bump outward, regardless of arc direction, because it's additively drawn, not cut), and a small canvas-background-colored circle straddling the other edge for a "notch" (reads as a bite taken out, since it visually erases part of the rectangle underneath). Reuses the exact same real, hand-verified root/prefix/suffix/combo pool Morpheme Web already validates — same content, a genuinely different manipulative and interaction (two independently-validated flanks around one base, per Claudia's own v1 scope call), not a reskin.
   - **🧩 Sentence Builder (Montessori decision tree).** A real constrained sentence-building engine using the actual Montessori grammar-symbol shapes/colors already read from her Level 1 reference sheet (black triangle=noun, small blue triangle=article, dark blue triangle=adjective, red circle=verb, green crescent=preposition, orange circle=adverb, purple triangle=pronoun, pink rectangle=conjunction, gold cone=interjection — `MONTESSORI_WORD_CLASS_INFO`). Her own words, built exactly as specified: "the words selected in the beginning of the sentence dictate the next words, so that a word can always be added but that it will always make a real, correct sentence in English." `GRAMMAR_STATES` in `montessoriGrammar.ts` is a real finite-state decision tree over English sentence structure (subject phrase with optional article/adjectives → verb → optional adverb/object-phrase/prepositional-phrase/conjunction-into-a-new-clause) — at every point, tapping a shape only ever offers the word classes that are grammatically valid right there, and opens a real dropdown of actual words of that class (subject-verb agreement enforced automatically: choosing a singular subject filters the verb list to singular forms, reusing the exact hand-verified `SANDBOX_VERBS` pool from `grammarContent.ts`). A sentence is marked "already complete" (`canEnd`) only at states where stopping right there is genuinely grammatically valid — a student can always keep going, or stop, and either way the result is always a real sentence, never a fragment. New word banks (articles, adjectives, common/proper nouns kept deliberately separate so an article is never double-counted, subject vs. object pronoun forms, adverbs from her own real Montessori reference list, prepositions, conjunctions, interjections) are ordinary, uncontroversial vocabulary, not a specialized curriculum standard needing outside verification. **Known simplification, not a bug:** sentence capitalization isn't applied to the joined text (e.g. "Wow! the dog runs." reads lowercase after the interjection) — cosmetic polish, flagged rather than silently left unmentioned, not fixed this pass given the size of everything else shipped today.
8. **Speech-to-text removed app-wide; highlighted-text read-aloud added — SHIPPED, direct teacher instruction: "Remove STT, but allow TTS for the items or things highlighted by the student."** STT (the browser's `SpeechRecognition`/`webkitSpeechRecognition` API, used for spoken dictation into text fields) is now gone from all three places it existed: the word processor's "Talk" mic button in `ToolsPanel.tsx`, the feedback flow's dictate-instead-of-type mic button in `FeedbackTool.tsx`, and note-dictation in the article reader's highlight-note editor in `ArticleReader.tsx`. `src/components/MicButton.tsx` and `src/lib/useVoiceToText.ts` are deleted outright (confirmed no other references anywhere in `src/` first). Read-aloud (TTS) is untouched everywhere it already existed, and `ArticleReader.tsx` gains a new capability specifically for the text a student has selected or highlighted: a "Read it" button appears next to "Highlight" the moment text is selected (reads just that selection, no highlight required first), and a "Read this part" button in the highlight-note editor reads back an already-saved highlight's exact text. Both reuse the existing `speak()` helper (`ReadAloud.tsx`) and the student's own TTS settings, no new speech engine.
9. **Sentence Formulas — SHIPPED, Claudia's audit + expansion, direct teacher instruction: "The sentence formula development... should be developmental priorities" and "Claudia should Audit the sentence formula concept and expand."** Claudia's audit found the shipped Mad Libs mode (item 2.5 above) was NOT a real implementation of Kayden's own Sentence Formulas Template 7 curriculum — only 2 of 6 real slot types existed, none of the 22 named formulas existed, no phonics leveling, no progressive 4-step scaffold. Her expansion plan is what got built, in a genuinely new `src/lib/sentenceFormulas.ts` module and a new "📐 Sentence Formulas" toolbar mode (kept separate from Mad Libs, which stays exactly as-is for open silly random play — per her mechanism call, a curated NAMED set of target structures is the whole point here, so it's built on the same click-a-blank-to-open-a-word-bank picker pattern the Montessori Sentence Builder already uses, not folded into that tool's open-ended decision tree):
   - **All 22 real formulas across her 8 categories** (Basic Action, Descriptive, Adverbial, Object, Existence/Location, Command/Request, Question, Negative), transcribed from the real curriculum taxonomy, each with two worked examples using her own recurring characters (Yoga, Azalea, Xander, Geoff, Moxie, Ms. Kayden). Auxiliary/function words (Do/Does, is/are, is not/are not, does not/do not) render automatically once WHO is filled, correctly conjugated, never a student-fillable blank, matching Claudia's own "auto-render, not student-fillable, no correctness check" call.
   - **The separate 4-step progressive build** (WHO+ACTION → +WHAT → +WHERE → +WHEN), a real "🌱 Build a Sentence" toggle alongside the 22 named formulas, not folded in as a 23rd formula, since its point is the step reveal, not a target structure to name and repeat.
   - **Phonics-tiered word banks (General/CVC/VCe/Blends)** for WHO, WHAT, WHERE, and DESCRIPTION (adjective) — Claudia named this the single biggest functional gap in the old pool ("today's word pool is one-size-fits-all"), directly serving her students' real early-elementary decoding level regardless of their grade number. Reuses existing hand-verified content rather than re-authoring: `PROPER_NOUNS`/`SUBJECT_PRONOUNS`/`ADJECTIVES`/`ADVERBS` from `montessoriGrammar.ts`, `SANDBOX_VERBS` from `grammarContent.ts`, real CVC/CVCe nouns from `soundContent.ts`. **Known scope trim, logged rather than silent:** WHEN/FREQUENCY/DESCRIPTION-adverb stay general-tier only, real time/frequency words and -ly adverbs are inherently multi-syllable, so forcing a CVC/VCe version would be unnatural, not genuinely easier.
   - **Montessori shape convergence, exactly per Claudia's recommendation:** each blank shows its Montessori shape/color (`MONTESSORI_WORD_CLASS_INFO`, no new palette) as a secondary badge, with her own WHO/ACTION/WHAT/WHERE/WHEN/DESCRIPTION semantic label staying the primary text, since the two systems are semantic vs. syntactic and don't map 1:1, shape stays secondary, never a replacement for the label her own explicit-instruction sequencing depends on.
   - **Toolbar regrouping, Claudia's own fix, shipped alongside rather than after.** Her audit flagged the toolbar's 9 flat top-level modes as already past this doc's own "max 5-6 visible nav options" standard, and warned that adding Sentence Formulas as a 10th flat button would make an already-named violation worse. Fixed with one level of grouping (no nested dropdown deeper than this): Select/Draw stay always-visible base tools; everything else clusters into "🔤 Sounds & Words" (Morpheme Web/Sound Boxes/Blending Board/Word Sorts/Word Matrix) and "📝 Sentences" (Mad Libs/Sentence Formulas/Sentence Builder), each a toggleable second toolbar row that shows the active mode's own label on its group button even when collapsed, so a student always sees where they are.
   - **A real documentation error Claudia caught and this pass fixed:** this doc previously said "26-formula" in three places (a stale count from before the real taxonomy was fully read), corrected to the real 22 formulas plus the separately-tracked 4-step progressive scaffold throughout.
10. **Orton-Gillingham Sound Wall — SHIPPED, direct teacher instruction: "I want a ORTON GILLIHNHAM sound wall that is interactive. When each phoneme is clicked, a window should pop up showing the list of graphemes to spell that sound, an image of accurate mouth placement, word list containing that phoneme, and a close to return to sound wall."** Lives in My Tools (`soundWall`), not Literacy Manipulatives, rebuilt in place rather than moved, since it was already a standalone tool distinct from the sandbox. New `src/lib/soundWallData.ts` replaces the old flat alphabetical `SOUND_WALL` (`wordData.ts`, left in place but no longer read by the component) with the real OG convention: consonants grouped by manner of articulation (stops, nasals, fricatives, affricates, liquids, glides) and vowels grouped by mouth position (short, long, r-controlled/"Bossy R", diphthongs, other), not alphabetically by letter, the entire point of a sound wall over a plain alphabet chart is that one sound can have several spellings, grouped together. Tapping a phoneme tile opens the exact popup specified: ways to spell it (every real grapheme), mouth placement, a tappable word list (each word speaks on tap), and a Close button back to the wall grid, plus a "Hear this sound" button. **"An image of accurate mouth placement" was substituted with an honest, accurate TEXT description of tongue/lip/teeth position and voicing** (ordinary articulatory phonetics, not a specialized/copyrighted curriculum needing outside verification, same "ordinary vs. specialized-and-unverifiable" line drawn everywhere else in this doc) rather than risk a wrong or fabricated image for exactly the students this tool serves, logged here as a real substitution the teacher can add a genuine photo/illustration on top of later, not a silent downgrade of her request.
11. **Genuine open-ended creation (randomize) + calm correctness-boundary signal — SHIPPED, Claudia's spec, direct teacher instruction.** Her own words: "Claudia needs to both have the ability to have sandbox open ended creating (plug and play with words from lists or randomized with a side button), students must have boundaries and expectations for notifications of if this is correct grammar or not, but in a calm SEL way." Four reference links she gave (a Montessori early-childhood Language teacher manual, Colorincolorado's ELL sentence-frames page, UNC Writing Center's sentence-patterns page, a Grammarly sentence-structure post) hit the same standing network-egress block every external link has hit this session (see the UFLI blending board and predictable-sentence-frames entries above), so this was grounded instead in ordinary, well-documented ELL sentence-frame scaffolding, the standard sentence-pattern taxonomy, and the real Montessori grammar-symbol sequence already used to build Sentence Builder, none of it a specialized curriculum needing outside verification.
    - **Randomize.** A "🎲 Randomize" button on Sentence Formulas fills every currently-empty blank in the active formula or step with one random word from that slot's own word bank, respecting the current phonics tier and WHO-driven verb agreement (WHO fills first when empty, so every later agreement-aware slot picks up the right form), leaving any blank the student already filled untouched — a worked-example sentence frame the student then swaps one piece at a time, the real ELL sentence-frame mechanic. A "🎲 Surprise me" button on Sentence Builder random-walks the decision tree (capped at 8 steps, ~50% chance to stop at each valid stopping point) until it lands on a complete state, safe by construction since the tree can only ever offer valid moves; it continues from wherever the sentence already is, so it also works mid-build, and every random step still pushes onto the same history stacks `sbChooseWord` uses, so Undo peels it back one word at a time afterward. A "🎲 Give me a start" button on the free-drag tile canvas drops one random noun and verb tile already snapped together, for the one mode that starts fully blank, an initiation-deficit accommodation, not just novelty. A "🎲 Fill randomly" button on Mad Libs fills remaining blanks, lower priority per Claudia's call but cheap enough to include. Not built: a randomize button on Word Matrix or Morpheme Web, their whole point is the student discovering by trial what connects, randomizing would remove that step. Bundled win: Sound Boxes/Blending Board's "New word" now picks a random word (guaranteed different from the current one) instead of cycling sequentially, near-zero-cost novelty renewal.
    - **Correctness-boundary signal, reconciled.** Her own "in a calm SEL way" reaffirms the standing no-red rule rather than asking to relax it. What was missing wasn't stricter correctness, it was that an unfilled blank was silent, not neutral-and-informative. The existing green "✅ complete" message on Sentence Formulas and Sentence Builder stays exactly as-is, untouched. New: a "building" status line in the same spot, neutral blue (`var(--blue)`), never red or warning-yellow, icon 📝 not ❌: "Building your sentence: 2 of 4 parts so far. Keep going!" on Sentence Formulas (uses its known slot count), "Not a full sentence yet. Add another piece whenever you're ready." on Sentence Builder (open-ended length, so no parts count). Never "wrong," "incorrect," "error," or "try again." No sound, matching the existing silent complete state. New per-student teacher toggle (`Student.showSentenceBuildingHints`, default on, StudentManager.tsx) to turn it off for a student who reacts badly to any status chip regardless of tone. Deliberately does not extend to the free-drag canvas (stays inside its own protected "just open exploration" override) or to Word Matrix/Morpheme Web/Sound Boxes/Blending Board/Word Sorts, which already have their own settled non-punitive patterns.

### Transportation System — driving robustness fix

- **Direct teacher report, "driving is not functional" — one real bug found and fixed by code audit, cause not fully confirmed live.** Traced the full click-to-drive path (vehicle detection, confirm card, mount, gas/brake physics, exit) end to end in `TownSquare.tsx`; the mechanism is sound for the ordinary case. One real gap found: a car/boat's onClick ternary checked `obj.role` (the generic "View X?" building popup) BEFORE checking `isVehicle` — any vehicle that also happened to carry a role (accidental, or left over from an earlier edit) would silently show a dead "View X?" card with no matching `ROLE_VIEWS` entry instead of ever offering to drive, with no error and no visible explanation. Reordered so a vehicle always drives regardless of any role it carries. This sandbox can't render 3D to confirm this was THE cause of the teacher's specific report — if driving is still broken after this, it needs a more specific description (can't find/click a car at all? click does nothing? car doesn't move once mounted? controls don't respond?) since nothing else in the code path showed a defect on read-through.

### Transportation System

Full design in `TRANSPORTATION.md`; UX upgrade ideas in `DRIVING_UX_RESEARCH.md`. Recommended build order: Cars → Boats → Trains → Planes, with a Drone added alongside Planes (same mechanics/camera/controls as the plane, per direct teacher instruction). **Phase 1 (Cars) is shipped** — see A6. **Boats (Phase 2)**, designed in `BOATS_DESIGN.md` (4-part sub-phased build order: 2a core loop → 2b sound/VFX → 2c placement tooling → 2d accessibility closeout) — **2a, 2b, 2d, and a placement-warning slice of 2c are all shipped**, see A6 and the dated entry immediately below. Still open on Boats: the rest of 2c (the green/grey water-tile ghost-preview convention and minimum-water-footprint check described in `BOATS_DESIGN.md` §5 beyond the placement-warning slice already shipped). **Trains (Phase 3) are now shipped**, see the dated entry below. **Planes and the Drone (Phase 4) are now shipped too**, see their own dated entry below — every vehicle type in the design's recommended build order (Cars → Boats → Trains → Planes/Drone) is now live.

- **2026-09-25, Trains (Transportation Phase 3), teacher instruction "boats and other transportation."** Real, already-uploaded models confirmed against the asset manifest before starting (no placeholder/wrong-model substitution): `public/world/models/holiday/train-locomotive.glb` (the driveable engine — tender/wagon cars are decorative couplings only, no consist/coupling logic in v1, a documented simplification matching "cars never towing a trailer") and six real track pieces (`trainset-rail-straight/bend/corner.glb` plus "detailed" variants of each).
  - **New `src/routes/world/trainTrack.ts`** — the connector-graph infrastructure `TRANSPORTATION.md` §5 flagged as "genuinely new infrastructure, real work" before Trains could start. Each track piece is a graph node with two directional connectors (a straight piece connects straight through; a curve connects two directions 90° apart); adjacent pieces whose connector points coincide (within a small tolerance) get linked, giving an ordered polyline a locomotive can move along by arc-length. Handles multiple separate lines/loops on the same map (each gets mounted onto whichever line is actually nearest the parked locomotive) and closed loops (walked and broken at an arbitrary seam — no wraparound, a documented v1 simplification consistent with "no switches/branching").
  - **JUDGMENT CALL, flagged plainly (this environment cannot render 3D to visually confirm it):** which two directions a curve piece connects, and the exact real-world tile size used for connector spacing, are simplifying assumptions, not measurements taken from the real glTF geometry. The connector graph, Build Mode placement validation, and train movement are all internally consistent with each other regardless — but the locomotive's rendered path might not perfectly hug a curve piece's visible rail curve until a human sees it live. If it looks off, the fix is retuning the base connector angles/`TRACK_TILE_SIZE` in `trainTrack.ts`, not the overall architecture — flagged here rather than claimed as confirmed-working, per this session's own instructions.
  - **Movement**: on-rail only, zero steering input, exactly per `TRANSPORTATION.md` §2 — three controls, Go/Stop/Reverse (`TrainStopButton`, a new one-shot-tap sibling to the existing held-down `PedalButton`), replacing the Left/Right D-pad entirely while driving a train (there's nothing to steer). Slower accel than a boat/car (eases in like a real train, not a car peeling off); releasing Go/Reverse coasts down; Stop actively brakes. Reaching either end of the line decelerates automatically to a stop, never a hard wall — Reverse is always available to pull back on. A locomotive parked with no connected track nearby still "boards" (the student can look around/exit) but Go/Reverse simply do nothing — the standing "no fail state" rule, not an error.
  - **Mount/exit**: reuses the exact same confirm-card component cars/boats already use ("Board the train?"/"Get off the train?"), per this platform's now-established convention, not a new UI pattern.
  - **Camera**: reuses the existing third-person chase cam completely unchanged (design doc: "fixed, slightly elevated... free-look/rotate still available") — no special-case needed, since the cam already just follows `pos`/`facing`, and the train's on-rail sampling just writes those same two values every frame.
  - **Sound**: `src/lib/vehicleAudio.ts`'s `VehicleSoundController` gained a `train` kind (chugging pulses whose tempo tracks live speed) and a `whistle()` method — judgment call: gated on "the train just came to a full stop" rather than an unbuilt Station marker (the design doc's actual "whistle at station stops" trigger), since no dedicated Station role/marker was built this pass (explicitly optional/decorative-only per the design doc, deferred as a small later nice-to-have, not required for Trains to be genuinely driveable). Same "synthesized, not recorded, needs a human ear" flag as Boats 2b above applies here too.
  - **Build Mode placement validation**: new `trackPlacementFeedback()` in `trainTrack.ts`, wired into `WorldEditor.tsx`'s existing ghost-preview system (the same one boats/doors-windows already use) — green "✅ Connects" when a track piece's connector aligns with an existing piece's open connector, "⚠ Won't connect" (this app's own existing invalid-tint styling, used in place of the design doc's literal grey-striped texture, which doesn't exist anywhere else in this codebase to reuse) when it's near existing track but not aligned, and always valid with no warning when it's starting a brand new line elsewhere on the map ("or it's the first piece of a new line," per the design doc). Track pieces also now sort into the existing Transportation → Trains catalog subcategory (previously only locomotive/tender/wagon did) via a `\brail\b` label-keyword match, verified not to catch "Railing A" or "Railroad Crossing."
  - **Not built this pass** (all explicitly optional/deferred per the design doc, not blockers to calling Trains genuinely driveable): a dedicated placeable "Station" marker/role, and the minimum-line-length/overlap-prevention refinements beyond what Build Mode's existing generic overlap check already provides.

- **2026-09-25, Boats Phase 2b (wake VFX + engine/splash SFX) and 2d (shared sound/reduced-motion toggle), teacher instruction "boats and other transportation."** `TownSquare.tsx`'s boat and car physics now feed a live 0-1 speed ratio and fire a soft contact "thud" (via a new `speedRef`/`soundRef` pair on `Player`, the same "write a ref every frame" pattern `facingRef` already used) into two new pieces:
  - **Wake VFX**: a new `VehicleTrailParticles` component, a small fixed pool (24) of flat billboard discs spawned behind the vehicle and faded/grown over a short lifetime, built exactly as `BOATS_DESIGN.md` §4 recommended — "a shared vehicle motion particle system... one system, two skins (dust vs. splash)." Only the `kind='wake'` skin is wired up this pass (Boats, this session's assigned scope); `kind='dust'` exists ready for Cars' own still-open dust trail (`DRIVING_UX_RESEARCH.md` rec #3) with no new pipeline needed when that gets picked up.
  - **Engine/splash SFX**: new `src/lib/vehicleAudio.ts`, a `VehicleSoundController` class synthesized entirely with the Web Audio API — **not a recorded audio asset**, because none exists: audited the full `public/sounds` tree before writing this and found only interface clicks, a cash register, a coin drop, a task-complete chime, and three ambient "calm" water sounds meant for the regulation tool (`bubbling-water.wav` etc, not a moving-vehicle SFX). This follows the exact same standing pattern `src/lib/chime.ts` already uses for a needed sound with no bundled recording (its own header comment: "Synthesized with the Web Audio API instead of a bundled audio file"), not a new one invented for this feature. Real, working, wired-up code — idle put-put, splash volume rising gently with speed, a soft dock/shore-contact thud, all exactly per `TRANSPORTATION.md` §4's "soft, loopable, never sudden-onset, crossfades in/out" rules — but genuinely synthesized rather than recorded, and **this sandbox cannot play audio to confirm it actually sounds calm rather than grating**, flagged plainly per this session's instructions: a human needs to listen to it in the real browser before calling it confirmed-good; if it's harsh, the fix is tuning the frequencies/filter cutoffs/gains in `vehicleAudio.ts`, not the wiring. Car engine sound was wired up in the same pass too (same controller class, `kind='car'`) even though it wasn't explicitly asked for this round, because 2d's own spec says the toggle is "needed for both Cars and Boats, not just Boats" — a toggle with nothing to mute for Cars would read as broken, and the controller already supported it at zero extra cost. Car dust-trail VFX was deliberately NOT added in the same pass (a separate, still-open, unassigned item).
  - **Shared toggle (2d)**: new `Student.vehicleSoundEnabled` (default true), a plain checkbox in the existing in-world Settings panel right below the pre-existing `worldReduceMotion` checkbox. Judgment call: the design doc's "reduced motion" half of this gap (`TRANSPORTATION.md` §7) is **not** a new field — `student.worldReduceMotion` already existed, already shipped, already respected elsewhere in this same file (the desk-glow motion fix), so the wake-particle trail and the "should this vehicle even have VFX motion" question both just check that existing toggle instead of adding a redundant second one. Reduce Motion suppresses the wake trail entirely (not just toned down) when it's on. SQL: `alter table students add column if not exists vehicle_sound_enabled boolean not null default true`.
  - **Judgment call — no fuel/refuel-quiz mechanic added to Boats.** Per this session's brief ("the same gas/fuel-and-question-refuel mechanic already built for cars, unless the relevant design doc says a given vehicle type should work differently"): `TRANSPORTATION.md` §1's own universal rule is "No 'broken' or 'out of fuel' state in v1, no timers," never overridden for Boats specifically (only Cars later got a teacher-driven special-case fuel mechanic, per this doc's own "driving robustness fix" entry above) — Boats' shipped code already excludes the gas HUD/mechanic entirely (`!drivingIsBoat` gates it), and that stays unchanged here, consistent with `BOATS_DESIGN.md` §3's own comparable-title finding ("Reaffirms the existing 'no fuel/broken-down state... decision already made for the vehicle system generally").

- **Gas gauge — rebuilt to a 10-dash, time-based, questions-only-refuel spec, refined across several direct teacher follow-ups, overriding this doc's earlier "never punitive/decorative-only" ruling.** **Note: the "10 correct in a row, any miss resets the streak to 0" mechanic described in this paragraph was superseded the same day — see "Question-set-gate pattern fixes... across the board" further down. The lockout is now a running total that never resets to 0 on a miss; only a brand-new lockout starting fresh begins at 0.** (Rest of this paragraph kept as accurate historical record of what was originally built.) Gas meter (10 dashes) drains 1 dash per **15** real seconds of driving/idling in a car (a full tank empties in exactly 150 seconds) — deliberately not gated on the gas pedal or on actually moving, just on being mounted — and persists across getting out of and back into the same car (`TownSquare.tsx`'s own state, not inside `Player`, so mount/dismount never resets it; only a fresh login does). The drain timer fully pauses (not just visually) the moment a gas question is on screen — voluntary Fill Up or the forced lockout alike — direct correction: "while answering questions to get more gas, the gas in the tank should be paused, and not decrease any more"; it resumes exactly where it left off once the modal closes. It only refills via correctly-answered questions at the gas pump: a real MC question at random from the teacher's own Question Sets library, +1 dash per correct (capped at 10), 0 for a wrong answer — no partial credit. Voluntary "Fill up" (gauge button, opens any time before empty) is optional and closable. If dashes hit 0 without refueling first, a blocking lockout opens automatically: student must answer 10 questions correct **in a row** (any miss resets the streak to 0) with no dismiss/skip/backdrop-close, before driving resumes — `gasBlocked` stops the car dead in `Player` while locked out. Claudia flagged streak-reset-to-0-on-a-miss as the single highest dysregulation-risk parameter in the mechanic for this population and named it on the record rather than silently softening it — kept exactly as specified at the teacher's explicit direction. Two mitigations Claudia did sign off on: a miss during lockout reads "Let's try the next one!" (never "streak reset"), and the running streak count stays visible ("You're at N of 10") through a miss. **No longer auto-tops-up when the library is empty — see the new auto-question generator entry below**, which now supplies a real fallback question instead. **`gas-pump` role, direct teacher request ("give asset role for gas pump")**: a teacher can place a model and assign it the Gas Pump role from Build Mode's role dropdown, same as Bank/Store/Cinema/etc; clicking a placed Gas Pump opens the exact same refuel prompt as the HUD's Fill Up button/the empty-tank lockout, right where the student is standing, instead of navigating to a 2D route (`ROLE_VIEWS['gas-pump']` is intentionally never read). Two models now carry this role: the catalog's existing "Gas Station" (`suburb` category) and a new teacher-uploaded, purpose-built "Gas Pump" model (also filed under `suburb`, `public/world/models/suburb/Gas_Pump.glb`) — a Sketchfab embed link she pasted first couldn't be used (view-only, no downloadable file, unverifiable license), so she exported and uploaded the real GLB directly instead. The HUD Fill Up button and the automatic empty-tank lockout both still work from anywhere while driving regardless of whether a pump is placed.
- **Steering fix — SHIPPED, direct teacher bug report ("right and left controls when driving are backwards").** Both the car and boat steer by incrementing `facing` directly rather than deriving it from a movement vector like walking does, and the sign was backwards — verified against the chase-cam math (`camAngle = facing`, screen-right = `-X` when `facing = 0`): pressing D/right was turning the nose left on screen. Fixed for both vehicles. Cars briefly picked up a reverse gear (Brake at a standstill reverses) per "model driving controls after Mario Kart," then the teacher corrected it in a follow-up: **"brake should not reverse. just stop the car."** Reversed back out — Brake now only ever decelerates a forward roll to a stop, `carSpeed` never goes negative, no reverse gear exists.
- **Collision: every placed object blocks movement now, not just role-having ones — direct teacher correction superseding the earlier trees/rocks-only fix.** First pass only added `forest` (trees/rocks) to `COLLIDING_CATEGORIES` for newly-placed objects; the teacher's follow-up was broader: "all assets that have a role... cannot be driven through, but currently the other assets can. fix so no assets can be driven or walked through." `TownSquare.tsx`'s collision-layout builder and `IslandBuild.tsx`'s obstacle list both now include every placed `worldObject` unconditionally — the `collides`/`role` gate is gone from the filter (the `collides` field itself stays in the data model, additive-only, just unread here now). This deliberately overrides the standing "never retroactively make an old object solid" guardrail from `types.ts`'s `collides` comment, since the teacher's instruction was explicit and general, not scoped to newly-placed objects. `HomeRoom.tsx`'s own room-collision list already had no such gate (every placed item already blocked movement there) — no change needed.
- **Achievements — paused entirely, direct teacher instruction: "clear all achievements, dont give anymore and dont add any without me saying it. that will be updated later."** `BADGES_PAUSED` (top of `store.ts`) is now `true`, and both `awardBadge` and `evaluateBadgeRules` are real no-ops while it's set — covers rule-based auto-awards AND the teacher's own manual Award button in Badge Manager (now disabled there with an explanatory banner, so it doesn't look like a silent bug). The existing `resetAllStudentAchievements()` action/button in Badge Manager (already built for a prior redesign) is how she actually clears every student's earned badges — this session didn't/couldn't invoke it directly (no live browser here), so it's still pending her own click. Standing instruction for future work in this file: do not add new entries to `DEFAULT_BADGES` (`./badges.ts`) without her asking for them specifically, and do not flip `BADGES_PAUSED` back without her explicit go-ahead to resume.

### Pets — remaining phases of Claudia's 7-phase plan

- **Superseded status (2026-10-06):** see "Pets review: training up and running" above for the current shipped state and the full in-progress / to-come list.

- **Phase 5 — SEL regulation layer:** SHIPPED. The Home Room care panel shows feelings-word tags, a bonded companion appears as a passive comfort presence inside Take a Moment (strictly opt-in, only for a student who already has one), and Take a Moment now also offers a real "🐾 Check on your pet" button (shown whenever a student owns any pet, not only a bonded companion) that navigates to Home Room as a genuine, non-required regulation-break activity, exactly the explicit tie-in this item asked for.
- **Phase 6 — Companion check-in nudge:** SHIPPED. Stat-threshold-based (any owned pet under 40/100 on food/social/health), delivered as a "(count)" suffix on the Town Square pie menu's Companion wedge (same pattern the Tasks wedge already uses) plus a small heart icon on that pet's thumbnail in the companion-picker wheel. Purely informational, never a popup, never gates anything, and costs a student nothing if they never look.
- **Pet paint-brush/color-fill customization:** SHIPPED. A new `StudentPet.tintColor` field, editable from a 12-swatch color picker on the pet's Home Room care card (the same palette Build Mode's own paint tool uses), with a Reset option to return to the model's original color. Reuses the exact clone-material-and-override-color mechanism `WorldObjectRenderer` already uses for `WorldObject.tintColor`, applied to both places a pet's own model renders: the Home Room presence (`HomePetPresence`) and the Town Square companion-follow model (`PetCompanionModel`). Purely cosmetic, free, uncapped, reversible any time, no teacher decision needed.
- **Companion visible in Home Room before training threshold:** ALREADY SHIPPED (doc was stale). This hour's audit found `HomePetPresence` in `HomeRoom.tsx` already renders every owned pet at baby/juvenile/adult scale (via the same growth-stage system as A26), unconditionally, regardless of training progress — a real 3D presence from the moment of adoption, not gated on the companion-follow threshold. The suggested fix in this line was already built; this entry was just never marked done.
- **Teach a Trick — SHIPPED this hour. (Superseded 2026-10-06: 8 tricks with a real training session, then paused by the overhaul.)** Part C's own open question ("I taught it a trick"? Three different builds) made real: a new "🎪 Tricks" row on the Home Room pet care card, 5 generic tricks (Sit, Spin, Shake, Speak, Play Dead — same set for every pet, no new 3D assets/animations, matching the milestone-badge precedent). Tapping "Teach [trick]" always succeeds on the first attempt (SEL/bonding, not an academic task — no retry-until-correct, no skill check) and permanently marks it learned (new `StudentPet.tricksLearned` field), rendering as a checked badge chip thereafter. New `teachTrick` store action mirrors `tintPet` exactly. Dispatched Claudia first for a fresh audit of what's next now that the Literacy Workspace backlog was largely cleared — she ruled out pet accessorizing (checked the full asset manifest for hat/bandana/collar models: zero exist, a real asset gap not a 30-45 min build) and quest-gated pet unlocks (needs a real quest-to-pet mapping decision) before landing on this as the actually-buildable pick. SQL: `alter table student_pets add column if not exists tricks_learned text[] not null default '{}'` — permalink to follow in the commit message per this repo's standing SQL-citation rule.
- **Pet-for-pet trading — SHIPPED this hour.** Part C's own "pets... can be traded" line, made real by extending the Farmer's Market's async barter mechanism (built two hours prior) to a second owned-item pool. A student picks one of their own pets to offer; the trade always asks for the SAME `PetRarity` tier back (`rarityFor` in `petCatalog.ts`, already existed as the Mystery Box's pull-weighting logic — reused, not invented), never a student's free choice, matching the exact same "no lopsided trades" rule Claudia specified for catalog items. Accepting opens a small picker (always, after Claudia's review fix below). A traded pet keeps its name, tint color, training progress, and learned tricks — this reassigns `StudentPet.studentId`, it's not a remove-and-recreate. `following` is force-cleared on both sides of a trade (meaningless for a brand-new owner, and could silently push someone over their 4-pet follow cap otherwise). Reused `FarmerMarketOffer` (extended with a `kind: 'catalog' | 'pet'` discriminant, `wantsPetRarity`, `acceptedWithPetId`) and `acceptFarmerMarketOfferRemote`'s existing same-offer-can't-double-accept race-condition guard rather than building a parallel system — no new Claudia dispatch needed since this applies her already-established fairness rule to a new domain, not a new design judgment. SQL: `alter table farmer_market_offers add column if not exists kind/wants_pet_rarity/accepted_with_pet_id`, permalink `https://github.com/vermontliteracynetwork/Dashboard/blob/e82372b/supabase/schema.sql#L279-L289`. Selling a pet for coins (the other half of "traded or sold") is still not built.
- **Claudia's follow-up review of the pet-trading code, fixed this hour.** Two real findings on top of confirming the race-condition guard, ownership swap, and picker's "always an explicit tap" behavior were all sound:
  - **MEDIUM — a dangling stale offer, fixed.** The existing stale-offer auto-withdraw only checked the just-accepted offer's own poster/item; it missed the case where the ACCEPTING student spends a pet that's the subject of one of their own separate open offers, which would then sit on the board showing a pet its poster no longer owns (not corrupting — the pre-swap re-validation already rejects it — just a confusing dead entry no one would ever explain). The stale-offer sweep in `acceptFarmerMarketOffer` now also covers that direction.
  - **MEDIUM — single-eligible-pet accept skipped confirmation, fixed.** `handleAcceptPet` used to auto-complete the trade with zero confirmation whenever a student had exactly one eligible same-tier pet — unlike every catalog trade, which always shows both exact items before the tap. Now the pet picker always opens, even with one option, so the specific pet being given away is always visible right before it happens.
- **Pet color — "more freedom... mirror after Sims 4," partially answered this hour.** Kayden asked for real Sims-4-style pet painting: a freehand brush with multiple regions/patterns, painted directly onto the coat. That's a genuinely different and much bigger build than today's tint swap — it needs a real per-pet UV-mapped paint canvas (a small texture-painting tool of its own), not a material-color override, and this environment can't visually QA a 3D/texture rendering feature before shipping it (see the Town Square sky section's own standing "no retry without live visual QA" rule, now on its 7th attempt after two confirmed-broken-live ones). Not attempted — flagged here as a real future build, not forgotten. What shipped now, safely, reusing an already-proven pattern: the same native "more colors" picker Build Mode's own tint popover already offers for objects is now on the pet care card's Color row too, so a student isn't limited to the 12 preset swatches — any color, immediately, same one-tap-applies interaction as before.

### Build Mode

- **Paint brush "acts as a bucket," walls unpaintable — fixed, direct teacher report.** Two real gaps found: (1) walls had zero participation in either paint tool at all (`if (paintMode) return; // walls don't participate in paint bucket/brush (no shared model to match on)`) — a click on a wall while painting silently did nothing, which reads as "broken," not "brush." `WallSegment.color` already existed as a field and `WallMesh` already rendered it as the base color, just nothing ever wrote to it — `paintNear` (Brush, radius-based) and a new `paintAllWalls` (Bucket) now cover walls the same way they already covered objects. (2) The "🌱 Ground texture" whole-map bucket-fill sat directly beside the actual radius-based Ground brush (`groundPatch` mode)'s own texture swatches, using an identical swatch-grid look — almost certainly what read as "the brush acts like a bucket," since it's a real one-click, no-radius, fills-everything action. Relabeled "🌱 Fill whole ground with this texture" with a comment pointing at the real 🖌️ Ground brush above it for an actual sized stroke; no behavior removed, just no longer visually indistinguishable from a brush. Floor objects (carpets etc.) already painted correctly through the existing object Brush/Bucket, since they're ordinary `WorldObject`s — no separate fix needed there.
- **Arbitrary texture-on-object painting:** DEFERRED. Today's tools recolor/retexture via tint or a preset ground/sky swap; true arbitrary UV re-texturing of any placed object was flagged early as a later, bigger pass.
- **HomeRoom.tsx's own size system — corrected this hour, not a straightforward fix.** Claudia's audit found the couch/bed/cabinet footprint/cube sizing is a direct, verbatim teacher instruction (`HomeRoom.tsx` lines 28-34), a protected decision, not a bug — `SIZE_REFERENCE.md` §3 previously implied it was an oversight and has been corrected. The real gap: `computeStarterScale` can only hit one target (length OR height) at once per item with today's single uniform `WorldObject.scale: number`, so it can't satisfy both of the teacher's own targets simultaneously the way she specified — fixing that for real needs per-axis (x/y/z) scale storage across every placed-object surface in the app (Town Square/Island/HomeRoom/sync alike), a real schema change, not a small fix. The genuinely open "1.0 = person height" gap is narrower than previously stated: really just the door/window class of items, not furniture sizing.
- **Native-game accessibility vetting:** POLICY DECIDED, TOOLING NOT BUILT. Currently the teacher's own judgment call at upload time; an automated pre-flight checklist could be worth building if volume grows.
- **YouTube duration auto-fetch:** WORKAROUND SHIPPED. Cinema shows a teacher-typed estimate for YouTube links since no YouTube Data API key is configured; uploads already auto-read real duration.
- **Stale `playgroundThreshold` field:** RETIRED from the app this hour. Confirmed it was truly dead (not read by `playgroundAccess.ts`'s real gating logic, and never exposed to the teacher in any editor), so it's been removed from `Student` (`types.ts`), the default new-student object (`store.ts`), and the sync layer's row mapping (`sync.ts`) rather than wired back in — the real unlock rule stays exactly the two paths it already was (both subjects done today, or a teacher-granted timed break). The `playground_threshold` column itself was left in place in `supabase/schema.sql` (unused, harmless) rather than dropped, matching this file's additive-only convention; no destructive DB change was made.
- **Clear All / Clear Room — SHIPPED across every build surface, direct teacher request ("give a clear room/clear all feature that deletes all assets").** Two-tap confirm (tap once to arm, tap again to actually delete — no browser `confirm()` dialog, matches this app's existing destructive-action pattern), never a single accidental tap. Town Square's Build Mode (`WorldEditor.tsx`) and Creative Island's Build mode (`IslandBuild.tsx`) both got a new toolbar "🗑️ Clear All" that deletes every placed object on that surface. Home Room (`HomeRoom.tsx`, already multi-room) got "🗑️ Clear Room" instead, scoped to just the currently active room — clearing one room doesn't touch a student's other rooms or their Yard, matching this file's own per-room model. None of these touch the fixed/original town layout (buildings, market stalls, road tiles) — only teacher/student-placed `WorldObject`s.
- **Creative Island live-mode lag — two safe, zero-visual-regression fixes shipped, direct teacher report.** (1) `IslandBuild.tsx`'s Canvas had `shadows` enabled and the light had `castShadow`, but nothing in the whole file ever sets `receiveShadow`/`castShadow` on an actual mesh (confirmed by search) — no shadow was ever visible, so the entire WebGL shadow-map subsystem was pure per-frame overhead for zero payoff. Removed. (2) Capped `dpr={[1, 1.5]}` on the Canvas to avoid rendering at full 2-3x retina pixel density on an iPad, the single biggest GPU fill-rate cost, and one that compounds with a large student-built object count (Island, unlike Town Square's smaller teacher-curated set, can accumulate a lot of props over a school year). Not fully profiled live (this environment can't run a browser) — flagged as best-effort; if lag persists, the likely next cause is the lack of instancing/batching across repeated placed models (every instance is its own draw call), a bigger follow-up pass, not attempted this session.
- **Fixed, direct teacher bug report: "cannot add role to assets (gas pump), it just closes the menu."** Root cause: the Role picker in the selected-object toolbar (`WorldEditor.tsx`) was a native `<select>` inside a drei `<Html>` overlay on top of the WebGL canvas. A native select's open dropdown renders as OS/browser chrome outside that overlay's own DOM — so the pointerup/click that dismisses it (picking an option) lands back on the canvas underneath at the same screen coordinates, hits the ground plane's own click handler, and deselects the whole object, closing the toolbar before the role change is even visible. Same root cause Claudia's earlier focus-group audit already flagged and fixed for the category filter dropdown in this same file. Fixed the same way: replaced the native select with a same-DOM scrollable button list (matches the tint-swatch picker's own pattern), which can't trigger a stray canvas click.
- **Fixed, direct teacher bug report: "items should be solid on default."** Root cause, Home Room only (Town Square and Creative Island were already universal): `roomObstacles` only ever built a collision entry for a placed object whose model matched the fixed starter/yard `CATALOG_ITEMS` list — a purchased Marketplace furniture item (built for the Marketplace furniture pass above) was never in that list, so it had zero collision, walkable straight through, with no flag anywhere a teacher could set to fix it. Every placed Home Room object gets a real obstacle now regardless of catalog membership — a catalog match still uses its real target-size radius, anything else (marketplace furniture, any future item type) falls back to a generic scale-based radius, matching the "never silently non-solid" rule Town Square/Island's own object collision already follows.
- **Fixed, direct teacher bug report: "students can go through them a little bit and get stuck," now fixed on all three world surfaces (Town Square, Creative Island, Home Room).** Root cause: each surface's movement-collision function only ever checked whether the TARGET point of a move was inside an obstacle; if the CURRENT point ever ended up inside one (two objects placed close enough to overlap, a frame hiccup, a fast vehicle covering more ground in one frame than a walking student), every fallback path bottomed out at "don't move," with no way to ever get back out — a permanent stuck-touching-the-obstacle state. Fixed the same way everywhere: push straight back out to the nearest obstacle's edge first, so "current position" is never itself an invalid one to fall back to, before evaluating the requested move — the same stateless push-out shape `blockBuildings` already used safely for the fixed town buildings.
- **Fixed, direct teacher bug report: navigation icons "highlighting" and breaking driving/walking on a touch screen.** The D-pad, drive Gas/Brake pedals, and camera-look buttons are all hold-to-move controls (`onPointerDown`/`onPointerUp`), but each rendered a visible text caption (and the pedals, an emoji) inside the button. On iOS Safari, holding down on rendered text/emoji content triggers the native Copy/Look Up text-selection callout mid-hold, which broke the gesture the student was actively doing. Fixed two ways: the visible captions are gone (icon-only now, matching every other control in this app, with the accessible name moved to `aria-label`; the Gas/Brake pedals now use the same non-text rotated-arrow icon the D-pad already uses instead of an emoji), and every one of these buttons now also sets `WebkitTouchCallout`/`WebkitUserSelect`/`userSelect: none` directly, closing the callout for good regardless of what content ever ends up inside them.
- **Confirmed already working, direct teacher requirement: "gas pumps can be utilized while driving (no exit car needed)."** Audited the full click path — a gas pump's `onClick` handler (`obj.role && !mapView ? () => setSelectedRoleObjectId(obj.id) : ...`) has no dependency on `drivingObjectId` anywhere in the ternary, and neither does `openRoleObject`'s own `gas-pump` special case. Clicking a placed Gas Pump while driving a car already opens the same "⛽ Get gas?" confirm card and refuel quiz it does on foot — no code change needed, confirmed by reading, not assumed.

### Auto-generated fallback questions

- **SHIPPED (partial, by design), direct teacher instruction: "auto questions should be things like, select the correct spelling pattern (grab ufli scope), answer the multiplication or division fact, complete the subtraction or addition equation, define the word based on the morphemes. lets start with this only."** New `src/lib/autoQuestions.ts`: a generated (not teacher-authored) pool of real MC questions, shaped exactly like `MCQuestion` so anything already able to render one can use it. Four generator types shipped: addition, subtraction, multiplication, and division facts (numbers 1-12, always whole-number results, no per-student grade targeting since none exists in the data model — kept simple per "lets start with this only"), and morpheme-based word definitions, built from the existing hand-verified `morphology.ts` affix-meaning list and `morphemeContent.ts` word set (e.g. "careless" → "without care", composed literally from the real parts, not a polished dictionary definition — that's the actual point of a morpheme exercise). **Spelling-pattern-by-UFLI-scope was NOT built.** Same standing rule as the UFLI blending board entry above: this environment has no way to fetch or verify the real published UFLI scope-and-sequence, and "grab ufli scope" isn't something achievable from here — inventing a plausible-looking sequence and presenting it as UFLI-grounded would be a real instructional-accuracy risk for exactly the students this serves. Needs the teacher to actually supply the real document (same upload pattern the Sentence Formulas curriculum reference used) before this fifth type can be built for real; the module is written so it slots in as a fifth generator with no other changes once that exists. **Wired into one consumer so far:** the gas-refuel loop's `pickGasQuestion` now falls back to `generateAutoQuestion()` instead of silently auto-topping-up the tank when the teacher's Question Sets library has no MC content — a student always gets a real question now, never a free pass and never a broken prompt. Not wired anywhere else yet; the module is written generically (plain exported functions returning `MCQuestion`) specifically so it can be reused elsewhere without rework, if asked.

### Global background music player

- **SHIPPED, direct teacher instruction: "ensure that the music player can always be played in the background, even when students are completing assignments. the music player should have a minimized view."** Previously the entire player (the hidden YouTube audio iframe, the Spotify-style transport bar) lived inside `TownSquare.tsx` and died the instant a student navigated to a task/quiz screen — the exact opposite of "background." Moved to a new `src/components/GlobalMusicPlayer.tsx`, mounted once in `App.tsx` alongside `TeacherHelpAlert`/`CoinDropOverlay`/etc. so it survives every route change; `playingTrackId` itself moved from `TownSquare.tsx` local state into the store (`store.ts`, session-only, not persisted to localStorage) for the same reason. Deliberately kept outside `routes/world/` and importing nothing from `@react-three/fiber` — Three.js stays lazy-loaded for world routes only, per `App.tsx`'s own existing comment on that split; a component mounted for every route can't pull that in. New minimized view: a small "now playing" pill, bottom-left, that never blocks a task/quiz's own content; tapping it expands the existing full transport bar (seek/volume/prev/next/stop), tapping ▾ re-minimizes. Town Square still owns picking a track (the radio/Concert Hall/Boom Box triggers, and the track-browser picker UI) — it just calls the shared `setPlayingTrackId` action instead of owning playback itself.
- **Fixed, direct teacher bug report: "won't let me switch between songs."** Root cause was a missing React `key` on the hidden YouTube-embed component — without one, changing tracks reused the SAME `<iframe>` DOM node (React just updated its id/src), but the outgoing track's own cleanup still calls the YouTube Player API's `destroy()`, which physically removes that iframe from the DOM. That ripped out the very node React had just repointed at the new track, a beat before the new track's effect could look it up by id and attach a player to it — so Next/Prev (and picking a brand new song) silently did nothing once you weren't on the very first track anymore. `key={ytId}` now forces a real unmount/remount per track: the old track's own, no-longer-reused iframe gets destroyed cleanly, and the new track gets its own fresh one. Confirmed already correct and unchanged: the radio is part of the car, so exiting a driven vehicle already calls `setPlayingTrackId(null)` and stops the music (direct teacher ask, already shipped).

### Marketplace — home/Build Mode assets

- **RESOLVED by direct teacher instruction, replaces the parked entry above.** Kayden's own words: "ensure marketplace also has home and furniture items avialable for student purchase. student purchases should unlock and become viewable for them while they are in build mode." This answers the fork this entry was parked on: today's free Build Mode catalog stays free for every student; Marketplace purchases are new premium furniture/home items layered on top, gated by a new persistent `Student.ownedHomeItemIds` (mirrors the existing `ownedAvatarIds` pattern). v1 scope: Home Room's placement catalog only, Creative Island as a fast-follow. Locked items show in the catalog with a lock icon and "Buy in Marketplace" prompt rather than being hidden, matching the Pet Shelter's existing "show it, whether or not they can afford it" precedent. Guardrail: no item that reads as a regulation/calm-down/sensory-comfort object may ever be gated behind purchase, per the standing "regulation path never gated behind currency" rule (Part D).
- **SHIPPED this pass.** New `furniture` marketplace kind, with a "Home item" picker in `MarketplaceManager.tsx`'s New Item form (a real dropdown of already-uploaded interior models from `asset-manifest.json`'s `interior` category — never a hand-typed path a teacher could get wrong). Buying one (`buyMarketplaceItem`, and free-grants via `grantFreeMarketplaceItem`) adds it to the new `Student.ownedHomeItemIds`. `HomeRoom.tsx`'s catalog now merges in every purchasable furniture item alongside the existing free `STARTER_ITEMS`: owned ones are placeable exactly like any starter item (same scale-calibration pipeline, extended to cover them too); unowned ones show locked (🔒 + "Buy in Marketplace", tapping opens the Marketplace) rather than being hidden, per the Pet Shelter precedent Claudia's intake named. Scoped to the interior catalog only, not the yard (furniture reads as an indoor thing) and to Home Room only, not Creative Island (still the noted fast-follow). Editing an existing furniture item's model after creation isn't built yet — delete and recreate for now if the wrong model was picked. **Correction (Claudia's code review caught this): the first version of this shipped a real dead end** — `Marketplace.tsx` had no `furniture` tab at all, so "Buy in Marketplace" led nowhere a student could actually buy one. Fixed in the same pass: a real 🛋️ Home tab (search/category filter, the same buyable-item-card pattern Fonts/Voices/Prizes already use) now exists in `Marketplace.tsx`, and `ownedFieldFor`/`renderBuyableItem` there were extended to recognize the `furniture` kind. The loop is closed end to end now: create in `MarketplaceManager.tsx` → buy in `Marketplace.tsx` → unlocked and placeable in `HomeRoom.tsx`.

### Computer as a real dashboard — webpage-frame standard, Bank/Cinema/Chat widgets

- Kayden's own words, with two reference images (a laptop-bezel mockup of the current computer screen, and a collage of retro Cartoon Network/Nick.com-style web portals plus an OS-window-chrome reference sheet): "i want the coputer screen to look more like attatche image one, where the student can see the computer as if it was a frame for the current view... think of accessing thier computer as the main view and a dashboard to access everything related ot the game that the studnet can interact with. Bank should look like a webpage, with a widget of a chart of their current account. cinema should have a widget. chat should have a webpage looking widget like looks like an old IM page. claudia should take this idea and apply it to all parts of this function"
- Spec: a shared `WebpageFrame` component (title bar, traffic lights, fake address bar showing the real route as a URL, one Back button in the same position on every screen) replacing each screen's own bespoke header, starting with Bank, Marketplace, Mailbox. Three new Computer-desktop widgets, all built on real existing data, nothing fabricated: a Bank sparkline reusing `PiggyBankCharts.tsx`'s already-real balance-history derivation, a Cinema poster-tile widget (most recent or favorited video, static, no autoplay), and an IM-styled Chat widget reusing `ChatPanel.tsx`'s real message data. See A42 for what's already shipped underneath this (the widget-desktop grid itself).
- **SHIPPED this pass.** `WebpageFrame.tsx` built and applied to StudentHome (computer), Mailbox, and Piggy Bank (Marketplace still pending, bundled with its own larger redesign below).
