Bank widget now shows a real sparkline (`bankDayTotals`, same per-day running-balance derivation `PiggyBankCharts.tsx` uses, just unlabeled and small) plus the live balance — no fabricated history. New Cinema widget shows the real most-recently-added video's real thumbnail (teacher's own `coverImageUrl` or the real YouTube auto-thumbnail), static, no autoplay, "Now Showing." Chat widget shows the real last message in the student's own thread, styled as a small IM-window nested inside the widget-card (`.widget-webpage`/`.widget-card-tall` in `index.css`) — no fabricated unread count, since no read/unread field exists on `ChatMessage` yet.
- **Claudia's code review, fixed same pass:** (1) the Back button had an inline `minHeight: 32` overriding `.btn-sm`'s standard 44px touch target on every `WebpageFrame` screen — removed, the class default applies everywhere now. (2) Collapsing Mailbox/PiggyBank/Marketplace to one Back button lost the old "walked up to this building in Town Square, tap Back, land back in Town Square" path (they all defaulted to the Computer instead) — `TownSquare.tsx`'s own navigation into these screens now passes `state: { from: 'town' }`, which `WebpageFrame` reads to send Back to Town Square instead of the Computer when that's really where the student came from; a caller with its own explicit `backTo` (the Computer's own "Close, back to Town Square") is unaffected. (3) The Bank widget's balance text (`--success` green on white) and the Chat widget's sender label (`--blue` on a light blue bubble) both failed WCAG AA contrast — darkened to `#16803c` and `#1e4fa8`. **SHIPPED, laptop bezel — now on every `WebpageFrame` screen, direct teacher follow-up ("do the laptop-bezel redesign for all webpage screens").** `.laptop-frame`/`.laptop-screen`/`.laptop-deck` in `index.css` originally wrapped only the Computer screen (StudentHome.tsx) in a real physical laptop mockup (dark bezel + keyboard deck) matching the teacher's reference image. Extended this pass to the other three `WebpageFrame` call sites — `PiggyBank.tsx`, `Mailbox.tsx`, `Marketplace.tsx` — reusing the exact same three CSS classes rather than inventing new styling, so all four screens read identically. Decorative only, no interaction; `.laptop-screen`'s `overflow: hidden` only clips content bleeding past its rounded corners (it has no fixed/max height, so it never actually truncates a long page like Marketplace's or Piggy Bank's scrollable history), and every fixed-position modal/FAB still escapes the bezel exactly as before, since neither wrapper div sets a `transform`. **Still not done (at the time):** Cinema/Chat/Arcade/Gallery/Farmers Market/Silly Quizzes don't use `WebpageFrame` at all (different chrome patterns each), so they're outside this mechanism unless asked for specifically.
- **SHIPPED (2026-09-25), the rest of the rollout — direct teacher instruction: "computer dashboard continuation."** All six screens the note above flagged as outside the mechanism now use `WebpageFrame` and the `.laptop-frame`/`.laptop-screen`/`.laptop-deck` bezel the same way Mailbox/PiggyBank/Marketplace already did — Cinema, Arcade, Gallery, and Farmer's Market (`Cinema.tsx`/`Arcade.tsx`/`Gallery.tsx`/`FarmersMarket.tsx`), each keeping its own real content untouched (Cinema's curtain-photo backdrop and percentage-positioned video overlay, Arcade's Scratch shelf/embed, Gallery's tap-to-enlarge grid, Farmer's Market's trade board) underneath the new shared chrome. Back-button routing is unregressed: Cinema/Arcade/Farmer's Market are real Town Square roles (`ROLE_VIEWS` in `townLayout.ts`), so `WebpageFrame`'s existing from-town/from-Computer default (the `state: { from: 'town' }` mechanism above) covers them automatically with no extra code, exactly like Mailbox/PiggyBank did; Gallery and Silly Quizzes have no Town Square building at all (verified in `PlaygroundView.tsx` — they're only ever reached from the Playground), so both pass an explicit `backTo="/student/playground/view"` instead of falling through to the Computer default, preserving what their old three-button header already did. Silly Quizzes (`SillyQuizzes.tsx`) got the same treatment. **Chat is the one adaptation, not a straight port:** `ChatPanel.tsx` is a shared teacher/student overlay modal opened from six different call sites (Student Home, Help overlay, the student/teacher chat alerts, Teacher Home, Student Live View), not a routed page, so wrapping it in the full `.laptop-frame` bezel or a router-based Back button would have broken every one of those callers' `onClose` contracts. Instead, `WebpageFrame` gained an optional `onBack` prop — when passed, the Back button calls it directly instead of navigating, leaving every routed screen's existing `backTo` behavior untouched. `ChatPanel` now opens with `<WebpageFrame url={chatUrl} onBack={onClose} backLabel="✕ Close" />` in place of its old plain dark title bar, with the fake address bar itself doing double duty as the "who is this thread with" identifier (`chat/kayden` for a teacher's view of a student's thread, `chat/teacher` for the student's own side) — a real "looks like an old IM page" touch, per the teacher's original wording, that a plain title bar didn't have. **Re-checked against the original spec paragraph for anything not marked SHIPPED, per direct instruction:** all three Computer-desktop widgets it asked for (Bank sparkline, Cinema poster-tile, IM-styled Chat preview) were already marked SHIPPED above and independently verified still wired to real data in `StudentHome.tsx` (`bankDayTotals`, `latestVideoCover`, `lastChatMessage`) — there was nothing outstanding to build here; the "two widgets" framing in that follow-up request didn't match what's actually still open in this section.

### Marketplace — teacher earn-source editing + Amazon-style browse

- Kayden's own words: "marketplace needs to evolve. as a teacher, in game tab, i need full ability to edit all prices, avaibility windows and resons for earning, etc. for everyting marketplace related. the design needs to be more real onlline shoping inspired, have a search, etc. marketplace needs to be like amazon, still having a website frame that wil be standard and applied to all 'webpages'"
- Spec: new `earnMethods?: EarnMethod[]` field on `MarketplaceItem` ('purchase' | 'daily-spin' | 'bonus-spin' | 'quest-reward' | 'farmers-market'), edited as a chip multi-select per item in `MarketplaceManager.tsx`. Existing items default to all wheel-eligible values on migration (matches today's real unfiltered-pool behavior, no regression); new items default to purchase-only until the teacher opts them into a wheel. `dailySpin.ts`'s segment generator gets a real filter on this field for the first time (today it draws from the entire item list with no eligibility check at all). Characters and Emotes get brought into the same editable price/availability table the other item kinds already have (today Characters have zero per-item editing, Emotes only has a price override). Student-facing redesign: real search bar, keep the existing category chips, oversized "Add to cart" buttons, wrapped in the same `WebpageFrame` standard as the rest of the Computer. **Settled, won't build:** star ratings, written reviews, "customers also bought" cross-sell — real Amazon patterns that either open an unsupervised peer-content surface or add manipulative purchase pressure this population doesn't need; left out deliberately, not by oversight.
- **SHIPPED this pass, the data-model half + Characters pricing + a real search.** `MarketplaceItem.earnMethods` (types.ts) plus `EARN_METHOD_LABELS`/`earnMethodsFor`/`itemEarnsVia` (`marketplaceSeed.ts`) are real now — `MarketplaceManager.tsx`'s `EarnMethodChips` lets a teacher toggle any item between Purchase only/Daily Spin/Bonus Spin/Quest reward/Farmer's Market per item, and `dailySpin.ts`'s segment pool now actually filters on it (`itemEarnsVia(it, 'daily-spin')`) instead of drawing from every eligible item unconditionally like it always has. Characters got the exact override-map treatment Emotes already had (`avatarPriceOverrides`/`setAvatarPriceOverride`, new `CharacterPricesSettings` panel) — a real bug was caught and fixed while wiring this in: `buyAvatar` in `store.ts` was charging the raw hardcoded catalog price even before this pass, ignoring any override entirely (unlike `buyEmote`, which already read from its override map) — now fixed, so a teacher-set Character price is the price actually charged, not just the price shown. A real search box (Characters/Emotes/Power-Ups tabs, which had none; Fonts/Voices/Prizes already had their own via `ItemFilterBar`) and `WebpageFrame` now wrap `Marketplace.tsx`. **Not yet done (at the time):** the full Amazon-style layout pass (sidebar categories, bigger product-grid treatment) — today's category-chip tab row was kept as-is per the "it already works, don't reinvent it" guidance, so what shipped is closer to "search added + one shared frame" than a visual redesign; that's still open if the teacher wants the fuller Amazon look.
- **SHIPPED (2026-09-25), the full Amazon-style layout pass — direct teacher instruction: "marketplace" (continuing the request above).** A real left-hand sidebar (`.shop-sidebar` in `index.css`) replaces the old chip-tab row as the category nav (Characters/Emotes/Writing/Whiteboard/Voices/Prizes/Power-Ups/Home/Pets/My Stuff/Receipts) — same `tab` state and click handlers as before, just rendered as a vertical nav instead of wrapped pills, and it collapses to a horizontal row under 720px so it still works at phone width. Every product tile (`.shop-product-grid`/`.shop-product-card`, new classes) moved off the old loose flex-wrap onto a real CSS grid (`repeat(auto-fill, minmax(168px, 1fr))`) with uniform card sizing — the several places that used to hardcode a one-off card width per item kind (140/150/156px for characters/emotes/powerups) now all size from the same grid track, a genuinely more "product grid" look than before. The buy button on every tile is now a full-width, oversized "🛒 Add to Cart · $price" button (`.shop-add-to-cart-btn`, 48px tall) in place of the old small rounded price chip, with the same green "in cart"/disabled-when-unaffordable states as before, still wrapped in the same `WebpageFrame` + laptop bezel from the earlier pass. The existing search box and every `ItemFilterBar` category/tag dropdown (Fonts/Voices/Prizes/Furniture) are untouched — nothing about the working filter functionality was removed, only the outer chrome around it. **Deliberately scoped to Marketplace only, not the shared classes:** `PetShelter.tsx` and `PetJournal.tsx` also render with `.shop-item-grid`/`.shop-item-card`/`.shop-price-chip`/`.shop-panel`/`.shop-header`/`.shop-ribbon`/`.shop-balance-chip`/`.shop-item-icon-frame` — all of those original classes were left completely untouched, and `Marketplace.tsx` was switched onto brand-new sibling classes (`shop-product-grid`/`shop-product-card`/`shop-add-to-cart-btn`/`shop-sidebar`/`shop-main`/`shop-layout`) instead of redefining the shared ones, specifically so this redesign can't leak into the Pet system's screens, which other agents own. **Reaffirmed, not revisited:** star ratings, written reviews, and "customers also bought" cross-sell stay out, per the "Settled, won't build" line above — nothing in this pass added any comparative or peer-visible signal to a product tile.

### Bakery Match — question-gated match-3 mini-game (Town Square)

*(Renamed from "Bakery" to "Bakery Match" 2026-09-25, per the round-2 build entry below — "Bakery" is the building/location, "Bakery Match" is the game students play inside it.)*

- **SHIPPED (2026-09-25), new building/location — direct teacher instruction: "i want to do a candy crush like game but bakery theme with a bakery building asset i will upload," plus a same-day follow-up adding a post-game treat wheel with a pet-feeding tie-in.** The first of the "mini games embedded with question set logic" concept (car/gas, cafe were her illustrative examples, not literal asks) to actually ship, chosen because match-3 was the concrete, scoped one. A new `WorldObjectRole` (`'bakery'`) lets a teacher assign any placed object the same way she already assigns Bank/Cinema/Gas Pump — `ROLE_VIEWS.bakery = '/student/bakery'` routes through the existing generic `openRoleObject` navigation in `TownSquare.tsx`, so no special-cased click handling was needed (unlike Gas Pump's in-world modal). Her own uploaded 3D building (`Bakery_by_Poly_by_Google_-...glb`, same "Poly by Google" CC-BY source already used for Cinema/Windmill/etc. elsewhere in this world) is in `public/world/models/buildings/bakery.glb`, auto-discovered by the Asset Directory's build-time manifest scan — she places it and assigns the role herself in Build Mode, same as any other building.
- **Match-3 engine, `src/lib/matchThree.ts` — a from-scratch, clean-room implementation** (grid init that avoids pre-existing runs, horizontal/vertical run-of-3+ detection, adjacent-swap validation, gravity/cascade clear-and-refill, valid-move check with a silent reshuffle instead of a "stuck board" dead end) written against the publicly-documented match-3 mechanics, not copied from any reference project — keeps this platform's standing rule (GPL/AGPL reference-only, nothing copied in) clean with zero licensing string attached.
- **`src/routes/student/BakeryMatch3.tsx` — free-play, not an assigned Task.** Question-sourcing follows the Gas Pump pattern exactly (`TownSquare.tsx`'s `gasQuestionPool`), not Platformer's Task-mastery-queue pattern: a random MC question pulled from the whole teacher Question Sets library, falling back to `generateAutoQuestion()` when empty. A question break pops up every 4 cleared match-groups, non-punitive framing throughout ("💛 Not quite! Let's keep baking" — never "wrong"), correct answers add a small coin bonus but a miss never costs anything or blocks continued play. Matched tiles pay real Class Cash via `recordTransaction` at the same `CENTS_PER_COIN = 5` rate Platformer uses, settled either when the student clicks "Finish Baking & Spin" or, as a safety net, on unmount if they navigate away mid-game. Wrapped in `WebpageFrame` + the `.laptop-frame` bezel, matching the now-platform-wide "webpage" convention with zero special-casing needed (it's a real `ROLE_VIEWS` entry, so the from-town/from-Computer Back routing just works).
- **Tile art and SFX are teacher-provided commercial assets, not emoji placeholders** (the original plan) — she uploaded real art before the placeholder was ever shipped. Six treats (croissant/donut/muffin/pretzel/loaf/cinnamon roll) from her purchased "Bakery Asset Pack v1.1" (itch.io, jimal-art — commercial-use license, attribution requested: credit jimal-art.itch.io) live in `public/bakery/tiles/`; four SFX (match/combo/fail/pop) from her "Triple Treat SFX" pack live in `public/sounds/bakery/`. A separate CC0 "Tiny Treats Baked Goods" 3D pack she also uploaded (Isa Lousberg, isalousberg.com) was not used — it's FBX/OBJ/glTF 3D models, and the wheel/tiles only need flat 2D icons, which the commercial pack already provided; kept in mind as a future option if she wants an actual 3D treat model rather than a flat icon somewhere.
- **Post-game treat wheel, `src/components/BakeryTreatWheel.tsx` — direct follow-up instruction: "when the game is completed, the student should have a wheel spin (same as daily and bonus wheels) with only these bakery treat options. players can eat these and they can give them to their pets. if they give them to their pets, their food bar will increase one notch."** Reuses the same `spin-wheel` library and visual shape as `DailySpinWheel.tsx`, trimmed to just the six Bakery treats at equal odds (no cash economy — this wheel is a bonus flourish, not another income source). "The game is completed" reads as ambiguous for an endless free-play board with no level/win condition, so it's resolved as the student's own call: a "🎡 Finish Baking & Spin for a Treat!" button appears once they've earned any coins this visit, settles the coin payout, then opens the wheel. After landing on a treat, two real choices, both wired to existing systems rather than a new inventory layer: **"🍽️ Eat it!"** just closes out the moment (flavor, not mechanical — she didn't ask for an eating effect); **"🐾 Give to a pet"** lists the student's own owned pets (`StudentPet[]` filtered by `studentId`) and, on pick, calls the pet system's existing `carePet(petId, 'feed')` action — already built with exactly the food/social/health-bar system this ask needed (`StudentPet.food`, 0-100, soft floor, never punished by absence), so "food bar increases one notch" is `carePet`'s existing `+25` feed amount, zero new store/schema work required.
- **Round 2, SHIPPED (2026-09-25) — direct teacher instruction: rounds/challenge screens, the dice-vs-dropdown question-source pattern, a 100-question tracker, and a Cake Character unlock.** Four real asks bundled into one pass:
  - **Rounds + Challenge Screen** ("show a little challenge screen between each round of the game, think candy crush levels for the length of each round"). The board is now structured into rounds — round *N* needs `5 + (N-1)×2` cleared match-groups (5, 7, 9, 11…, a little longer each round, like a level getting harder) — with **no move limit and no fail state**, consistent with this platform's standing non-punitive design (no lives, nothing to lose). Hitting the target freezes the board and opens the Challenge Screen ("🎉 Round N Complete!"), which is also where the question-set-gated question now lives — replacing the earlier mid-board "every 4 matches" interrupt with one question at the natural checkpoint a level-complete moment already is. Answering (right or wrong, non-punitive either way) unlocks "Start Round N+1"; the board itself carries over between rounds rather than resetting.
  - **Question-source picker, `src/components/QuestionSourcePicker.tsx`** — direct teacher instruction: "students have a few options 1) they can select a dice icon and have a dice spin random... drawing randomly from all of their assigned question sets 2) they can select from a dropdown menu for their questions to draw from their current focuses." Built as a small reusable component (first used here, meant for any future question-set-gated game): a 🎲 button (random, pooled across every teacher question set, the existing Gas Pump-style behavior) and a 📋 dropdown of the individual Question Sets library (there's no direct data link from a `QuestionSet` to the separate `Focus` "current focus" concept today, so "current focuses" is read as "pick a specific one of your question sets" rather than the wordlist-only `Focus` type, which has no MC-question content to draw from). Shown once before Round 1 (a "How do you want your questions?" start screen) and again on every Challenge Screen, so the choice is a standing option through the whole session, not a one-time setting.
  - **Bakery Match tracker** — `Student.bakeryQuestionsAnswered` (new, additive, default 0 — `types.ts`/`sync.ts`/`schema.sql`), incremented by the new `recordBakeryQuestionAnswered` store action every time a Challenge Screen question is answered, right or wrong ("a tracker for every question answered," not a mastery-only count). Shown as a small "🏆 N/100 questions answered" pill in the game's header at all times.
  - **Cake Character unlock, `src/lib/characterCatalog.ts`** — direct teacher instruction: "if the student answers 100 questions in the Bakery Match game specifically, they will earn the ability to upgrade their character to this cake character. ensure it uses the human player assets for movement and animations. it cant just have its arms out." Crossing 100 adds `'cake'` to a new `Student.unlockedCharacterIds` array and bumps a new transient `lastCharacterUnlock` store field (same shape/purpose as the existing `lastCoinEarn` trigger), which the Bakery Match screen watches to show a one-time "🎂 You unlocked the Cake Character!" celebration with an Equip/Later choice; a small header button lets the student re-equip any time after. **The technical constraint the teacher's own warning anticipated, confirmed directly by reading the uploaded GLB's own glTF JSON**: her uploaded Cake Character model is a completely static, unrigged single mesh (0 skins, 0 animation clips of its own) — animating it can't come from the file itself. `TownSquare.tsx`'s `PlayerModel` was extended with a `CharacterSkinOverlay`: the default player model's own cloned skeleton and idle/walk `AnimationClip`s (`/world/models/characters/player.glb`) stay exactly as they are — its two default body meshes are hidden, and the Cake mesh is rigidly parented onto the skeleton's `'torso'` bone specifically because that's the one bone both the idle clip (a subtle rotation sway) and the walk clip (root-driven bob it inherits, plus its own rotation) actually animate, so the Cake visibly sways and bobs with the same motion instead of sitting frozen in a bind pose — an honest "rigid mascot" look (no separate limb articulation, since the source mesh has no limbs), not the broken "arms out" T-pose the instruction warned against. This is a genuinely new character-catalog system (`equipCharacter`/`equippedCharacterId`, also new/additive on `Student`), not a one-off hack — `CHARACTER_CATALOG` in `characterCatalog.ts` is built to hold more skins later. The skin model lives in `public/world/character-skins/` (deliberately *outside* `public/world/models/`, which is auto-scanned into the teacher's Build Mode Asset Directory — a character skin isn't a placeable world object). **Needs a live teacher look, same standing caveat as every 3D change in this doc**: the skin's auto-computed scale/position (a bounding-box height match against an eyeballed torso-to-head-top span, since this sandbox cannot render WebGL/Three.js to check by eye) is a reasoned estimate, not a verified fit.
- **Round 3, SHIPPED (2026-09-25) — direct teacher instruction, three messages in a row plus a full rules rewrite ("update game play logic immediately"), superseding round 2's webpage shell, coin economy, and round-target formula above.** Two halves, shipped together as one rewrite since the rules message landed mid-build on the shell/UI half:
  - **Shell removal — "games should not show up as a webpage... lose the webpage concept entirely."** `BakeryMatch3.tsx` no longer uses `WebpageFrame` or the `.laptop-frame`/`.laptop-screen`/`.laptop-deck` browser-in-a-laptop chrome every other Computer screen still uses — replaced with a standalone full-viewport `.bakery-shell` (new scoped `bakery-*` classes in `index.css`, none reused/edited on the shared classes, so Arcade/Cinema/FarmersMarket/Gallery/SillyQuizzes/StudentHome are unaffected). `WebpageFrame`'s one real function beyond chrome — a way back to wherever the student came from (Town Square vs. the Computer, read off `location.state.from`) — is preserved as a plain pill button (`.bakery-back-btn`, top-left, icon + label, no address-bar styling). **Flagged follow-up, not done this pass:** the other `laptop-frame`/`WebpageFrame` games (Arcade, Cinema, Farmers Market, Gallery, Silly Quizzes) could get the same standalone-shell treatment later if the teacher wants it generalized; out of scope here, this pass only touched Bakery Match.
  - **New cover/main-menu screen, replacing the old "How do you want your questions?" picker as the entry point.** A real title screen (`phase === 'menu'`) with a big "🥐 Bakery Match" title, the game's own six treat PNGs (`bakeryTiles.ts`'s `TILE_ART` — no new art requested or sourced, "using the assets within the game itself" per the teacher's own framing) scattered decoratively behind the menu card, and two buttons: "▶️ Play New Game" and "🏆 View Leaderboard." Question source (`QuestionSourcePicker`, unmodified — still shared with the Gas Pump) is no longer an equal-weight up-front choice; it now lives on a `phase === 'setup'` screen reached only via Play New Game, defaults to random already selected, and is chosen once and locked for that whole game (see rules rewrite below) — satisfies the standing "hidden by default, random unless a student opts in" instruction without conflicting with the later "picked once, locked for the game" rule.
  - **Drag/slide-to-match, additive to tap-tap.** Pointer events (`onPointerDown`/`onPointerMove`/`onPointerUp`, one code path for mouse/touch/pen, `setPointerCapture` so a drag keeps tracking off the small tile) on each tile: press, drag past an 18px threshold toward a neighbor, release — resolves to the same swap tap-tap already used. Refactored the match-detection/swap logic that used to live inline in `handleTileClick` into one shared `attemptSwap(a, b)` both the tap-tap path and the drag path call, so `matchThree.ts`'s `swapTiles`/`findMatches`/`isAdjacent` calls exist in exactly one place — `matchThree.ts` itself was not touched. A `suppressClickRef` stops the tap-tap click handler from double-firing right after a drag resolves.
  - **Visual polish + background.** A warm bakery-toned gradient background (`.bakery-shell`) behind the whole screen; the header/round-progress/board all got a real "panel" treatment (cream card, rounded border, drop-shadow button styling) in place of the old bare `chrome-frame` div + plain CSS-grid-of-buttons look — built with CSS, not the newly-available `public/ui/*` icon packs, since the bakery tile art plus a themed gradient read as a real game already without forcing pack assets in; `Icon`/`ICON_CATALOG` entries already used elsewhere (`play`, `trophy`, `arrowLeft`) cover the few icons this screen needed, so nothing new was added there either.
  - **CANDY CRUSH INSPIRED MATCH GAME RULES — a full rewrite of round 2's mechanics, same-day follow-up instruction with a complete spec.** A game is now `TOTAL_ROUNDS = 3` rounds of `MOVES_PER_ROUND = 3` successful moves each (a "move" = one swap that produces at least one match — the existing swap/match gate already only lets a matching swap through, a shake-and-revert doesn't count), replacing the old `5 + (N-1)×2`-match-groups-per-round formula entirely. After each round's 3rd move, the student must answer `QUESTIONS_PER_GATE = 3` questions correctly (not 1) before the next round — `QuestionScreen`'s existing "wrong never resets progress, just retry the same question" behavior covers the miss case unchanged, `done`/`total` now mirror the Gas Pump lockout's own multi-question framing (`gateCorrectCount`/`QUESTIONS_PER_GATE`) instead of the old fixed `0/1`. Question source is chosen once on the new `setup` screen and locked for the whole game — the in-round "Want different questions?" `<details>` reselection toggle is removed entirely (was already de-emphasized, now gone outright per direct instruction). After round 3's own question gate is passed there is no round 4: the game ends, the treat wheel spins exactly once (the old voluntary mid-game "🎡 Finish Baking & Spin" cash-out button is removed entirely, there is no more early spin), and the student returns to the main menu.
  - **Coins removed, replaced by a private per-game XP + leaderboard system.** `recordTransaction`/`CENTS_PER_COIN`/the 🪙 coin display/the payout-on-unmount effect are all gone — this game no longer pays Class Cash at all. In their place: an N-tile match earns N XP (a straight rename of the old tiered `matched.size >= 5 ? 8 : ... : 3` coin formula to literally `matched.size`, cascades still each counted separately), accumulated across the whole game and shown live (`⭐ N XP`) in the header. On game completion, `recordBakeryGameResult(studentId, xp)` (new store action, `store.ts`) appends `{ xp, date }` to a new `Student.bakeryLeaderboard` array (`types.ts`, additive/optional, same pattern as `bakeryQuestionsAnswered`) — wired through `sync.ts` both directions and a new `bakery_leaderboard jsonb not null default '[]'` column in `schema.sql`. **Strictly private, same standing no-cross-student-comparison rule as everywhere else in this platform**: there is no read path anywhere that shows one student's Bakery Match scores to another — the only place `bakeryLeaderboard` is ever read back is the same student's own "🏆 View Leaderboard" panel on their own main menu (most-recent-first).
  - **⚠️ Required teacher action, not yet done automatically anywhere in this codebase:** `schema.sql` changes are never auto-applied to the live Supabase database — a human has to paste and run the new `alter table students add column if not exists bakery_leaderboard jsonb not null default '[]';` line in the Supabase SQL Editor. Until that runs, any write to that column fails with a schema-cache error; `sync.ts`'s existing `isPermanentSchemaError` handling means it won't spam-retry forever, but the leaderboard feature will not actually persist across sessions until the teacher applies this migration. Flagging this explicitly, not as a buried footnote, per the standing lesson from earlier schema changes this same day.
  - **What did NOT change, by direct instruction:** `matchThree.ts` itself (logic only — the drag gesture wires into its existing exported functions, none were touched), `recordBakeryQuestionAnswered`'s own semantics (still fires once per correct challenge answer — the gate just now asks for 3 of them instead of 1, so it fires 3x per gate instead of 1x, same "every question answered" tracker meaning), the Cake Character unlock celebration, and `BakeryTreatWheel.tsx` (still the same six-treat equal-odds spin, just triggered once per finished game instead of voluntarily mid-game).
  - **Needs a live teacher/student check — no live browser/3D rendering available in this sandbox to verify any of it:** the new cover screen's actual visual balance and text/tile-art contrast against its gradient background, whether the drag-gesture's 18px threshold feels right on a touchscreen vs. a mouse, and general spacing/readability of the new panel styling around the board.
  - **Visuals reconciled against a teacher-approved interactive mockup, same day ("go ahead with the bakery mockups").** The mockup was built as two clickable artboards before this rewrite finished; once shown, differences from what had already shipped were brought in line rather than treated as separate work: `.bakery-shell`'s background became a single radial cream-to-caramel gradient (`#ffe8c7` → `#f7b96e`) instead of the earlier diagonal 3-stop linear one; `bakery-play-btn`/`bakery-secondary-btn` became true pills (`border-radius: 999px`) with the mockup's exact orange gradient (`#ff9e5e` → `#ff7a3d`) on Play; the question-source picker moved off its own screen entirely onto a small centered modal opened only by a new quiet top-right gear icon on the main menu (`bakery-gear-btn`) — Play New Game now starts a game directly with whatever source is currently set, matching "gear is the ONLY way to reach it before Play New Game" exactly; the in-round progress readout became 3 small fill-as-you-go pips (`bakery-move-pips`/`bakery-pip`) under a centered "Round N of 3" label instead of "N/3 moves" text, with the XP total moved into its own white pill on the right — all three in one `bakery-topbar` row; the board gained a real two-layer frame (`bakery-board-frame`, a caramel/brown outer "tray" around the existing cream `bakery-board-panel`); and the leaderboard became its own small modal (title "My Bakery Match XP," an explicit "Just for you. No one else can see this." privacy note) instead of swapping in place inside the menu card. **One real behavior change, not just cosmetic:** leaving mid-game now goes through a real exit-confirm (a ✕ button replaces the plain back-to-town pill during play, opening a "Leave this game?" panel with a primary "Keep Baking" and a quieter "Leave to Main Menu" text link) instead of the earlier build's silent immediate exit — "Leave to Main Menu" returns to Bakery Match's own main menu (not out to Town Square), where the existing back-to-town pill is still the one way to actually leave the app, so the "always a way back" requirement stays satisfied. `QuestionSourcePicker.tsx` itself was still not touched, per the standing constraint that it's shared with the Gas Pump — only wrapped in a new small panel here.
- **Round 4, SHIPPED (2026-09-29) — direct teacher instruction, reversing round 3's cash removal: "In game activities (like the bakery match) need to give money prizes... based on very question answered correctly, the student earns $.50. At the completion of the game, a message showing money earned with an animation of coins should appear. Ensure the bank register updates for the game earnings. Cash prize should be default now. Bonus spin occurs when total question goal for that game is achieved."** Cash is back, additive to (not replacing) the XP/leaderboard system round 3 built — XP still drives the private leaderboard and match scoring unchanged, cash is a separate reward tied specifically to the question gates.
  - **`REWARD_PER_QUESTION_CENTS = 50`** per correct challenge answer — **superseded same day by Round 5 below: this no longer banks live per-question, see Round 5's "deferred to completion" rule.**
  - **Completion message with its own coin animation**, satisfying the "money earned with an animation of coins" ask specifically at game-end: a modal (`showEarnings` state, `.bakery-earnings-card` in `index.css`) shows three bouncing coin emoji and the session's total earnings (`sessionEarningsCents`) via `formatMoney`. Shown first on completion; tapping through opens the existing Bakery Treat Wheel (unchanged) next — two separate prizes in sequence, not merged into one screen.
  - **"Cash prize should be default now"** — read as the platform-wide default posture for in-game activities going forward (Bakery Match specifically was the one round 3 had explicitly turned cash off for; this reverses that one exception, not a claim that every other activity's reward config changed).
  - **Bonus Spin on reaching "total question goal for that game"** — read as finishing the whole game (every round's question gate passed, the same point that already triggers `recordBakeryGameResult`/the treat wheel), since Bakery Match has no other named "total question goal" concept at the time this was written (Round 5 below adds a real, separate escalating goal). Grants `Student.bonusSpinAvailable = true` via `updateStudent`, the exact same flag/pattern A13's other "finished everything" rewards already use (store.ts), plus a `$0` `recordTransaction` purely to log a register row ("🎉 Finished Bakery Match: bonus spin!") — same 2-line pattern used elsewhere, no new mechanism invented. This is a *second*, separate wheel turn (the student's Daily Spin wheel) on top of the Bakery Treat Wheel spin that already happens at the same moment — two different wheels, two different prizes, both earned by the same finish.
  - **What did NOT change:** `matchThree.ts`, the XP/leaderboard system, `BakeryTreatWheel.tsx` itself (still six treats, equal odds, no cash on that wheel specifically), the rounds/gate structure.
- **Round 5, SHIPPED (2026-09-29), same-day follow-up refining Round 4 — direct teacher instruction: "ensure totals earned do not enter the bank unless the game is completed... Keep a count of money earned while playing, right near the xp counter. XP tracker should now show the main bakery game goal of getting to answer 100 questions. Allow the student to click on the main bakery game goal (100 questions) to get a pop up message... if 100 questions are answered within the bakery game, you earn $100 and the new challenge would go to answer 200 questions (starting the count again from 0). $200 earned from that goal, and so on."**
  - **Banking deferred to game completion, a real behavior change from Round 4.** The per-question `recordTransaction` call is gone; correct answers now only increment a local ref (`sessionQuestionsRef`) and the live `sessionEarningsCents` display state. Everything actually gets banked in exactly one `recordTransaction` call, inside the `round >= TOTAL_ROUNDS` branch of `handleChallengeCorrect` — if a student abandons the game early (the existing exit-confirm path), nothing from that session ever reaches the bank, satisfying "do not enter the bank unless the game is completed" literally.
  - **A new, separate escalating cash-milestone goal — additive to the per-question reward, not a replacement.** `Student.bakeryMilestoneTier`/`bakeryMilestoneCount` (new, additive fields, `types.ts`/`sync.ts`/`schema.sql`, same "teacher must run the SQL migration" caveat as every prior additive Student field in this section) track a goal that starts at 100 questions for $100, then grows to 200 questions for $200, then 300 for $300, and so on — deliberately a *different* counter from the lifetime, never-resetting `bakeryQuestionsAnswered` that drives the Cake Character unlock (Round 2), so crossing this new goal repeatedly can never accidentally interact with that one-time unlock. A shared pure function, `advanceMilestone(tier, count, correctAnswers)` (module-level in `BakeryMatch3.tsx`), is the single source of truth for the tier/count/payout math, used both for the live header display and the real settlement at game completion, so the two can never drift apart. **Milestone progress and payout are settled at the exact same moment as the per-question reward** (same `round >= TOTAL_ROUNDS` branch, same reasoning: a milestone crossed but the game then abandoned never pays out or advances the tier, matching the same "nothing banked, nothing committed, until completion" rule).
  - **The XP header pill is now the goal pill, direct teacher instruction ("XP tracker should now show the main bakery game goal").** The `⭐ N XP` display is gone from the header; in its place, a tappable `🎯 count/target` pill shows live progress toward the current milestone goal (computed from what's actually persisted plus this session's not-yet-settled correct answers, via the same `advanceMilestone` function). XP itself and the private per-game leaderboard are untouched underneath — only what's *shown* in that one header slot changed, not the underlying scoring/leaderboard system.
  - **A live money-earned pill sits right next to the goal pill** ("keep a count of money earned while playing, right near the xp counter") — `🪙 {formatMoney(sessionEarningsCents)}`, updating in real time as questions are answered, even though the actual bank credit is deferred to completion per the rule above.
  - **Tapping the goal pill opens a new explainer popup** (`showGoalInfo` modal), dynamically worded from the student's actual current tier/target (never hardcoded "100 then 200") — states the current goal, current progress, the reward for reaching it, and what the next goal after that will be.
  - **"View Piggy Bank" button added to the game-completion earnings message**, navigating to `/student/piggy-bank`, alongside the existing "Nice!" button that continues to the treat wheel.

### Literacy Workspace — UFLI grapheme tiles

- **UFLI blending board — not started, flagged blocker.** `LITERACY_MANIPULATIVES_FEATURE_AUDIT.md` Part 3 already scopes what a v1 needs, starting with "a grapheme dataset... matching UFLI's published systematic-phonics scope-and-sequence." Neither this session nor Claudia (Read/Grep/Glob only, no web access) can fetch or verify the real published UFLI sequence — this environment's network policy already blocked the teacher's own UFLI reference link earlier (`LITERACY_WORKSPACE.md`'s "Note on outside references"). Inventing a plausible-looking phonics sequence and presenting it as UFLI-grounded would be a real instructional-accuracy risk for exactly the students this tool serves. Needs either the teacher supplying the real UFLI scope-and-sequence (a document upload, same pattern as the Sentence Formulas curriculum reference), or explicit sign-off to build from best-available general phonics-sequence knowledge instead, clearly labeled as not verified against the real UFLI text.
- **Kayden's raw feature wishlist for a Literacy Manipulatives rebuild, saved verbatim/described — not yet built, still open under Claudia's discovery-questions document above.** She wants: a real UFLI blending board (referencing `https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=o9hDsQru0bk`, a demo video, and `https://research.dwi.ufl.edu/op.n/file/bca9ju45kvvrvoan/?embed` — attempted to fetch both this session, both blocked by this environment's network egress proxy, same standing block as before; names only, contents unverified); a "UFLI virtual word work mat" (`https://research.dwi.ufl.edu/op.n/file/gc8nkxns914enc7d/?embed`, also blocked, unverified); the format/decision-tree style of the attached **Sentence Formulas Template 7** PDF; Montessori grammar symbols as real manipulatives/reference images; and Montessori "predictable sentence frames" (referencing `https://www.imontesomething.com/blog/Montessori-EFL-Grammar.php`, also blocked, unverified — the two pasted example images were read directly instead, see below). Same standing caution as the UFLI bullet above applies to anything from the two blocked UF research.dwi.ufl.edu links or the blocked YouTube video specifically: their real content can't be verified from here, so nothing should be built as "this is what the real UFLI tool does" without the teacher supplying it directly (a screen recording, screenshots, or a document upload), the same pattern that unblocked the morpheme-tile content.
  - **Sentence Formulas Template 7 — read in full, a real and much bigger spec than what "Sentence Formulas mad-lib mode" currently implements.** The uploaded PDF is Kayden's own actual working document: the full 22-formula Sentence Formulas Template 7 set (across 8 categories), plus its separate 4-step progressive-build scaffold (Basic Action, Descriptive/Linking-Verb, Adverbial, Object, Existence/Location, Command/Request, Question, Negative) — today's mad-lib mode only has 6. Each formula is a color-coded slot row (WHO=yellow, ACTION=green, WHAT=red, WHERE=magenta, WHEN=orange, DESCRIPTION=cyan) ending in a `. ! ?` punctuation-choice column, with worked examples using her own real names and pets (Mom, Dad, "Ms. Kayden," Xander, Geoff, Azalea the cat, Yoga — her own comic character, referenced elsewhere as "Yoga Comic Sentence Formulas") — not generic placeholder names. Word banks are explicitly leveled by phonics stage (a "General" bank, then separate CVC and VCe word banks per slot color) so the same formula can be practiced at an easier or harder decoding level, something the app's phonics-agnostic word lists don't do today. Two blank template layouts exist (Horizontal grid, Vertical grid) plus a linked "Flippity Randomizer" (an external randomizer tool, not something to build in-app — the app's own randomize-a-sentence mechanic already covers that need). A later page ("Question words, articles, conjunctions") shows a harder, silly-sentence tier using her Yoga-comic-universe characters and invented nonsense nouns ("gurgle wurple," "Squirellanida") — a real example of the exact "expanded to ensure high quality" content tier Claudia's discovery document's Question A/F (licensing, grade range) should account for, since this tier is 100% her own original content with zero licensing question, unlike the DSF morpheme material.
  - **Montessori Grammar Symbols — two reference sheets read directly (images pasted into chat, not blocked links).** Level 1: 9 symbols (black triangle=noun, small blue triangle=article, dark blue triangle=adjective, red circle=verb, green crescent=preposition, orange circle=adverb, purple triangle=pronoun, pink rectangle=conjunction, gold cone/teardrop=interjection), each with a full definition and example words — this is the real, standard Montessori grammar-symbol set (large red circle for verb, black triangle for noun, etc. are the actual internationally-standard shapes/colors, not something invented for this request). Level 2 adds 9 more refined subtypes (proper/collective/abstract/spiritual noun variants on the black-triangle noun shape; transitive/intransitive/linking/auxiliary verb variants on the red-circle verb shape; infinitive, participle, gerund) as shape/color only, no definitions given yet.
  - **Montessori "predictable sentence frames" — two example images read directly.** A grammar-symbol-tagged sentence ("Walk down the hallway." / "She dances and sings beautifully." / "He ate on the table." / "A fuzzy cat sleeps.") is followed immediately by its own symbol-tile version directly below it (the same words' shapes/colors, no text, laid out as a strip) — the symbol strip functions as a wordless "parse" of the sentence directly above it, letting a student match words to grammar roles without reading comprehension being the bottleneck. A second image shows a physical (photographed, not digital) version of the same idea for conjunctions specifically: real word cards ("the green crayon," "and," "the red crayon") laid out under matching grammar-symbol shapes, with real crayons placed underneath as the concrete referent — "the green crayon AND the red crayon" / "the yellow crayon OR the blue crayon," teaching a conjunction by physically pairing two concrete objects rather than an abstract definition alone.
  - **Kayden's answers to Claudia's discovery questions — real decisions, protected, logged verbatim/mapped below. Still not built (see the build-order decision at the end).**
    - **Licensing (Q A1/A2), RESOLVED.** Her own words: "All permission for all licensing is given. This is exclusively for private personal use and I have full permission." She is asserting she personally holds the rights/permission to use the DSF Word Origins content (and, by the same statement, any other uploaded curriculum reference) for this private, personal, non-commercial classroom tool. This clears the licensing blocker on the morpheme-tile content specifically — real DSF Unit 1 morphemes, definitions, and passage content can now be built from directly, not just "inspired by the structure." Does not change anything about the UFLI links, which remain blocked for a different reason (this environment's network policy, not licensing) and still unverified.
    - **Sandbox vs. graded/assignment mode (Q B3), RESOLVED.** "Sandbox format unless I have built an assignment (like polypad created activities, think geogebra)." Two real modes, not one: (1) the existing open, unscored sandbox stays exactly that — full unrestricted access ("when in sandbox, all morphemes and otherwise words should be shown"); (2) a genuinely new **teacher-authored Activity mode** (Polypad-Activities/GeoGebra-style: a teacher builds a specific, scoped exercise from the same manipulatives) — inside an activity, "I have ability to turn in [on] specific morphemes," i.e. the teacher restricts which morphemes/content are available for that one activity. This is a real, separate, bigger build (an activity author + player, not just content) — explicitly lower priority, see build-order decision below.
    - **Markers/draw tool availability, RESOLVED.** "Markers can always be used, even when manipulatives are active." The draw/marker tool must stay reachable in every mode, sandbox or activity, never locked out by which manipulative is active. Today's Draw toolbar mode already satisfies the letter of this (Words/Draw/Mad Libs/Web are all switchable, draw is never gated behind another mode being off) — worth an explicit UX pass once sandbox content work above is further along, to confirm marker access reads as "always there," not just technically reachable.
    - **New content type — Word Matrices, RESOLVED to build.** "Licensing is good for morpheme webs and matrices." A word matrix (real structured-word-inquiry convention — a grid showing a base/root in the center with its prefixes and suffixes arranged around it, all the words in the family formed by combining them) is a genuinely different visual/manipulative from the existing Morpheme Web (which is tap-to-attach, single root at a time) — a new sandbox content type to design and build, not a Morpheme Web variant.
    - **Real student level (Q F), RESOLVED.** "My students that are using are grades 5-6 but currently are academically very low so early elementary skills are needed." DSF Level 1 Unit 1 (which reads as an early/entry level on paper) is actually correctly targeted, not too easy — build for early-elementary skill level content regardless of the students' actual grade number. No per-student grade-tier picker needed for this population; one appropriately-easy pool is the right scope.
    - **Spelling patterns, RESOLVED to build broadly.** "All spelling patterns." Read together with the licensing statement above, this is instruction to build out real spelling-pattern content broadly (not just the DSF morpheme set) — the CVC/VCe phonics-leveled word banks already read from Sentence Formulas Template 7 are one real source for this with zero licensing question (her own document); the UFLI blending-board item stays blocked specifically on this environment's network access, not on permission, so it's a separate open item, not resolved by this answer.
    - **Build order, RESOLVED — governs everything above.** "Build all sandbox features before assignments." Every sandbox-mode item (expanded Morpheme Web content from the real DSF Unit 1 roots, Word Matrices, broader spelling-pattern content, Sentence Formulas Template 7's full 22-formula set, Montessori grammar symbols as real manipulatives, Montessori predictable sentence frames) comes first; the teacher-authored Activity mode (the Polypad-Activities/GeoGebra-style restrictable-content layer) is explicitly deferred until the sandbox is done. Next build session should work this list in sandbox-first order, not touch Activity mode yet.

### Town Square sky

- **Full sky texture — 5th attempt (drei's procedural `<Sky>` shader) confirmed BROKEN by the teacher's own live screenshot, reverted to a single flat color.** The shader attempt shipped without a live visual check (this sandbox can't render 3D) despite the file's own standing "no retry without live visual QA" rule — the teacher previewed the branch and the sky rendered as jagged translucent white/blue shards, not a clean gradient, the same class of failure the rule was written to prevent. Direct instruction after seeing it: "one cohesive color of sky texture." `SkyboxBackground` in `TownSquare.tsx` now always sets `scene.background` to a single flat `THREE.Color` (the teacher's chosen `skyColor`, or `#bfe3ff` by default) — no shader, no texture, the same technique `WorldEditor.tsx`'s own Build Mode preview already uses safely. The `<Sky>` import and component are removed entirely, not just made conditional. **This is now the 5th attempt to fail and the 2nd to be confirmed broken by a real look (not just theorized) — treat any future sky-rendering change as needing a live screenshot from the teacher before it ships, not shader-structure reasoning alone.**
- **6th attempt — a real equirect sky texture, direct teacher request, shipped opt-in — CONFIRMED BROKEN live, then fully removed.** "Add the sky textures i've added to build mode for fill sky." Two real uploaded images were made pickable from Build Mode's Fill Sky panel, applied via `EquirectangularReflectionMapping` — the same technique every prior photo-sky failure above used. The teacher's own live screenshot of production (`independent-work-dashboard.vercel.app/#/world/town`) confirmed the predicted failure: large jagged dark-blue shard/triangle shapes scattered across the sky, the same failure class as every attempt before it. Direct instruction: "these dark blue shapes... need to be removed. it should not be there at all." **`EquirectangularReflectionMapping` is now confirmed broken by a real live look on two independent source images in this codebase (the 4th attempt's Kenney skybox and this 6th attempt's teacher-uploaded images) — this is a standing rule, not a per-image one: do not retry this mapping technique again on a new image, in either `TownSquare.tsx` or `WorldEditor.tsx`.** Full removal, not just hiding: `SkyboxTexture`/`BuildSkyTexture`/`SKY_TEXTURE_OPTIONS`/`SkyTextureBoundary.tsx` (the fallback error boundary built for this feature) are all deleted from both files; Town Square and Build Mode's Fill Sky panel now always render a single flat `THREE.Color`, full stop, regardless of what's stored in `app_settings.sky_texture` — the column itself stays in `schema.sql` (additive-only, never dropped) but is never read for rendering again. Build Mode's "🌤️ Sky" panel keeps its flat-color fill + Reset, just without the texture picker underneath it.
- **7th attempt — a real seamless-tileable sky pack, direct teacher upload, shipped opt-in, STILL NEEDS A LIVE TEACHER LOOK.** Kayden uploaded a genuine seamless/tileable 512x512 texture pack (Screaming Brain Studios, CC0, 96 images across 8 cloud styles — Bumpy/Cloudy/Fading/Fuzzy/Gradient/Puffy/Simple/Wispy Sky — x mood), asking that the whole sky show one of these patterns "with no breaks." This is a fundamentally different asset and technique from every prior attempt above, not a retry of the banned one: those were single wide equirectangular panoramas force-mapped with `THREE.EquirectangularReflectionMapping` (confirmed broken twice, real jagged shard artifacts both times); these are small isotropic patterns explicitly built to repeat edge-to-edge with no visible seam, the same idea as `WorldEditor.tsx`'s own `GROUND_TEXTURE_OPTIONS` ground textures, which already tile safely today via `RepeatWrapping`. Shipped the same way: new `SkyDome` component in `TownSquare.tsx` — a large (radius 180, comfortably past the fog's own 90-unit far distance and anywhere the camera actually reaches) `BackSide` sphere with the chosen texture tiled `RepeatWrapping` (6x3 repeats), `fog={false}` so it isn't washed out to flat fog color at that radius. All 96 images copied into `public/world/textures/sky/`, cataloged as `SKY_TEXTURE_OPTIONS` in `townLayout.ts` (shared with `WorldEditor.tsx`, same pattern as `ROLE_VIEWS`). Build Mode's Fill Sky panel got a two-level picker back (category chips, then a thumbnail grid per category) writing to the store's existing `skyTexture` field (already there, unused since the 6th attempt was fully removed — no schema change needed) — picking a thumbnail applies immediately, a "Remove sky texture" button returns to the flat-color default. Opt-in only: `skyTexture` defaults to `null`, so every existing world is unaffected until a teacher actually picks one. **Still genuinely new 3D rendering this sandbox cannot visually verify before shipping — treat this exactly like every sky change before it and get a live look before trusting it**, though the odds are meaningfully better here than prior attempts: the failure mode that broke every equirect attempt (pole/seam distortion from forcing a single non-panoramic image to cover the whole sphere) does not apply to a texture that's deliberately tiling, and a soft cloud/noise pattern (no hard directional lines) tolerates the sphere's own top/bottom UV pinch far better than a photo would.
- **8th pass, 2026-09-25 — audit only, no rendering-technique change, because none was needed.** Dispatched specifically to "fix the Town Square sky, for real this time" and told not to assume this doc's own account (above) was still current. It wasn't quite: the brief handed down described the last known state as "reverted to solid-color-only," which was true of the 6th attempt but is stale against the 7th attempt directly above it — `skyTexture`/`SkyDome` are live in the codebase today, opt-in and still carrying that entry's own "STILL NEEDS A LIVE TEACHER LOOK" flag, which this pass could not resolve either (this sandbox still cannot render WebGL/Three.js — that constraint hasn't changed). What this pass actually did, since a real screenshot was never possible:
  - **Read `SkyboxBackground` and `SkyDome` line by line and traced the exact render path.** `SkyboxBackground` is unconditional and simple: `scene.background = new THREE.Color(skyColor ?? '#bfe3ff')`, nothing else — the flat-color default every student who hasn't had a texture picked for them actually sees is correct and about as low-risk as 3D rendering gets. `SkyDome` (the opt-in 7th-attempt texture) checked out as sound on paper too: `BackSide` sphere so the camera (always well inside radius 180) sees the inner surface; `RepeatWrapping` + `repeat.set(6, 3)` is the same safe tiling `WorldEditor.tsx`'s own ground textures already use, not the banned equirect single-panorama mapping; `fog={false}`/`depthWrite={false}`/`renderOrder={-1}` are the standard, correct skybox-dome combination. Worked out the one real remaining risk by hand rather than leaving it as a vague "should be fine": the sphere's UV pinch at its top pole (the classic place a tiled sphere-dome shows visible distortion) is very unlikely to ever be on screen — Town Square's own look-up is hard-capped (`CAMERA_PITCH_CAP`, ~46° of the roughly 90° needed to reach the zenith) and Build Mode's orbit preview caps its polar angle the same way (`maxPolarAngle={Math.PI / 2.1}`), so neither camera can actually steer up to the distorted point. Genuinely reassuring on paper, still unconfirmed live, which is exactly the distinction this entry is trying to be honest about rather than rounding it up to "confirmed."
  - **Real bug found and fixed: the Build Mode Fill Sky picker never actually previewed the pick.** `WorldEditor.tsx`'s own 3D preview Canvas rendered only the flat `<color attach="background">`, never `SkyDome`, regardless of what a teacher had picked in the Fill Sky panel — so the one tool built to let her choose a sky texture had no way to show her what she'd chosen; she could only ever find out by leaving Build Mode and walking into live Town Square, i.e. shipping blind by construction, which is the exact failure pattern this whole section exists to stop. Fixed by extracting `SkyDome` (and `SKY_DOME_RADIUS`) out of `TownSquare.tsx` into a new shared `src/routes/world/SkyDome.tsx` (same shared-component pattern `WorldObjectRenderer.tsx` already uses between these two files) and rendering it in `WorldEditor.tsx`'s own Canvas too, gated on the same `skyTexture` store field, wrapped in its own `Suspense`. Both routes now render sky from the exact same component, so they cannot silently drift apart the way flat-color-vs-texture almost did. This doesn't replace a teacher's own live look at production — this sandbox still can't provide that — but it means her own next look, in her own browser, no longer has to be in the student-facing app to be a real check.
  - **Leftover-comment cleanup (the "conflicting state from earlier failed attempts" this pass was asked to hunt for).** No dead imports or dead texture-loading code turned up — the 6th attempt's `EquirectangularReflectionMapping`/`SkyTextureBoundary` were genuinely fully deleted, exactly as the 6th-attempt entry above claims. What had rotted instead were comments left over from before the 7th attempt existed: `store.ts`'s `skyColor`/`skyTexture` field comments and three `schema.sql` column comments still described `skyColor` as "a horizon fog tint layered over the real skybox photo" and `skyTexture` as "a real equirectangular sky image," both describing the 5th/6th-attempt design that no longer exists; `WorldEditor.tsx` still had a standalone comment claiming "Fill Sky is flat color only now, full stop," written before the 7th attempt put the texture picker back. None of these were read by any code — comments don't execute — so none of this was a live bug, but a future change built on any of them (a person or another agent trusting "flat color only, full stop" or literally trying to reintroduce equirect mapping because a comment says that's what `skyTexture` already means) could have reintroduced exactly the failure class this whole file's history warns against. All four corrected to describe what's actually live.
  - **Also fixed while in this code: 96 em-dashed teacher-facing labels.** Every entry in `SKY_TEXTURE_OPTIONS` (`townLayout.ts`) used an em dash in its `label` (e.g. `'Bumpy Sky — Blue 01'`), which is both the Fill Sky panel's visible tooltip (`title={t.label}`) and its `aria-label` — real teacher-facing copy, not a code comment, so it was a plain violation of this repo's standing no-em-dash rule. Rewritten to a colon (`'Bumpy Sky: Blue 01'`), the same fix pattern this doc's own Farmer's Market review entry used for the same class of violation elsewhere.
  - **Explicitly NOT claiming this is "confirmed fixed."** Nothing about the flat-color default changed this pass because nothing about it was wrong — it was already the one sky approach with a real live confirmation behind it, and it remains that. The 7th attempt's `SkyDome` texture path is unchanged in its actual rendering logic (only where it's rendered — now also in Build Mode's preview — changed), so its status is exactly what the entry above it already said: opt-in, off by default, reasoned through carefully here, still not live-verified. The honest summary: the sky a student sees today (flat color, unless a teacher has explicitly picked a texture) is correct and needs no fix; if and when a teacher does pick a texture, get a real screenshot from her before calling that path done, same standing rule as every attempt before this one.
- **9th attempt, SHIPPED (2026-09-25) — a real live screenshot finally arrived, and it was broken.** The teacher sent a live screenshot of production Town Square with a texture picked: large jagged white/light-blue shard shapes over the flat blue base, the exact same artifact class every equirect-photo attempt produced, even though the 7th attempt's tiled-sphere technique is a genuinely different, normally-safe approach (confirmed: `WorldEditor.tsx`'s own ground textures use identical `RepeatWrapping` tiling without issue). Ruled out the texture content itself first — read the actual PNG pixels directly (`bumpy-sky-blue-01.png`): fully opaque, soft organic cloud blobs, nothing jagged in the source art at all, so the bug is in the 3D rendering, not the image. Root cause: a full `SphereGeometry` has a pole singularity — every triangle converging at the north/south pole gets extreme, near-degenerate UV coordinates, and with `repeat.set(6, 3)` those pole triangles smear a large stretched swath of the tiled texture across themselves; the 8th pass's camera-angle-cap reasoning (camera can't steer up to the pole) turned out to not fully hold in practice. **Fix: switched `SkyDome` from a `SphereGeometry` to a `BoxGeometry` skybox** — same size, same `BackSide`/`fog={false}`/`depthWrite={false}` material, same tiled texture, but a cube has no poles at all: each of its 6 faces gets its own ordinary, independent 0-1 UV rect, so the smear is structurally impossible now, not just unlikely. `repeat` reduced from `(6,3)` to `(4,4)` since a cube face doesn't need the same aspect-corrected tiling a sphere's equator did. One real, disclosed tradeoff: the pattern doesn't perfectly continue across a cube's edges the way it wrapped around a sphere's equator — a soft repeating cloud pattern should read as fine there, but this is flagged, same as every sky change, as needing a live look before calling it done.
- **Cinema curtain frame — SHIPPED, direct teacher request after seeing it live.** The Cinema background used to switch to a `curtain-screen.png` image with a fake "screen" rectangle painted into the curtain photo itself, while the real video played in an unrelated fixed 640px box centered by flexbox — the two never lined up, so the video visibly floated next to the painted screen instead of sitting on it. Fixed: `Cinema.tsx` now always uses the plain `curtain-closed.png` (no painted screen), and the real video/title bar are absolutely positioned to match the exact rectangle that used to be painted into `curtain-screen.png` (measured directly from that now-unused image: left 24.53%, top 24.54%, width 50.89%, height 50.83% of the 1920x1080 photo — a true 16:9 box). `curtain-screen.png` is no longer referenced anywhere but was left on disk rather than deleted.
- **Mailbox navigation — simplified per direct teacher request.** Removed the What's New/Town Square/Home three-button row in favor of a single "⬅️ Back" button to `/student/home`, now that Mail is reachable straight from the Computer's own widget grid (this session's Computer redesign) rather than only by walking to the Post Office.

### Build Mode: Solid-by-default, faster NPC unstuck, Sims-4-style manipulation

- **SHIPPED (2026-09-25) — direct teacher instruction: "make all assets Solid (blocks walking through) on default placement. they should always be checked on for Solid unless i or the student goes in and explicitly clicks the check to make it walk through."** Supersedes Claudia's earlier per-category default (buildings/furniture solid, decorative nature/character categories walk-through, roads excluded) — `defaultCollidesForCategory` in both `WorldEditor.tsx` and `IslandBuild.tsx` now unconditionally returns `true` for every category, including roads (previously deliberately walk-through) and every decorative/nature item. The per-object Solid checkbox is unchanged and remains the only opt-out. **Flagged plainly, not silently absorbed**: a teacher placing a road tile now needs to manually uncheck Solid on it, same as any other walk-through exception — it no longer defaults that way.
- **SHIPPED (2026-09-25) — direct follow-up instruction, anticipating exactly the risk the change above creates: "dont let players or npc get caught in Solid assets. if they get stuck and cant move, they should rotate and try to walk another direction."** Wandering NPCs (`WanderingNPC` in `TownSquare.tsx`) already had an 8-second timeout failsafe from an earlier "never get stuck in an endless loop" fix, but 8 seconds of visibly pushing against a wall is a bad look, and a lot more objects are now solid than before. Added a much faster, no-progress-based check: every ~0.4s while actively walking toward a target, compare current position against position at the last check — under ~0.08 units of real movement means the NPC is wedged (sliding along an obstacle's surface via `blockObstaclesSlide` without actually advancing), and it abandons that target and picks a fresh random direction almost immediately (a ~0.1s redirect pause, not the normal 1.5-4s "arrived and pausing" pause) — reads as "turn and try another way," not "give up and stand still." The player side needed no change: `blockObstacles`/`blockObstaclesSlide` were already hardened by an earlier fix ("a player/Neighbor must never end up caught inside an asset") to always resolve to a valid, reachable position, including the dist-0/exact-center edge case. NPCs standing still between wander legs is pre-existing, unchanged, correct behavior (`pauseUntil`), not something this pass needed to touch.
- **FIXED (2026-09-29) — urgent teacher report: "student players are stuck outside the boundary in both cars and while walking, all npcs are trapped."** Root cause: the "all assets solid" pass made flat ground pieces solid too (road tiles, paths, floors, train rails, carpets, grass patches), and `TownSquare.tsx`'s `recomputeCollisionLayout` ignores the per-object Solid flag entirely. A town built from road tiles became a wall-to-wall chain of rectangles, so `blockBuildings`' push-out from one tile landed inside the next, shoving players, cars and NPCs toward the lot edge and pinning them there. Fix: anything you walk or drive ON is never an obstacle. A measured model is skipped from collision when it's flat (new shared `isFlatModelSize` in `WorldObjectRenderer.tsx`, the same footprint-vs-height test `FLAT_LIFT` already used) or shorter than 0.3m once scaled; before its size is measured, a name pattern (road/path/floor/rail/carpet/grass/tile, etc.) keeps it off the circle fallback so it can't trap anyone for its first frames. Everything with real height (buildings, trees, fences, furniture) stays solid exactly as before. **Needs a live check (this sandbox can't play-test the 3D scene):** walk and drive across roads, confirm NPCs wander freely, and confirm walls/fences/buildings still block.
- **SHIPPED (2026-09-25) — direct teacher instruction, comparing to Sims 4's `bb.moveobjects` cheat.** Three related Build Mode manipulation upgrades in `WorldEditor.tsx` (Town Square's editor only — `IslandBuild.tsx`'s parallel copy was intentionally left as-is this pass, flagged here so it isn't silently forgotten):
  - **Option/Alt + click-drag = grab and freely move any placed object immediately.** The existing drag-to-move system required a select-then-drag two-step and always grid-snapped; held Option now skips the "must already be selected" gate (first click both selects and starts the drag) and disables grid snapping for the duration of that drag/placement — genuine "click it and put it anywhere" placement, the direct cheat analog. Released Option/plain clicks keep the exact prior behavior (select first, then drag, always snapped) unchanged.
  - **Touch-device equivalent, SHIPPED (2026-09-25), direct follow-up: "if item is selected and cross hairs are selected, it is as if the user had clicked and held the option key... full control and simple placement."** Touch users have no Option key, so the existing ✥ Move popover (opened by tapping the crosshair icon on a selected object's toolbar) now doubles as the touch equivalent — while it's open, dragging the object is the exact same free, unsnapped placement Option+drag gives a mouse user, without needing a keyboard at all. New `moveModeActive` state in the main `WorldEditor` component, kept in sync from `SelectedObjectToolbar`'s own `openPopover === 'move'` state via a new `onMoveModeChange` callback prop (that component owns whether the popover is open; the parent owns the actual drag/snap logic, so the callback bridges the two) and folded into the same `freePlacement` check Option-held already uses.
  - **`,`/`.` rotate, `[`/`]` shrink/grow — for the item you're about to place, not just an already-placed selection.** Direct instruction: "when i have an asset selected, before i place it, let me press , to rotate the object one click left, . to rotate right, [ to shrink it one unit, ] to increase it one unit." Two new state values (`ghostRotationAdjust`/`ghostScaleAdjust`, reset to 0/1 whenever a different asset gets armed) layer on top of the ghost's own computed default rotation/scale, applied identically to the ghost preview, its footprint outline/overlap check, and the actual object created on click — what you see while aiming is exactly what gets placed. **This reassigns the existing shortcuts, doesn't just add new ones**: `[`/`]` used to rotate an already-placed selection 15° per press and `-`/`=` used to nudge its scale — `[`/`]` now means shrink/grow (a ×1.1/÷1.1 step, the same proportional-nudge math the popover's own hold-repeat buttons already used, chosen over a flat unit delta specifically because Claudia's earlier size-unit audit already flagged that a fixed delta can wildly over-scale a correctly-tiny object) and `,`/`.` now means rotate 15°, for both the pre-placement ghost and an already-placed selection alike, one consistent set of keys for whichever is currently the "active" thing to manipulate. `-`/`=` are left bound to the old scale behavior too (harmless extra alias, not removed) so nothing that worked before silently stops working.
- **SHIPPED (2026-09-25) — direct instruction: "remove task icon from bottom right hand side as it is already in the main pie menu."** `TownSquare.tsx` had a small floating "Back to task dashboard" 📋 FAB stacked in a bottom corner, navigating to `/student/home` — genuinely redundant with the pie menu's own 📋 Tasks entry (opens the in-world Today Tasks overlay), which a teacher flagged as visual clutter now that it's a second, separate way to reach a task view. Removed outright; the car radio button that used to be described as "stacked above" it keeps its own fixed position, comment updated to not reference a FAB that no longer exists.

### Build Mode: placed-object collision now matches the placement-preview footprint (Phase 1b)

- **SHIPPED (2026-09-25) — direct teacher instruction: "the box that is shown around the assets when they are placed (the temporary square to show boundary before placement) should act as the actual boundary when making objects solid so players and npcs cant walk though, and transportation cant travel through."** This is exactly the Phase 1b gap `TownSquare.tsx`'s own `WORLD_OBJECT_COLLISION_RADIUS` comment had been flagging since Front 1 Phase 1 ("A real per-object rotated-footprint system is Phase 1b, not this pass") — every placed object's real gameplay collision was a generic circle sized off its `scale` value alone, with no relationship to the model's actual measured width/depth, so an oblong object (a long wall, a wide building placed via Build Mode) had a walkable strip down its long sides even though `WorldEditor.tsx`'s own `FootprintOutline` (the box a teacher sees while placing/dragging it) already drew its real, accurate rotated footprint via `useModelSize`'s `THREE.Box3().setFromObject(scene)` measurement. Now they use the same number.
  - **The engineering problem and its fix:** `recomputeCollisionLayout` is a plain function (called from a `useEffect`, not a component), so it can't call `useModelSize` itself — that hook needs `useGLTF`, which only works inside the render tree. Built the bridge the task called for: `useModelSize` moved out of `WorldEditor.tsx` and into `WorldObjectRenderer.tsx` as a shared export (`src/routes/world/WorldObjectRenderer.tsx:39`, imported by both files now — one implementation, not two), and a new pair of components in `TownSquare.tsx`, `ObjectFootprintProbe`/`ObjectFootprintTracker` (`TownSquare.tsx:581`), render one invisible `useModelSize` instance per *distinct* placed-object `modelPath` actually in use (de-duped — several placed objects sharing one model, e.g. multiple of the same tree, cost one `useGLTF` call, not one each), each wrapped in its own `<Suspense fallback={null}>` so one slow-loading model can't blank the whole scene while it measures. Each probe reports `{ hx: size.x/2, hz: size.z/2 }` (the model's real, unscaled, unrotated local-space half-extents — identical basis to `FootprintOutline`'s own box and to the hand-entered `BUILDING_RAW_HALF_EXTENTS` above it) into a module-level cache, `OBJECT_FOOTPRINT_SIZES` (`TownSquare.tsx:345`), keyed by `modelPath`. A new `objectFootprintVersion` state bump (in the main `TownSquare` component) re-fires the existing `recomputeCollisionLayout` effect whenever a probe reports a newly-measured size, even though `worldObjects` itself didn't change — so a freshly-placed or just-loaded object's collision upgrades from its circle fallback to its real footprint as soon as that's available, not only on the next unrelated edit.
  - **Rotated-rectangle push-out reused, not reinvented, per the task's explicit instruction.** `recomputeCollisionLayout` now builds a real rotated rect (`{x, z, rotationY, hx: raw.hx*scale, hz: raw.hz*scale}`, same math as the existing `BUILDING_FOOTPRINTS`) for every placed object whose model has a known measured size, and merges those with the fixed buildings' own footprints into one combined array, `RECT_FOOTPRINTS`. `blockBuildings` — kept that name deliberately, despite now covering more than buildings, specifically so placed objects inherit its existing, teacher-hardened, edge-case-tested push-out logic (nearest-edge response, `BUILDING_COLLISION_MARGIN` clearance padding, the dist-0/exact-center handling) for free, with zero new push-out code to trust — now iterates `RECT_FOOTPRINTS` instead of `BUILDING_FOOTPRINTS` alone. Every existing call site (`blockObstacles`, `blockObstaclesSlide`, the player's per-frame walk/car movement, the map-view double-tap teleport, `WanderingNPC`'s random-target validity check) already called `blockBuildings`/`blockObstacles`/`blockObstaclesSlide` for building collision, so all of them now automatically also collide with real object footprints — no new call sites needed, confirmed by reading each one (`blockObstacles`/`blockObstaclesSlide` themselves at `TownSquare.tsx:459`/`498`, `WanderingNPC`'s target-validity/movement calls at `TownSquare.tsx:1088`/`1123`, and the full `Player` walk/car branch further down). `WORLD_OBJECT_COLLISION_RADIUS`'s circle formula is kept as a **fallback only** now, for the brief window before a model's real size has finished loading/measuring, or if it fails to load entirely — objects on the fallback stay in `STATIC_OBSTACLES` (the circle array) exactly as before; objects with a known real size are removed from `STATIC_OBSTACLES` and live only in `RECT_FOOTPRINTS`, so there's no double-blocking with two different shapes for the same object.
  - **Transportation:** cars/walking both go through `Player`'s shared `blockObstaclesSlide`/`blockBuildings` calls, so they're covered by the same fix with no separate work — confirmed by reading the car-driving branch, which calls the identical functions the walk branch does. Trains stay track-constrained (`findTrainPath`/`sampleTrackPath`), never free-roaming, so this doesn't apply to them, same as before. Planes deliberately have **no** obstacle collision while airborne — `TownSquare.tsx`'s own comment on that branch states it outright ("the plane is flying over the town, not through it"), a pre-existing, documented design decision this pass didn't touch. **Flagged, not fixed: boats.** `slideWithinWater` (the boat movement function) only ever constrained position to stay within water tiles (`isWaterAt`) — it never called `blockBuildings`/`blockObstacles`/`STATIC_OBSTACLES` at all, before or after this change, so a placed object sitting in/near water (a dock, a rock) still doesn't block a boat. This is architecturally separate from what this pass touched (boats were never on the rect/circle obstacle system to begin with, unlike cars), and extending them onto it is a real follow-up decision, not a same-pass extension of an existing path — flagging it explicitly rather than silently leaving the teacher's "transportation cant travel through" ask partially unmet without saying so.
  - **The non-negotiable safety bar (never permanently stuck) — genuine new edge case found and fixed, not just asserted safe.** A fixed building never moves during gameplay, so `blockObstaclesSlide`'s old "clear the CURRENT position first" pre-push loop only ever needed to handle circle obstacles (every placed object, before this change) — it never pushed the current position out of a rect, because rects (buildings) literally couldn't move under a standing player mid-session. Now that placed *objects* are rects too, and objects *can* move/resize/rotate live under a standing player via Build Mode's realtime sync, that assumption breaks: a teacher dragging a large object directly onto a currently-standing student's exact spot could leave the old pre-push loop not clearing it. Fixed by running the current position through `blockBuildings` first (`TownSquare.tsx`'s `blockObstaclesSlide`, ~line 502), before the existing circle pre-push (`TownSquare.tsx:520`), so the function's "always resolves to a valid position, current position is never itself invalid" guarantee — the exact property the original stuck-forever bug fix relied on — now holds for the merged rect set too, not just the old building-only one.
