  - **Confirmed a moved/resized/rotated object updates its live collision, not just a freshly-placed one:** `recomputeCollisionLayout` derives every rect/circle fresh from the live `worldObjects` array (`o.position`/`o.rotationY`/`o.scale`) on every call — nothing about an object's footprint is cached across a move/resize/rotate except the model's own raw unscaled `hx`/`hz` (which doesn't change when an object is moved, only its scale/rotation/position do, and those are re-read every time). `WorldEditor.tsx`'s drag/rotate/scale handlers (`updateWorldObjectH`) write straight to the store's `worldObjects`, which is the existing `useEffect([layoutOverrides, worldObjects, wallSegments, drivingObjectId, objectFootprintVersion])` dependency this pass extended, not replaced — confirmed by reading the drag-commit call site (`WorldEditor.tsx:2164`), not assumed.
  - **What still needs a live teacher check (this sandbox cannot render or play-test the 3D scene, same standing limitation as every other 3D-rendering change in this file's history):** the actual visual read of an oblong placed object (a long wall, a wide custom building) now genuinely blocking along its full long edge instead of leaving the sides walkable; that a just-placed object's brief circle-fallback window (before its model finishes measuring) isn't visually noticeable as a "wrong-shaped" obstacle for a moment; and specifically the new edge case above — dragging a large solid object directly onto the student's own live position and confirming it pushes them clear within a frame or two rather than trapping them, since this exact scenario (a rect obstacle newly created under a standing player) never existed in this codebase before today's change and has no prior real-world testing to lean on.

### Build Mode: multi-select grouping, Canva-style rigid-body move/rotate/resize

- **SHIPPED (2026-09-25) — direct teacher instruction, second half of the same request whose first half (touch equivalent of `bb.moveobjects`) shipped earlier this hour: "if an object is selected and the user is holding the shift key (or for touch screens, clicking a multi object select button by the duplicate) those objects can be grouped together (think canva controls for elements) and moved as one. they can be ungrouped as well. they can be resized with the same keys and buttons as one element would be."** Town Square's `WorldEditor.tsx` only — `IslandBuild.tsx`'s parallel toolbar was intentionally left untouched this pass, flagged here so it isn't silently forgotten, same as the Option/Alt-drag pass before it.
- **Data model:** `WorldObject.groupId?: string` (`src/types.ts`), additive/optional like every other recent field on that type — undefined on every existing object, no migration needed. Wired through the same generic pattern `customRoleUrl`/`roomId` already used: `rowToWorldObject`/`worldObjectToRow` in `src/lib/sync.ts`, and `alter table world_objects add column if not exists group_id text;` in `supabase/schema.sql`.
- **Multi-select extends the existing single-selection system, not a second parallel one.** `selection` (the existing `Sel` type) is unchanged and still means "one thing, normal select" for placed/layout/wall objects alike. A new `multiSelectIds: string[]` holds 2+ placed-object ids once a teacher starts building a multi-selection — grouping only ever applies to teacher-placed `WorldObject`s, never the fixed town layout items or drawn walls. Exactly one of `selection` / `multiSelectIds.length>=2` is ever "the current selection"; every place that already cleared `selection` on a mode switch (hammer/paint/wall toggle, undo/redo, Escape, placing a new object, clicking the ground, Clear All) now clears `multiSelectIds` too, so a stale group selection can never linger into an unrelated tool.
- **Shift-click (desktop) / a new "Multi-select" toggle (touch) add to the pending selection instead of replacing it** — the touch toggle is a `captionBtn` (icon + a visible text caption underneath, not icon-only — the standing WCAG finding for any *new* button in this toolbar) placed directly next to the existing Duplicate button, literally where the teacher asked for it. Clicking an already-grouped object while adding/removing pulls in (or drops) every one of its group's current live members at once, so shift-clicking any single member always keeps the whole group together.
- **Clicking any grouped member auto-selects the whole group**, direct spec. Falls back to a normal single-select if a group has been whittled down to 1 live member (e.g. the rest were hammer-deleted individually one at a time) rather than landing on an empty/no-toolbar state.
- **The real design work — rigid-body transform math, wired to the SAME buttons and keys a single object already has** (`,`/`.` rotate, `[`/`]` scale, the Move popover/drag), not a separate group-only control set:
  - **Rotate** (`rotateGroupBy`, `WorldEditor.tsx`) turns the whole selection around its shared centroid as one object — every member's position orbits that pivot AND its own `rotationY` turns by the same delta — matching Canva's real group-rotate behavior, explicitly **not** each object spinning in place around its own center (a row of 3 houses sweeps around like one signpost, not each house individually spinning 90° on the spot). Documented here per direct instruction, since the two look very different in-scene and this was a judgment call, not a spec'd formula.
  - **Resize** (`rigidGroupScale`) multiplies each member's own `scale` by the same factor AND its distance from the centroid by that same factor, so the group's actual footprint grows/shrinks as a unit and members keep their relative layout, instead of each object growing in place with the group's overall footprint unchanged.
  - **Move** — the simple case, same idea run N times: the D-pad (`moveGroupBy`) applies one shared delta to every member; click-and-drag uses a new `dragGroupOrigin` snapshot (every member's starting `[x,z]`, captured on drag-start) so the live pointer position can derive that same shared delta smoothly, mirroring how the single-object drag already worked.
  - **The wiring itself:** `rotateBy`/`nudgeScale`/`nudgeScaleBy`/`nudgePosition` — the exact functions the single-object toolbar, its keyboard shortcuts, and the drag-commit effect already called — each now checks `multiSelectIds.length >= 2` first and defers to the rigid-body math above instead of falling through to the single-`selected`-object path. No parallel group-only button set exists for rotate/resize/move; same buttons, different math underneath, per direct instruction.
- **Group / Ungroup.** Group (`groupSelected`) assigns every current member a fresh shared `groupId` (a brand-new id even if the selection mixed members of two older groups, so it merges them under one id rather than silently favoring one side). Ungroup (`ungroupSelected`) clears `groupId` from every member, leaving them exactly where they sit — no snap-back to pre-group positions — and the current multi-selection stays active/movable as a unit; only a *future* reselection of any one of them stops pulling in the others.
- **Delete on a group** removes every member, same confirm-chip pattern (tap to arm, tap again to commit) single-object delete already uses, and the same "one real delete call per object so undo still works, just one Undo per object, not one for the whole batch" tradeoff the paint bucket already made for the identical reason.
- **A genuinely different toolbar was built for a group selection (`SelectedGroupToolbar`), not the single-object one reused verbatim** — but it calls the exact same handler props (`rotateCwFine`/`rotateCcwFine`/`onDragRotate`/`growHold`/`shrinkHold`/`nudge*Hold`), so "same buttons, different math" holds for every control it does show. What it deliberately drops, and why: Color tint and Name/role (both inherently per-object, no single coherent value across a mixed group); Duplicate (not part of this request for a group); and the Resize popover's Mini..Giant presets/exact-size input (those set an *absolute* size using one object's own native model height as the reference unit — meaningless across a group of different models/sizes). Only the relative +/- nudge survives for group resize, since it's the one operation that stays coherent regardless of what's in the group. Group/Ungroup replace the dropped controls.
- **Judgment call, explicitly flagged:** the group toolbar's on-screen position and height are approximated from just the first selected member (centroid x/z is real and correct; the "how tall to float above the group" number uses only that one member's own measured height) rather than the true combined bounding box of every member, which would mean loading every distinct member's own GLTF just to measure it. Reasonable most of the time; could sit a little low/high if the anchor happens to be much smaller/larger than the rest of the group.
- **What still needs a live teacher look (this sandbox cannot render or play-test the 3D scene, same standing limitation as every other 3D-rendering change in this file's history):** whether the rigid-body rotate/resize math actually *reads* right in 3D (does a rotated row of objects look like it swept around together, does a resized cluster look like it grew as a unit); whether the new "Multi-select" touch button and the group toolbar's Group/Ungroup/Delete-all buttons are clear and easy to hit; and the group-drag click-and-drag gesture feeling as smooth as the existing single-object drag it mirrors.
- **Not built this pass, left for later if wanted:** `IslandBuild.tsx`'s parallel copy of the whole object toolbar/selection system (same explicit deferral as part 1's Option-drag work); a way to select a group without touching any member first (e.g. a rubber-band/marquee select); combining/merging two already-formed groups directly from the toolbar rather than via a fresh ad-hoc multi-select-then-Group.

### Sync: stop retrying a permanent schema mismatch forever

- **SHIPPED (2026-09-25) — direct teacher report, a live screenshot of the red sync-trouble banner reappearing on the Bakery Match screen** (`update students: Could not find the 'bakery_questions_answered' column of 'students' in the schema cache`). Root cause: this session's own `alter table` statements (the Bakery Match tracker/character columns, and separately `app_settings.ground_bounds` from the map-expansion pass) had been written into `supabase/schema.sql` but never actually run against the real production database — `schema.sql` is not auto-applied, a human has to run it in the Supabase SQL Editor, and that step hadn't happened yet. Gave the teacher the exact `alter table ... add column if not exists ...` statements to paste in directly (the real fix — no code change makes a missing column exist). Separately, a real bug in the retry code: `pushWithRetry` in `src/lib/sync.ts` treated a schema mismatch exactly like a transient network blip — after its normal retry attempts it queued the write into `pendingWrites`, which gets retried again every 20 seconds, on reconnect, and on manual "Retry" forever, hammering the database with a request that can never succeed and re-showing the alarming red banner to whichever student happened to be on that screen. Added `isPermanentSchemaError`, checking PostgREST's own error code (`PGRST204`) or its message text for this exact failure class — a permanent schema error is now reported once (`onPersistentSyncFailure`, the same banner) and never re-queued for infinite retry, while a genuine network failure still gets the full retry-then-queue-then-reconnect treatment exactly as before.
- **URGENT HOTFIX (2026-09-30) — direct teacher report in all caps, "FIX CODE NOW," a live screenshot of every Question Set save failing in production** (`upsert question_sets: Could not find the 'description' column of 'question_sets' in the schema cache`). Same root cause as the 2026-09-25 incident directly above, repeated despite `isPermanentSchemaError` already existing: the Focus-merge-into-Question-Sets work earlier this session added `description`/`is_focus`/`focused_at` to `question_sets` in `supabase/schema.sql` and started sending those three fields in every `pushQuestionSet` upsert, but the matching `alter table` migration was only ever written to this repo file, never actually run against the live Supabase project — this sandbox has no Supabase credentials or CLI (verified: no `supabase` binary, no `SUPABASE_*`/`DATABASE_URL`/`POSTGRES_*` env vars, no `.env.local`, only `.env.example`). Because the permanent-schema-error handling stops infinite retries but still reports the failure and still leaves the write unsaved, every question-set save was blocked outright, including an in-progress CSV import of 25 questions that appeared to "succeed" in the UI but was silently failing to persist to the server — a real, active data-loss risk, not just an annoying banner. **Stopgap fix, shipped immediately:** `pushQuestionSet` in `src/lib/sync.ts` no longer sends `description`/`is_focus`/`focused_at` in its upsert payload, so ordinary question-set saves (name, subject, questions, tags, etc.) work again right now. **Trade-off, stated plainly:** starring a set as the Focus set and writing its description will *appear* to save in the UI (they're still held in local Zustand/localStorage state) but will not persist to the database or sync to other devices until the migration below is actually run. **⚠️ Required teacher action, not yet done automatically anywhere in this codebase:** run this in the Supabase SQL Editor for this project, then ask for `description`/`is_focus`/`focused_at` to be re-added to `pushQuestionSet`'s upsert so starring/descriptions start actually persisting:
  ```sql
  alter table question_sets add column if not exists description text;
  alter table question_sets add column if not exists is_focus boolean not null default false;
  alter table question_sets add column if not exists focused_at timestamptz;
  ```
  **Standing lesson, now twice-confirmed:** any `schema.sql` edit made in this sandbox is inert until a human pastes it into the Supabase SQL Editor — a future session should flag this explicitly at the moment a migration is written, not just when it breaks in production.
- **RESOLVED (2026-10-01) — direct teacher bug report with a screenshot: tapping the star on a Question Set in `QuestionSetsManager.tsx` didn't persist as a focus set.** Root cause was exactly the trade-off flagged above: the stopgap had been live since 2026-09-30 and the migration had never been confirmed run, so every star tap updated local state only and reverted on the next reload/sync. Teacher ran the `alter table question_sets add column ...` statements above with no errors; `description`/`is_focus`/`focused_at` are back in `pushQuestionSet`'s upsert payload (`src/lib/sync.ts`). Starring a set and writing its description now actually persist to Supabase. Verified: `npx tsc --noEmit` and `npm run build` both pass clean.

### Build Mode: removed the "overlapping" placement warning

- **SHIPPED (2026-09-25) — direct teacher instruction: "in buildmode allow objects to overlap."** Checked first whether anything actually blocked overlapping placement — it didn't: `footprintOverlap` (`WorldEditor.tsx`) was already advisory-only per Claudia's original spec, its own warning text literally read "that's OK, just checking," and nothing in `WorldEditor.tsx` or `IslandBuild.tsx` ever rejected a click for overlapping another object (confirmed by reading `handleGroundClick`'s actual placement commit path — the only real block in there is a door/window needing to be on a wall). So the instruction reads as: even a non-blocking warning still made overlap feel disallowed. Removed the whole feature rather than just softening its wording — deleted `footprintOverlap()` and the `placementOverlap` variable, its "⚠ Overlapping X" banner message, and its contribution to the ghost/outline warning tint (`tintColor`, `GroundCellOutline`, `FootprintOutline` colors) — kept the other three real, still-blocking warnings (door/window needs a wall, boat needs water, track won't connect) exactly as they were, since those are genuine rejected-click cases, not overlap.
  - **Scope**: `IslandBuild.tsx` never had this check to begin with (confirmed via grep), so nothing to remove there.

### Sky rendering: 10th attempt, actually fixed (stopped tiling instead of switching geometry again)

- **SHIPPED (2026-09-25) — direct teacher report, "i am getting frustrated about the sky rendering," with a live screenshot of the same jagged-shard artifact class the 9th attempt (this same day, switching from a tiled sphere to a tiled box) was supposed to have fixed.** Root-caused properly this time instead of guessing at another geometry swap: pixel-diffed the actual texture's opposite edges (`cloudy-sky-blue-01.png`, checked directly) and found them already matching almost exactly (~1-2 average per-channel difference out of 255) — this specific art style was never the problem, and never needed `repeat.set(4,4)`/`(6,3)` tiling in the first place. The 9th attempt's box swap traded one real bug (a sphere's pole triangles smearing a REPEATED texture) for a different real bug (a box's 12 face-edges, each independently tiling the same pattern with no phase coordination, meeting abruptly — exactly what "torn paper" shard edges look like for a soft gradient texture). Fix: `SkyDome.tsx` is back to `SphereGeometry` (a box's 12 hard edges are strictly worse than a sphere's single pole for an UNtiled texture) with `repeat` left at its default `(1,1)` — the image is shown once, wrapped smoothly around the whole sphere, not repeated. An untiled pole only pinches that one copy of the image at a single point; there's no repeated pattern left for neighboring UV triangles to disagree about, which is what actually caused both previous attempts' visible artifacts. Segments raised to 64x40 so the sphere's own polygon facets don't show near the pole.
  - **What still needs a live teacher check**: this is genuinely a different, specific fix from the 9th attempt's guess, not another blind geometry swap — but this sandbox still cannot render WebGL, so it hasn't been seen. If this specific texture (`cloudy-sky-blue-*`, `bumpy-sky-*`, etc.) still shows any artifact after this, the next thing to check is whether OTHER sky textures in the same picker (not yet pixel-diffed) tile less cleanly than this one, since the fix's safety depends on the source image actually being edge-continuous, not on the code path alone.

### Fix: mounting a vehicle could teleport a student off the map, stranding them

- **SHIPPED (2026-09-25) — direct teacher report with a live screenshot: a student's avatar sat off the walkable lot entirely (`Math.round(playerPos.x)` reading -194 in the debug coordinate chip, `GROUND_BOUNDS_MAX` is 70) with WASD/arrows and click-to-walk both apparently doing nothing.** Root cause: `TownSquare.tsx`'s `teleportTarget` mechanism (used by map double-tap, vehicle mount/dismount, and NPC-approach snapping) is the ONE place in this file that ever writes `pos.current.x`/`z` directly — every other movement path (WASD, click-to-walk, cars, boats, planes, NPC pathing) already goes through `clampGroundX`/`clampGroundZ` (`TownSquare.tsx:88-95`). `startDriving` (`TownSquare.tsx:3611`, mounting a car/boat/plane/train) set `teleportTarget` straight from the vehicle object's own stored `position`, unclamped — so a vehicle placed (or somehow left, by whatever means) outside the current lot bounds would fling a student who mounted it to that exact out-of-bounds spot. Once there, normal WASD movement should have re-clamped back in-bounds on the very next frame — students being unable to walk it off likely means they were still mounted/driving at that point, where "arrows" drive the vehicle instead of walking, and a wildly out-of-bounds starting position for `blockObstaclesSlide` may have kept read as blocked in every direction.
  - **Fix, two layers**: (1) `startDriving` now clamps the vehicle's mount position through `clampGroundX`/`clampGroundZ` before setting `teleportTarget` (`TownSquare.tsx:3611`). (2) Defense-in-depth: the single `teleportTarget` consumption point inside `Player`'s `useFrame` (`TownSquare.tsx:1594`) now ALSO clamps on the way in, so no future `teleportTarget` writer (there are 4 call sites total: map double-tap already clamped, vehicle mount now fixed, vehicle dismount, NPC-approach) can reintroduce this exact "student stranded off the map" failure by forgetting to clamp its own x/z first — matches this app's standing "never leaves a student stuck" bar the same way the earlier NPC-stuck-in-Solid-object fix did.
  - **Not fixed, and can't be from here**: whatever placed the actual vehicle object at that far-out position in the first place is still sitting there in the world data — this fix stops a student from being teleported to it, but the vehicle itself may still visually sit off in the void until the teacher walks up to it in Build Mode (now reachable again) and repositions or deletes it. No 3D/browser rendering available in this sandbox to confirm the fix visually or to inspect that vehicle's actual stored coordinates.
  - **FOLLOW-UP SAME DAY — the fix above didn't hold: a second live report, same coordinate exactly** (x=-194 again, a different student, standing next to two NPCs also apparently out there). Since `SPAWN_POSITION` is a fixed `(0, 6)` with no persisted player position (confirmed — nothing in this codebase saves/restores a student's last x/z), a stuck student reappearing at the exact same extreme coordinate on a fresh load ruled out stale position state and pointed at the real root cause: `clampGroundX`/`clampGroundZ` only bound movement WITHIN whatever `groundBounds` the store currently holds — but `groundBounds` itself was only ever range-checked (`clampGroundBoundsValue`, `[GROUND_BOUNDS_MIN, GROUND_BOUNDS_MAX]` = `[12, 70]`) on WRITE, inside `expandGroundBounds` (`store.ts`). Both places that LOAD it from `app_settings.ground_bounds` — `store.ts`'s realtime subscription handler and `sync.ts`'s initial fetch — trusted the raw database value as-is via `?? DEFAULT_GROUND_BOUNDS`, which only catches `null`/`undefined`, not an out-of-range value already sitting in that row (manual SQL, a stale value from before `GROUND_BOUNDS_MAX` existed as a concept, anything). A bad bound loaded this way silently became the new ceiling every "clamped" movement path in the app measured against, so mounting a vehicle at `x=-194` clamped to... `-194`'s own now-corrupted bound, not actually stopping anything. **Real fix**: `townLayout.ts` gets a new `sanitizeGroundBounds(raw)` that per-edge range-checks (falling back to `GROUND_HALF` per missing/non-numeric/out-of-range edge, never trusting the shape of what's in the row) — called at both load sites (`store.ts`'s realtime handler, `sync.ts`'s initial fetch) in place of the old `?? DEFAULT_GROUND_BOUNDS`. This is the actual fix; the earlier same-day fix was real but insufficient on its own since it clamped against a ceiling that could itself be wrong.
  - **Still can't be fixed from here**: if `app_settings.ground_bounds` genuinely holds a bad value right now, this fix makes the app stop trusting it (sanitizing down to a safe range on every load) but doesn't clean up the stored row itself — cosmetically harmless (the app now behaves as if bounds were sane regardless), but worth a look in the Supabase table editor if the teacher wants the stored value itself corrected. Same standing limitation as above: no 3D rendering available to visually confirm this actually resolves the reported black-void/broken-terrain view.
  - **SECOND FOLLOW-UP SAME DAY — reported still broken after the bounds-sanitize fix, in a verified-fresh incognito window (ruling out any client-side caching) with no JavaScript error in the console (ruling out a crash).** Confirmed via the Vercel API that production was actually running the fixed commit (`list_deployments`/`get_deployment` showed the correct SHA, state READY) — so this was a genuinely still-live gap, not a stale deploy. Root cause, finally isolated by grepping every single `pos.current.x =`/`pos.current.z =` assignment in `TownSquare.tsx`, not just the ones near the two previous fixes: the train's on-rail movement (`sampleTrackPath`, `TownSquare.tsx:1653-1655`) is the ONE movement branch that was deliberately never clamped, by design ("never a wall-style hard block" for a train reaching the end of its track, per `TRANSPORTATION.md`) — every other branch (WASD, click-to-walk, car, boat, plane, NPC pathing) already clamps its own output. If a train's resolved track sits outside the normal lot bounds (from the same underlying data problem as the vehicle-mount incident above, whatever originally caused it), riding that train puts a student's position there every single frame, forever, with no reclamp — matching all three reports' identical symptom (frozen-looking view, stuck on one exact spot, arrows doing something that isn't recognizable as walking, since Go/Reverse throttle a train, not steer a person).
  - **Real fix, finally the actual one**: rather than special-case the train branch (or keep hunting for a fourth unclamped path some other vehicle type might have), added one unconditional `clampGroundX`/`clampGroundZ` pass on `pos.current` at the very end of the Player's `useFrame`, right before the frame's position is committed to the rendered mesh (`TownSquare.tsx`, just before the final `groupRef.current.position.set(...)`) — after every branch has run, regardless of which one. This closes the gap for the train case specifically and, going forward, for any future movement branch (present or not-yet-written) that forgets to clamp its own output — a single, permanent backstop instead of a fix scoped to one vehicle type, matching this codebase's standing "never leaves a student stuck" bar the same way the NPC-stuck-in-Solid-object fix and the ground-bounds-sanitize fix above do.
  - **What still needs a live check**: whether this was in fact a train (not confirmed — inferred from being the only remaining unclamped path in the whole file, not from inspecting this teacher's actual world data, which this sandbox has no access to). If a student is still able to reach a genuinely broken state after this, the next place to look is whatever placed that far-out object in the first place — likely traceable to the same original incident that produced the very first `x=-194` report, still sitting somewhere in this world's saved data.
  - **THIRD FOLLOW-UP SAME DAY — direct teacher instruction, blanket this time rather than a new coordinate/screenshot: "make sure that players can never leave the boundary of the playable game space, they are never placed outside of it and they can never access it."** Treated as a demand for an exhaustive audit rather than another single-path guess. Re-grepped every `pos.current.x =`/`pos.current.z =`/`teleportTarget.current =`/`.position.set(` in `TownSquare.tsx` from scratch (not just near prior fixes): confirmed every `teleportTarget` writer (map double-tap, vehicle mount, vehicle dismount, NPC-approach) is clamped; confirmed `PetCompanion`'s own follow-position math was already clamped (an earlier pass, its own comment cites the same "no player, Neighbor, or pet" bar); confirmed the camera rig (`camX`/`camZ`, `TownSquare.tsx:1944-1946`) offsets only `CAMERA_DISTANCE = 5.2` units from the (already-clamped) player position, nowhere near the ground's decorative visual radius (`GROUND_HALF*4 = 88`) or the sky dome (`SKY_DOME_RADIUS = 180`), so it can't itself be the source of a "black void/broken sky" view; confirmed `groundBounds`' in-memory initial state (`DEFAULT_GROUND_BOUNDS`, all `22`s) is never `NaN`/undefined so `clampGroundX`/`Z` can never themselves compute `NaN`. **Found one genuinely unclamped path**: `WanderingNPC`'s own movement write (`TownSquare.tsx`, was line ~1125, `pos.current.x = bx; pos.current.z = bz;` with no clamp) — a Neighbor/Townsperson, not the player, but still a "no player, Neighbor, or pet can ever leave the map" gap per the standing rule PetCompanion's own comment already states. Added the same unconditional final-frame `clampGroundX`/`clampGroundZ` pass this component was still missing, right before its own `groupRef.current.position.set(...)`, matching Player's and PetCompanion's own backstops exactly. **The player's own path was already fully covered by the prior fix** — no new gap found there in this pass; if a student still reaches an out-of-bounds view after this, it did not come from any position-write this file makes, and the next place to look is the underlying stray world-data object (still unresolved, see above) or something outside `TownSquare.tsx` entirely, neither of which this sandbox can inspect without the teacher's own live Build Mode look.

Poly Pizza's own documented split: Quaternius entries are CC0, "Poly by Google" entries (the archived Google Poly library) are CC-BY 4.0, attribution required. Treated the "jeremy" Chicken the same conservative way (attribution given) since it isn't a Quaternius upload and its exact per-model license couldn't be confirmed directly.

The manifest generator (`scripts/generate-asset-manifest.mjs`, runs on every build) auto-discovers anything dropped into `public/world/models/<category>/`, so no manual registration step was needed — confirmed via a clean `npm run build`, both the manifest regeneration and the zero-weapons check passed.

This app's model pipeline is GLB/GLTF-only (`generate-asset-manifest.mjs`'s own `MODEL_EXTENSIONS` explicitly excludes `.fbx`, and every loader in this codebase is `GLTFLoader`) — this sandbox has no FBX-to-GLB conversion tool available to fix that here. Quaternius publishes the same Pig model in GLB format directly in his own "Animals" asset pack on itch.io — the teacher re-downloading/re-uploading that specific format would resolve it. `public/world/models/pets/animal-pig.glb` already exists in the catalog (a different, simpler-style pig model from an earlier pack) as a stand-in in the meantime, not a replacement.

### Icon system: emoji placeholders being replaced with real UI-pack art

- **SHIPPED (2026-09-25) — direct teacher instruction: "start to convert all emojis over to icons and images. the emojis are good placeholders, but should be changed when we have applicable assets."** Teacher uploaded five more UI/icon packs in the same batch as the paper book pack above. Checked each license before touching anything (same discipline as the "Free_version.zip" rejection earlier this session):
  - **Cleared and copied into `public/ui/`:** Kenney UI Pack 2.0 (`kenney/` — CC0), Basic GUI Bundle by Penzilla Design (`penzilla/` — royalty-free commercial use per the uploaded `PenzillaDesign_StandardLicense.pdf`, but with two real obligations this pass had to satisfy: (1) a MANDATORY visible credit, added as a small footer line on `TeacherHome.tsx` — "Graphics created by Penzilla Design" — since the license requires it somewhere reachable in the project, not just a code comment; (2) the license also requires sending a link to the live project to PenzillaDesign@gmail.com once it's public — **that's a teacher action item, not something this session can do**), Vector UI Pack by dobo_ui/Duplo (`dobo/` — itch.io, commercial use permitted per the pack page, credit appreciated not required), Cartoon UI Pack by Chequered Ink (`cartoon/` — confirmed via web search since no license file was bundled: "ANY and ALL uses including commercial use, with or without giving credit, except you may not sell or redistribute the unaltered assets").
  - **`HyperCasual_Game_UI.zip` — flagged, then used on direct teacher decision.** No bundled license file, and the filenames (`noAdsBtn`, `rewardAdsBtn`, `restoreBtn`, `privacyIcon`) match the pattern of a paid mobile-game-template asset kit (Unity Asset Store / CodeCanyon style) rather than a simple free itch pack; a web search turned up multiple similarly-named packs with different licenses and couldn't confirm which one this specific file is. Surfaced this to the teacher directly (AskUserQuestion) with the risk spelled out plainly; she chose "Use it anyway" over skipping it or tracking down the license. Copied into `public/ui/hypercasual/` on that basis — **this is a known, accepted risk the teacher chose with the ambiguity stated up front, not a license this session confirmed clean**, worth remembering if the pack's real source ever surfaces with stricter terms. `Garden_cozy_kit.zip` — explicitly held per direct instruction ("apply everything except the garden kit... we will use that" for a future garden game), not touched, not a licensing question.
  - **Built the actual swap infrastructure**, not just copied files: `src/lib/iconCatalog.ts` (`ICON_CATALOG`, a semantic name like `"close"`/`"coin"`/`"star"` mapped to a real PNG path, picking whichever pack's art best fits each icon) and `src/components/Icon.tsx` (`<Icon name="..." />`, same pattern as the existing `AvatarImg`/emote catalog). This is a seed covering roughly 35 of the highest-frequency emoji glyphs, not the whole app yet — a codebase-wide scan found **293 distinct emoji glyphs, 1,610 total occurrences**, scattered across dozens of files with no existing shared button/icon component to funnel through (checked; there isn't one), so a full conversion is real, ongoing, multi-file work, dispatched to a background agent (see below) rather than attempted in one pass.
- **SHIPPED (2026-09-25) — first real conversion pass (background agent), roughly 100 of the 1,610 total occurrences (~6%), on the highest-traffic student-facing surfaces.** Own scan first to prioritize by how often a student actually sees the screen, then converted call sites to `<Icon name="..." fallback="<original emoji>" />`, added two more verified catalog entries (`music` → Penzilla `Icon_Small_Blank_Music.png`, `cash` → Penzilla `Icon_Small_Blank_Dollar.png`), and left the long tail as emoji anywhere no pack had a real semantic fit rather than forcing a mismatch. Converted, in order:
  - `src/routes/world/TownSquare.tsx` — the persistent Sims-4-style pie menu (What now?/More/My Home wedges), its More/Music/Today-Tasks/What-do-I-do modals, and every bare `✕` close button in the file.
  - `src/components/Marketplace.tsx` — header (shop ribbon, Class Cash balance chip), cart, checkout, receipt, and the sidebar category tabs (Prizes/Home/Voices).
  - `src/components/ToolsPanel.tsx` — the always-on Tools menu (My Tools trigger, Thesaurus/Dictionary/Sound Wall/Word Processor/Whiteboard/Quiet Tool), used from Town Square, the Computer, and every subject screen.
  - `src/routes/student/StudentHome.tsx` (Computer desktop app tiles: Mail/Marketplace/Badges), `src/routes/student/SubjectDashboard.tsx` (task confirm/close/home), `src/components/TaskChecklist.tsx` (the checkbox state icon + read-aloud/help buttons on every task row — the single highest-repetition spot in the app), `src/routes/student/PlaygroundView.tsx`, `src/components/InventoryHotbar.tsx` (My Stuff/backpack panel), `src/components/FeedbackTool.tsx`, `src/components/ArticleReader.tsx`, `src/routes/student/FarmersMarket.tsx`, `src/routes/student/PetShelter.tsx` (balance chip, home/gift buttons — same pattern as Marketplace for consistency).
  - Deliberately left as emoji (no real fit in the four cleared packs, not a shortcut): tasks/clipboard 📋, meditation/help 🧘, paw 🐾, backpack 🎒, map 🗺️, speech bubble 💬 (explicitly instructed to keep unless a real fit turns up), palette 🎨, shopping cart 🛒, pencil/edit ✏️, book 📖/📚, link 🔗, ticket/Skip Pass 🎫, party 🎉/✨, handshake 🤝, and the small set of Task/Badge/MarketplaceItem/FeedbackOption `.icon` **data fields** (teacher-editable, some already support real image URLs) — a separate, working system, intentionally untouched this pass.
  - **What's left for the next pass:** `src/routes/teacher/WorldEditor.tsx` (196 occurrences, teacher-only, lowest priority), `src/routes/student/GrammarSandbox.tsx` (121), `src/routes/teacher/ActivityLibrary.tsx` (99), `src/routes/world/IslandBuild.tsx` (95), `src/lib/sentenceScene.ts` (90), `src/routes/teacher/AssignmentsIndex.tsx` (74), `src/lib/changelog.ts` (65, the What's New book itself — left untouched this pass since its entries are prose paragraphs, not UI chrome), `src/routes/teacher/LessonPlanBuilder.tsx` (63), `src/routes/world/HomeRoom.tsx` (61, student-facing, good next target), `src/lib/marketplaceSeed.ts` / `src/store/store.ts` / `src/lib/steps.ts` (data-driven `.icon` fields, same "separate system" call as above), plus the rest of the teacher-only screens. `WebpageFrame.tsx`'s shared back-button fallback text (`✕ Close` / `🌳 Back to Town Square` / `⬅️ Back to Computer`, used by nearly every "webpage" screen) is typed as a plain `string` prop, not `ReactNode` — a good small next step, but needs that type widened first, so left alone this pass rather than doing it as a rushed side effect.
- **What still needs a live teacher check:** no 3D/browser rendering in this sandbox, so none of this has been visually verified — check icon sizing next to text on the pie menu wedges (22px) and the TaskChecklist row buttons (16-18px) in particular, and confirm the `check`/`close`/`lock` icons read clearly at the small sizes used in dense areas like the Marketplace cart and the pet stat row.

### Books reskinned with the Humble Gift Paper UI System pack

- **SHIPPED (2026-09-25) — direct teacher request, uploaded `Humble Gift - Paper UI System v1.1` (itch.io, HumblePixel; license read from the pack's own `License.pdf`: commercial and non-commercial use allowed, modification allowed, no redistribution/resale, no logo/trademark use, credit optional) and asked to use it for "the books, including the change log."** `BookPanel.tsx` is the one shared page-turning book component behind the Joke Book, Pet Book, InventoryHotbar's two book views, and the What's New changelog (`TownSquare.tsx:4161`), so reskinning that one file covers all of them at once, same pattern as task #113. Copied three pieces of the pack's art into `public/ui/paper/`: `book-page.png` (`Paper UI Pack/Folding & Cutout/8 Shop/1.png`, a parchment sheet with a dog-eared corner and an ornate border) now fills the page background via `backgroundImage`/`backgroundSize: 100% 100%` instead of the old flat CSS gradient, with a new `pageContentStyle` inset padding wrapper since the image itself now carries the border look the old `boxShadow: inset` was faking; `book-ribbon.png` (`Paper UI Pack/Folding & Cutout/2 Headers/1.png`, a banner ribbon) sits behind the title text, replacing the plain dark pill; `book-nav-button.png` (`Content/5 Holders/2.png`, a round paper-and-ink holder) is now the Prev/Next corner buttons' background in place of a flat cream circle with a border. The flip animation itself (`goTo`/`onFlipEnd`, the rotateY 3D transform, the stuck-`flipDir` safety net) is untouched — this pass only changed what the pages and chrome look like, not how the flip works. Added a changelog entry (`2026-09-25-book-paper-reskin`) since every book a student opens looks different now.
- **What still needs a live teacher check:** the parchment texture's built-in border at the exact `pageStyle` aspect ratio used across the different book sizes (Joke Book vs. the wider changelog vs. InventoryHotbar) since `background-size: 100% 100%` stretches the source art's own ~410×430 proportions to fit whatever box each caller renders at, which could look slightly stretched on a very wide or very short book instance — no 3D/browser rendering available in this sandbox to check that visually.

### Question-set-gate pattern fixes, applied "across the board"

- **SHIPPED (2026-09-25) — five direct teacher fixes to the shared question-answering UX every question-set-gated interaction uses (Gas Pump's forced refuel lockout in `TownSquare.tsx`, and Bakery Match's per-round Challenge Screen), reported against a live screenshot of the Gas Pump lockout showing the exact bug in (1):**
  1. **Progress is a real running total now, never a "must be in a row" streak.** Direct instruction: "the students progress should never be lost when questions need to be answered. it should never be a certain number in a row, but rather a certain number in general. if they get one wrong, that question doesn't contribute to the total amount they need, but it also doesn't restart the count." `answerGasQuiz` used to call `setGasLockoutStreak(0)` on a miss — the exact bug the screenshot showed ("Answer 9... You're at 1 of 10"). Now a miss shows gentle feedback and simply doesn't increment the count; the count itself is never reset except when a brand-new lockout starts fresh from 0 dashes. Bakery Match's Challenge Screen didn't have this bug (it's one question per round, no in-a-row concept at all), so nothing needed fixing there — mentioned for completeness, not because it was broken.
  2. **Both question views are genuinely bigger** — `overlay-panel` width raised (Gas Pump: 380px → 560px/92vw; Bakery Match: 400px → 540px/92vw), padding 24px → 32px, prompt/choice text sized up (choices now 52px tall, `1.05rem`), matching each other.
  3. **A visual progress bar replaces the "Answer 9 questions..." sentence.** Reused `SubjectProgressBar` (`src/components/SubjectProgressBar.tsx`) as-is — the exact same done-of-total bar the to-do list already uses — instead of building a second one; `done={gasLockoutStreak}`, `total={10}`.
  4. **The Gas Pump lockout now has a real ✕, gated behind a confirmation with a real, honestly-stated cost.** It used to have no close button at all while locked out ("unable to do anything else until 10 questions are answered correctly" was the original direct instruction) — now ✕ always shows, but during a lockout it opens a confirm panel first ("you'll lose your progress on filling the tank, the car will stay out of gas, and you'll have to get out of the vehicle") rather than closing immediately; confirming resets the lockout state and calls the existing `stopDriving` to actually park the car and step the student out, so the stated consequence is real, not just a warning. Bakery Match's Challenge Screen wasn't touched here — there's no vehicle/lockout shape to it (leaving without answering just... isn't offered as an action on that screen, same as before; nothing to add a cost to).
  5. **Read-aloud is always available on the question prompt now**, on both screens — `<ReadAloud text={...prompt} settings={student?.ttsSettings} />`, the same component/pattern already used for sign text and dozens of other places, next to the prompt text rather than buried.

- **SHIPPED (2026-09-25) — single shared `QuestionScreen` component, replacing both bespoke modals above with one teacher-approved design.** Direct teacher instruction, "push that screen" — a visual/structural unification of the pattern above, not a new question-answering feature. New `src/components/QuestionScreen.tsx`; both call sites migrated onto it:
  - `src/routes/world/TownSquare.tsx` — Gas Pump's forced-refuel lockout (`gasQuizQuestion` render block, ~line 4725; new `handleGasCorrect`/`handleGasExit` handlers replace the old `answerGasQuiz`).
  - `src/routes/student/BakeryMatch3.tsx` — Bakery Match's per-round Challenge Screen (`phase === 'challenge'` render block, ~line 378; new `handleChallengeCorrect`/`handleChallengeExit` handlers replace the old `answerChallengeQuestion`/`startNextRound`).
  - **Look**: warm cream (`#FFF6E9`) full-bleed screen, Lexend body / Baloo 2 headings (both already loaded app-wide via `src/index.css`, no new font loading needed), a top bar with only a ✕, a pip-style progress readout, and a placeholder 🧰 toolbox popover (Calculator/Scratchpad/Text Size/Highlight buttons render but are disabled no-ops — none of these have real functionality anywhere in this codebase; the teacher explicitly asked to reserve the space, not asked for the tools themselves), no subject icon/label, no "Question N" text anywhere, per the mockup's later iteration rounds. A local pip renderer was built rather than reusing `SubjectProgressBar` — that component's visual (continuous fill bar, "🏁 X of Y done" caption) doesn't match the mockup's discrete-segment spec and different caption copy ("X of Y answered correctly"), so building a small one matching the exact spec was the better fit than bending an existing component to look like something else.
  - **Exit-confirmation is now universal and always real** — the ✕ never exits immediately anywhere, always opens the "Leave this question set?" overlay first (a hard, explicitly repeated teacher requirement). This is new for Bakery Match specifically: its old Challenge Screen had no way to back out mid-question at all; "Leave Anyway" there now returns to Bakery Match's own picker menu (`setPhase('picker')`). For Gas Pump it's the same real-cost exit as before (resets the lockout, calls the existing `stopDriving`), just voluntary Fill Up now also gets a confirm on the way out where it previously closed immediately — a deliberate side effect of making the ✕ behavior truly universal per the teacher's instruction, flagged here in case she'd rather the voluntary flow skip the confirm.
  - **Listen now reads the full question, not just the prompt** — extends the existing `speak()` mechanism (`src/components/ReadAloud.tsx`) to read the prompt followed by every choice in order ("...Option A, 36. Option B, 42...."), a direct teacher requirement.
  - **Answer/retry model, the one real interaction change:** a wrong pick marks that choice and lets the student try the *same* question again in place (no new question is fetched on a miss, and nothing about the count/streak/round ever resets on a miss) — a correct pick shows the success state for a beat (~900ms) and then fires `onCorrectAnswer`, which is what each call site uses to actually advance (add a gas dash / extend the lockout streak / award Bakery coins and start the next round). Previously, both sites fetched a *new* random question after a miss and required an extra manual "Next question"/"Answer another" click even after a correct one; this collapses both into the single retry-until-correct-then-auto-advance shape the shared mockup specifies. Numeric thresholds/amounts are all unchanged (10-in-a-row-doesn't-reset gas lockout math, 5-coin Bakery bonus, dash amounts) — only the click-flow around them changed.
  - **Bakery Match specifics:** `recordBakeryQuestionAnswered` (progress toward the 100-question Cake Character unlock) used to fire once per attempt, since the old screen only ever allowed one attempt per round; it now fires once per round on the eventual correct answer instead, keeping the same "one count per round" cadence rather than double-counting retries. The old Challenge Screen's "Round N Complete! You cleared X treats" heading and its inline "want different questions next round?" `QuestionSourcePicker` don't fit the shared screen's fixed layout (no room for extra header/footer content) — the round-complete heading is simply gone (the pip progress + card fills the same "you're between rounds" moment), and the question-source picker moved up into the main "playing" view itself (a collapsible "Want different questions?" `<details>`), so the feature is still there and now available any time during a round, not just between rounds.
  - **Question images**: both call sites already carried an optional per-question `imageUrl`/`imageAlt` (`MCQuestion` in `types.ts`) and already rendered it in their old modals, so `QuestionScreen`'s optional image block just passes those straight through — no new image system needed, nothing deferred here despite the task brief flagging this as a possible follow-up.
  - **Needs a live visual check — no 3D/browser rendering available in this sandbox to confirm any of it:** the exact pip/card/button spacing and colors against the approved mockup, the toolbox popover's position/sizing, the Baloo 2/Lexend fonts actually rendering (vs. falling back), the exit-confirm voluntary-Fill-Up behavior change called out above, and the new retry-in-place answer flow on both the Gas Pump lockout and a Bakery Match round transition end to end.

- **SHIPPED (2026-09-25) — direct teacher report: "all the questions have answer a as the correct answer. ensure both questions and answer orders are shuffled."** Question *order* was already fine (`ensureQuizState`'s `shuffleQuestions ?? true` in `store.ts` already defaults on) — the real bug was answer-choice order. Root cause, confirmed by reading the authoring flow: `QuizEditor.tsx`'s `blankMC()` defaults a brand-new question's `correctIndex` to `0`, and there is no shuffle applied anywhere at display time in three of the four places a student sees a multiple-choice question — so whichever slot a teacher happened to type (or leave) the correct answer in at authoring time is exactly the slot a student sees it in, every time. `QuizTask.tsx` had the machinery to fix this already (`mcOrder`, a memoized per-question shuffle) but it was gated behind `task.quiz?.shuffleAnswers ?? false` — off by default, and `QuizEditor.tsx` has no UI to ever turn it on, so it was silently dead code. **Fix, four places:**
  1. `QuizTask.tsx` — `shuffleAnswers` now defaults to `true` (same default-on posture as `shuffleQuestions` right next to it), so ordinary teacher-authored quizzes shuffle unless a teacher explicitly opted out.
  2. `PlatformerTask.tsx` — had no shuffle at all (its own old comment said so directly: "MC choices here render in plain order, no shuffle"). Added the same memoized `mcOrder` pattern as `QuizTask.tsx`, keyed on the active question's id, threaded through both the answer buttons and the "Read the choices" TTS line so the spoken letters match what's on screen.
  3. `components/QuestionScreen.tsx` (shared by the Gas Pump lockout and Bakery Match's Challenge Screen — the one place both games actually route MC questions through) — never shuffled either. Added an internal `order` shuffle memoized on `prompt` (this component already resets its own local answer state on a new `prompt`, so the same key keeps the shuffle stable across re-renders of the same question — a wrong pick, the toolbox opening, etc. — without reshuffling out from under the student mid-question).
  4. `lib/autoQuestions.ts`'s generated fallback questions (addition/subtraction/multiplication/division, morpheme definitions) already shuffled correctly at generation time — confirmed, not touched.
  - **Not touched**: `QuizEditor.tsx` itself — the authoring UI still lets/encourages a teacher to type the correct answer anywhere including first; that's fine now that display-time shuffling is universal and no longer opt-in, so authoring order no longer matters to what a student actually sees.

### Bakery Treat Wheel + Bakery Match visual polish pass (direct teacher screenshots vs. the approved mockups)

- **SHIPPED (2026-09-25) — direct teacher report with two screenshots of the live Bakery Match main menu and game board: "game design does not look as good as mockups. fix this."** Re-read the actual approved mockup artboards (`MainMenu.dc.html`/`GameBoard.dc.html`) rather than re-guessing the design — the underlying structure (card layout, wood-tray board frame, pip/XP topbar, decorative floating treats) already matched, but a real gap existed: `.bakery-title`, `.bakery-blurb`, `.bakery-modal-title`, `.bakery-round-label`, `.bakery-xp-pill`, and `.bakery-leaderboard-list li` were all missing `font-family: 'Baloo 2'` (the app's own `:root` falls back to plain Nunito for anything that doesn't set it explicitly), so the mockup's chunky playful display font was silently never rendering anywhere in the live game — the single biggest visible gap between the two. Fixed all six, and made the title itself bigger/bolder (2.1rem → 2.6rem, weight 800) to read more like the mockup's 72px hero treatment. Also increased the floating decorative treat images' size and opacity (they were present in code but small/faint enough to be nearly invisible against the background) so they actually read as the mockup's scattered-treats background texture. **Needs a live visual check — same standing sandbox limitation as every other visual pass in this doc**: whether Baloo 2 is actually loading (vs. silently falling back) can only be confirmed by a teacher looking at the real page.
- **SHIPPED same report (2026-09-25) — a second, separate live message mid-turn about the post-game prize wheel specifically: "1) show the image of each item on this wheel 2) make the wheel pop up larger 3) show an animation of the item recieved with sparckles and a celebretory sound effect."** `BakeryTreatWheel.tsx` already tried to draw each treat's real PNG on its wheel segment (`image:` per item, via the `spin-wheel` library), but at the old 240×240px wheel size with a full text label sharing the same slim 60°-wide segment, the images were too small/crowded to actually read — matches the direct "show the image of each item" ask even though the code was technically already attempting it. Fixed: wheel grown to 360×360 (popup itself grown from `maxWidth: 420` to `560`), `imageRadius`/`imageScale` increased and pushed further toward the rim, label font size raised to match the bigger wheel. **New win-reveal animation**: the won treat's image now pops in with an overshoot-bounce (`wheelWinPop` keyframe) surrounded by 8 sparkle particles (alternating ✨/⭐) that burst outward from the center and fade (`wheelSparkleBurst`, CSS custom properties `--sx`/`--sy` per particle so each one gets its own angle/distance, computed once per win via `useMemo` keyed on the prize so it doesn't jitter on re-render). The "celebratory sound effect" ask is `playAchievementChime()` — already called at the reveal moment before this pass, kept as-is since it already exists specifically for a celebration beat.

## PART C — The Teacher's Own Notes & Raw Ideas

Preserved close to verbatim so intent isn't lost in summarization. Status notes in brackets show what's since been built.

### On the pets system — full original brief

> Given verbatim as a same-session instruction: "Claudia's job is to completely build out the pets component…"

> What is the pet actually for? Pet is for game play interest, taking a break (take a break with your pet lets you do pet training interactions). Claudia should research the neuro game Mightier and reference their SEL gaming to how the kids interact with pets. Pets are HIGHLY reinforcing for my students and a complex pet system will motivate them to complete assignments, answer question sets, and interact with the platform. "I taught it a trick"? "It's the thing I check on first"? Three different builds.

**[Status]** The motivation/reinforcement loop is built (training tied to real task completion, milestones, growth stages). A take-a-break-with-your-pet interaction mode IS shipped: see A29's "🐾 Check on your pet" button (Pets Phase 5, Part B), which offers a trip to Home Room as a real optional regulation-break activity. The specific "Mightier" SEL-gaming research reference was never actually done — see the Parking Lot entry added for it below.

> Where does the pet live, and who sees it? Pets live at home and with enough training (task completion) they can walk beside the player as they go. Public pets can have some status, like completing tasks can unlock certain pets, pets can be earned in daily and bonus wheels, etc.

**[Status]** Home Room care + Town Square companion-follow: built. **Daily-wheel pets: SHIPPED (doc was stale)** — `dailySpin.ts`'s `getDailySpinSegments` already reserves one guaranteed pet-adoption wedge every single day, seeded deterministically per date; Bonus Spin reuses this exact same segment generator (`store.ts`'s bonus-spin resolution calls it directly), so bonus-wheel pets are covered too. Task-completion-gated species unlocks: still not built — every species is open to every student via purchase, the daily/bonus wheel, or the Mystery Box.

> Decay, neglect — pets should have social, health, and food bars. They are like Sims and only decrease when playing the game, not while gone. Soft need decay. Pets never die but they can be traded or sold. Students can have up to 4 pets each.

**[Status]** Built exactly as specified. **Trading between students: SHIPPED this hour** — the Farmer's Market's async barter mechanism now covers pets too (same rarity-tier-only fairness rule already used for fonts/colors/voices), see the Pets section of Part B for the real shape. Selling (for coins, not barter) is still not built.

> How is a pet acquired, and what does that moment feel like? Marketplace purchase, farmers market, daily and bonus wheel, completing quests, can be traded. Each student should start with a coupon... that allows them to select one of the loaded pets for free, any pet of their choice... This should only happen the first time, otherwise they have to buy or otherwise earn pets.

**[Status]** The free-first-pet coupon is built exactly as specified. Direct-purchase and the Mystery Box are built. **Daily/bonus-wheel pets: SHIPPED (doc was stale)** — see the correction above. Quest-completion pets and farmers-market/trading acquisition are not built.

> Does the pet do anything, or is it a companion? Roughly by build cost: companion (idle/follow/react) → caretaking (feed/pet/play/clean) → customization (name it, dress it) → emotional-regulation object.

**[Status]** Companion and caretaking tiers fully built. Naming and Teach a Trick are built; since the 2026-10-06 pets review, teaching is a real cue/trick/treat training session with 8 tricks that unlock as training grows, and learned tricks can be shown off at home and in Town; dressing/accessorizing is not — checked the full asset catalog, zero hat/bandana/collar/bow/scarf/cape models exist to attach, so this is a real asset gap, not a build gap. The emotional-regulation-object tier (Phase 5) already SHIPPED in an earlier hour, not still open as this line previously implied.

> Can two students have the same pet? Every pet type available to everyone unless specifically gifted by the teacher or earned from assignments or quest completion.

**[Status]** Built as the default. Teacher-gift and quest-earned exclusivity is not built.

> Tell me what to do to get animations for the animals/pets.

**[Status]** A logistics question, not a feature request — shipped pet models do walk/idle-animate (birds/fish float instead, by design).

### On the platformer game — original notes

> 1) In platformer game, if you fall in a hole in the ground, you die and lose a heart. 2) Display hearts in right-hand corner of game view screen. 3) Move the to-do-list floating button to top right corner — right now it blocks the screen. 4) Add X/close button where the current to-do list is.

**[Status]** All four built: hole-falls cost a heart, hearts display top-right, the to-do FAB was relocated then folded into the consolidated Town Square pie menu, and a close button exists on the checklist overlay.

### Population & design context, as given

- Students are K-8 with high support needs: autism, ADHD, dyslexia, and other communication disorders.
- Pets, the economy, and social/reward systems are framed as tools specifically for motivation and engagement with this population, not decoration.
- Standing workflow instruction: all development pushes to both the working branch and `main` so changes reach students as soon as they're ready.
