## PARKING LOT — Historical Chat Requests Audited, Not Yet Built

Per the standing Intake Protocol (`.claude/agents/claudia.md`): no teacher input should go undeveloped or get lost. This section is the first real pass at it — extracted from a full-session chat digest (471 of the teacher's own messages across this project's history), grep'd for request-signal phrasing, then each candidate individually verified against the current codebase before listing here (most historical requests, it turns out, have already been built somewhere along the way and are correctly NOT listed below — verified, not assumed). Genuinely still-open items, each concrete enough to build directly when picked up:

- **Style spin-offs, parked 2026-10-04 (Claudia's Style intake, not requested, so nobody builds them by drift):**
  - **Teacher's own character: where does it show?** She said the wardrobe is for "students and me (teacher)" but not where her character appears. Open as STYLE.md Decision 19.
  - **Students designing their own items.** Not requested (Studio and AI are teacher-only). Possible later as a palette-only "Pattern Painter" the teacher approves before use.
  - **Pet accessories (hats for pets).** Surfaced in review, not requested.
  - **"Dress for the Day" life-skills activity** (choose clothing for the weather or an event). Claudia's extension tied to the independence goal; parked until the teacher says yes (Style Phase 7).
- **Computer UI — card/widget-based dashboard redesign: SHIPPED, now A42.** See Part A, A42, including the settled Math/Literacy/Playground sizing decision. Drag-to-rearrange and per-student theming are still open, tracked there.
- **Build Mode punch-list — corrected this hour: 4 of 5 items were already done, last hour's audit under-searched.** One detailed teacher message, several sub-items; a second pass this hour (searching the actually-shared `WallMesh.tsx`/`WorldObjectRenderer.tsx` components too, not just `WorldEditor.tsx` alone, which is what missed these the first time) found:
  - **Wall corner/intersection joining: ALREADY SHIPPED**, not a gap. `WallMesh.tsx` extends each wall's rendered box by its own thickness past both endpoints specifically to cover the seam at a corner ("the same corner-overlap trick most grid-based wall systems, Sims 4 included, use"), per that file's own comment citing this exact teacher report.
  - **Click-and-drag rotation: SHIPPED last hour** (see above).
  - **Door/window placement on a wall: ALREADY SHIPPED**, not a gap. `WorldEditor.tsx`'s `DOOR_WINDOW_RE` gate blocks placing a door/window anywhere except snapped onto a wall (`wallSnapped`), with a live ghost preview.
  - **Movement-collision glitch near placed objects: ALREADY SHIPPED**, not a gap. `blockObstaclesSlide` in `TownSquare.tsx` replaced the old radial-push-out-only approach specifically to fix this reported glitch: try the real diagonal step, slide along one axis if that's blocked, or don't move at all this frame — "same 'move the player slightly over or stop all movement' shape... only ever changes position by an amount the player's own input already implied, so it can't glitch," per that function's own comment.
  - **Student-facing catalog mirroring the teacher's, filtered to owned, with a marketplace indicator: NOT a gap — superseded by a later, more specific teacher decision.** `HomeRoom.tsx`'s own header comment documents a direct later instruction narrowing this down to a small fixed 5-item interior starter catalog ("they don't need any of the other things yet, they have not earned them"), with a real earn-to-unlock system *intentionally* not built yet, same "don't build blind" caution used elsewhere. The full-catalog-mirror ask predates this and was superseded, not left undone.
  - **Genuinely still open: showing only a selected asset's exterior (not interior) when viewed from outside a building.** This is a real backface-culling/hollow-shell-geometry question tied to how each specific house-shell model was authored, not a quick UI fix — the kind of graphics change this project's own sky-texture history (4 failed attempts) warns against building blind. Needs either a live visual check per asset before shipping, or Claudia's input on a safer general approach, not a same-hour fix. Direct teacher framing for the whole punch-list: "Sims 4 is my favorite building platform and i want the controls for mac to be the model for our build mode."
  - **Click-and-drag rotation — SHIPPED this hour.** A new 🔄 handle sits beside the existing 90°/15°-step rotate buttons on `SelectedObjectToolbar` (`WorldEditor.tsx`) — press and drag left/right to spin the selected object freely, same screen-space-delta technique (`dx * sensitivity`) the Town Square click-drag camera rotate (task #67) already uses, just applied to `rotationY` instead of the camera, via `setPointerCapture` so the drag keeps tracking even once the pointer leaves the small handle. The step-button rotate stays exactly as it was — this is a genuine alternative, not a replacement, per the teacher's own "as an alternative to the current button-based rotate" framing. Only in the teacher's Build Mode (`SelectedObjectToolbar` isn't used anywhere else in the app yet, so there's no student-facing Home Room equivalent to keep in sync with this).
- **Farmer's Market — barter/negotiation mechanic — SHIPPED this hour (v1), seasonal-appearance half explicitly deferred.** Direct teacher ask: a separate location (distinct from the regular Store) where students trade items with each other instead of paying coins, framed as a way to teach negotiation as a real skill. Dispatched Claudia first since this touches real ABA/SEL judgment (fairness, no comparative "winner," negotiation-skill mechanism for this population) — her spec is what got built. New `FarmerMarketOffer` type/table (`farmer_market_offers`, permalink `https://github.com/vermontliteracynetwork/Dashboard/blob/e47e6cf/supabase/schema.sql#L269-L278`), store actions (`postFarmerMarketOffer`/`withdrawFarmerMarketOffer`/`acceptFarmerMarketOffer`), a new `'farmers-market'` `WorldObjectRole` (teacher assigns it to any placed object in Build Mode, same mechanism Post Office/Welcome Center already use — no new 3D model/asset needed), and a student screen (`FarmersMarket.tsx`) with a Trade Board (open offers from other students) and My Offers tabs. **Deliberately async/turn-based, not live simultaneous haggling** — there is no presence/online-status system anywhere in this app to build a real-time two-student negotiation session on, so a student posts an offer and any other student can Accept it whenever they next look, the same async shape `chat_messages`/Mailbox already use. **Scoped to fonts/colors/voices only** (the app's owned-catalog items, reusing `Student.ownedFontIds`/`ownedColorIds`/`ownedVoiceIds` directly rather than inventing a new generic inventory system) **and same-kind trades only** (font-for-font, color-for-color, voice-for-voice) as the built-in fairness rule Claudia specified — no free-typed haggling, no cross-kind trades, no rarity/value scoring, and a trade is never shown as a scored/comparative outcome anywhere in the UI. **Explicitly deferred, per Claudia's own spec:** the "appears at certain times/seasonal" half of the original ask — there's no existing pattern for a whole *location's* visibility being date-windowed (only per-item, via `MarketplaceItem.availableFrom/availableUntil`), and building one blind in the same hour as the trade mechanic itself risked rushing both; a teacher can informally treat it as "market season" today by choosing when to mention/open the role. Also deferred: counter-offers beyond a straight accept/decline, free-text negotiation messages (a real moderation surface), avatar/prize trading (prizes are real-world teacher-fulfilled redemptions, not re-tradeable), and a dedicated teacher-side trade log/manager screen (the trade board itself is the only visibility right now).

- **Mightier SEL-gaming research task, never done.** Kayden's original pets brief (Part C) directly instructed: "Claudia should research the neuro game Mightier and reference their SEL gaming to how the kids interact with pets." This was never actually researched or used to ground any pet-system design decision — the pet system that shipped was built from her own brief's other concrete asks (companion/caretaking/customization tiers, decay rules, acquisition paths), not from any Mightier-specific finding. Stays parked until either the research is actually done and folded into a real design note here, or Kayden confirms she no longer wants it. **Update 2026-10-06:** partly addressed with general-knowledge notes only (Pets v2 overview, section 3f); a source-checked pass is still owed.

- **Claudia's code review of the Gallery + Farmer's Market, both fixed this hour.** Neither feature had a review pass before shipping (a busy sprint), so this hour was spent auditing and fixing rather than building something new. Findings and fixes:
  - **HIGH — Farmer's Market accept race condition, fixed.** `acceptFarmerMarketOffer` only checked local client state before swapping owned items; two students accepting the same offer within the realtime-sync latency window could both pass validation, both locally trade away an item, with only one upsert "winning" remotely and the loser's item silently changing with no explanation. Fixed with a real conditional remote write (`acceptFarmerMarketOfferRemote` in `sync.ts`, a Supabase `.update(...).eq('id', id).eq('status', 'open').select()`) — only the caller whose write actually finds the row still `open` is allowed to touch either student's ownership; the loser gets "Someone else already took this trade." `acceptFarmerMarketOffer` is now async; `FarmersMarket.tsx`'s accept handler awaits it.
  - **HIGH — three Farmer's Market student buttons used the teacher-only-exempt `chip-filter-sm` class, fixed.** That class deliberately skips the 44px touch-target floor for teacher management lists; it had leaked onto three real student actions (dismiss trade message, Withdraw, Change item) with no other student route doing this anywhere in the app. Swapped to `btn btn-sm` with an explicit `minHeight: 44`.
  - **MEDIUM — stale duplicate offers, fixed.** A student could post two open offers for the same item; once one was accepted, the other would sit on the board forever silently failing for anyone who tried it, no explanation given. `acceptFarmerMarketOffer` now auto-withdraws any other open offer from the same student for the same item the moment one of them trades.
  - **MEDIUM — em dash in GalleryManager's teacher-facing copy, fixed.** Rewritten as two clauses joined by a colon instead, per this repo's standing "no em dashes in student- or teacher-facing text" rule (this was rendered `<p>` copy, not a code comment).
  - Everything else Claudia checked passed: no leaderboard/comparison, plain-language non-blaming rejection copy, no dead-ends, consistent nav, icon+label pairing, and the shipped code matches what both Parking Lot entries above describe.
- **Playground — meme/personality-quiz/mini-game gallery — image and quiz halves SHIPPED, mini-games still parked.** From an early Playground-overhaul brainstorm, referencing the Kinzoo messaging app's kid-facing marketplace as a model: "a gallery where i can add videos and images for the kids to look through... fun things like memes, mini games, and silly personality quizzes." Images: a new `GalleryItem` type/table (`gallery_items`, permalink `https://github.com/vermontliteracynetwork/Dashboard/blob/9b4505d/supabase/schema.sql#L254-L260`), a teacher `GalleryManager.tsx` and student `Gallery.tsx`, both shipped an earlier hour. Deliberately scoped to images only — `Cinema.tsx` already covers the video half exactly.
  - **Silly Personality Quizzes — SHIPPED this hour, after a Claudia design pass.** Dispatched Claudia first since this is a real ABA/SEL tone call for this population: a typical internet quiz sorts a student into ONE labeled "type," which risks reading as a literal label a rigid/anxious student fixates on, and a peer-visible "silly" result still functions as informal ranking even when the app itself never ranks anything. Her verdict: GO, but not that shape — 4+ silly low-stakes multiple-choice questions (favorite snack/sound, never mood/social behavior/personality), 2+ possible outcomes that are always a silly non-comparative object or vibe ("You're a Disco Waffle!"), never a trait or role label. Built exactly to her spec: new `SillyQuiz`/`SillyQuizQuestion`/`QuizOption`/`QuizOutcome` types and a `silly_quizzes` table (permalink `https://github.com/vermontliteracynetwork/Dashboard/blob/7c5d18f/supabase/schema.sql#L291-L305`), a teacher `SillyQuizManager.tsx` (build questions/outcomes, same teacher-authored-static-content shape as Cinema/Gallery), and a student `SillyQuizzes.tsx` (one question at a time, client-side tag tally with a random tiebreak, unlimited "🔀 Take again" retakes, never framed as a "true self" result). **Results are never persisted and never shown to anyone but the student who took it** — no results table exists at all, per Claudia's explicit "this isn't optional" call on the standing no-leaderboard/no-cross-student-comparison rule (PART D). Reached from a new tile in the Playground alongside Gallery. **Claudia's follow-up code review, this hour:** verified faithful to her own spec on the one "not optional" item — read through `pickOutcome`'s tally/tiebreak logic and the full take-flow, confirmed a result never leaves the student's local component state (no push to the store, no network call, nothing in `schema.sql` to persist it to). Also confirmed the teacher form can't save a broken quiz (orphaned options are filtered out before save, minimums enforced). One real gap found and fixed: the teacher quiz-list delete button fired immediately on one click, unlike every sibling manager (Gallery/Cinema), which all use a two-step confirm before a destructive delete — now matches that pattern.
  - **Still not built:** mini games (distinct from the native games already shipped; no scope has been written for what a "mini game" here would actually be, a real design question, not implementation).

**Items checked and found already shipped** (so they don't need to be re-flagged by a future pass): the Minecraft-style coordinate-plane overlay in Map view (`TownSquare.tsx` — direct teacher instruction, fully built), the "!" priority-conversation indicator over Neighbors that only shows for an unfinished assignment and clears on completion (same file — teacher explicitly asked for exactly this, already implemented, including a documented earlier "always on" version that was replaced), the nonfiction article reader with per-tab highlight colors/note-taking/font-size/TTS (`src/components/ArticleReader.tsx`), the Joke Book's page-turn book UI (task #113), the student Wishlist feedback category, and Transportation's Cars+Boats phases (A6).

- **Expandable map ("lots") — direct teacher ask: "allow me as the teacher to expand what the playable map can be... use arrows to expand each lot. The main lot is the town square." Phase 1 (per-edge Town Square bounds) SHIPPED this pass; multiple named lots and the multi-area student map are still open — see below for exactly what was scoped out and why.** The ask had 5 real parts; researched each against the actual code before building anything (not assumed):
  1. **Expandable bounds — SHIPPED.** The walkable square used to be one fixed radius, `GROUND_HALF = 22` (`townLayout.ts`), referenced identically for both x and z at ~40 call sites across `TownSquare.tsx` (movement/collision clamps for the player, pets, NPC wander, planes, click-to-walk, map-view double-tap teleport) plus `WorldEditor.tsx`'s own placement clamp and ground/grid rendering. Replaced with a real per-edge `GroundBounds { north, south, east, west }` (`types.ts`), each wall independently push-able outward — new `groundBounds` app-wide setting (Supabase `app_settings.ground_bounds jsonb`, same singleton-row live-instant pattern as `ground_texture`/`sky_color`: `schema.sql` line ~762, `sync.ts`'s `pushGroundBounds`/`fetchAll`, `store.ts`'s `groundBounds` state + `expandGroundBounds(edge, deltaMeters)` action + `onAppSettings` wiring). `WorldEditor.tsx` gets a new always-visible "📐 Lot size" panel (top-right of the 3D viewport, dismissible) with 4 press-and-hold arrow buttons, same `useHoldRepeat` pattern the existing object-move arrows already use — each tap/hold pushes one wall out by `GROUND_BOUNDS_STEP` (6m), floored at `GROUND_BOUNDS_MIN` (12m, keeps the fixed spawn point and every hand-placed anchor safely inside) and capped at `GROUND_BOUNDS_MAX` (70m, a judgment call flagged in `townLayout.ts`'s own comment — not a measured technical limit, just untested past that size). **Mechanical refactor, not a redesign:** every one of the ~40 clamp call sites in `TownSquare.tsx` now calls `clampGroundX`/`clampGroundZ` (reads `useStore.getState().groundBounds` imperatively inside `useFrame` callbacks — no extra plumbing needed since `useFrame` already re-runs every frame) instead of the old `THREE.MathUtils.clamp(v, -GROUND_HALF+1, GROUND_HALF-1)`; I classified each site's axis (x vs z) from its own variable name (`targetX`/`targetZ`, `bx`/`bz`, `cx`/`cz`, `nx`/`nz`, `pt.x`/`pt.z`, object-literal `x:`/`z:` keys — every single instance in this file already paired an x-named clamp immediately before its z-named twin, no ambiguous cases) via a scripted pass, then hand-verified the remainder that the script couldn't classify (single-letter `x`/`z` params, `home[0]`/`home[1]`). The overhead Map view's `CoordinateGrid` (grid lines, axis labels, quadrant numerals I-IV, N/S/E/W compass) is now a real bounds-aware rectangle instead of a fixed square, and the map camera's height (`mapHeightFor`, was a static `MAP_HEIGHT` constant) and the decorative ground mesh's visual radius/texture-tile density (`visualGroundRadiusFor` in `TownSquare.tsx`, `groundBoundsMaxExtent`-driven in `WorldEditor.tsx`) both now track the live bounds so an expanded wall never runs off the edge of the rendered grass or falls outside the map's frame. **Judgment call, stated plainly:** `WorldEditor.tsx`'s own ground-plane mesh/grid-helper backdrop (the teacher's 3D editor view, not the student's) is sized off the single largest wall (`groundBoundsMaxExtent`) rather than a true off-center asymmetric rectangle — a wall not yet expanded just gets a little extra green backdrop past its real edge, which is harmless (placement/movement is still enforced by the real per-edge clamp, this is only the visual backdrop), and safer than guessing this mesh's rotation-sign convention for an off-center position with no way to visually verify it in this sandbox. **Shrinking a wall is deliberately not exposed** — the store action supports negative deltas and floors at `GROUND_BOUNDS_MIN`, but there's no check yet for whether shrinking would strand an object a teacher already placed near the old edge, so growth-only ships as the safer v1; a real "can this wall safely shrink" check (scan `worldObjects`/`layoutOverrides` for anything past the new line) is the natural follow-up if she asks for it. **This is real 3D rendering this sandbox cannot visually verify — needs a live teacher look**, specifically: does the Lot panel's arrows read clearly, does the expanded ground actually look continuous (not a seam) when a wall is pushed past the old `GROUND_HALF*4` visual radius, and does the Map view's rectangle (vs. the old always-square grid) still read clearly to a student.
  2. **Multiple named lots/areas, added the way a Room is added — NOT built this pass, scoped honestly instead of forced.** Researched both precedents named in the ask before deciding: `HomeRoom.tsx`'s "add a room" flow is a student picking a `kind` from `HOME_ROOM_SIZES` (fixed footprints: large/medium/small/xsmall/closet/yard) that gets appended to that student's own `HomeRoomDef[]` — a genuinely reusable "pick a size, get a new footprint" pattern, but scoped to one student's private floor plan, not a shared class-wide area. `IslandBuild.tsx` (Creative Island, `/world/island`) is real precedent that a *second* shared playable 3D area can exist and be reached from Town Square (role `'island-dock'` → this route) — but read closely, it is a **994-line, fully self-contained, one-off implementation**: its own fixed `ISLAND_RADIUS = 24` (not the new `GroundBounds` system), its own player-movement component, its own object-collision math, its own camera — none of it shares code with `TownSquare.tsx` beyond `WorldObjectRenderer`. It is not a generic "lot" abstraction a teacher could invoke N more times; it's confirmation that *a* second area is buildable, not a reusable engine for arbitrary many. Building genuine "teacher names a new lot, it gets its own ground/objects, students travel there" requires either (a) generalizing `TownSquare.tsx` itself to render N lots (a real architectural change to a 4800+ line file built around one implicit global area — every NPC-wander/obstacle-footprint/building list would need a `lotId` scope) or (b) stamping out `IslandBuild.tsx`-style one-offs per lot (fast per-lot but duplicates ~1000 lines of movement/collision/render code each time and drifts from Town Square's own mechanics, e.g. no vehicles/pets/Neighbors on the Island today). Neither is a same-pass addition on top of Phase 1's bounds work without real risk of a half-built area a student could get stuck in (this app's standing "never gets stuck, never a dead end" bar) with zero way to visually confirm it works in this sandbox. **Parked, not abandoned** — a concrete design exists (option (a), a `lotId`-scoped `WorldObject`/`groundPatches`/NPC system with a `Lot { id, name, groundBounds, travelObjectId }` table, teacher "add lot" flow modeled on `HomeRoom`'s size-picker UX, and a travel mechanism modeled on the Island's role-based dock/teleport point) for whoever picks this up next.
  3. **Student-facing map showing multiple areas — NOT built, blocked on #2.** `TownSquare.tsx`'s existing map toggle (`CoordinateGrid`, updated in #1 above to be bounds-aware) only ever renders the one area it's already inside; a multi-area equivalent needs #2's lot data to exist first (what would it even list). No changes made here beyond what #1 already required.
  4. **Ground painted with different textures/colors per area — no gap found; already covered by the existing single-area system (see #5), and there's no second area yet (#2) for "per area" to mean anything beyond what already exists.**
  5. **Free-hand texture brush ("dirt and such... draw pathways and gardens") — confirmed already shipped, not rebuilt.** Read `paintGroundAt`/`handleGroundPointerMove`/`handleGroundPointerDown` in `WorldEditor.tsx` before assuming a gap: the ground-paint brush (`paintMode === 'groundPatch'`) is already continuous and unsnapped — explicitly NOT grid-snapped, per its own existing comment ("a pond or patch of dirt reads more natural free-form than grid-locked"), painting a new circular patch every half-radius of pointer movement while dragging, exactly the mechanic needed to draw a winding path or garden bed by hand. `GROUND_TEXTURE_OPTIONS` already had 9 real textures beyond grass (Clover, Dirt, Sand, Snow, Stone, Cobblestone, Wood, Arcade Carpet, Water — all real files already uploaded under `public/world/textures/wests/` and `public/world/textures/`, confirmed on disk, not placeholders) with an adjustable brush radius (0.5-10 units) and an Erase toggle — this was never actually capped at "grass/water mixed regions" the way the task brief's own second-hand history suggested; that scope only ever applied to the water-detection helper (`isWaterAt`/`WATER_TEXTURE_PATH` in `townLayout.ts`, used for boat placement), not the paint tool itself. **One small real addition, not a rebuild:** added a `'Path'` texture option (`/world/textures/wests/paving%202.png`, a second real uploaded stone-path variant distinct from the existing wider "Stone"/Cobblestone tiles) since the ask specifically named "pathways" and a second, narrower-reading path texture rounds that out. Everything else about this request was already fully built.

- **Match-3 (Candy Crush style) minigame with question sets gating between turns — Kayden asked about wiring in an existing Kotlin Candy Crush clone (github.com/TobseF/Candy-Crush-Clone); not buildable as a direct integration.** That repo is Kotlin Multiplatform on the KorGE engine, a completely different stack from this app's Vite/React/TypeScript, with no compatible hook for pausing on a real question between turns short of forking their source and standing up a separate Kotlin/Gradle build pipeline. Claudia's read: the underlying ask (short match-3 bursts broken up by real retrieval-practice questions) is sound and fits this population well, gentler motor/timing demand than the existing Platformer, though it draws on visual pattern-matching rather than movement skill, so it's a different competence channel, not a replacement for A15. Two open options, teacher has not yet chosen either: (1) reuse the existing Platformer + Quiz task type (A15) as-is, since it already delivers "real game, real question set gates between turns," just not match-3 shaped, or (2) scope and build a genuinely new native match-3 minigame in this app's own stack, using the Kotlin repo only as a mechanics reference (match/cascade resolution logic), never as source code. Stays parked until Kayden confirms which path, or declines both.

---

## PART D — Standing Design, Safety & Accessibility Rules

### Engagement over sensory caution (STANDING TEACHER OVERRIDE, 2026-10-04, applies to every feature, past and future)

- Kayden, verbatim: "sensory overload in terms of animations and music, sound effects is not a specific concern for the two students i am creating this for. in fact, those are huge drivers of engagement and should be included. note for all futre consideratiolns"
- Animation (bounce, sparkle, glitter, glow, trails), music and sound effects are among the biggest engagement drivers for the two students this platform is built for. Include them and design them well. Do not cut, mute, or tone down a feature on generic sensory-load grounds, and do not add "calm only" filters, busy-pattern warnings, or no-autoplay rules for sensory reasons. This supersedes Claudia's general sensory-load guidance (also updated in `.claude/agents/claudia.md`).
- Still kept, because they are safety/access rather than taste: photosensitive-seizure safety (no large-area flashing faster than 3 times per second); the existing Less Motion and sound toggles stay available as opt-in per-student settings, never defaults; clarity rules (meaning never carried by flashing or color alone, no interruptions mid-task) still apply.
- Already reflected in: Style (STYLE.md: animated and sound-making items are a feature, Studio effects shelf, the busy-pattern check and "Calm styles only" filter removed).

### Style rules (added 2026-10-04 with the Style design, apply once Style ships)

- **Comfort Gear is always free.** Any Style item that works as a sensory or access tool (ear defenders, glasses, hearing aids, wheelchair set) is never sold, time-limited, or earn-gated.
- **No manufactured scarcity or status in any shop.** No rarity tiers, no "owned by N," no countdown timers, no numbered editions.
- **No weapons anywhere in Style**, including held items and AI prompts.
- **Students never see a generation or prompt box.** All AI features are teacher-only, teacher-reviewed, spend-capped, and logged.
- **A student's look changes only when that student changes it** (or when the teacher uses Lock look for them). Published item art is frozen.
- **Unlock moments are queued to safe moments**, never mid-question.

### Regulation & safety

- Help/calm-down (QuietTool) lives inside the Town Square pie menu now (a deliberate, teacher-confirmed override of the earlier "never gated" rule — see A1). Outside that one specific override, the general principle still holds everywhere else: a student's path to help should never require more than the standard navigation depth every other screen uses.
- Zero-weapons rule enforced automatically in the world-object catalog.
- A hard interruption (Wizard ThunderSword) always still leaves Help/calm-down reachable, even while otherwise non-dismissable.
- Reinforcement schedules follow real ABA principles: continuous reinforcement for shaping (pet training only from genuine task completion), and any variable-ratio mechanic (Mystery Adoption Box) is designed to never produce an empty/"nothing" outcome.
- Pets are the highest-dopamine part of the platform (teacher, 2026-10-06); every pet tradeoff is decided in favor of engagement. Pets must read as middle school, never babyish. Grammar inside pet training is wrapped, not tricked: the student always sees they are building a sentence, the teacher always sees the skills, and nothing a pet needs for comfort sits behind grammar.
- Pets and the economy never punish absence — decay only progresses during active play, floors above zero, pets never die.
- No leaderboards or cross-student comparison anywhere in the platform.

### Accessibility

- WCAG 2.1/2.2 AA is the standing bar: 44×44px minimum touch targets, 4.5:1 minimum text contrast, consistent navigation placement (deviations need an explicit, documented reason).
- Every screen keeps a visible way back to Town Square/Home — a genuine dead end is a real bug.
- Read-aloud (TTS) available everywhere a student reads or types, including reading back any text the student has selected or highlighted. Voice-to-text (STT) was removed app-wide by direct teacher instruction (2026-09-22) — typing/tapping is the only text-entry method generally. Scoped exceptions exist only where directly requested: Literacy Manipulatives' Text Box/Symbol Sentence/Sentence Formula fields (A23), and the Zones of Regulation check-in's "leave a note" field (A46), both reusing the same `useTextBoxVoiceToText`-pattern hook, transcribed to text, never stored as audio.
- App-wide dyslexia-friendly font toggle; color is never the only signal (always paired with icon/text).

### Copy & tone

- No em dashes anywhere in student- or teacher-facing copy.
- Errors explain what went wrong and what to do next, in plain language.

### Process

- Claudia (in-house game-design/instructional-design/gamification reviewer) audits concepts, mechanics, and shipped code against ABA/SEL research and this platform's standards. She does not build features herself.
- **Every new design doc Claudia produces gets written to a file under `docs/` in this repo, and linked from this doc's "Related documents" table** — a doc that only reaches the teacher's inbox (a PDF, a chat message) is not something Claudia can reference on a future dispatch. This rule exists because that exact failure happened once already.
